### fileops-b1: Empty Trash follows a symlinked info/ in a drive's trash and deletes whatever it points at, with no question

- **Severity:** critical; kind security
- **What is wrong.** New in batch 2: Empty Trash now empties every mounted drive's trash. all_trash_dirs() admits a volume trash when `.Trash-$uid` and `files/` are real directories owned by the user, but never looks at `info`. empty_trash() then calls read_dir(td.info()), which follows a symlink, and queues every entry found there for permanent delete. delete_one() does lstat + remove_dir_all on `<stick>/.Trash-1000/info/<name>`; the symlink is an intermediate component, so the real directory behind it is deleted recursively. These paths are not in `items`, so the confirmation never names them, and when no trash holds a real item the code skips the question entirely (`if items.is_empty() { self.delete_permanently(paths, ...) }`). I reproduced the exact collection + delete_one logic in a scratch folder (review/C1/info_link.rs): with `info -> ../../../victim_home` the trash passed both admission checks, zero items were shown, no question was asked, and the victim folder's contents were deleted. At HEAD only the home trash was emptied, so this did not exist.
- **Trigger.** A removable drive (ext4/btrfs/xfs/f2fs with the folder chowned to uid 1000, or NTFS where every file is owned by the mounting user) carries `/.Trash-1000/` with an empty real `files/` and `info` as a symlink to `../../../../../../../home` (extra `..` stop at /, so no user name or mount depth is needed). The owner mounts the drive in Fox, and at any later time while it is mounted right-clicks Trash in the sidebar and picks Empty Trash. If the home trash is empty there is no dialog at all; otherwise the dialog lists only the genuinely trashed items. Fox then runs remove_dir_all on `/run/media/alva/<label>/.Trash-1000/info/alva`, i.e. the whole home directory, permanently.
- **Status:** FIXED. all_trash_dirs() admits a drive's trash only when its info/ is a real folder of the user's (or absent); Empty Trash lists a half only when it is a real directory, queues from info/ only plain .trashinfo files, and the no-question shortcut now covers nothing but such record files.

### fs-view-b1: git badge scan still runs repository-chosen commands: Fox and git disagree about `.git/commondir`

- **Severity:** critical; kind security
- **What is wrong.** fs-1 is not closed. Fox decides which config to vet from its own reading of `<git_dir>/commondir`, then starts git with only `--git-dir`; git re-derives the common dir from the same file by different rules and reads the config there. Three differentials, all confirmed with git 2.55 and with Fox's real repo.rs/config.rs compiled into a harness (scan_allowed returned Ok(()) each time while Fox's exact `git status` command line ran a `filter.x.clean` command):
1. Lexical vs real path. Fox folds `..` textually (join_lexical); git uses strbuf_add_real_path (symlinks resolved first). `commondir` = `s/..` with `.git/s` a symlink to `../evil/sub`: Fox vets `.git/config`, git reads `evil/config`. A symlinked `.git` plus a relative commondir does the same.
2. Fail-open on a file Fox cannot read. read_small returns None for non-UTF-8 content or more than 1 MiB, and None is treated as 'no commondir' (common_dir = git_dir). git reads raw bytes and strips every trailing \n/\r: a commondir naming a non-UTF-8 folder, or `../evil` followed by 1.1 MB of newlines, sends git to `evil/config`. The second form needs only regular files, so it works from a FAT/exFAT stick or a zip.
3. The scan then also removes GIT_COMMON_DIR from the environment (git_status.rs:371), which is the one thing that would have pinned git to the vetted directory.
The config reader itself held up: about 3,500 random configs accepted by both Fox and `git config --file` gave identical key lists (values differ only in tab vs space inside a value).
- **Trigger.** Extract an archive (or plug in a stick) containing a folder with `.git/{HEAD,objects,refs,index}`, a harmless `.git/config`, `.git/commondir` = `../evil` + 1.1 MB of `\n` (or `s/..` + symlink, or a non-UTF-8 name), `evil/{config,objects,refs}` with `[filter "x"] clean = <command>`, and `.gitattributes` `* filter=x`. Navigate into the folder in Fox. No click needed: within about 100 ms `git status` runs the command as the user, again every 5 s and on every file event.
- **Status:** FIXED. git is now told which common directory to use (GIT_COMMON_DIR = the one whose config Fox vetted) instead of working it out again from .git/commondir by rules of its own; a commondir file that exists but cannot be read as a small text file makes the folder 'not a repository'. New end-to-end test: both crafted layouts run their filter under plain git and do not under Fox's scan.

### audio-b1: Saving tags on a WAV that another program is still writing loses the rest of the recording or leaves the finished file playing short

- **Severity:** high; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** Batch 2 treats a final `data` chunk whose size is 0 or overstated as left behind by a dead recorder and repairs it (riff.rs:106-116, wav.rs:288-310 and the rewrite closure). A file that is still being recorded, copied or exported has exactly the same layout, and nothing checks that the file is quiescent. `read` reports no write_blocker for it.

Both branches were run against live writers in the scratch directory:

- **Rewrite branch** (any ffmpeg-made WAV, because ffmpeg puts LIST/INFO before `data`): `replace_file` copies what exists at that moment and renames the temp over the path. The recorder keeps writing into the now-unlinked inode and exits cleanly. An 8 s ffmpeg recording tagged at 3 s ended as a 3.53 s file (312,450 bytes) with a finalised-looking header; 4.5 s of audio were gone. Fox returned Ok.
- **In-place branch** (no leading tags): the `data` size field is patched to the partial length. With a copy in progress (a finished 705,644-byte WAV written in 16 KiB steps), the save returned Ok. After the copy finished, 5 header bytes differed and ffprobe reported 3.53 s instead of 8.0 s. The audio bytes are all there but lie outside the data chunk, and Fox then refuses the file ("data after its last chunk"). With a live ffmpeg recorder on this branch, two saves returned Ok and the recorder then overwrote the tags.

HEAD already orphaned the writer on the rewrite branch. The in-place header patch is new in batch 2: HEAD left the data size field alone, so a copy that finished still played in full.

The only guard is the length comparison at wav.rs:302. It covers the microseconds between load and open on the in-place branch only. The rewrite branch has no check at all, and `replace_file` has the same gap for an MP3 whose tag outgrows its padding while the file is being copied.
- **Trigger.** 1. Start a recording with an ffmpeg-based recorder to set.wav, or start copying a large WAV into a folder with cp or another program (Fox's own copies use a hidden staging name and are not affected).
2. While it is still running, open Properties on the file, type a Title, press Enter. Fox shows "Saved".
3. Recorder case: when the recording stops, set.wav holds only the audio up to the moment of the save. Copy case: the finished copy plays only up to that point, and Fox reports "This WAV has data after its last chunk that Fox does not recognise".
- **Status:** FIXED. Both writers ask the kernel (a read lease, broken by an ignored signal) whether another program has the file open for writing and refuse if so; where leases are unavailable a file modified in the last 10 s is refused. replace_file re-stats the source after copying and keeps the original if it changed; the in-place paths compare mtime as well as length. New test with a live writer on all three layouts.

### cloud-core-b1: A pass keeps downloading into, and moving pivots for, a folder the guard has just judged to be an empty stand-in: when the real folder returns, stale files are uploaded over the other machine's edits

- **Severity:** high; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** When the scan finds an empty ~/Cloud while the manifest has entries, only the tombstones are held; every other action of the pass still runs. For a path the other machine changed, the table says (local absent, remote r != m, pivot m) -> Download, and actions::download_remote then sets the pivot to r (`ctx.manifest.set(doc.path.clone(), doc.sha256.clone())`). New remote files are downloaded too and get pivots. All of this is written into the stand-in directory.

When the real folder is back, the old file (content m) now reads as (local m, remote r, pivot r) = "changed here only" -> Upload. The write is conditional on the mirror's version, which still matches, so it goes through. The other machine then sees "remote changed only" and downloads the old content over its own edit. No conflict copy is made anywhere. The files that were downloaded into the stand-in read as "deleted here" and are tombstoned (up to 10 without a question), so the other machine moves its new files to the Trash.

Two further effects of the same cause:
- The download makes the stand-in non-empty, so on the next pass (30 s later) `scan_files == 0` is false. With 10 or fewer synced files, the deletions that were held one pass earlier are then sent without any answer. That breaks owner decision 2.
- Answering "Restore them" (the dialog's default) on the stand-in downloads everything with fresh pivots and ends the same way.

Reproduced in a sandbox copy of src/cloud with the crate's own Machine/FakeCloud harness:
- 12 synced files, B edits f03 and adds one file, A's root swapped for an empty directory, one pass, root swapped back: the cloud and B both end with the old "content 3", B's new file is in B's Trash, no conflict copy exists.
- 8 synced files: pass 1 holds 8 (EmptyFolder), pass 2 sends 8 tombstones and B trashes all 8.
- Same result for a sub-folder stand-in (an unmounted volume inside ~/Cloud): 7 tombstones unasked, B's edit replaced by the stale file.

At HEAD this situation tombstoned everything at once, so this is the remaining half of cloud-e2e-1 trigger (b), not a new regression.
- **Trigger.** Preconditions: signed in; the other machine has edits or new files this machine has not pulled; ~/Cloud (or a sub-folder) is for at least one pass an empty but listable directory. That happens when:
- ~/Cloud is a mount point whose volume is not mounted when Fox starts;
- the user pressed "Create folder" in the "Cloud folder is missing" dialog, or ran mkdir, after moving the folder away;
- a volume mounted inside ~/Cloud is not mounted.

On this PC ~/Cloud is on the same filesystem as the home folder, so only the Create-folder/mkdir and sub-mount routes apply here.

Steps: leave the held-deletions question unanswered (or choose "Restore them"), then mount the volume or put the original folder back.

Result: every file the other machine edited in the meantime is replaced there by this machine's older copy, with no conflict copy; up to 10 files the other machine created are moved to its Trash and never arrive here. With 10 or fewer synced files, all of them are deleted from the cloud and trashed on the other machine 30 s after the question first appears, unanswered.
- **Status:** FIXED. A folder in which none of the synced files is present is a stand-in: the pass reports the hold and does nothing else (no downloads, no pivot changes, no clean-up) until the user answers. 'Empty' is now judged by the synced files, so a stray or self-downloaded file cannot lift the hold. New pass test: stand-in, other machine edits, real folder returns: the edit arrives, nothing stale is uploaded, nothing is tombstoned. Not covered (accepted): a sub-folder stand-in, and restoring into a stand-in and then swapping an old copy of the folder back.

### cloud-wire-b1: Nothing stops an older Fox on the second machine from syncing the same account under the old document-id scheme

- **Severity:** high; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** Batch 2 files each document under sha256(path) with a server timestamp in updated_at. HEAD and earlier builds file it under the percent-escaped path with an integer updated_at. There is no guard between the two: no schema marker, no rule in firestore.rules (it is still a plain owner-only `allow read, write`), no version check in the client. The briefing only says 'both machines must run this build'.

What a mixed pair does, checked against `git show HEAD:src/cloud/firestore.rs` and `remote_index.rs`:
- This build ignores every old-scheme doc (the id check above), so nothing the old machine uploads, edits or deletes ever arrives here. Both pills say Synced.
- The old build's `from_fields` has no id check and accepts the new docs (updated_at becomes None). It only sees them on a full list, because its delta query compares against an integer.
- When the old build uploads, it PATCHes its own escaped-id doc, so the path now has two docs. HEAD's `seed_full` inserts by path in listing order, so the last-listed doc wins. Listing is by document id: for a path starting with a digit or an uppercase letter, the hex-id doc can sort after the escaped-id doc. I did not test the ordering against Firestore.
- When the stale new-scheme doc wins, the old build sees local == manifest and remote != manifest, takes arm 3c and downloads the older version over the edit just made there, with no conflict copy.
- The blob of that edit is then referenced only by a doc this build ignores, so this build's clean-up deletes it (next finding).
- **Trigger.** Deploy this build on the PC while the laptop still runs HEAD or an older build, signed in to the same account. On the laptop, edit ~/Cloud/2024/report.pdf (or any path starting with a digit or capital). The laptop uploads the edit under the old id. At the laptop's next full list (a Fox restart, or within 6 h) the PC's doc for that path can win in its index, and the laptop then replaces the edited file with the PC's older version. After 24 h the PC's clean-up also deletes the edit's blob from Storage. In the other direction, nothing the laptop changes ever reaches the PC.
- **Status:** FIXED. This build's documents live in users/{uid}/files2 and its blobs in users/{uid}/blobs2: an older build on the other machine only ever sees its own data, and this build's clean-up never lists the old blobs. Both rules files already cover any path under users/{uid}.

### cloud-wire-b3: Held-deletions dialog names at most 7 files (none in a small window); 'Delete everywhere' then deletes the whole set

- **Severity:** high; kind owner-decision
- **What is wrong.** Owner decision 2 says the user is shown exactly what would be deleted, and nothing they were not shown may be deleted on the other machine. The dialog cannot do that:
- The report carries only the first 40 sorted paths (`guard::SAMPLE_MAX`).
- The dialog draws at most 8 rows of them: 7 names and '...and N more.'
- There is no scrolling.
- The list gets only the rows left after the body text. By my hand calculation (nothing was rendered), a window about 600 px high at scale 1.25 leaves `list_room` at 0: a count and no names.
- The full set exists only in the owner engine's memory. The log line records the count, not the paths.

A TooMany hold is always more than 10 files, so it is never shown in full. 'Delete everywhere' approves every path of the set.
- **Trigger.** Delete a folder of 300 files in ~/Cloud on purpose. Five files in another folder have also gone missing by accident (dragged out of ~/Cloud). The dialog says '305 of 900 synced files are missing', lists the first 7 paths alphabetically (all from the deleted folder) and '...and 298 more.' Click Delete everywhere: the five files nobody was shown are tombstoned, and the other machine moves its copies to the Trash.
- **Status:** FIXED. The sync owner writes the complete held set to ~/.lantern/cloud/held-deletions.json (and logs where) before it publishes the hold. A window loads it and checks it is that very set (the id is recomputed); the dialog lists every file in a scrolling box (wheel, arrow/page keys, a draggable always-visible scrollbar, at least 4 rows even in a short window). 'Delete everywhere' is offered only when the complete, verified list is shown; otherwise only Restore / Decide later, with a line saying why.

### fileops-b2: "Delete permanently instead?" is offered for a mount point, and permanent delete / Empty Trash recurse into mounted filesystems

- **Severity:** high; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** Nothing in trash(), trash_paths() or delete_one() knows about mount points. (a) New in batch 2: a folder that IS a mount point cannot be trashed (slow mounts return NoTrash up front; a local mount fails the rename with EBUSY, which I confirmed), so Fox now raises "<name> can't be moved to the Trash ... Delete it permanently instead?". Confirming runs remove_dir_all on the mount point, which deletes everything on the mounted phone / sshfs server / USB drive and only then fails on the mount point itself. At HEAD the Delete key did nothing here. The dialog gives no hint that the folder is a mounted device. (b) A folder that CONTAINS a mount point is renamed into the Trash with the mount still attached (verified in a private mount namespace: rename of the parent succeeds and the mounted files are visible under the new path); Empty Trash or Delete Permanently then recurses into the mounted filesystem. (b) behaved the same at HEAD, but the Replace flow now also sends such folders to the Trash.
- **Trigger.** (a) An sshfs folder in the home directory, a phone under ~/.lantern/mounts/<phone>, or a drive's folder under /run/media/alva: select the folder, press Delete, read "can't be moved to the Trash: phones and network folders have no Trash" (or "Device or resource busy"), click Delete Permanently, expecting the leftover folder to go. Every file on the server/phone/drive is deleted. (b) Trash ~/mnt while ~/mnt/server is mounted, later Empty Trash: the server's files are deleted.
- **Status:** FIXED. New mount_guard.rs: a folder with something mounted on or below it is never trashed (TrashError::Mounted, shown as a notice, never offered for permanent delete), never deleted by the worker, never moved by copy, never handed to the root delete; sudo rm now runs with --one-file-system --preserve-root=all.

### fileops-b5: Privileged move with Replace: rollback deletes a complete copy when mv only failed to remove part of its source

- **Severity:** high; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** Outside my slice proper (sudo.rs), but it is C1's Replace script. The comment says that after a failed `mv "$1" "$2"` whatever sits at "$2" is "only its own partial copy". Across filesystems mv copies everything, then removes the source; if removing the source fails part-way it exits non-zero with "$2" complete and "$1" partly deleted. The rollback then runs `rm -rf "$2"`, destroying the only full copy; the files mv had already removed from the source are gone from both sides. The old target is restored correctly.
- **Trigger.** Root mode (or the permission fallback): cut a folder on one filesystem and paste it over an existing folder of the same name on another, answer Replace. The source folder contains something root cannot remove after copying, e.g. a file with the immutable attribute or a mount point further down. mv copies all of it, deletes part of the source, fails; the script deletes the copy.
- **Status:** FIXED. move_argv Replace script rewritten: rename-only first (mv --no-copy to a staging name), else cp -a to staging, old item to its Trash slot, staging renamed into place, source removed last; nothing ever removes the real name or the Trash slot. New test; the old rm -rf of the target is gone.

### integration-b2: A symlinked folder inside the Trash can now be entered; Delete there says "removed from the Trash" and permanently deletes the live files it points at

- **Severity:** high; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** Two batch-2 changes that do not fit together. C6 (fs-2) made a symlink to a folder list as a folder (`FileEntry::listed`, is_dir = target's), so it can be clicked into, also in the Trash view. C2 (input-12) made Delete / "Delete Permanently" decide by path shape: `trash::locate()` is purely lexical, so `~/.local/share/Trash/files/<link>/<anything>` counts as "in the Trash". But below a trashed symlink the listing shows the link's live target. Delete on a row there raises `permanent_delete_question` ("Delete “x” permanently? It will be removed from the Trash for good.") and on confirm the worker's `delete_one` resolves the link component and unlinks / remove_dir_all's the real file or folder. At HEAD a symlink to a folder was listed as a file and could not be entered, so this path did not exist. The same applies to a link inside a trashed folder (e.g. a trashed Wine prefix: `dosdevices/z:` -> `/`), and to a link planted in a stick's `.Trash-1000/files`.
- **Trigger.** `ln -s ~/Pictures/album ~/Projects/album`; trash the link `~/Projects/album` with Delete. Open Trash, click the `album` folder (it opens and shows the photos, path under Trash/files/album). Select photos, press Delete, click Delete Permanently on the question that says they are removed from the Trash. The real files in ~/Pictures/album are gone for good.
- **Status:** FIXED. New trash::is_trashed / shows_trash decide by where the item really is (its folder resolved), not by the shape of its path. Delete, drop-on-Trash, Restore and the Trash menu use them: a row below a trashed link to a folder is a live file and goes to the Trash like any other.

### root-b1: Privileged Move with Replace deletes the finished copy when mv's source-removal step fails (cross-filesystem), and trashes the old item before the new one has landed

- **Severity:** high; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** move_argv's Replace script assumes that when `mv -T "$1" "$2"` fails, "whatever it left under the name is only its own partial copy" and removes it with `rm -rf -- "$2"`. That holds for a same-filesystem rename, but not when source and target are on different filesystems (btrfs subvolumes such as @ and @home count: rename returns EXDEV). There GNU mv copies the whole tree first and only then removes the source; if any part of the removal fails, mv exits non-zero with a COMPLETE copy at "$2" and a source that is already partly or mostly deleted (its rm pass carries on past errors). The script then deletes the complete copy and puts the old item back, so everything mv had already removed from the source exists nowhere. As root, removal fails for: a file created in the source folder while it was being moved (rmdir ENOTEMPTY), an immutable file (chattr +i), a mount point or read-only snapshot inside the tree, an I/O error on a failing drive.

This is also the one place where the privileged path does not follow owner decision 1: the old item goes to the Trash first and the new item is then copied straight onto the real name with no staging name. copy_argv (same file) and the unprivileged worker (ops/worker.rs copy_and_land + remove_moved_source) both stage, then trash, then rename, and never delete a landed copy. The no-Replace branch of move_argv is fine (a failed mv leaves both sides alone). Not run against real sudo or two filesystems; this is from reading the script and GNU mv's copy-then-remove behaviour.
- **Trigger.** Any sudo Move whose conflict was answered Replace, across filesystems: (a) root mode on in /opt/app, cut a folder in ~/ (or on a USB drive), paste, name exists, Replace; or (b) outside root mode, a Move with Replace that the worker hands to sudo after a permission error. If one item of the source cannot be removed after the copy (e.g. a program writes a new file into the folder during the move, or it holds an immutable file or a mount point), mv exits 1, the script runs `rm -rf` on the complete new copy at the destination and restores the old item. Result: the old item is back, the new copy is gone, and every source file mv had already removed is lost. The user sees only mv's "cannot remove ..." line in a failure notice.
- **Status:** FIXED. move_argv Replace script rewritten: rename-only first (mv --no-copy to a staging name), else cp -a to staging, old item to its Trash slot, staging renamed into place, source removed last; nothing ever removes the real name or the Trash slot. New test; the old rm -rf of the target is gone.

### cloud-core-b2: Held deletions: the user can see at most 7 file names, and the full list exists nowhere outside the owner process

- **Severity:** medium; kind owner-decision
- **What is wrong.** Owner decision 2 says the user is shown exactly what would be deleted, and nothing the user was not shown may be deleted on the other machine.

The engine publishes only the first 40 paths of a held set (HeldDeletions::sample, the only thing in status.json and in SyncReport). The dialog draws at most 8 list rows (cloud_ui/draw.rs LIST_ROWS_MAX), which is 7 names plus "...and N more.", with no scrolling. The log line carries only the counts.

"Delete everywhere" approves the complete set by its id, so for any held set larger than 8 the user confirms deletions of files whose names were never on screen and are not written anywhere they could look them up. A follower window could not show more than 40 even if the dialog scrolled, because the full set never leaves the owner process.
- **Trigger.** Move a folder with 50 files out of ~/Cloud, or have ~/Cloud empty with 172 synced files. The dialog says "50 of 172 synced files are missing", lists 7 names and "...and 43 more.". Pressing "Delete everywhere" tombstones all 50; the other machine moves 50 files to its Trash, 43 of which the user was never shown.
- **Status:** FIXED. The sync owner writes the complete held set to ~/.lantern/cloud/held-deletions.json (and logs where) before it publishes the hold. A window loads it and checks it is that very set (the id is recomputed); the dialog lists every file in a scrolling box (wheel, arrow/page keys, a draggable always-visible scrollbar, at least 4 rows even in a short window). 'Delete everywhere' is offered only when the complete, verified list is shown; otherwise only Restore / Decide later, with a line saying why.

### cloud-wire-b2: The last check before a blob is deleted counts only documents that sync itself accepts

- **Severity:** medium; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** gc.rs documents this query as the check that does not trust the mirror: 'Firestore itself, asked right now, has no doc with that hash'. But the reply goes through `parse_query` and `from_document`, which drop any doc sync ignores: one filed under another id than sha256(path), one with an unsafe path, one with a missing size or mtime. Those same docs are also absent from the mirror, because `list_all` uses the same filter. So for a blob referenced only by such a doc, all three checks agree on 'unused' and the blob is deleted once it is 24 h old.

The query also uses `limit: 1`, so if the one doc returned is an ignored one, a valid doc with the same hash is not seen either.

Separately, a 200 reply that carries no `document` and no `readTime` (an empty array, or an error object inside the array) is read as 'not in use'. I did not confirm Firestore ever sends that; it is a hardening point, not a demonstrated failure.
- **Trigger.** A doc written by a pre-batch-2 build (see the previous finding) names blob S. This build's next pass after a full list, once S is more than 24 h old: `list_blobs` returns S, the mirror has no doc for it, `sha_in_use(S)` returns false because the only matching doc is filtered out, and `delete_blob(S)` runs. The other machine's uploaded content is gone from the cloud while its doc still names it.
- **Status:** FIXED. sha_in_use reads the raw reply: any document naming the hash counts (also one sync would not accept), and 'unused' is taken only from a reply that plainly says so (readTime, no error); anything else is an error and the blob stays.

### cloud-wire-b4: An open held-deletions question switches to a new set without re-arming its input guard

- **Severity:** medium; kind owner-decision
- **What is wrong.** When a pass computes a different held set while the question is open, the next 250 ms poll replaces the dialog's id and list in place. `armed_at` is left as it was, so the dialog takes clicks at once. A click on 'Delete everywhere' already on its way lands after the swap, and `answer_held` accepts it because the id now matches the engine's. The user approved a set they had no time to read.

The same gap is in `dialog.rs` `answer_held`: when an answer is refused because the set changed, the view is swapped and stays armed, so the second click of a double-click confirms the new set.

`armed_at` is also fixed when the dialog is queued, not when it reaches the front. A question queued behind a failure notice is already armed the instant the notice is dismissed.
- **Trigger.** The question is open for 40 files deleted on purpose. A second deletion is in progress (another folder removed, a script, a drive dropping out), and a pass publishes a larger set. Within a fraction of a second of the swap the user clicks 'Delete everywhere' as intended for the first list. The larger set is approved and tombstoned.
- **Status:** FIXED. A held question that switches to another set (report poll, or a refused answer) is deaf for 0.7 s again and starts at the top of the new list; a question that comes to the front after another dialog was answered is deaf for 0.7 s as well. New test.

### fs-view-b2: A file name can run a shell command through a launcher that puts a field code inside nested quotes

- **Severity:** medium; kind security
- **What is wrong.** A field code inside a quoted argument is replaced by the path wrapped in single quotes, on the assumption that the surrounding text is a shell script in which the code stands bare. Hand-written launchers very often write `Exec=sh -c 'viewer "%f"'` or `Exec=sh -c "viewer \"%f\""`. There the single-quoted path lands inside the script's own double quotes, where `$(...)` and backticks are still expanded. Run against the real exec.rs: `sh -c 'cat "%f" >/dev/null'` with a file named `a $(touch PWNED_EXEC) b.txt` produced argv[2] = `cat "'/…/a $(touch PWNED_EXEC) b.txt'" >/dev/null` and the touch ran. (For ordinary names the same launchers now fail too: the quotes become part of the name.) At HEAD such a code was left literal and the path appended as its own argument, so no file name could reach a shell. The spec calls a field code inside a quoted argument undefined. None of the launchers in /usr/share/applications has this shape today; ~/.local/share/applications was not examined.
- **Trigger.** Have any launcher (user-written, a game or AppImage installer's) with `Exec=sh -c '… "%f" …'` or the `\"%f\"` form and a MimeType Fox maps. Receive a file named e.g. `x $(curl … | sh).png` (archive, download, phone). Open it with that app (default app or Open With): the command substitution runs as the user.
- **Status:** FIXED. Fox no longer writes a file's name into a launcher's script text at all (shell_quote is gone). A code quoted on its own ("%f", --file="%f") is the plain code; a file or URL code that shares a quoted section with anything else makes the Exec line one Fox refuses to run ('has a command Fox cannot read'). New tests with a $(...) file name.

### fs-view-b3: Pinned tabs of another window are erased: the merged list is handed back to Settings but never to the tabs

- **Severity:** medium; kind fix-does-not-fix
- **What is wrong.** For favourites the merged list is adopted by the window (App::persist_favorites reloads the sidebar from it). For pinned tabs nothing adopts it: after a save that changed pins, `settings.pinned_tabs` and `synced` contain the other window's pins, but the app has no tab (and no absent_pins entry) for them. The next time this window writes pins, wayland_loop.rs:915 or store_session (settings.rs:342 `self.pinned_tabs = app.pinned_tab_paths()`) rebuilds the list from its own tabs only; merge_list sees the other window's pin as 'in base, not in mine' = removed here, and deletes it from the file. The other window does not write it back, because its own list equals what it last synced. This is the app-19 loss for pins, now deterministic.
- **Trigger.** Open Fox windows A and B. In B pin a tab on /x. In A pin a tab on /y (file now holds [/y, /x]). Close A (or pin/unpin anything else in A): the file becomes [/y]. Close B: nothing is written. Next start: the /x pin is gone.
- **Status:** FIXED. save_to no longer adopts the file's pinned-tab list (nothing gives the window tabs for it): with only its own pins in 'synced', a pin made in another window is always 'added there' to the merge and stays. New two-window test.

### root-b2: Privileged copy/move/new-folder still run outside root mode, with no badge and no question, whenever Fox's sudo ticket is cached

- **Severity:** medium; kind owner-decision
- **What is wrong.** Owner decision 3 wants root rights visible, scoped and confirmed. Root mode itself now is, but the automatic fallback is not. Any worker copy or move that hits a permission error is handed to `priv_run`, and New Folder / New File do the same on PermissionDenied (src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs lines 912-917 and 939-941). `priv_begin` (src/priv_ops.rs lines 197-203) starts every op except Remove straight away with `sudo -n`. Fox's own `sudo -S` runs leave a ticket keyed to Fox's pid (no tty), valid for sudo's timestamp_timeout (5 minutes by default), and permanently with NOPASSWD. During that time the fallback runs as root with no ROOT badge, no question and no password; for a command under 250 ms nothing is drawn at all (`SudoPrompt::visible`). So the cached ticket acts as the sticky, invisible global switch the decision ruled out: leaving root mode removes the badge but not the root rights of the next paste. A Move takes an item out of a system folder, and a Replace swaps a system file (old one to the Trash), without anything on screen saying it is being done as administrator. The briefing lists this under the implementer's flagged risks ("worth an owner decision"); I report it because it contradicts decision 3 as written.
- **Trigger.** 1) Do any root action in Fox and type the password (e.g. root mode in /opt, New Folder). 2) Leave root mode (badge gone). 3) Within 5 minutes, with no root mode on: paste or drop a file into /usr/local/bin or /etc (answer Replace if asked); or right-click empty space in /opt, New Folder; or, with /etc and home on one filesystem, drag /etc/hosts to the Desktop and choose Move. Each is carried out by `sudo -n` at once: the system folder is changed as root (the file is moved out of /etc, or a system file is replaced) and no administrator dialog appears.
- **Status:** FIXED. A privileged op runs unasked only when visible root mode covers every folder it changes (PendingPrivOp::folders + root_covers). Anything else (permission fallback of a paste, New Folder in a protected place, undo) starts with a question 'This needs administrator rights' / Continue as Administrator; Enter does not answer it. The sudo ticket is dropped (sudo -k) when root mode ends.

### audio-b2: A v2.4 footer flag is trusted without checking the footer exists: the save zeroes the first 10 bytes of audio

- **Severity:** low; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** `total_len` includes 10 footer bytes whenever the v2.4 header sets bit 0x10. `parse` never checks that those bytes are a footer ("3DI" plus a copy of the header). `mp3::write` then treats `total_len` as tag: the in-place path pads over that range with zeros, and the rewrite path copies the audio from `old_total` on.

If the flag is set but no footer follows the frames, the 10 bytes are the start of the first MPEG frame, often the Xing/LAME info frame. This breaks the module's own rule that anything not understood is carried through or refused.

Run in the scratch directory (case m04): a v2.4 tag with flag 0x10 and no footer, one artist edit, write returned Ok, and the first audio frame header was no longer in the file. HEAD behaved the same, but batch 2's claim "v2.4 footer (dropped, tag rewritten in place)" covers this path. I do not know of a tagger that writes such a header, hence low.
- **Trigger.** An MP3 whose ID3v2.4 header has the footer bit set but has no "3DI" footer after the frames. Open Properties, edit any field, Save. "Saved" is shown and the first 10 bytes of the audio stream are overwritten with zeros (rewrite path: dropped).
- **Status:** FIXED. parse() blocks a v2.4 tag whose footer bit is set with no '3DI' footer behind the frames; the save is refused. New test.

### audio-b3: A read-only WAV is rewritten when its tags sit before the audio (the MP3 path refuses, the WAV rewrite path does not)

- **Severity:** low; kind other
- **What is wrong.** `mp3::write` opens the file for writing first so that a file the user may not write is refused. `wav::write` has no such check. Its in-place branch fails with "Permission denied" on a read-only file, but the rewrite branch only needs a writable folder: the temp file is renamed over the read-only original.

Run in the scratch directory (case w14): a 0444 WAV with a leading LIST chunk, write returned Ok, the inode changed, the mode stayed 0444 and the content changed. The same file with tags after the audio returned Err("Permission denied"). Present at HEAD too.
- **Trigger.** `chmod 444` a WAV whose LIST/INFO chunk precedes `data` (every ffmpeg-made WAV) in a folder you can write. Open Properties, edit Title, Save. "Saved" is shown and the write-protected file has been replaced.
- **Status:** FIXED. wav write opens the file for writing up front, like the MP3 writer: a read-only WAV is refused on the rewrite path too. New test.

### audio-b4: The temp-file rewrite drops extended attributes, ACLs and ownership, and detaches hard links

- **Severity:** low; kind other
- **What is wrong.** `replace_file` carries over only the mode bits. The new inode has no user.* extended attributes, no ACL, the saving user as owner, and it is no longer the inode other hard links point at. The parent directory is not fsynced after the rename either.

This path runs for every WAV with tags before the audio (all ffmpeg output) and every MP3 whose tag outgrows its padding. The in-place paths keep all of this, so behaviour depends on the file's layout.

Run in the scratch directory: `user.xdg.origin.url` set on a WAV and an MP3 was gone after one save on each (inode changed). A second hard link to a WAV kept the old content (case w15). Unchanged from HEAD.
- **Trigger.** Save a tag edit on an ffmpeg-made WAV, or an MP3 edit that outgrows the tag's padding, where the file carries user xattrs or an ACL, or has a second hard link. After "Saved" the attributes are gone and the other link still holds the old tags.
- **Status:** FIXED. replace_file carries the owner and every extended attribute (ACLs included) to the new file, sets the mode last, fsyncs the folder after the rename, and refuses a file with hard links instead of splitting them (in-place saves keep working for those). New test.

### audio-b5: "Only the fields you changed" is measured against the file at save time, not against what the dialog loaded

- **Severity:** low; kind fix-does-not-fix
- **What is wrong.** `apply_changes(old, new)` is documented as "`old` is what the editor showed", and the briefing says the writers pass "the merged view the user saw". Both `mp3::write` (line 351) and `wav::write` (line 237) pass `current`, re-read from the file at write time. `new` is the editor's full snapshot: `AudioEdit::save` sends `current_tags()`, all eight fields plus artwork taken from `self.meta` as loaded when the dialog opened.

If the file's tags changed between opening the dialog and Save, every field that differs counts as edited and is overwritten with the dialog's stale value. That includes `set_artwork(None)`, which removes every picture frame when the dialog loaded before a cover existed. `id3v1::update` and the WAV INFO edits use the same `current`.
- **Trigger.** 1. Open Properties on a track in ~/Cloud.
2. Cloud sync (or another tagger or DJ tool) replaces the file with a version that has a new artist, key or cover.
3. Change only Title in the still-open dialog and Save.
The newer artist, key and cover are reverted to what the dialog loaded (the cover is deleted if there was none), and that file is then synced back as the newest version.
- **Status:** FIXED. The editor passes what it loaded (write_from(path, shown, tags)); the writers apply only the fields that differ from that, onto whatever the file holds now, for ID3v2, RIFF INFO and the ID3v1 trailer. New test: another program's artist, key and cover survive a title edit.

### background-b1: Unreadable folder (or dead phone mount) is still shown as an empty folder, on both the direct and the off-thread listing path

- **Severity:** low; kind other
- **What is wrong.** Not a regression: HEAD behaved the same. Reported because the review brief asks for an error instead of an empty list, and batch 2 added the information needed and then discards it. fs::read_directory now returns None when read_dir fails, but both consumers turn None into an empty Vec: fs::list_directory (every local reload, tab reload, tree expansion) and poll_dir_loads (slow mounts). The pane then draws nothing and the status bar says "0 folders, 0 files" (sections/status.rs), which cannot be told apart from a folder that really is empty. On a slow mount a failed refresh also replaces a populated listing with the empty one and drops the selection.
- **Trigger.** 1) Open a folder the user cannot read (a root-owned 0700 directory inside the home folder, /root, lost+found). Fox shows a blank pane and "0 folders, 0 files". 2) Browse a phone folder, pull the cable, press F5 or wait for a refresh: the listing fails with ENOTCONN and the folder shows as empty. In both cases the user is told the folder is empty when it is not; deleting the 'empty' folder (through the root path for case 1) removes contents they never saw.
- **Status:** FIXED. A folder whose listing failed is remembered (fs::is_unreadable): the pane and the status bar say 'This folder can't be read' instead of showing an empty folder, and a slow-mount refresh that fails keeps the listing that was on screen.

### background-b2: During a format the drive's sidebar row disappears, so 'Formatting...' is not shown and a hidden Format dialog cannot be brought back

- **Severity:** low; kind fix-does-not-fix
- **What is wrong.** The busy label and is_formatting() are found by matching the job key (the row's device, e.g. /dev/sdb1) against the rows in app.drives; the sidebar draws only app.drives. format_drive_ext4 formats the whole disk: UDisks wipes the signatures and the partition table first, so the partition node goes away, and the whole-disk node has no FSTYPE until mkfs closes the device. fs::collect_unmounted skips nodes without a filesystem type, so the device watcher sends a list without that drive for the whole mkfs. Result: no row, so no "Formatting..." label, and open_drive_format_dialog (which needs drive_by_device and a row to right-click) cannot reopen the dialog. The briefing's claims 'the sidebar row says so' and 'Format... on that drive brings the dialog back' therefore do not hold while the format actually runs. After pressing Hide, nothing on screen says a format is in progress until the status note at the end; a drive that vanished from the sidebar reads as 'done, unplug', which is the broken-filesystem case fs-13 was about. Reasoned from the code and UDisks/lsblk behaviour; not run against hardware.
- **Trigger.** Right-click a USB stick, Format to ext4, confirm, press Hide. Within a second the stick's row leaves the Devices list and stays gone until mkfs finishes; no 'Formatting' text anywhere, and no row to reopen the dialog from.
- **Status:** FIXED. While a format runs the status bar says 'Formatting <name>… keep the drive plugged in', whether or not its dialog is shown; drive_busy / is_formatting also match by the whole disk, so the row the disk shows as during and right after mkfs is marked busy and the dialog can be reopened from it.

### background-b3: Quick Look's single-flight lock: one loader that never returns blocks every later preview

- **Severity:** low; kind regression
- **What is wrong.** media-1 made loaders take a process-wide mutex (TURN) for the whole load. load() opens whatever the entry is: load_text does File::open + read with no file-type check, and ffmpeg_frame_png waits on ffmpeg with no timeout. A FIFO (open blocks until a writer appears), a tty or other blocking device node, or an ffmpeg that hangs holds TURN forever. Every later QuickLook::open spawns a thread that waits on TURN and never delivers, so each overlay stays on "Loading..." until Fox is restarted, and ql.loading() keeps needs_anim true (60 fps while the overlay is open). At HEAD each preview had its own thread, so a blocked one only affected itself. file_info::build_info already has this guard ('Regular files only: opening a FIFO blocks this (single) worker for good'); Quick Look does not.
- **Trigger.** Select a named pipe (mkfifo in any folder, or one under /run/user or /tmp) or a tty under /dev and press Space. Press Esc, select a normal picture, press Space: it never loads. Same for every file until restart.
- **Status:** FIXED. The Quick Look loader previews regular files only (a FIFO or tty gets 'No preview available'), and ffmpeg (Quick Look and thumbnails) runs with a 20 s deadline through the new bg::output_with_deadline, which git uses too.

### background-b4: Every device-list update stats all favourites on the render thread

- **Severity:** low; kind regression
- **What is wrong.** refresh_favorite_availability() calls path.exists() for every favourite that is not on a known slow mount. The watcher sends a new list whenever anything in it differs, and Drive carries used/free bytes, which change with any write to the system disk; the frame path kicks the watcher every 2 s. So while the window is in use the favourites are stat'ed on the UI thread about every 2 s (at HEAD: once, at startup). For local paths that is microseconds. For a favourite under an automount point that is not mounted at that moment (fstype 'autofs' is not in is_slow_fstype; trash/mod.rs already special-cases it) each stat triggers a mount attempt and blocks until it succeeds or times out.
- **Trigger.** A favourite inside an x-systemd.automount / autofs share whose server is unreachable (NAS off, laptop away from home). While the Fox window is being used, the UI thread blocks in exists() for the mount timeout, again on each device-list update.
- **Status:** FIXED. Favourites are re-checked only when what is mounted where changed (not on every free-space update), and a favourite under an autofs mount point is taken at its word like one on a slow mount.

### cloud-core-b3: A confirmation also releases deletions the user was never shown: the share limit is applied only to what is left after removing the confirmed paths

- **Severity:** low; kind owner-decision
- **What is wrong.** On the pass that applies a confirmation, candidates not in the confirmed set are judged "as if the confirmed ones were not there". Up to max(10, 25 % of the manifest) additional deletions therefore go out in the same pass without being shown, although the pass as a whole deletes far more than the limit that caused the question.

The briefing claims "Any deletion the user was not shown is still held"; that is true only for the ScanFailed and EmptyFolder reasons.

Reproduced in the sandbox: 100 synced files, 30 removed -> held (TooMany, 30 shown). Confirmed, then 20 more removed before the confirming pass. That pass sent 50 tombstones and reported nothing held.
- **Trigger.** A large folder is still disappearing when the question is answered: a slow move-out or rm in progress, a failing or unmounting disk. The user confirms the 30 files listed; the next pass also deletes up to 25 % more of the library from the cloud and from the other machine, without a question.
- **Status:** FIXED. The remainder of a confirming pass is judged against the manifest less the confirmed set, as the next pass would judge it: 30 confirmed plus 20 new gives 30 released, 20 held. New test.

### cloud-core-b4: A symlinked folder inside ~/Cloud is never scanned but is written through: downloads land outside the sync root, and its paths are then deferred on every pass forever

- **Severity:** low; kind security
- **What is wrong.** The scan skips symlinks without marking them unknown. Paths under a symlinked folder therefore read as plain "absent" while create_parents and File::create follow the link.

What follows:
- A remote doc `link/x` is downloaded to the link's target, outside ~/Cloud. is_safe_rel_path only checks the string. Only new files can be created this way: an existing file makes the action defer.
- On every later pass the path is (absent, live, pivot) -> TombstoneRemote, or Download after a "Restore". Both stop at `file_at`, which follows the link in the parent, and return Deferred. Nothing is deleted, but each such file logs one line per pass, the status stays "Syncing..." for good, and remote edits never arrive.
- If the folder is more than 10 files and more than 25 % of the manifest, the held-deletions question appears for files that are not missing, and neither answer settles it.
- notify adds its recursive watches with follow_links(true), so changes under the link target wake the engine for paths the scan never looks at.

Reproduced in the sandbox: B has `Music` as a symlink to a folder outside its root; A's three songs were written into that outside folder; every following pass reported deferred=3; A's later edit of a song never reached B.

The scan skipped symlinks at HEAD too; there the same setup ended in real tombstones, so this is safer than before but still inconsistent.
- **Trigger.** On one machine make ~/Cloud/Music a symlink to a folder on another disk, while the other machine has a real Music folder with synced files. The files are created in the link target outside ~/Cloud, the pill never leaves "Syncing...", fox-cloud.log gains one line per file every 30 s, and later changes to those files do not sync in either direction.

With a compromised account, a remote doc can create new files anywhere a symlinked folder in ~/Cloud points.
- **Status:** FIXED. The scan marks a symlink (and everything below a linked folder) unknown instead of skipping it: nothing is downloaded through it, nothing below it reads as deleted, no deferral loop. create_parents refuses to create folders through a link. New scan test.

### cloud-wire-b5: An answer to the held question is only read between passes and expires after 10 minutes

- **Severity:** low; kind half-finished
- **What is wrong.** The UI lane assumed the engine picks an answer up between two files. It does not: `take_answer` runs only at the top of the loop, and `pass()` blocks for the whole pass. `guard::take_answer` then discards an answer older than `ANSWER_TTL_MS` (10 min) and deletes the file. An answer given during a pass that lasts longer than 10 minutes is silently dropped when the pass ends. Nothing is deleted wrongly; the failure is that the answer does nothing.
- **Trigger.** A hold is on screen. Before answering, the user drops a few GB into ~/Cloud, which starts a long upload pass. They click Restore or Delete everywhere. The pill says 'Applying your choice...' for 10 minutes, then the question opens again. Each answer is lost unless it is given within the last 10 minutes before the pass ends.
- **Status:** FIXED. An answer given while a long pass ran is accepted when that pass ends (it still has to name the set that pass recomputed); the 10-minute lifetime only applies to answers older than the pass.

### cloud-wire-b6: A revoked sign-in shows as a quiet 'signed out' in every window except the one that owned sync

- **Severity:** low; kind fix-does-not-fix
- **What is wrong.** On a revocation the owner removes the session file (`forget_revoked`) and publishes SignInRequired. A follower thread notices the missing file first and ends itself as SignedOut, before it reads the owner's last report. `poll_cloud` then computes `revoked = false`, so that window gets the muted 'Sign in to sync' pill, which is drawn only inside ~/Cloud. The loud 'Cloud sync stopped: sign in again' pill that C9 describes for cloud-net-10 appears only in the owner process.

App state is cleared correctly everywhere, and the Cloud button offers sign-in. The gap is only that the stop is not announced.
- **Trigger.** Two Fox processes are running; the first one started owns sync. Change the account password elsewhere. The follower window shows nothing outside ~/Cloud. If the owner is closed, no window says that sync stopped.
- **Status:** FIXED. A follower that finds the session file gone looks at the owner's report (now, and once more a tick later) and ends as Revoked when it says SignInRequired, so every window shows 'Cloud sync stopped: sign in again'.

### cloud-wire-b7: The longer pill labels run into the progress strip and its cancel button; the pill is a 34 px target

- **Severity:** low; kind regression
- **What is wrong.** The short-label fallback protects only the counts at the left of the bar. The progress strip is centred, up to 460 px wide, and drawn after the pill, with its cancel button at its right end. The new labels ('Sync paused: 43 files missing', 'Cloud folder is missing, sync paused') make the pill roughly 320 to 400 px wide; at HEAD it was about 150 px. By my arithmetic (nothing was rendered), the pill reaches under the strip and its cancel button in windows narrower than about 1100 px at scale 1. The strip's zones are added later, so they win clicks there. The pill's label is text on the same layer and shows through the strip. The centred status note overlaps the same way.

The pill's click zone is the status bar height, 34 px before scale (STATUS_BAR_H), below the generous-target requirement. After 'Decide later' it is the only way back to the held question. The implementer flagged the height.
- **Trigger.** A window about 1000 px wide, a copy running, and held deletions (loud pill). The pill's cloud icon and the start of its label sit under the progress strip's cancel button and tail.
- **Status:** FIXED. The pill uses its short label when the long one would reach under the progress strip or status note; in a window too narrow even for that, the strip/note is centred in the space left of the pill. A loud pill's click target is 48 px high (it reaches 14 px above the bar).

### fileops-b3: Clicking the greyed-out Replace button cancels the whole paste

- **Severity:** low; kind regression
- **What is wrong.** The conflict dialog's scrim zone covers the whole window including the panel (draw_overlay_with_scrim adds it over the full backdrop, and lntrn-ui resolves a press to the last zone added that contains the point). The new disabled Replace button registers no zone, so a press on it resolves to ZONE_CONFLICT_SCRIM, which is "cancel": cancel_paste() drops every answer given so far and every item that had no conflict. The new op dialogs avoid exactly this with ZONE_OP_DIALOG_PANEL; the conflict dialog was not given one. Nothing on disk changes, but the paste silently disappears.
- **Trigger.** Copy 50 files onto a phone or network folder where one name exists. The dialog shows Replace greyed out. Click it (a dimmed button is easy to miss with poor eyesight): the dialog closes and none of the 50 files is copied, with no message. Same for a rename conflict on such a folder.
- **Status:** FIXED. The greyed-out Replace button keeps its zone (a press does nothing), and the conflict dialog's panel is a zone of its own: only Cancel or a click outside the panel gives the paste up.

### fileops-b4: A move refused with permission denied on a phone or network folder is still sent to sudo

- **Severity:** low; kind fix-does-not-fix
- **What is wrong.** The package's stated rule is that permission failures on slow mounts are reported, not escalated, because root cannot see a user's FUSE mount. copy_and_land() applies it (`!in_place && !is_slow_path(&item.src)`), and offer_root_delete() applies it for deletes, but the rename branch of a move does not. finish_op then calls run_privileged_items, which raises the password dialog for a command that cannot work.
- **Trigger.** On an sshfs/NFS/CIFS share, cut and paste (or drag-move) a file between two folders of the same share where the rename is refused with EACCES. Fox asks for the administrator password, then shows sudo's error instead of simply reporting "permission denied".
- **Status:** FIXED. rename_into_place escalates a permission error to sudo only when neither side is on a slow mount.

### fileops-b6: Staging items left by a killed copy are never cleaned up

- **Severity:** low; kind other
- **What is wrong.** Answering the leftover-temporary question: a staging item is never mistaken for user data (hidden name, created exclusively, ignored by cloud sync), and a normal failure, cancel or close removes it. But nothing ever removes one that outlives the process: there is no sweep at start-up or anywhere else (the only references to the `.fox-part-` prefix are its creation and the cloud ignore rule). The partial copy stays in the destination folder, or in the home directory for a folder bound for ~/Cloud, invisible unless Show Hidden Files is on, using disk space indefinitely.
- **Trigger.** Start copying a 40 GB folder to a USB drive, then the session is killed, the machine loses power, or Fox is SIGKILLed. The drive keeps a hidden `.fox-part-<pid>-<n>-<name>.fox-tmp` folder of up to 40 GB that Fox never mentions or removes; the drive appears mysteriously full.
- **Status:** FIXED. A listing notices staging items no running Fox is working on (by name and /proc/<pid>/comm) and Fox asks once per item: 'An unfinished copy was left in this folder', Keep / Move to Trash. Nothing is removed unasked.

### fs-view-b4: Terminal=true apps start in a pinned terminal tab, not the visible one, when lntrn-terminal has pinned tabs

- **Severity:** low; kind fix-does-not-fix
- **What is wrong.** The wrapper relies on 'the first run of the script is the window's tab'. lntrn-terminal's startup (lntrn-terminal/src/app.rs:619-620) calls restore_pinned_tabs() before spawn_tab(), and spawn_pinned_tab (tabs.rs:88) also execs $SHELL. So with one or more pinned terminal tabs the script is exec'd several times at once; the `mkdir "$0.ran"` race is won by the tab forked first, which is pinned tab 1. The application (nvim, htop) then runs in that pinned tab, in the pinned tab's folder, while the active tab (the last one spawned) shows an ordinary shell prompt. To the user the app did not open; when it exits it takes the pinned tab's shell with it. Not run in the real terminal (launching apps was out of bounds); derived from the terminal's source.
- **Trigger.** Pin a tab in lntrn-terminal (config.pinned_tabs not empty). In Fox: right-click a .txt, Open With, an entry with Terminal=true (Neovim, htop). A terminal window opens showing a plain shell; the app is running in the first pinned tab.
- **Status:** open. needs a command hand-over in lntrn-terminal (an -e argument); that is another crate. Until then a Terminal=true app starts in the first pinned terminal tab when the terminal has pinned tabs (none are pinned on this PC).

### fs-view-b5: `git status` is waited for without a limit: a FIFO in the repository stops all git badges for the session

- **Severity:** low; kind other
- **What is wrong.** repo.rs takes care never to open a FIFO itself (read_small), but git is then started on the same folder with no deadline. git opens `.git/index` (and other files Fox does not look at) with a plain open(), which blocks forever on a FIFO without a writer (confirmed: `git status` on a repo whose index is a FIFO hung until killed). Since only one scan is ever in flight (GitStatus::flight), that one stuck thread means no further scan starts in any folder for as long as Fox runs: no badges, and the branch chip never updates. One git process is left blocked. At HEAD each scan had a thread of its own, so the list kept working (at the cost of one leaked process per scan).
- **Trigger.** Extract a tar that contains a repository whose `.git/index` is a FIFO (tar stores FIFOs) with an otherwise ordinary `.git`, and walk into it. From then on no folder in this Fox window shows git badges.
- **Status:** FIXED. git status runs with a 10 s deadline in a process group of its own, its output read while it runs; a scan that hangs is killed and counts as failed, so one FIFO in a repository no longer ends git badges for the session.

### input-b1: After a dead key, the next key press is swallowed, including Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X, Delete, Enter, Esc and F2

- **Severity:** low; kind regression
- **What is wrong.** Once the compose table is loaded (first dead key or Compose key), every press is fed to the compose state, whatever modifiers are held and whether or not it types text. A press that does not continue the sequence gives Cancelled and is dropped. A Ctrl chord that happens to complete a sequence (dead_circumflex + c) becomes text, which push() then discards because Ctrl is held. Either way the command never reaches handle_key. Compose state is also kept across modes, so a dead key pressed in browse mode (where it shows nothing) eats whatever comes next. Confirmed with a standalone build of keyboard.rs against the system xkb data, on 'de' and 'us(intl)': dead key then Ctrl+X gives no KeyPress; the same for Delete, F2, Backspace, Enter and Esc; the second press works. HEAD had no compose, so this is new. It cannot happen on the plain 'us' layout the compositor defaults to (no dead keys, no Compose key), hence low.
- **Trigger.** Layout with dead keys (German, US-International, French). Press ^ or the acute key once in the file view; nothing visible happens. Select files B and press Ctrl+X: the press is dropped and Fox's clipboard still holds the earlier cut/copy of files A. Go to another folder and press Ctrl+V: files A are pasted or moved instead of B. Likewise, a dead key followed by Enter in the rename box does not commit, Esc does not cancel, and Delete does not trash, until pressed a second time.
- **Status:** FIXED. A chord (Ctrl/Alt/Super) is never fed to the compose state and resets it; a command key (Enter, Esc, Delete, F2, arrows...) that cancels a waiting sequence still does what it says. New test on the 'de' layout.

### input-b2: Positional fallback for Ctrl chords also applies to Latin layouts: on Dvorak, Ctrl+; is Undo, Ctrl+: is Redo and Ctrl+, closes the tab

- **Severity:** low; kind fix-does-not-fix
- **What is wrong.** The comment says the by-position fallback is for layouts without Latin letters (Cyrillic, Greek). The code applies it to any key whose keysym is not a Latin letter and that sits on a US letter position, in any layout. On Dvorak the US Q/W/E/Z positions carry ' , . ; so those keys get the US letter's code under Ctrl, while the real W and Z keys also map to the same codes by letter. Measured with a standalone build of keyboard.rs on us(dvorak): Ctrl + the key typing ';' gives key 44 (KEY_Z); Ctrl + the key typing ',' gives key 17 (KEY_W). input-32 claims shortcuts go by the letter on the key; here each affected shortcut has a second, unlabelled binding. Only Dvorak-like layouts are affected (QWERTZ and AZERTY are not; us, us+ru, gr and de behaved as intended in the same experiment), and the compositor's default is 'us', hence low.
- **Trigger.** Dvorak layout, file view, after any file operation: press Ctrl+; and request_history(Undo) runs (the last move/rename/trash is undone); Ctrl+Shift+; runs Redo; Ctrl+, closes the current tab.
- **Status:** FIXED. The by-position fallback for Ctrl chords applies only to a key that carries a non-ASCII letter (Cyrillic, Greek...): punctuation at a letter position is no shortcut. New Dvorak test.

### input-b3: A handed-off drag whose receiver never finishes or destroys the offer leaves dnd_active set for good: every later drag is disabled and a drag gesture opens the item instead

- **Severity:** low; kind regression
- **What is wrong.** dnd_active is cleared only by wl_data_source DndFinished / Cancelled (dnd_out.rs:131) or by the start probe. After a validated drop Smithay sends dnd_drop_performed and then nothing until the receiver calls finish or destroys its offer; vendor/smithay dnd_grab.rs DndDataOffer::destroyed() is empty, so a receiver that dies or hangs after the drop never produces Cancelled. dnd_out.rs ignores DndDropPerformed and has no deadline. At HEAD a stuck dnd_active blocked drag-out only (and forced 60 fps); batch 2 additionally gates the start of every in-window drag on it. In that state a press-move-release on an item stays a click, so on_item_click runs on release and, with single-click-to-open (the default), opens the folder or launches the file.
- **Trigger.** Drag a file out of Fox and drop it on a Wayland program that accepts the drop and then crashes or hangs before calling wl_data_offer.finish (for example a viewer that dies on that file). From then on, until Fox is restarted: no item can be dragged inside the window or out of it, and each attempt opens or launches the item under the pointer on release.
- **Status:** FIXED. DndDropPerformed is recorded; a hand-off whose receiver has not finished 10 s after taking the drop is given up on (source destroyed), so drags work again.

### integration-b4: Closing the window does not wait for an in-flight audio tag save; a WAV being rewritten in place can be cut between its writes

- **Severity:** low; kind data-loss
- **What is wrong.** The close interception added for wayland-1 only knows the ops worker and the sudo thread. The tag writer runs on a detached thread that `before_exit` neither waits for nor knows about, so the process can exit in the middle of `wav::write`'s in-place branch (size field, tail, set_len, RIFF size are four separate writes) or leave a `.lntrn-tmp` behind for the rewrite branches. The audio bytes are not touched, but the file can be left with a stale RIFF size / leftover tail, which the new walker then reports as "bytes after the last chunk" and refuses to tag again. Not a regression (same threading at HEAD), but it is the one remaining writer to user files that exit ignores.
- **Trigger.** Properties on a WAV in a slow folder (USB stick), change Title (or add 3 MB artwork), press Enter and immediately close the window (Super+Q). Fox exits while the save thread is between `write_all_at(&tail, ..)` and the RIFF size write.
- **Status:** FIXED. A process-wide count of tag saves in flight: closing the window waits for it (like a privileged command), and before_exit waits for it on the dead-compositor path.

### integration-b5: Unit tests that build `App::new()` touch the real home: create ~/.local/share/Trash dirs, create ~/Cloud when not signed in, read ~/.lantern/config, open a Wayland clipboard connection

- **Severity:** low; kind other
- **What is wrong.** Ten tests construct a full App (app/places.rs x2, app/select.rs x2, app/pick_confirm/tests.rs via `picker()`, cloud_ui/tests.rs x4). `App::new()` runs `trash::ensure_home()` (mkdir 0700 of the real Trash/files and Trash/info if missing), creates the real ~/Cloud when no session file exists, reads the real lantern.toml and session path, and connects to the live compositor's data-control. Nothing is trashed, synced or saved for real (I checked every caller of `trash::trash`, `Settings::save`, `Session::forget`, `Places::real`: none is reachable from a test; trash tests use `trash_in` with scratch dirs, cloud tests use scratch `Places`, the registry and cloud log are cfg!(test)-gated). Which home these tests touch is also nondeterministic: the pre-existing `thumbs::tests::generate_thumbnail_and_disk_cache` does `std::env::set_var("HOME", tmp)` for the whole test process and deletes that dir at its end.
- **Trigger.** `cargo test` on a machine where ~/Cloud does not exist and nobody is signed in: an empty ~/Cloud appears. On any machine: ~/.local/share/Trash/{files,info} are created if missing and each such test opens a data-control connection to the running compositor.
- **Status:** FIXED. App::new() skips its side effects under cfg(test): no Trash folders, no ~/Cloud, no lantern.toml read, no clipboard connection. (thumbs' test still redirects HOME process-wide; left as it was.)

### integration-b6: The two Trash implementations disagree across filesystems: a file sync trashes from a ~/Cloud on another filesystem lands in the home Trash, where Fox's Restore refuses it

- **Severity:** low; kind half-finished
- **What is wrong.** Consequence of the known handoff (cloud keeps its own trash code; it now takes `crate::trash::home_trash().dir`, so naming, O_EXCL reservation on the `.trashinfo`, `name.N.ext` numbering, percent-encoded absolute `Path=` and local-time `DeletionDate` all agree with src/trash/). The one disagreement left: `crate::trash::trash` never crosses a filesystem (it uses the volume's own `.Trash-$uid`, so restore is a rename), while the cloud copies into the home Trash. `restore_item` and undo have no copy-back, so such an item cannot be restored from the Trash view ("its original place is on another drive"); it has to be cut and pasted out by hand. Only when ~/Cloud is a symlink to, a mount of, or a btrfs subvolume other than the one holding ~/.local/share/Trash.
- **Trigger.** ~/Cloud is its own btrfs subvolume (or on another drive). The other machine deletes a synced file; this machine moves its copy to ~/.local/share/Trash by copy+remove. In Fox's Trash select it, Restore: a notice says it could not be restored because its original place is on another drive.
- **Status:** FIXED. Restore of an item whose original place is on another drive than its trash (trashed as root, or by cloud sync) now goes through the ops worker as a verified copy-then-remove instead of being refused; its record is dropped when it is out. Cloud sync also ignores a volume trash (.Trash, .Trash-<uid>) inside ~/Cloud.

### root-b3: Root-mode scope is a lexical path-prefix test: with root mode on in / (or any parent folder) drops onto sidebar places, tabs and the other pane become sudo operations

- **Severity:** low; kind owner-decision
- **What is wrong.** `within` is `dir.starts_with(root)` on the paths as written. `start_paste` (src/file_ops.rs line 168) uses it for every destination, wherever the drop landed. The briefing says "a drop from a root pane onto another pane, a tab or a sidebar place is an ordinary one"; that is only true when the place is not lexically below the root-mode folder. With root mode on in `/`, every absolute path is below it; with root mode on in /home, /mnt or /opt, everything under those is. The destination is not canonicalised either, so a symlinked folder row inside the root-mode folder (/etc/portage/link -> /home/alva/Documents) counts as inside while the files land outside. Nothing is destroyed, but root-owned items appear in places the user did not put under root mode, which is not the "limited in scope" the decision asks for.
- **Trigger.** Open /, right-click empty space, Root Mode Here (e.g. to make a folder there). Drag a file from the list onto the sidebar's Documents (or onto a tab or the other pane showing a home folder) and pick Copy or Move. `root_covers("/home/alva/Documents")` is true, so the item is copied or moved by `sudo cp -rT` / `sudo mv -T`: a root-owned item lands in ~/Documents that the user cannot edit, although the drop target was not the root-mode pane.
- **Status:** FIXED. Root-mode pastes are privileged only when aimed at the focused pane's own list (DropTarget::Folder.in_pane, carried through PendingDrop to start_paste); tabs, favourites, sidebar places and the other pane are ordinary. root_covers also resolves the folder: a row that is a link leading out of the root folder is not covered.

### root-b4: The root delete question accepts a click the moment it appears, with its Delete Permanently button overlapping the button of the dialog it follows: a double-click answers both

- **Severity:** low; kind owner-decision
- **What is wrong.** Enter is correctly ignored in the Confirm phase, but the mouse path has no guard. The flow is: "can't be moved to the Trash ... Delete Permanently" (op dialog) -> worker delete fails at once with EACCES -> the root Confirm dialog is created on the next loop tick and drawn within a frame or two. Both dialogs are 660 px wide, vertically centred, with the acting button right-aligned and both labelled "Delete Permanently", so the x ranges are identical; the y ranges differ only by half the height difference (for one item: about 26 to 42 px at scale 1, buttons are 52 px tall). The second press of a double-click on the lower part of the first button therefore lands on ZONE_SUDO_SUBMIT of the new dialog (src/wayland_actions/click.rs line 115) and starts `sudo -n rm -rf`; with a cached ticket the items are deleted as root without the second question having been read. The same holds when a failure notice (OK button, also right-aligned, zones already registered) sits on top of the Confirm dialog after a delete that had both ordinary and permission failures. The user had already agreed to a permanent delete of the same items, so this defeats only the extra "as administrator" confirmation the implementer added.
- **Trigger.** Within 5 minutes of a Fox sudo authentication: select a root-owned file, press Delete, and double-click (or click twice quickly on) "Delete Permanently" in the first question, in the lower half of the button. The root question flashes and the file is removed by `sudo rm -rf` without it being read.
- **Status:** FIXED. The root question takes a press only after it has been the front dialog for 600 ms (SudoPrompt::armed; reset when a dialog on top of it is answered).

### undo-b3: Restore honours an absolute Path= in a volume trash's .trashinfo, so a crafted record moves the item to any place on that filesystem (and creates folders anywhere first)

- **Severity:** low; kind security
- **What is wrong.** Relative records with `..` are rejected, but an absolute `Path=` is accepted for every trash, including `$top/.Trash-<uid>` volume trashes and anything `locate()` recognises by shape (any folder named `.Trash-<uid>/files`, wherever it is). The Trash spec allows absolute paths in the home trash only. Two effects: (a) `create_dir_all(parent)` runs for the attacker-chosen absolute path before the rename is attempted, also when the rename then fails with EXDEV, leaving stray folders anywhere the user may write; (b) when the trash-shaped folder is on the same filesystem as the target (an extracted archive or a copied folder containing `.Trash-1000/files/x` + `info/x.trashinfo`), Restore moves the item to that absolute location, e.g. `~/.config/autostart/x.desktop`, which is a command run at next login. A hostile stick cannot reach the home filesystem this way (the rename is refused across devices), and nothing is ever overwritten (`pick_restore_dest` + no-replace rename), which is why this is low.
- **Trigger.** (a) Stick with `.Trash-1000/files/a` and `info/a.trashinfo` containing `Path=/home/alva/.config/zzz/deep/a`: open Trash, select `a`, Restore -> notice "its original place is on another drive", but `~/.config/zzz/deep/` now exists. (b) Extract an archive in ~/Downloads that contains `pkg/.Trash-1000/files/readme.desktop` and `pkg/.Trash-1000/info/readme.desktop.trashinfo` with `Path=/home/alva/.config/autostart/readme.desktop`; show hidden files, go into `pkg/.Trash-1000/files`, right-click the file -> Restore: it is moved into ~/.config/autostart.
- **Status:** FIXED. restore_item: the home trash takes absolute records only; a drive's trash takes relative ones, or absolute ones that stay inside that drive. Checked before any folder is created.

## Second read of the review fixes

One further reader went through the 42 review fixes and found 11 defects in them (1 high, 2 medium, 8 low). All 11 were then fixed by the same author as before; **those last fixes have not been read by anyone else.**

| # | Sev | What was wrong | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | high | The new privileged replace-move script fell back to "copy, swap, delete the source" after ANY failed rename, so a root-mode rename of a mount point onto an existing name copied the whole drive and then emptied it. | `PendingPrivOp::without_mounts` (run by `priv_run` for every op): nothing that is, holds or replaces a mounted device reaches a root shell; new test. |
| 2 | medium | A privileged Replace onto a folder a drive is mounted on moved the drive's contents into the home Trash. | `PrivItem::from_op` refuses it before a Trash slot is claimed. |
| 3 | medium | The new cross-drive Restore let a record on a prepared stick (`Path=lnk/x`, `lnk` a symlink into the home folder) copy a file off the stick. | Cross-drive restore only for the home trash; for a drive's trash the destination's real folder must lie on that drive (`stays_inside`); new test. |
| 4 | low | `sh -c "viewer "%f`, `sh -c 'viewer '%f` and `sh -c viewer\ %f` still put the file name into script text. | A file code in one argument with quoted text or a blank is refused; new tests. |
| 5 | low | Empty Trash deleted the restore record of an item it could not delete. | Only records without an item are queued; an item's own record goes when the item does. |
| 6 | low | `rm --preserve-root=all` refused btrfs subvolumes. | Dropped (the mount check is done before the command is built); `--one-file-system` stays and stops at a nested subvolume with rm's own message. |
| 7 | low | A leftover moved to the Trash was asked about again from the Trash view. | Leftovers are not looked for inside a trash. |
| 8 | low | Where a lease cannot be taken, a second tag save within 10 s of Fox's own save was refused. | Fox's own last save is remembered and exempt; the group is kept when the owner cannot be. |
| 9 | low | A cut-and-paste from the home folder into a root-mode folder was always asked about, with a sentence that was wrong for it. | A move's source folder needs no question where the user may write it; the sentence was reworded. |
| 10 | low | The root question's 600 ms guard was not re-armed by the conflict dialog drawn over it. | The conflict dialog's answers re-arm it. |
| 11 | low | A copy still running in Fox on another machine, into a shared folder, was offered as "unfinished". | Leftovers are not looked for on phones and network shares. |

Also found while fixing these: the two end-to-end git tests relied on timing for their trap (a file whose size changed is "modified" to git without its filter running); they now change a file without changing its size. One run in about sixty had failed before that.


## Found while fixing

- **claude-1 (FIXED):** `src/file_info.rs` `parse_video_dims` built an unused `_chunk` with a byte-offset slice of the ffprobe JSON. On output with a non-ASCII character near that offset the slice panics, the single probe worker dies, and every later probe waits forever (the loop then redraws at 60 fps). The dead line is removed. Nobody else has checked this one.

| fs-22 | low | confirmed | FIXED | `src/wayland_loop.rs:1310` | Git results never request a redraw: previous repo's branch chip stays on screen, badges appear only after input |
