## Critical (9)

### app-1: Rename to ".", ".." or a name containing "/" + Replace recursively deletes the current folder, its parent, or an arbitrary path

- **Where:** `src/app/edit.rs:38` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Typed names must be a single plain path component (no '/', '.', '..'); checked in commit_rename.
- **Also reported as:** input-25, dialogs-2

**What is wrong.** commit_rename never validates rename_buf. keycode_to_char can type '.' and '/', so the new name can be ".", "..", "/", "/abs/path" or "sub/..". join() then yields parent/. (== the directory being browsed), parent/.. (its parent) or an absolute path. All of those exist, so the shared conflict dialog opens with that path as the *target*, and Replace (which is also the Enter key in the dialog, key.rs:172) runs remove_dir_all on it. I confirmed with a rustc experiment that std::fs::remove_dir_all("docs/.") deletes every child of docs (then fails on the final rmdir with EINVAL) and remove_dir_all("docs/..") deletes docs AND all its siblings and returns Ok. This is a permanent delete (no trash, no undo). Names containing '/' without a conflict silently move the file into another directory instead of renaming it.

**Trigger.** Select a folder (or Ctrl+A in the rename box of any file), press F2, type `.`, press Enter. Dialog says "<current folder name>" already exists. Press Enter (= Replace) or click Replace: every file in the folder you are looking at is permanently deleted. Typing `..` instead wipes the parent directory (e.g. all of /home/alva when renaming inside ~/Documents); typing `/` targets the filesystem root (everything the user can write).

```rust
let new_path = old.parent().unwrap_or(&old).join(&self.rename_buf);
if new_path != old {
    if new_path.exists() && !self.root_mode {
        ... self.pending_rename = Some(PendingRename { from: old, to: new_path });
// file_ops.rs:388  resolve_rename_conflict(Replace):
let _ = if pending.to.is_dir() { std::fs::remove_dir_all(&pending.to) } else { std::fs::remove_file(&pending.to) };
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced end to end and reproduced the std behaviour. commit_rename only checks !rename_buf.is_empty(); keycode_to_char emits '.' (key 52) and '/' (key 53). My rustc run: parent.join(".") != old is true, exists() and is_dir() are true, and file_name() is Some("docs"), so the dialog names the current folder. remove_dir_all("docs/.") returned EINVAL after deleting every child; remove_dir_all("docs/..") returned Ok and emptied the parent, siblings included. Enter in the conflict dialog is Replace (key.rs:172-174), and resolve_rename_conflict (file_ops.rs:387-393) has no check. One correction to the trigger: start_rename selects only the stem for files with an extension, so typing '.' on photo.jpg gives '..jpg'; the one-keystroke path applies to folders and extensionless files (select-all), or after Ctrl+A. Absolute names (join replaces the base, verified) and names with '/' move the file elsewhere silently.

**Fix.** In commit_rename, before building new_path, require that Path::new(&rename_buf).components() is exactly one Component::Normal and that the buffer has no '/' or NUL; otherwise treat as cancel (clear buffers, reload). Apply the same check to the Save picker's save_name_buf. Checking 'from.starts_with(to)' in resolve_rename_conflict would not catch '..' because the path is not normalised, so the name check is the real guard. Worth considering separately: Enter defaulting to the destructive Replace.

### audio-1: WAV save truncates the file at the last chunk the walker understood, destroying audio (data size 0, or plain RIFF over 4 GiB)

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/wav.rs:341` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (B): New src/audio_tags/wav/riff.rs: walk() now returns Walk { chunks, len, problem } and each Chunk carries both its real `size` and the `declared` size. The walk reports when it did not account for the whole file: bytes after the last chunk that are not a chunk, a non-data chunk that overruns the file, a plain RIFF over 4 GiB. src/audio_tags/wav.rs write() refuses any file with a problem before opening it for writing (message ends "Fox left the file as it was."). The one irregular layout that is handled: a FINAL data chunk with declared size 0 followed by bytes the walk cannot read as chunks is treated as 'the rest of the file is data' (same as ffmpeg and players); its size field is set to the real length first, then tags are appended on the even boundary, then the RIFF size is fixed. No audio byte moves or is overwritten. The rewrite branch (metadata before the audio) writes each kept chunk's header with its real size and copies exactly that many bytes, erroring on a short copy. Verified against real ffmpeg output (decoded-audio md5 identical before/after, ffprobe reads the tags).

**What is wrong.** walk() stops at the first chunk id that is not printable ASCII (line 54-56 `break`) and never reports that it stopped before EOF. write() then assumes everything after the last parsed chunk is disposable and calls set_len(keep_end). Any file where the chunk walk ends early loses everything after that point. Reproduced with a standalone rustc harness that includes the real module: (1) header with data size 0 followed by 176,400 bytes of audio: file went 176,444 -> 1,158 bytes, write returned Ok. (2) 4.72 GB plain-RIFF file whose data size is the wrapped low 32 bits: file went 4,724,464,025 -> 429,497,844 bytes, write returned Ok. (3) same file with data size saturated to 0xFFFFFFFF: truncated to 4,294,968,454 bytes and THEN returned Err("WAV would exceed 4 GB"), so the user sees a failure message but the tail of the recording is already gone. The rewrite branch (line 360-365) has the same flaw: it copies only `keep` chunks, dropping unparsed bytes.

**Trigger.** Open Properties on a WAV left unfinalised by a crashed/killed recorder (RIFF and data sizes still 0, audio follows; players read to EOF and play it fine), or on a multi-hour recording over 4 GiB written as plain RIFF. Type a title, press Enter. UI shows "Saved" (or "Save failed: WAV would exceed 4 GB"); the audio after the declared data extent is permanently deleted.

```rust
let keep_end = keep.iter().map(|c| c.end()).max().unwrap_or(12).min(len);
let in_place = meta.iter().all(|m| m.offset >= keep_end);
if in_place {
    ...
    f.set_len(keep_end).map_err(io_err)?;
    f.write_all_at(&tail, keep_end).map_err(io_err)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** Reproduced all three cases against the real module. (1) data size 0 followed by 176,400 bytes of audio: 176,444 -> 1,128 bytes, Ok(()). (2) sparse 4,724,464,025-byte plain RIFF with wrapped data size: -> 429,497,806 bytes, Ok(()). (3) same file with data size 0xFFFFFFFF: truncated to 4,294,968,416 bytes and then Err("WAV would exceed 4 GB"). Cause: walk() breaks on the first non-printable chunk id (54-56) without reporting that it stopped short of EOF; write() takes keep_end = last parsed chunk end and calls set_len(keep_end) (341) before any validation; the only guard is 'has a data chunk'. The rewrite branch copies only `keep` chunks, dropping the same bytes. ffmpeg writes data size 0 as a placeholder for seekable output, so a killed recording has exactly shape (1) and still plays in most players. Save comes straight from the Properties tag editor (properties_audio/mod.rs:227).

**Fix.** Make walk() return the offset where it stopped. In write(), before opening for write: if stop offset < len (beyond a single pad byte) or any chunk was clamped, return Err without touching the file; compute new_len and do the 4 GB check first; never set_len below the original length unless the removed range is exactly the old trailing metadata chunks. Same guard in the rewrite branch. Not marked safe because it changes what Fox does with malformed WAVs (some files with benign trailing junk will now be refused) and should be designed together with audio-2/audio-3; refusing is nonetheless the right conservative default.

### cloud-e2e-1: Missing, empty or unreadable local tree is read as "user deleted everything": mass tombstones, then the other machine unlinks its copies

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:290` (Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1, C9): New src/cloud/scan.rs: the scan is fallible. A missing or unlistable ~/Cloud is RootError (the pass does nothing, and the engine spends no remote reads); an unreadable folder, a listing that errors midway, or a file that will not stat/hash is recorded as 'unknown' and the pass skips every path it covers (no upload, no download over it, no deletion). The sync root is no longer created anywhere in the engine or the pass (reconcile.rs create_parents refuses to recreate a root that vanished mid-pass). New src/cloud/plan.rs (pure decision table) + src/cloud/guard.rs: a pass now plans first, counts the tombstones it wants to send, and holds them when the scan had any read failure, when the scan found 0 files while the manifest has entries, when the manifest was just set aside, or when more than max(10, 25%) of the manifest would be deleted. Everything else in the pass still runs. Held sets are reported through SyncHandle::held_deletions(); confirm sends exactly that set on the next pass (once, 10 min validity), decline drops the manifest entries so the files are downloaded again. Files deleted locally because of a remote tombstone now go to the freedesktop Trash (src/cloud/trash.rs) instead of being unlinked. Engine side in src/cloud/engine.rs. UI side of 'pause and ask'. New module src/cloud_ui.rs (+ src/cloud_ui/{pill,view,dialog,draw,tests}.rs). App::poll_cloud (called from App::idle_tick in src/app/notify.rs, throttled to 4 Hz) reads SyncHandle::report(); when report.held is Some it pushes an OpDialog::Cloud question onto the existing op-dialog queue in every window, whatever folder is shown: title 'Cloud sync is paused', 'N of M synced files are missing from this computer's Cloud folder' + the reason, the explanation, a boxed list of the sample paths ('...and K more'), and three 52px buttons: 'Restore them' (primary, Enter), 'Decide later' (Esc, click outside), 'Delete everywhere' (danger, kept apart at the left edge). Nothing is sent to the engine except by the Restore / Delete buttons, which call the new SyncHandle::answer_held(id, confirm). AskState (pill.rs) makes the question open once per hold and not again after Decide later; the pill (status bar, now drawn in every folder while something is held, filled in the warning colour, 'Sync paused: N files missing') reopens it on click. An open question follows the report (set changed -> view and id updated with a note; hold gone -> dialog closes). The sync owner also sends one desktop notification (notify-send) per hold. src/wayland_loop.rs no longer compares status(); src/sections/status.rs draws the pill through cloud_ui::draw_pill and ends its own text where the pill begins; src/op_dialogs.rs got the Cloud variant; src/wayland_actions/click.rs routes the pill and the dialog buttons; src/main.rs has the new zone ids 910-915.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-1

**What is wrong.** Scenario 6. "Local deleted" is inferred purely from "manifest has the path but scan_local did not return it". scan_local cannot tell a deletion from a failure: a missing root is silently recreated empty (also by run_loop sync.rs:79 and App::new/ensure_cloud_dir), a read_dir error returns nothing for that whole subtree, rd.flatten() drops per-entry errors, and a file whose hash fails is skipped. Every such path with a live remote doc goes to arm 4a and is tombstoned (the doc's sha256 is overwritten with "", so the path->blob mapping is lost). The other machine then hits arm 5a and delete_local() does std::fs::remove_file (not Trash). There is no sanity guard such as "scan returned 0 files but manifest has N".

**Trigger.** (a) Copy ~/.lantern to a new install (it carries both config/fox-cloud-session.json and cloud/manifest.json) and launch Fox: ~/Cloud is created empty, the manifest lists every file, the first pass tombstones the whole library and the PC then deletes all its files. (b) `mv ~/Cloud ~/Cloud.bak` (or ~/Cloud on a not-yet-mounted volume) while any Fox process is running: within 30 s everything is tombstoned and removed from the other machine. (c) A synced subfolder becomes unreadable (chmod, EIO, EMFILE in read_dir): that subtree is tombstoned. User sees the log fill with "x remote ..." and the files vanish on the other machine.

```rust
std::fs::create_dir_all(&root)?;            // 290: recreates a missing ~/Cloud
let Ok(rd) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) else { return; };   // 49: unreadable dir == empty dir
Err(e) => { super::log_line(&format!("hash {} failed: {e}", abs.display())); continue; }  // 86-89
(None, Some(r), Some(m_sha)) if !r.deleted && r.sha256 == m_sha => {
    tombstone_remote(authed, &mut manifest, path, &device)   // 381-383
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced: reconcile_with does create_dir_all(root) (290), run_loop does too (sync.rs:79), App::new calls ensure_cloud_dir (app/mod.rs:351). scan_local/walk returns nothing on read_dir failure (49-51) and skips entries on any error. Every manifest path missing from the scan with a live remote whose sha equals the manifest hits arm 4a (381-383) and is tombstoned with sha256 "". On the other machine arm 5a (391-393) calls delete_local = std::fs::remove_file, not trash. No count/ratio/root-identity guard exists anywhere in sync.rs or reconcile.rs. Reachable: mv ~/Cloud while Fox runs (the recursive watcher fires, pass in ~2 s), copying ~/.lantern to a new install, or chmod/EIO on a subfolder. Sub-claim about hash failures is narrower than stated: an unreadable file still in the stat cache is not re-hashed, so it stays in the scan; only a changed-and-unreadable file would be tombstoned.

**Fix.** Changes sync semantics, so not mechanical, but should be done: (1) make walk() fallible and abort the pass on read_dir/iteration errors; (2) do not create the root inside reconcile_with/run_loop, treat a missing root as an error status; (3) refuse arm 4a when the scan is empty while the manifest is not, or when more than a threshold share would be tombstoned, and surface an error. A marker file tying manifest to root is a good addition. Risk: a legitimate 'delete everything' needs a way through (threshold plus explicit confirmation).

### cloud-core-2: No arm for (local absent, remote tombstone, manifest present): stale manifest entries live forever and make sync delete a restored file

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:415` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. New reconcile arm removes the stale manifest entry when a path is deleted on both sides.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-net-1, cloud-e2e-2

**What is wrong.** The table has no arm for local=None, remote=tombstone, manifest=Some, so it falls to the catch-all and the manifest entry is never removed. The manifest is only written at pass start and pass end (lines 306, 437), and the sync thread is killed with the process (SyncHandle._stop is never set, no join). So any pass interrupted after tombstone_remote or delete_local leaves such entries. Deleting the same file on both machines does too. Later, when a file with the same content reappears at that path, case 5a sees remote tombstone and local == manifest and permanently deletes it. If it reappears on the other machine instead, case 4a re-tombstones it and that machine deletes it. This is live on the PC right now: manifest.json holds 168 entries (all under "Ember Nights (copy)/") whose local file is absent and whose remote doc is a tombstone; the log ends mid-burst of "× remote Ember Nights (copy)/..." lines with no pass completion.

**Trigger.** 1) Delete a folder in ~/Cloud and close the Fox window (or a file-picker dialog exits) while the pass is still writing tombstones. 2) Later restore the folder from trash (Ctrl+Z undo renames the trash file back, same content), or duplicate the same source folder to the same name again. 3) The next pass logs "× local ..." and the restored files vanish, not to trash. With the current manifest, re-creating "Ember Nights (copy)" from the same source will delete up to 168 of the copied files.

```rust
// 5a. remote tombstone, local clean → delete local
(Some(l), Some(r), Some(m_sha)) if r.deleted && l.sha == m_sha => {
    delete_local(path, &mut manifest)
}
...
// Anything else (shouldn't happen): log and skip.
_ => Ok(()),
```

**Skeptic's check.** Walked every arm for (None, Some(tombstone), Some(m)): arm 2 and 5c need manifest None, 2b/3x/5a/5b need a local file, 4a/4b need !r.deleted, 6/7 need no remote. It reaches `_ => Ok(())` and the entry is never removed. The state arises when both machines delete the same file, or when the process exits mid-pass after tombstone_remote but before the single manifest.save() at 437 (sync thread is detached, never joined). Once stale, a file reappearing at that path with the same bytes matches arm 5a (r.deleted && l.sha == m_sha) and is removed with remove_file. Verified read-only on disk: manifest has 340 entries, 168 of them (all under 'Ember Nights (copy)/') have no local file and a tombstone in remote-index.json.

**Fix.** Add before the catch-all: `(None, Some(r), Some(_)) if r.deleted => { manifest.remove(path); Ok(()) }`. Local, mechanical, cleans the 168 existing entries on the next pass, and a later re-created file then goes through arm 2b (upload) instead of 5a. Saving the manifest periodically during long passes is a separate, also worthwhile change (use write-to-temp + rename).

### fileops-2: Rename conflict "Replace" deletes the file itself on case-insensitive filesystems (vfat/exfat USB sticks, SD cards)

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:387` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. A rename onto the same directory entry (case-only change on vfat/exfat) skips the conflict dialog; Replace also refuses to delete the entry being renamed.

**What is wrong.** commit_rename (app/edit.rs:39-43) only compares the path strings (`new_path != old`) and then `new_path.exists()`. Its comment claims case-only renames of the same file are let through, but nothing implements that. On a case-insensitive filesystem renaming photo.JPG -> photo.jpg makes exists() true for the very same directory entry, so the conflict dialog appears; Replace then unlinks `to`, which is the file being renamed, and the following fs::rename fails (result discarded). For a folder it is remove_dir_all of the whole folder.

**Trigger.** On a FAT/exFAT stick or camera SD card: F2 on DSC_0001.JPG, change only the case (dsc_0001.jpg), Enter. Dialog says the name already exists; press Enter/Replace. The file (or entire folder) is permanently deleted.

```rust
ConflictAction::Replace => {
    let _ = if pending.to.is_dir() {
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&pending.to)
    } else {
        std::fs::remove_file(&pending.to)
    };
    self.perform_rename(pending.from, pending.to);
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** commit_rename (app/edit.rs:39-43) compares only path strings and then `new_path.exists()`; the comment claims case-only renames of the same file are let through but nothing implements that. On a case-insensitive filesystem (vfat, exfat, ext4 casefold) exists() resolves the new spelling to the same directory entry, so the conflict dialog opens. resolve_rename_conflict Replace (file_ops.rs:387-393) then calls remove_file/remove_dir_all on `pending.to`, which is the item being renamed, and perform_rename discards the failed rename result. Enter is bound to Replace (key.rs:172-175). I could not mount vfat in a read-only audit; the case-insensitive lookup is standard vfat/exfat behaviour, and the code path itself is unambiguous.

**Fix.** In commit_rename, skip the dialog when old and new_path are the same file (compare st_dev + st_ino from symlink_metadata) and go straight to perform_rename; rename(2) handles a case-only rename. That implements what the existing comment already promises. Keep the second half of the proposal (stop pre-deleting file targets) as a separate change: it alters Replace semantics. Edge: two hard links to one inode in the same folder become a silent no-op rename, which is harmless.

### fileops-1: Paste/drop into the item's own folder + "Replace" permanently deletes the source (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V, Enter)

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:456` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. An item pasted onto itself (same inode AND same folder) is never offered Replace: a copy becomes a Keep Both duplicate, a move is a no-op. A target folder that CONTAINS a source of the paste (/a/foo/foo into /a) is never offered Replace either and is never deleted; the item lands as a Keep Both name. The second half was found by the review pass. Judgment call made by Claude.
- **Also reported as:** app-2, input-1, dialogs-1

**What is wrong.** advance_paste computes target = dest.join(src.file_name()). When dest is the directory the source already lives in, target == src, target.exists() is true, and the conflict dialog pops ("name already exists"). Replace (which is also what Enter does, key.rs:172-176) removes `target` first - which IS the source - and only then queues the copy/rename. The worker's fs::copy / queue_cut's rename then fails with ENOENT and the error is swallowed (ops.rs:196, file_ops.rs:87). For a folder it is remove_dir_all of the whole source tree. Same code at lines 220-226 for the apply-to-all path. A second variant: source lies INSIDE the same-named target (src=/a/x/x pasted into /a) - Replace wipes /a/x including the source.

**Trigger.** Select a file or folder, Ctrl+C (or Ctrl+X), Ctrl+V in the same folder, press Enter (or click Replace) on the conflict dialog. The item is permanently gone (not in Trash). With Ctrl+A + 'Apply to all' + Replace every item in the folder is destroyed. Also reachable by dragging an item onto its own tab or onto the sidebar place/favorite for the folder it is already in, then Move/Copy -> Replace (drag_drop.rs valid_dest only rejects dest inside a source, not dest == parent).

```rust
ConflictAction::Replace => {
    let _ = if target.is_dir() {
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&target)
    } else {
        std::fs::remove_file(&target)
    };
    target
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced end to end and reproduced the std behaviour. paste() (121-155) sets dest = current_dir with no same-folder check; advance_paste computes target = dest.join(name) == src, target.exists() is true, dialog opens. Enter or Replace -> resolve_conflict -> apply_single_conflict_action removes `target` (456-462), which is the source, then queues (src, target). run_copy's fs::copy fails NotFound and lands in `Err(_) => {}` (ops.rs:196); for Cut, queue_cut's rename fails into `Err(_) => {}` (file_ops.rs:87). My scratchpad test: target.exists()=true, remove_file, copy => Err(NotFound), source gone. Same code in the apply-to-all arm (220-226). Drag variant also holds: drag_drop::valid_dest only rejects dest.starts_with(src), so dropping onto the tab or sidebar entry of the folder the item already lives in reaches the same dialog; only the split-pane branch has a same_dir guard. Ancestor variant (src=/a/x/x pasted into /a) follows from remove_dir_all(/a/x). Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V in place is the ordinary duplicate gesture, and the destructive choice is the keyboard default.

**Fix.** Guard once at the top of advance_paste, before the exists() branch: if canonical(src.parent()) == canonical(dest) then Cut -> drop the item, Copy -> use unique_keep_both_path(target) with no dialog; if src.starts_with(target) and src != target, skip the item. Because advance_paste handles these before any dialog, apply_single_conflict_action and the apply-to-all arm never see them. Prefer the parent-path comparison over inode equality, which would misfire on hard links in different folders. Also add the same-parent check to valid_dest for tab/sidebar/favorite drops, and consider making Enter a non-destructive default. Not marked safe: what a same-folder paste should do (auto-duplicate and under which name) is Alva's call, and it changes delete behaviour.

### fs-1: Browsing into an untrusted folder runs attacker-chosen commands via git (core.fsmonitor / clean filters)

- **Where:** `src/git_status.rs:115` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it critical; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): No repository-controlled code can run from a badge scan any more. New src/git_status/repo.rs finds the repository itself (walk up to the first valid .git dir or gitdir file, linked work trees via commondir, submodules, stops at a mount boundary and never steps onto a slow mount) and reads the branch straight from HEAD with no git process. New src/git_status/config.rs reads .git/config (and config.worktree when extensions.worktreeConfig is set) with a reader that follows git's own parser character by character and fails closed, then checks every key against an ALLOW-list of plain-data settings: filter.*, include/includeIf, core.fsmonitor/hooksPath/sshCommand/pager/editor/askPass/attributesFile/excludesFile, diff/merge drivers and tools, alias, credential, url, protocol, promisor remotes / extensions.partialClone, lfs.extension.*, submodule update=!cmd and every unknown section are refused. core.worktree is accepted only when it resolves to the folder the .git was found in. src/git_status.rs scan() now runs git only when that check passes, with --git-dir/--work-tree given explicitly (git never searches, so a planted bare repository cannot stand in), --no-pager, -c core.fsmonitor=false, -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null, --ignore-submodules=all, GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0, GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1, stdin null, GIT_DIR-style variables removed, and never on a slow path. Repositories owned by another user are not scanned (explicit --git-dir would skip git's own ownership check). When the scan is skipped the branch chip still shows, badges are absent, and one line per repository goes to stderr saying why. Tests cover each rejected construct, the layouts, and an end-to-end test against real git that first proves a repo filter runs under plain `git status` and then that scan() does not run it.

**What is wrong.** Every navigation into a non-slow directory makes the main loop call git.refresh(&app.current_dir) (wayland_loop.rs:1317; also on every watcher reload at 1306 and every 5 s at 1323), which runs `git -C <dir> status`. `git status` honours the repository's own .git/config, including keys that name commands to execute: core.fsmonitor is run as a hook, and filter.<name>.clean is run for any stat-dirty tracked file matched by .gitattributes. I confirmed both with git 2.55 in the scratchpad using Fox's exact command line: a repo with `core.fsmonitor=<script>` ran the script; a repo with `* filter=x` plus `filter.x.clean=<cmd>` ran the command even with `-c core.fsmonitor=false`. So merely opening a folder in Fox executes code chosen by whoever authored that folder.

**Trigger.** Extract a downloaded archive (or plug in a FAT/exFAT stick, or clone a repo containing an embedded bare repo) whose tree contains .git/config with `core.fsmonitor = sh -c '...'` (or a filter + .gitattributes). Navigate into it with Fox. No click on any file is needed; the command runs as the user within about 100 ms, and again every 5 s and on every file event in that folder.

```rust
if let Some(out) = git_stdout(
    dir,
    &["status", "--porcelain=v1", "-z", "--no-renames", "."],
) {
```

**Skeptic's check.** Reproduced with git 2.55.0 in the scratchpad using Fox's exact argv: a repo whose .git/config sets core.fsmonitor to a script ran that script on `git -C <dir> status --porcelain=v1 -z --no-renames .`; `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` alone did not. git_stdout (158-169) passes no -c overrides and no environment. The call is reached on every navigation into a non-slow directory (wayland_loop.rs:1311-1318), on every watcher reload (1306) and every 5 s in a repo (1319-1324); the only gate is slow_dir. git's safe.directory check does not help because an extracted archive or a FAT stick is owned by the user. So opening a folder that contains a crafted .git runs code as the user with no click on any file. I did not re-test the clean-filter variant; it is the documented behaviour of status on a stat-dirty tracked file.

**Fix.** No flag set fully neutralises a hostile repo (`-c core.fsmonitor=false` leaves filter.<name>.clean, and submodules bring their own config). Options: read the branch by walking up to .git/HEAD with no exec; compute badges only for repositories under an allow-list or after a per-repo trust prompt; or, in the Lantern spirit, parse .git/index and compare stat data in-process and never exec git. Whatever is chosen, also pass `-c core.fsmonitor=false` and GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 when git is run. This removes badges from untrusted folders, so it is a product decision.

### fileops-3: Undo of Create / Copy hard-deletes (remove_dir_all) whatever now lives at the path - no emptiness check, no Trash

- **Where:** `src/undo.rs:101` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C2): Undo of Copy and Create no longer deletes anything. New src/undo/steps.rs (uncopy, uncreate): the created item is moved to the Trash through the same trash module C1 added, and only when it is still the file that was made. Identity is a Stamp recorded per item when the action is pushed (src/undo.rs: device, inode, kind, birth time; size+mtime as a fallback for FAT). A different file under the same name is left alone and reported. Redo takes the trashed item back out of the Trash (recreate/recopy), no re-copy unless the Trash was emptied. The one removal left is rmdir of a created folder where no Trash exists (phone, network), which the system refuses unless the folder is empty. The result is shown: a status-bar line on success, a notice listing each refused item and why. Tests in src/undo/tests.rs.

**What is wrong.** Undoing New Folder / New File / paste-copy / duplicate permanently deletes the recorded path recursively, regardless of what has been put there since. Many operations never push an undo entry (cross-device moves from the phone or a USB stick - file_ops.rs:350 'none is offered' -, extract, compress, sudo/root ops, permanent delete, restore, anything written by other apps), so the Create/Copy entry stays on top of the stack while the folder fills up. The result string is discarded (key.rs:724-726 `let _ =`) so the user gets no hint of what Ctrl+Z just did. Same code for UndoAction::Copy at lines 136-148.

**Trigger.** Right-click -> New Folder, press Enter keeping the name. Cut photos on the phone (MTP) and paste them into the new folder (cross-device move: sources are deleted, no undo entry). Press Ctrl+Z expecting to undo the move: remove_dir_all wipes the folder and every photo in it; they are no longer on the phone either. Also: New File, write content in an editor all day, Ctrl+Z in Fox -> file permanently removed.

```rust
UndoAction::Create(paths) => {
    let mut removed = 0;
    for path in paths {
        let ok = if path.is_dir() {
            std::fs::remove_dir_all(path).is_ok()
        } else {
            std::fs::remove_file(path).is_ok()
```

**Skeptic's check.** UndoAction::Create and Copy undo call remove_dir_all / remove_file on the recorded path with no check of what is there now. Push sites confirmed: context_menu.rs:870 (New Folder), :895 (New File), file_ops.rs:337 and :832. poll_op_progress pushes nothing for a cross-device move (file_ops.rs:350-359), and extract, compress, sudo ops and other apps push nothing either, so a Create entry stays on top while the folder fills. New Folder, move phone photos into it (sources deleted by ops.rs run_copy), Ctrl+Z: the folder and its contents are permanently removed, with the result string thrown away (key.rs:726). Not even routed through Trash.

**Fix.** Undo of Create: use fs::remove_dir (fails on non-empty) for folders and remove files only when still zero length; otherwise refuse or move to Trash. Undo of Copy: move created items to Trash instead of unlinking. Show the returned description in the status bar. This changes what undo deletes, so it needs Alva's call on the exact semantics.

### input-2: "New File" truncates an existing file called "New File" to zero bytes

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:893` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. New File picks a free name and creates with create_new, so nothing is truncated.
- **Also reported as:** fileops-15, dialogs-3

**What is wrong.** std::fs::write creates OR truncates. The target name is always exactly "New File" with no uniqueness loop, and unlike New Folder no rename is started, so the first created file normally keeps that name. A second New File in the same folder silently empties the first one (it also follows a symlink named "New File"). It then pushes UndoAction::Create for a file it did not create, so Ctrl+Z afterwards removes the file entirely.

**Trigger.** Right-click empty space, New File. Open "New File" in an editor, write notes, save. Later right-click, New File again in that folder: the notes file is now 0 bytes. Confirmed with a standalone rustc test that the second fs::write leaves length 0.

```rust
let target = app.current_dir.join("New File");
...
match std::fs::write(&target, "") {
    Ok(()) => {
        app.undo_stack.push(crate::undo::UndoAction::Create(vec![target]));
```

**Skeptic's check.** CTX_NEW_FILE passes current_dir/"New File" (:778) and new_file_or_prompt calls std::fs::write(&target, ""), which creates or truncates and follows a symlink of that name. No existence check, no unique-name loop, and unlike New Folder no rename is started, so keeping the default name is the normal outcome. A second New File in the same folder empties the first, and the pushed UndoAction::Create means Ctrl+Z then removes the pre-existing file entirely.

**Fix.** Choose a free name (New File, New File 2, ...) and create with OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true); push the Create undo entry only when create_new succeeded. Starting the inline rename afterwards, as New Folder does, would make the default name less sticky. The sudo path uses touch and does not truncate.

## High (42)

### input-3: Rename that only changes letter case on a case-insensitive drive: Replace deletes the file itself

- **Where:** `src/app/edit.rs:43` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. A rename onto the same directory entry (case-only change on vfat/exfat) skips the conflict dialog; Replace also refuses to delete the entry being renamed.

**What is wrong.** The comment promises that case-only renames of the same file pass straight through, but the code only compares PathBufs (case-sensitive) and then calls exists(). On vfat/exFAT (USB sticks, SD cards) `photo.jpg` exists when the file is `photo.JPG`, because it is the same file. The conflict dialog opens and Replace removes `to`, which is the file being renamed; the following fs::rename fails and its error is discarded (`let _ =`). perform_rename also pushes a Rename undo entry even though nothing was renamed.

**Trigger.** On a FAT/exFAT USB stick: F2 on IMG_0001.JPG, change it to IMG_0001.jpg, Enter. The dialog says the name already exists; press Enter again (Replace) or click Replace. The photo is gone for good.

```rust
if new_path != old {
    // ... (case-insensitive renames on the same file are allowed through — fs::rename handles those)
    if new_path.exists() && !self.root_mode {
        ... self.conflict_dialog = Some(dialog);
// file_ops.rs:387-393
ConflictAction::Replace => {
    let _ = if pending.to.is_dir() { std::fs::remove_dir_all(&pending.to) } else { std::fs::remove_file(&pending.to) };
    self.perform_rename(pending.from, pending.to);
```

**Skeptic's check.** Code path read in full: the only tests are PathBuf inequality (case-sensitive) and new_path.exists(). On vfat and exFAT, which Linux mounts case-insensitive by default (udisks uses the defaults), exists() on the re-cased name resolves to the file being renamed. The dialog opens, and Replace runs remove_file(&pending.to), which unlinks that same file; the following fs::rename fails and 'let _ =' hides it, then an undo entry is pushed for a rename that never happened. The code comment claims case-only renames pass through, which the code does not do. Not run on a real FAT mount (I cannot mount one here); the filesystem behaviour is standard kernel behaviour. Rated high rather than critical: one file, needs a case-insensitive drive and an explicit Replace, though Enter is Replace and renaming .JPG to .jpg on a camera card is a natural thing to do.

**Fix.** Before raising the dialog, compare std::fs::symlink_metadata(&old) and symlink_metadata(&new_path) by dev() and ino(); if equal, call perform_rename directly. It must be symlink_metadata, not metadata: with metadata, renaming a symlink onto its own target would be treated as 'same file' and rename(link, target) would replace the real file with a self-referencing link. Hard links to the same inode are safe (rename is a no-op). Separately make perform_rename push undo only on Ok.

### app-3: root_mode is invisible and survives Back/Forward, tab switch and split-pane focus; Delete then becomes an immediate `sudo rm -rf` instead of Trash

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:88` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C3): Root mode is no longer an App-wide bool. It is `DirectoryTab::root_dir` (src/app/mod.rs), i.e. per tab and per split pane, and `App::root_mode()` (new src/app/root.rs) is true only while the focused pane shows exactly that folder. Every way out clears it before leaving: navigate_to, go_back, go_forward, pick_tree_navigate (src/app/nav.rs), switch_tab/new_tab/close_tab (src/app/tabs.rs), focus_pane (src/app/split.rs). `App::expire_root_mode()` runs every main-loop iteration (src/wayland_loop.rs) as a safety net so a future code path that forgets to clear it still cannot bring root mode back. Visible: new src/sections/root_badge.rs draws a bold white-on-danger-red ROOT pill (22 px * scale) at the head of the path strip in the nav bar (the path gives up the room, nothing is covered) plus a 3 px danger-coloured frame around that pane's file list; wired in src/render.rs and, defensively, src/sections/split.rs. Clicking the badge (ZONE_ROOT_BADGE) or the empty-area menu's 'Leave Root Mode' switches it off. Root mode also no longer reaches outside its folder: paste/drop is privileged only when the destination is the root folder or below it (`root_covers`, src/file_ops.rs start_paste), so a drop from a root pane onto another pane, tab or sidebar place is an ordinary one. Delete in root mode was already Trash-first after C1; the remaining sudo delete now always asks (see fileops-16).

**What is wrong.** root_mode is a single App-wide flag that only navigate_to() clears. go_back, go_forward, switch_tab/close_tab/new_tab (sync_from_tab) and focus_pane (it is not in PaneView and not swapped) all leave it set. The only indicator, window_title()'s " [ROOT]" suffix, is dead code (#[allow(dead_code)], never called), so the user cannot see the mode. While it is set: trash_selected() routes to delete_selected() -> priv_run(Remove) -> `sudo -n rm -rf` which runs with no prompt when a sudo ticket is cached (it usually is, the user just did a root op); paste/drop skip the conflict dialog and overwrite via sudo cp/mv; rename skips the conflict check (edit.rs:43) and runs `pkexec mv` over an existing name; New File/Folder are created root-owned. Additionally open_as_root() on a file (the menu item is shown for files too) just silently toggles the flag.

**Trigger.** Favourite /etc/portage -> right-click a folder -> Open as Root -> edit something (sudo ticket now cached). Click the Back arrow (or click another tab, or click the other split pane) to return to a home folder. Select files, press Delete: they are permanently removed with rm -rf as root, not moved to Trash, no undo, no prompt. Or paste a file whose name exists: it is overwritten without the Replace/Keep Both dialog.

```rust
pub fn navigate_to(&mut self, path: std::path::PathBuf) {
    ...
    self.root_mode = false;
// but go_back()/go_forward()/sync_from_tab()/focus_pane() never touch root_mode
// file_ops.rs:566
if self.root_mode { self.delete_selected(); return; }
```

**Skeptic's check.** grep for root_mode: set only in open_as_root (file_ops.rs:1005, 1008), cleared only at nav.rs:88 inside navigate_to. go_back and go_forward (nav.rs:254-278) set current_dir themselves and never reach that line; sync_from_tab and focus_pane do not touch the flag, and it is not part of PaneView. No file under render.rs or sections/ reads it, and window_title is #[allow(dead_code)], so the mode has no indicator at all. While set: trash_selected goes to delete_selected, then priv_run(Remove), which is 'sudo -n rm -rf' and runs with no prompt when a ticket is cached (sudo.rs:101-103, 125-126); paste and drop skip the conflict dialog (file_ops.rs:131-147, 173-187); rename skips it too (edit.rs:43). Also confirmed: open_as_root with a file selected finds no directory and silently toggles the flag, and build_item_menu offers 'Open as Root' for files. Without a cached ticket the sudo modal does say 'Permanently delete', which is the only warning the user gets.

**Fix.** Clear root_mode in go_back and go_forward and wherever another tab or pane is installed (or store it per DirectoryTab and swap it), draw a visible ROOT badge in the nav bar, and stop offering Open as Root on files or make it do something explicit. Whether root mode should survive Back within the same root-owned tree, and whether Delete in root mode should be rm -rf at all, are product decisions, so not a mechanical fix.

### fs-5: Dragging a file while the watcher reloads the folder: wrong file moved, or index-out-of-bounds panic on drop

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:184` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category panic; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Drag sources come from the snapshot taken at drag start; drag/pending indices are remapped by path on re-list and indexing is checked. (Also covers the unsafe-rated fs-5.)

**What is wrong.** apply_listing remaps the selection and the active rename by path, but every other piece of interaction state that is an index into `entries` is left pointing at the old positions: drag_item, pending_open, context_target (ContextTarget::Item), selection_anchor, last_click_idx. The watcher reload (wayland_loop.rs:1299) runs every loop iteration regardless of what the user is doing. The drop handler then does `app.entries[drag_idx]` with no bounds check (wayland_loop.rs:923-924): if the list shrank it panics, otherwise it silently uses whatever entry now sits at that index as the drag source. The same stale index makes context-menu Rename, Copy Path, Copy Name, Set Wallpaper and Properties act on a neighbouring file (context_menu.rs:600-660, 709).

**Trigger.** In ~/Downloads start dragging the last file in the list; while holding it, a browser finishes a download and deletes its .part file (or you run rm in a terminal). Release: Fox panics on `app.entries[drag_idx]`. Variant: a new file appears and sorts before the dragged one; on release the neighbouring file is moved or copied to the drop target instead. Variant: right-click a file, a file lands while the menu is open, choose Rename: the rename box opens on a different file.

```rust
self.entries = entries;
if !selected_paths.is_empty() {
    for e in &mut self.entries {
        e.selected = selected_paths.contains(&e.path);
    }
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** apply_listing remaps only the selection and the active rename by path. drag_item, pending_open, selection_anchor, last_click_idx and ContextTarget::Item stay as raw indices. A live drag keeps needs_anim true, so the watcher reload at wayland_loop.rs:1299 does run mid-drag. On release, wayland_loop.rs:923-924 does 'app.entries[drag_idx]' twice with no bounds check: the dragged item was selected at press (click.rs:446), so 'selected.is_empty()' is false and the index is evaluated; if the list shrank past it, that panics. If a file was inserted before it, entries[drag_idx] is the unselected neighbour, the condition is true, and the neighbour's path becomes the drag source, so the wrong file is moved or copied. The same unchecked index exists at wayland_loop.rs:395 for the press-to-drag-threshold window. The context menu also keeps the loop animating, so CTX_RENAME / Copy Path / Properties on ContextTarget::Item(idx) can hit a neighbour after a reload. The crash site is wayland_loop.rs:923; the root cause is the missing remap in apply_listing.

**Fix.** Two parts. (1) Replace the direct indexing at wayland_loop.rs:395 and 923 with app.entries.get(..) and abort the drag when it is None; that is safe on its own and removes the panic. (2) In apply_listing, capture the paths behind drag_item, pending_open, selection_anchor, last_click_idx and ContextTarget::Item before replacing entries and re-resolve them afterwards, clearing each when its path is gone, as is already done for renaming. Part 2 touches several interaction states, so review it. pending_tree_open and drag_tree_item index tree_entries and need the same treatment after rebuild_tree.

### sections-1: Save picker returns an existing file's path with no overwrite confirmation (callers then silently overwrite)

- **Where:** `src/app/select.rs:104` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it critical; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C8): New src/app/pick_confirm.rs: the Save branch of confirm_pick (src/app/select.rs) now goes through App::confirm_save_pick. It stats the typed name: free -> returned at once; an existing folder -> the picker goes into it (name left selected for retyping); an existing file (or dangling link / device node) -> a modal 'Replace "name"?' question; stat refused -> 'Save as "name"?' with 'Save Anyway'. The question is a new OpDialog::Pick variant (src/op_dialogs.rs, 24px title / 18px body / 52px buttons, all times scale), so it is modal like the other op dialogs: Esc, a click outside and Enter all mean Cancel, only a click on Replace returns the path. Both entry points (Enter in the name field / Enter with the list focused in key.rs, and the Save button in click.rs) already call confirm_pick and only close when pick_result is set, so both get the question and a portal caller gets exactly one. On a slow mount (fs::is_slow_path) nothing is stat'ed on the UI thread: the listing in hand answers for names it holds, otherwise a bg::Task asks the device while a 'Checking...' dialog with a Cancel button is shown (App::save_check, polled from app/background.rs). text_entry_active (key.rs) now returns false while an op dialog is open, so a held Enter cannot auto-answer the question through key repeat.

**What is wrong.** In Save mode confirm_pick() joins the typed name onto current_dir and returns it without checking whether the target already exists (or is a directory). Fox is the only place a 'Replace?' prompt can happen: lntrn-notepad's save_as_dialog (lntrn-notepad/src/actions.rs:147-166) writes to the returned path immediately, and lntrn-portal's SaveFile (lntrn-portal/src/filechooser.rs:149-157) hands the URI straight to browsers/GTK apps, which rely on the portal dialog to have confirmed overwrite.

**Trigger.** Notepad: new note -> Save As -> Enter (saves ~/Untitled.lnote). New note again -> Save As -> the picker again opens in Home with 'Untitled.lnote' suggested -> Enter. The first note is silently replaced. Same for a browser 'Save as' through the portal onto an existing file name: no prompt, old file gone.

```rust
PickType::Save => {
    if !self.save_name_buf.is_empty() {
        let path = self.current_dir.join(&self.save_name_buf);
        self.pick_result = Some(PickResult::Selected(vec![path]));
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** confirm_pick's Save branch joins save_name_buf onto current_dir and sets pick_result with no existence check; wayland.rs:540-547 prints it and exits 0. I read the callers: lntrn-notepad save_as_dialog (actions.rs:147-166) passes --save-name '<stem>.lnote', takes the trimmed stdout as the path and immediately calls save_file(); export_docx_dialog does the same. lntrn-portal's save_file forwards to the same picker, and portal clients rely on the chooser for overwrite confirmation. With --save-name given, save_name_editing stays false, so Enter falls through to 'KEY_ENTER if app.pick.is_some()' and confirms at once: two new notes saved with the suggested 'Untitled.lnote' silently replace each other. Rated high, not critical: one file per incident and the user did accept that name, but the flow is ordinary and no prompt exists anywhere in the chain.

**Fix.** In the Save branch: if the joined path is an existing directory, navigate into it instead of returning; if it is an existing file, show a Replace/Cancel confirm and only set pick_result on confirm. This needs the callers of confirm_pick (key.rs:618-619, 743-744; click.rs:503-505) to stop closing unconditionally and close only when pick_result is Some. The ConflictDialog can be reused but its Keep Both and Skip buttons make no sense here. Also validate the name as in app-1 ('/' or an absolute name replaces current_dir via join). New UI flow, so not mechanical.

### fs-4: Tree view (and therefore every file-picker dialog) lists slow mounts synchronously on the render thread

- **Where:** `src/app/tabs.rs:38` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): New src/app/tree.rs: the tree is built by a pure function over a lister. A pane's own folder comes from the listing it already holds; other fast folders are listed on the spot; other slow folders come from App::tree_cache, filled by the off-thread loader, and are requested when missing. A picker folder click keeps the listing of the folder being left for the tree and re-lists only the clicked folder. confirm_pick no longer stats picked rows (App::is_folder).
- **Also reported as:** app-15, input-10

**What is wrong.** reload() carefully sends slow-mount listings to a worker (app/dir_load.rs), but when the result lands apply_listing does `if self.view_mode == ViewMode::Tree { self.rebuild_tree(); }` (app/nav.rs:202), and rebuild_tree calls fs::list_directory again, synchronously, for the root and every expanded folder. toggle_tree_expand and cycle_view_mode do the same. Pick mode forces ViewMode::Tree (wayland.rs:449), so the file chooser never benefits from the off-thread loader at all. On jmtpfs each of those readdir+stat rounds is an MTP round-trip behind the global device lock.

**Trigger.** Switch to Tree view (or open a file dialog from another app) and go into a mounted phone folder, or expand a folder there. Fox freezes for the whole listing; if a copy or thumbnail is currently pulling a large file from the phone it freezes until that download finishes (minutes).

```rust
fn build_tree_recursive(&mut self, dir: &PathBuf, depth: usize) {
    let entries = fs::list_directory(dir, self.show_hidden, self.sort_by, self.sort_dir);
```

**Skeptic's check.** build_tree_recursive calls fs::list_directory for the root and every expanded folder with no is_slow_path check; grep finds is_slow_path only in nav.rs, split.rs, icons.rs, file_info.rs and wayland_loop.rs. The slow branch of reload() only defers the flat listing: when the worker's result lands, apply_listing ends with 'if self.view_mode == ViewMode::Tree { self.rebuild_tree(); }' (nav.rs:202-204), run from poll_dir_loads on the loop thread, which lists the same directory again synchronously. toggle_tree_expand and cycle_view_mode do the same on click. Pick mode forces Tree (wayland.rs:449), so every file dialog that browses a phone lists it on the UI thread; photo thumbnails on a phone go through the slow lane and hold the jmtpfs lock for a full download each, so each expand click in DCIM waits behind them.

**Fix.** Make the tree build slow-path aware: build the root level from the entries apply_listing was just handed instead of re-listing, and load expanded children on slow paths through a dir_load-style worker keyed by path, with rows rebuilt from a per-path cache when results land. Minimum stopgap: when is_slow_path(root), build depth 0 from self.entries and skip expanded children. New async plumbing, so not mechanical. Fold the pick filter (sections-5) into the same builder.

### audio-12: ID3v2 header that claims more bytes than the file holds makes the in-place path overwrite the entire MP3

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/mp3.rs:276` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. mp3::write refuses a tag whose header claims more than the file holds, before writing anything (regression test added).

**What is wrong.** When total_len exceeds the file length it is clamped to `len`, build(len) pads the new tag to exactly the file size, and the whole file is overwritten with tag + zeros. Reproduced: 8,365-byte file with a header claiming a 1 MiB tag followed by 20 real MPEG frames -> write returned Ok, same length, 0 MPEG frame headers left. Players resync past a bad tag size and play such files, so the audio was recoverable before the save.

**Trigger.** MP3 with a corrupt or truncated ID3v2 size field (interrupted download, damaged tag). Fox shows it with no duration; edit a field, Save: "Saved", the file is now only a tag and padding.

```rust
let old_total = tag.total_len.min(len as usize);
tag.apply(tags);
let new = tag.build(old_total);
if old_total > 0 && new.len() == old_total {
    let f = OpenOptions::new().write(true).open(path).map_err(io_err)?;
    f.write_all_at(&new, 0).map_err(io_err)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read mp3::write, read_head, id3::tag_len/parse/build. tag_len only validates the 10-byte header, so total_len can exceed the file; line 276 clamps it to len, build(old_total) pads to exactly len, and the in-place branch (279-281) pwrite()s that over the whole file. Reproduced with a standalone rustc harness that includes the real audio_tags module: 8,364-byte file (ID3 header claiming 1 MiB + 20 MPEG frames) -> write returned Ok, same length, 0 frame headers left. read() showed title 'abc' and no duration beforehand, so the file opens normally in Properties. Rated high rather than medium: the whole file is irreversibly replaced by tag+zeros and the UI says Saved; only the rarity of the trigger keeps it off critical.

**Fix.** In mp3::write, right after parsing: `if tag.total_len as u64 > len { return Err("ID3 tag is damaged (claims more bytes than the file holds)".into()) }` before any write. Local and mechanical. Do not instead treat it as 'no tag' in the write path: that would prepend a fresh tag and leave the broken one in front of the audio. Optionally also refuse the in-place path when no MPEG frame is found after old_total.

### audio-2: WAV with an overstated data chunk size: tags are appended inside the audio, never read back, and grow the file on every save

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/wav.rs:59` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (B): Same walker: a final data chunk whose declared size overruns the file (0xFFFFFFFF from `ffmpeg -f wav -`, a truncated download) is an 'unfinalised data' chunk. On save its size field is patched to what the file holds (in place: before the tail is written, so a failure cannot leave tags inside the audio; rewrite branch: header emitted with the real size), a pad byte is supplied for an odd length, and the tags land after it where the next walk finds them. Repeated saves no longer grow the file (tests save four times and compare length). An overrunning chunk that is NOT data (cut-off LIST, bext, ...) is refused. Also in wav.rs: a save that changes nothing is a no-op; more than one ID3 chunk, an ID3 chunk that is not a readable tag / holds bytes after the tag / has an ID3 blocker, a damaged LIST INFO, and a WAV without a data chunk are all refused instead of having the unreadable part dropped (the old code replaced an unparseable id3 chunk with an empty tag). LIST INFO entries are now kept as raw bytes (new src/audio_tags/wav/info.rs) so untouched entries are written back byte for byte.

**What is wrong.** walk() clamps an overrunning chunk size in memory only. write() appends the new LIST/id3 chunks at EOF and fixes the RIFF size, but leaves the data chunk header claiming 0xFFFFFFFF (or whatever overran). On the next walk the clamp makes the data chunk swallow the freshly written tag chunks: they become audio samples (a burst of noise at the end, up to ~3 MB when cover art is embedded) and are invisible as tags. Reproduced: data size 0xFFFFFFFF, save #1 176,444 -> 177,558 bytes, read-back title ""; save #2 -> 178,672 bytes, duration creeps up each time.

**Trigger.** Properties on a WAV produced by `ffmpeg ... -f wav - > out.wav` (pipe output leaves 0xFFFFFFFF sizes), an interrupted recording, or a truncated copy/download. Edit Title, Enter. "Saved" flashes, then every field goes blank again because the re-read finds no tags; each retry appends another tag blob into the audio.

```rust
// Streaming recorders leave bogus sizes behind — clamp to the file.
if pos + 8 + size as u64 > len {
    size = (len - pos - 8) as u32;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Reproduced in the harness with a data size of 0xFFFFFFFF (what ffmpeg writes for pipe output): save 1 176,444 -> 177,528 bytes, read-back title ""; save 2 -> 178,612; save 3 -> 179,696; reported duration grows each time. walk() clamps the size in memory only (59-61); write() appends LIST/id3 at EOF and patches only the RIFF size, so the next walk's clamp makes data swallow the new tag chunks. write returns Ok each time and the dialog shows Saved. Result is garbage appended inside the audio extent (up to MBs with cover art) and tags that never read back. Rewrite branch copies the bogus header verbatim (c.offset..c.end()).

**Fix.** Track a `clamped` flag per chunk in walk(). In write(), either refuse when any kept chunk was clamped, or patch that chunk's size field to the real length (write_all_at at c.offset+4) before appending; patching is what the user wants for ffmpeg-pipe files but it is a repair of the file beyond tags, so it is a product decision. Interacts with audio-1 (unparsed trailing bytes) and audio-3 (a clamped size can be odd, giving end()==len+1), so the three need one coherent rule plus tests for 0xFFFFFFFF, 0 and odd sizes.

### cloud-net-2: Remote doc `path` is used unvalidated as a local write path: absolute or `..` paths escape ~/Cloud

- **Where:** `src/cloud/firestore.rs:102` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Remote doc paths are validated (no leading /, no empty/./.. component, no NUL) when parsed and again in the action loop.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-6, cloud-core-3

**What is wrong.** FileDoc.path comes straight from Firestore and is joined onto the cloud root with PathBuf::join. join() with an absolute path replaces the base entirely (confirmed with rustc: `/home/alva/Cloud`.join("/etc/x") == "/etc/x"), and `../` components are resolved by the kernel. A doc whose path is `../.zshrc`, `../.config/autostart/x.desktop`, `../.lantern/bin/lntrn-terminal` or an absolute path matches arm 2 (None local, live remote, no manifest) and download_remote creates parent dirs and renames the downloaded blob over that target. On the following pass arm 4a even tombstones the doc, removing the evidence. The Firestore rules limit writes to the account owner, so the attacker needs the account password (email/password auth, no second factor), a copy of fox-cloud-session.json (refresh token), or control of the other synced machine; with that, a cloud-account compromise becomes arbitrary file overwrite / code execution on every machine that syncs. Download also follows a symlinked parent directory inside ~/Cloud while the scanner skips symlinks.

**Trigger.** Anyone holding the account credentials or refresh token PATCHes users/{uid}/files/x with fields path='../.zshrc', sha256=<hash of an uploaded blob>, size, mtime, deleted=false. Within 30 s each running Fox overwrites ~/.zshrc with the blob. The user sees nothing except a '↓ ../.zshrc' line in fox-cloud.log.

```rust
path: s("path")?,          // firestore.rs from_fields
...
fn abs_for(rel: &str) -> PathBuf { cloud_root().join(rel) }   // reconcile.rs:147
let abs = abs_for(&doc.path);
std::fs::create_dir_all(parent)?; std::fs::write(&tmp, &bytes)?; std::fs::rename(&tmp, &abs)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** from_fields takes path verbatim; seed_full/apply_delta key the index by it; reconcile_with arm 2 (None, Some(r) !deleted, None) calls download_remote, which does abs_for(&doc.path) = cloud_root().join(rel), create_dir_all(parent), write tmp, rename. rustc check: join("/etc/x") == "/etc/x"; join("../.zshrc") == "/home/alva/Cloud/../.zshrc" with tmp "…/.zshrc..fox-tmp", which the kernel resolves outside ~/Cloud. No normalisation or component check exists anywhere on that path (also resolve_conflict's step 3 and delete_local use abs_for). On the next pass arm 4a tombstones the doc. firestore.rules restricts writes to the owning uid, so the attacker needs the account password, the refresh token from fox-cloud-session.json, or the other machine; given that, it is arbitrary file overwrite in $HOME on every syncing machine. No such doc exists in the current index (checked: 0 absolute or '..' paths).

**Fix.** Validate in from_fields (return None + log): reject empty, leading '/', NUL, and any component equal to "", "." or "..". Note the proposed belt-and-braces `abs.starts_with(cloud_root())` does NOT catch '..' (Path::starts_with is component-wise without normalisation; rustc test returned true for '../.zshrc'), so the component check is the real guard. Optionally refuse when an existing parent component is a symlink. Dropping such docs means they never reach the action loop, which is the desired outcome.

### cloud-net-6: No read/write timeout on any cloud HTTP request: a stalled connection hangs the sync thread forever

- **Where:** `src/cloud/http.rs:31` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. All cloud requests go through one ureq Agent with connect/read/write timeouts.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-19, cloud-e2e-7

**What is wrong.** All requests (http.rs get/post_json/patch_json/put_bytes, auth.rs sign_in/refresh) go through ureq's default agent. In ureq 2.12.1 the defaults are timeout_connect = 30 s, timeout_read = None, timeout_write = None, timeout = None (agent.rs:256-259; the docs say 'requests may block forever on reads by default'). If the peer goes silent after the request is sent (laptop suspended mid-request, Wi-Fi roam or drop with a new address, NAT state lost), the blocked read never returns. The single sync thread is then stuck for good: no more uploads or downloads, the status mutex stays at Syncing so the pill says 'Syncing…' forever, and if the stall is inside auth::refresh the session mutex is held too. Only restarting Fox recovers. For a request whose body was not yet acknowledged the hang ends after the kernel's TCP retransmit timeout (about 15 minutes); for a response already in flight (blob download) there is no bound.

**Trigger.** Close the laptop lid, or lose Wi-Fi, while a sync pass is downloading or waiting on a response; resume on a different network. Fox shows 'Syncing…' indefinitely and nothing syncs until the app is restarted.

```rust
match ureq::get(url)
    .set("Authorization", &format!("Bearer {tok}"))
    .call()
```

**Skeptic's check.** Cargo.lock pins ureq 2.12.1; its AgentBuilder::new() defaults are timeout_connect 30 s, timeout_read None, timeout_write None, timeout None (agent.rs), and response/stream code sets the socket read timeout from config.timeout_read. No Agent/AgentBuilder/timeout appears anywhere in src/cloud; every call uses ureq::get/post/request, and no TCP keepalive is set. A response-pending read on a connection whose peer went silent (suspend/resume, Wi-Fi roam, NAT loss) blocks with nothing to retransmit, so it never returns; the single sync thread stops, status stays Syncing, and a stall inside auth::refresh also holds the session mutex (Authed::token locks across the call). The UI thread does not take that mutex, so the window itself stays responsive. Laptop suspend during a pass is an ordinary event for this two-machine setup.

**Fix.** One shared Agent built with timeout_connect(10 s), timeout_read(60-120 s), timeout_write(60 s), stored in Authed and used by http.rs and auth.rs. Use per-read/per-write timeouts, not the overall .timeout(), so large blobs still transfer; give read a generous value because the response to a several-hundred-MB upload arrives only after the server finishes storing it. Separately (from cloud-e2e-7): reconcile_with keeps iterating after a transport failure, paying a connect timeout per remaining file; stop the pass on the first transport-class error.

### cloud-net-7: Files that can never upload (>= 1 GiB, unreadable, Storage 402) are re-hashed, read whole into RAM and re-uploaded every 30 s forever

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:172` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2, C9): New src/cloud/failures.rs: per-path failure record at ~/.lantern/cloud/failures.json (account-scoped, read at the start of every pass), wait doubling from 1 min to 1 h (6 h when the server refused with a 4xx); a waiting path is skipped with no hashing, reading or sending until the wait is over or the local file, the remote doc or the planned action changes. scan.rs: a file of 1 GiB or more (storage::MAX_BLOB_BYTES, same number as storage.rules) is never opened, is inert on all sides (not a deletion) and is logged once. Exposed: SyncReport.stuck / stuck_sample, SyncHandle::stuck_files(), SyncProblem::CannotSync, StuckKind {TooBig, Unreadable, Failing}; SyncHandle::retry_failed() clears the waits (works from any window). UI for A2's list of files that cannot be synced: the pill says 'N files cannot be synced' / 'N files failed to sync' / 'N items could not be read' (inside ~/Cloud); a click opens the 'Cloud sync' details dialog with the account, the state in a sentence and the stuck files by name with the reason each (too big for the cloud, cannot be read, failed N times and waiting), '...and K more', and a 'Try again now' button (SyncHandle::retry_failed) when something is failing.

**What is wrong.** storage.rules rejects objects with `request.resource.size < 1024*1024*1024` false, but the client has no size check and no per-file failure memory. A file that has never synced has no manifest entry, so the stat cache misses and scan_local sha256-hashes the whole file on every pass; upload_local then loads the entire file into a Vec and POSTs it in one shot; the server refuses; reconcile_with returns Err; sync.rs treats Err with no backoff, so the next 30 s poll does it all again (and continuously once full_due is stuck, see the 6 h finding). Cost per attempt is the file size in RAM plus up to the file size in upstream bandwidth. A file larger than free memory gets Fox OOM-killed or drives the machine into swap. The same endless loop applies to any persistent per-file error: the log shows one file failing with 'Permission denied (os error 13)' 3523 times between 2026-07-05 and 2026-07-28, and 1376 files failing with Storage 'status code 402'.

**Trigger.** Drag a video or ISO of 1 GiB or more into ~/Cloud. Fox's memory jumps by the file size every 30 s, the uplink is saturated re-sending it, the status pill stays on 'Sync error', and fox-cloud.log gains two lines per attempt, indefinitely.

```rust
let bytes = std::fs::read(&local.abs)?;
let mime = mime_for(&local.rel);
storage::upload_blob(authed, &local.sha, &bytes, &mime)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** Kept separate from cloud-core-17 because the root cause is different: no client-side size cap and no per-file failure memory. storage.rules rejects objects >= 1 GiB; the client never checks size. A never-synced file has no manifest entry, so cached_sha misses (it needs both meta and entries; lines 301-305 only cache when manifest.get(rel) matches) and the whole file is sha256'd on every pass, then read into RAM and POSTed, the server refuses, reconcile_with returns Err, and sync.rs's Err arm has no backoff: the next 30 s poll repeats it (continuously once full_due is stuck). The same applies to every persistent per-file error; the log shows it happened for real: 1,376 Storage PUTs failing with 402 in one pass and one file failing with EACCES thousands of times. Not verified: whether Firebase rejects an oversized upload before or after receiving the body.

**Fix.** Skip (log once) files at or above a shared size constant that matches storage.rules before hashing/reading; keep a per-path failure record keyed by (size, mtime) with exponential backoff; stop the pass on quota-class errors (402/429). A skipped oversized file must stay out of `locals`-driven deletion logic (it has no manifest entry, so it is inert today). Design work, not mechanical.

### cloud-net-8: Conflict rename overwrites an existing '(conflict from <device>)' copy

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:265` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Conflict copies get a counted name that is free on disk, in the manifest and remotely.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-15, cloud-e2e-12

**What is wrong.** (Outside my listed files.) conflict_name is deterministic (`stem (conflict from <device>).ext`) and rename() silently replaces an existing destination. A conflict copy exists precisely because it holds edits the user has not merged yet; a second conflict on the same file replaces it, and upload_local then pushes the replacement to the same doc so the other machine's copy of the first conflict file is overwritten too. The old bytes survive only as an unreferenced blob. With two Fox processes it also happens within a single conflict: process A renames, uploads and downloads the remote version to the original path; process B, which scanned before A's rename and reaches the path later (HashSet iteration order differs per process), renames the just-downloaded remote version over A's conflict copy and uploads those bytes under the old local sha. On the PC the label is always 'device' (see the hostname finding), so names never differ by machine either.

**Trigger.** Edit notes.txt on both machines so a conflict occurs: 'notes (conflict from device).txt' appears. Do not merge it. Have a second conflict on notes.txt later. The first conflict copy's contents are gone on both machines.

```rust
let conflict_rel = conflict_name(&local.rel, device);
let conflict_abs = abs_for(&conflict_rel);
...
std::fs::rename(&local.abs, &conflict_abs)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** conflict_name (151-162) is a pure function of rel and device; resolve_conflict (252-281) calls std::fs::rename(&local.abs, &conflict_abs) with no existence check. Verified with a rustc experiment that rename silently replaces an existing file. upload_local then PATCHes the conflict path's doc with the new sha, so the other machine's copy is overwritten via arm 3c. Conflict files are not excluded by should_ignore. On this PC /etc/hostname does not exist, so device is the literal "device" (4311 of the docs carry it). Trigger (second conflict on the same file while the first copy is unmerged) is uncommon but it destroys exactly the edits the copy existed to preserve. The two-process variant in the description is plausible but not needed.

**Fix.** Before the rename, loop: while conflict_abs exists or manifest/remotes contain conflict_rel, append " 2", " 3"... to the stem. Local and mechanical. RENAME_NOREPLACE is nice-to-have, not required for the single-process case. Does not break anything: conflict copies are ordinary synced files.

### cloud-core-6: Every Fox process runs its own sync engine on the same ~/Cloud, manifest.json and remote-index.json with no lock

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:53` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category race; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1): New src/cloud/owner.rs: the engine runs only in the process holding an exclusive flock on ~/.lantern/cloud/sync.lock (released by the kernel when the process exits). src/cloud/sync.rs: every SyncHandle thread is owner or follower; a follower runs no sync, mirrors the owner's SyncReport from ~/.lantern/cloud/status.json (written by the owner only when it changes, not fsynced) and retries the lock every 3 s, so sync continues when the owner exits. Confirm/decline from a follower window reaches the owner through ~/.lantern/cloud/deletions-answer.json. Progress (mirror first, then manifest) is saved every 5 s during a pass, so a killed owner repeats seconds of work.

**What is wrong.** src/wayland.rs:441 calls app.init_cloud() unconditionally: for every window, for --desktop mode, and for every --pick/--pick-save dialog that lntrn-portal and lntrn-notepad spawn. Nothing makes Fox single-instance and nothing locks the sync state. Concurrent engines wake on the same inotify events, debounce the same 1.5 s and run passes at the same moment, each from its own copy of the manifest. Consequences traced: (1) manifest.json is last-writer-wins, so removals done by one process are re-added by the other, producing the stale entries of the critical finding above; two unequal-length overlapping writes also leave trailing garbage (reproduced with a rustc test), and a manifest that fails to parse is silently treated as empty. (2) In a real conflict both processes run resolve_conflict: A renames local X to the conflict name and downloads the remote version into X; B, working from its earlier scan, then renames X (now remote content) over the conflict copy, so the user's local version is lost on the path designed to keep both. (3) Both download to the same *.fox-tmp path. (4) A just-deleted file can be re-downloaded by the process whose index predates the other's tombstone. (5) Uploads and tombstones are duplicated. A short-lived picker is also killed mid-pass when the dialog closes.

**Trigger.** Have a Fox window open and trigger a file dialog from any app (portal Save/Open), or open two Fox windows. Then: edit a file in ~/Cloud that was also edited on the other machine. Both processes resolve the conflict and the "(conflict from ...)" copy ends up holding the remote content; the local edit is gone.

```rust
pub fn spawn(cfg: Arc<CloudConfig>, session: Arc<Mutex<Session>>) -> Self {
    ...
    thread::Builder::new()
        .name("fox-cloud-sync".into())
        .spawn(move || run_loop(authed, status_thr, stop_thr))
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland.rs:441 calls app.init_cloud() unconditionally before the pick branch, so every window, --desktop instance and --pick/--pick-save dialog (spawned by lntrn-portal/src/filechooser.rs:228 and lntrn-notepad/src/actions.rs) starts its own fox-cloud-sync thread. init_cloud only guards against a second spawn in the same process; there is no flock/pidfile. Manifest::save and RemoteIndex::save are plain std::fs::write (truncate in place, last writer wins) and load() maps a parse failure to an empty default. Each process works from its own manifest copy, so removals by one are re-added by the other, which is one way the 168 stale entries of cloud-core-2 arise. The log has 862 'sync paused' lines with consecutive 600 s pauses 2-5 minutes apart, which one process's doubling backoff cannot produce. The specific conflict-clobber interleaving is plausible (B's rename succeeds only after A's download completes) but I did not prove the timing.

**Fix.** Exclusive flock on ~/.lantern/cloud/sync.lock taken in SyncHandle::spawn; only the holder runs the engine, others retry the lock periodically so sync survives the owner closing. Do not simply skip init_cloud in pick mode: open_cloud_or_login (nav.rs:10-17) shows the login dialog when app.cloud is None, so load CloudState but skip the spawn. Also make both saves write-temp-then-rename. Threading and ownership change.

### cloud-core-7: Full Firestore list on every process start, including file-picker dialogs; tombstones are never purged so each launch costs 4370 reads

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:130` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1): src/cloud/remote_index.rs persists uid and last_full_ms (wall clock); RemoteIndex::needs_full(). src/cloud/engine.rs fetch(): a start (or an ownership takeover) does a delta pull unless the mirror is missing, unparsable, belongs to another uid, or the last full list is older than 6 h (or stamped in the future). A failed periodic full list is retried after 15 min while delta passes continue; an unseeded mirror is never reconciled against. Tombstone purging (cloud-net-16) is not part of this.

**What is wrong.** The persisted remote index is loaded and then immediately replaced by a full list, regardless of how fresh it is. One read is billed per doc, and tombstone docs are kept forever: the collection is 4370 docs for 172 live files. Eleven Fox launches per day (windows plus every portal Open/Save dialog) exhaust the 50k free reads. The log since the delta build has 650 full-list GETs answered with 429 and up to 152 quota pauses per day, far more than one process's 10 min to 2 h backoff could log.

**Trigger.** Open and close Fox windows or use file dialogs in other apps about a dozen times in a day. Sync shows "Sync paused (quota)" on both machines until the quota resets.

```rust
// Startup: full pull-then-reconcile so a freshly-signed-in machine (and
// the index mirror) catch up with the cloud before the user looks at it.
if let Outcome::RateLimited = sync_pass(&authed, &status, &mut index, true) {
```

**Skeptic's check.** run_loop loads the persisted index (126) and immediately runs sync_pass(.., true) (130), which calls list_all and seed_full regardless of the index's age. Verified the collection size on disk: 4370 docs (4198 tombstones), so each process start bills 4370 reads; about eleven starts exhaust the 50k free tier. Pick-mode processes start sync too (see cloud-core-6). The log shows 2680 'status code 429' lines.
