**Fix.** Persist a wall-clock last_full_ms in RemoteIndex and start with a delta pass when the stored index is younger than FULL_EVERY; fall back to a full list when the file is missing, unparsable or stale. Caveat: 3164 of the docs have no updated_at and are invisible to delta, so the periodic full list must stay. Changes startup sync semantics (a fresh sign-in on a machine with a stale index from another account must still do a full list: key the index by uid).

### cloud-net-3: After 6 h uptime any failing pass becomes a back-to-back full Firestore list loop (last_full is only reset on Outcome::Ok)

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:168` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. A failed full pass is retried after 15 minutes instead of on every loop turn; last_full advances on any non-rate-limited full attempt.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-4, cloud-e2e-4

**What is wrong.** Once the process has been up for FULL_EVERY (6 h), full_due is true. It is only cleared in the Ok arm. If the pass returns Err (any single file failing in reconcile_with, a Storage 402, a network error) full_due stays true, and because the outer condition is `pending_dirty || poll_due || full_due`, the next loop iteration (after the 500 ms recv_timeout) immediately runs another FULL pass. Each full pass is list_all over the whole collection, which is currently 4370 docs (4198 of them tombstones) = 4370 billed reads in 15 GETs, followed by a full reconcile that re-attempts the failing upload. That is roughly 4370 reads every few seconds: the 50k free daily quota is gone in about a minute, and on a pay-as-you-go plan it is tens of millions of billed reads per day. This is the same failure class as the earlier 50k-reads-in-six-minutes incident. When the Err is a network failure the loop instead spins on DNS/connect errors and writes a log line roughly twice a second.

**Trigger.** Leave a Fox window open for more than 6 hours while one file in ~/Cloud cannot sync (the log shows exactly such a file: 'Ember Mouse Cursor 2.png: Permission denied' failed 3523 times over 23 days; a file of 1 GiB or more, or a Storage 402, does the same). At the 6 h mark sync starts hammering Firestore continuously; status pill shows 'Sync error' then 'Sync paused (quota)' for the rest of the day, every day. Or: be offline at the 6 h mark and watch fox-cloud.log grow by two lines a second.

```rust
let full_due = last_full.elapsed() >= FULL_EVERY;
let poll_due = last_poll.elapsed() >= POLL_EVERY;
if pending_dirty || poll_due || full_due {
    match sync_pass(&authed, &status, &mut index, full_due) {
        Outcome::Ok => { ...; if full_due { last_full = std::time::Instant::now(); } }
        ...
        Outcome::Err => { pending_dirty = false; }
    }
    last_poll = std::time::Instant::now();
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced the loop (139-197): full_due = last_full.elapsed() >= 6 h; the gate is pending_dirty || poll_due || full_due; last_full is only assigned in the Ok arm (175-177); the Err arm only clears pending_dirty; backoff applies only to RateLimited. So after 6 h uptime any Err leaves full_due true and the next iteration, after the 500 ms recv_timeout, runs another full pass. reconcile_with returns Err when any single file fails (439-449), after list_all already succeeded and billed the whole collection (4370 docs on disk). Offline, the fetch itself fails and the loop logs a 'reconcile failed' line about twice a second. The log has 5576 'reconcile failed' lines, so persistent per-file failures are normal here. Line corrected to the start of the block.

**Fix.** Reset last_full after any full attempt that was not RateLimited (move the `if full_due { last_full = now }` out of the Ok arm so it also runs for Err), and gate the pass on `pending_dirty || poll_due` with full_due passed only as the mode. Local and mechanical. Consequence: a full list that failed for network reasons is retried 6 h later rather than immediately; if that matters, set last_full to now - FULL_EVERY + 15 min in the Err arm for a bounded retry.

### cloud-net-4: Delta cursor never moves past the newest doc, so every 30 s poll re-reads the whole last 5-minute batch forever

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:221` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): src/cloud/remote_index.rs: the cursor moves to the query's readTime on every pull, changed docs or not, and a delta asks for updated_at > cursor - 2 min (OVERLAP, for a write stamped just before a pull and committed just after). A quiet poll is one read; a doc is re-read only by the polls of the two minutes after it was written. The cursor alone does not rewrite the mirror file. Tests: pass_tests/polling.rs (40-doc burst: 40 reads inside the overlap, then exactly 1 read per poll), engine_more.rs::a_full_list_is_followed_by_deltas_on_the_servers_clock.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-5, cloud-e2e-3

**What is wrong.** cursor_ms is the largest updated_at ever seen and the query is `updated_at > cursor - 5 min`. Because the cursor only advances when a newer doc appears, every quiet poll returns ALL docs stamped within 5 minutes before the newest doc, again and again, and Firestore bills one read per returned doc. The comment in firestore.rs ('a quiet poll costs ~1 read') is only true when the newest write was a lone file. After any bulk add or bulk delete the cost per poll is the size of that batch, per Fox process, per machine, until some later write happens to move the cursor. Evidence from this machine's remote-index.json: 1205 tombstones are stamped between 19:08:07 and 19:11:03 on 2026-09-02 and the next stamp is 2026-09-22, so for 20 days every delta poll returned 1205 docs (about 2400 reads a minute per running process: free quota gone in ~20 minutes, or about 3.5 M billed reads a day on a paid plan).

**Trigger.** Copy a folder of 20+ files into ~/Cloud, or delete one, and leave Fox open. From then on each 30 s poll on each machine re-reads every doc in that batch. With 18+ docs in the trailing window a single always-open Fox exceeds 50k reads/day; the user sees 'Sync paused (quota)' the same day and again each following day until another file changes.

```rust
let since = index
    .cursor_ms
    .saturating_sub(super::remote_index::OVERLAP_MS);
super::firestore::query_changed_since(authed, since)
...
// remote_index.rs:58
if let Some(ts) = doc.updated_at { self.cursor_ms = self.cursor_ms.max(ts); }
```

**Skeptic's check.** sync_pass queries updated_at > cursor_ms - OVERLAP_MS (221-226); cursor_ms is the largest stamp ever seen (remote_index.rs:58-62) and does not advance with time; query_changed_since has no limit; apply_delta's changed count is discarded. Every poll therefore returns all docs stamped in the 5 minutes before the newest doc, and Firestore bills each. Checked the on-disk index: the largest 5-minute cluster is 1205 docs ending 2026-09-02 19:11:03 and the next stamp is 2026-09-22 17:47:45, so for that whole period each 30 s poll from each running Fox returned 1205 docs. Today only 1 doc sits in the window, which is why it is currently quiet.

**Fix.** Store the local wall-clock time at the start of each successful pull (last_pull_ms, persisted) and query updated_at > min(cursor_ms, last_pull_ms - OVERLAP_MS). A doc written since the last pull by a machine whose clock is up to OVERLAP behind is still caught, a burst is re-read for at most 5 real minutes, and a quiet poll returns zero docs. Changes sync semantics (what a delta can miss), so not mechanical; combine with cloud-e2e-8's own-write upsert so the overlap is not what makes own writes visible.

### fileops-9: Folder copy aborts midway on any symlink-to-directory or dangling symlink; file symlinks are dereferenced

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:65` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): New src/copy_tree/mod.rs: entries are classified with lstat; a symlink is recreated with read_link + symlink (never followed); a per-entry error is recorded and the tree continues; disk full / read-only / device gone abort the item and roll it back. A destination that cannot hold symlinks reports that entry ('the destination cannot hold symbolic links'). file_ops::copy_dir_recursive / copy_file (used by undo's redo) are now thin wrappers over it.

**What is wrong.** DirEntry::file_type does not follow symlinks, so every symlink goes to fs::copy, which does follow them. A symlink to a directory fails with InvalidInput ('neither a regular file nor a symlink to a regular file'), a dangling symlink with NotFound, and `?` aborts the whole recursive copy at that point (verified with a standalone build of this exact function: entries listed after the link were not copied). The error is then swallowed by run_copy, so the user gets a silently incomplete folder. Symlinks to files become full regular-file copies. Used by paste/drop copy, cross-device move, duplicate and redo.

**Trigger.** Copy or duplicate a folder containing a directory symlink or a broken link: a Wine/Proton prefix (dosdevices/c: -> ../drive_c), a project with node_modules/.bin, a dotfiles repo, ~/.steam. The copy stops partway with no message; the destination looks complete at the top level but is missing everything after the link.

```rust
if entry.file_type()?.is_dir() {
    copy_dir_recursive(&entry.path(), &target)?;
} else {
    std::fs::copy(entry.path(), &target)?;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** DirEntry::file_type does not follow symlinks, so every symlink reaches std::fs::copy, which does. Reproduced with rustc using this exact function: a directory containing a symlink to a directory returned Err(InvalidInput, "the source path is neither a regular file nor a symlink to a regular file") and left the destination with fewer entries than the source. `?` aborts the whole tree; run_copy swallows it (`Err(_) => {}`, ops.rs:196) and does not push the target to `created`, so there is no message and no undo entry for the partial tree. Callers: ops.rs:178 (paste, drop, cross-device move), file_ops.rs:827 (duplicate), undo.rs:237 (redo). For a cross-device move the source is kept because res is Err, so the harm is a silently incomplete copy.

**Fix.** Handle symlinks explicitly (read_link + std::os::unix::fs::symlink), recurse for directories, copy regular files, skip other types. Not mechanical for two reasons: symlink() fails on FAT/exFAT/MTP destinations and needs a fallback; and if per-entry errors are collected instead of propagated, copy_dir_recursive must still report failure, because run_copy deletes the source of a cross-device move when it returns Ok (ops.rs:184-190). Surface the partial result to the user.

### fileops-4: "Keep Both" is ignored when the op falls back to sudo: the existing file is overwritten

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:82` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): Permission failures now carry the whole OpItem (source, the target the user chose, replace flag) from the worker to finish_op; sudo.rs PendingPrivOp::Copy/Move take Vec<PrivItem> with exact targets. A Keep Both name is what the privileged command writes to.

**What is wrong.** When a rename/copy fails with PermissionDenied only the SOURCE is remembered (here and ops.rs:193-195). The conflict resolution (effective_target, e.g. 'foo (2).txt') is thrown away. The sudo fallback rebuilds the target as dest.join(src.file_name()) (sudo.rs:194-219) and runs `mv src dest/foo.txt` or `cp -r -- src dest/foo.txt` as root, silently overwriting the very file the user chose to keep. With a cached sudo ticket there is not even a prompt.

**Trigger.** Paste or drag config.toml into a root-owned folder (/etc/foo, /opt/..., /usr/local/...) that already contains config.toml. Choose Keep Both. Enter the password in the sudo modal (or none if the ticket is cached). The original config.toml is replaced; no 'config (2).toml' is created.

```rust
Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied => {
    paste.perm_fails.push(src);
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** queue_cut pushes only `src` to perm_fails on PermissionDenied (81-83) and run_copy does the same (ops.rs:193-195); the resolved effective_target is dropped. finalize_paste (280-285) and poll_op_progress (343-358) build PendingPrivOp::Move/Copy { sources, dest }, and sudo.rs build_argv (194-219) recomputes the target as dest.join(file_name) and runs `mv src target` / `cp -r -- src target` as root. With Keep Both in a root-owned folder the copy to 'name (2)' fails with EACCES, then sudo overwrites the original name. priv_run (1034-1047) runs immediately with a cached ticket, no prompt.

**Fix.** Carry (src, effective_target) pairs through perm_fails (PendingPaste, OpProgress::Done, OpHandle.done_payload) and give PendingPrivOp::Move/Copy explicit pairs; emit `mv -T -- src target` and `cp -rT -- src target`. Touches four files and the sudo prompt description. Also add `--` to the mv argv, which currently lacks it.

### fileops-10: Pasting a folder into itself or a descendant recurses until PATH_MAX, duplicating the tree hundreds of times

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:153` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. advance_paste skips a folder whose destination is itself or inside it.
- **Also reported as:** input-4

**What is wrong.** paste() has no 'destination is inside a source' check (drag-drop has valid_dest; paste does not). copy_dir_recursive creates dst first and then lists src, which now contains dst, so it descends into its own output forever. A standalone run of the exact function with a folder named 'Documents' produced 396 nested levels, each with a full copy of the payload, before failing with ENAMETOOLONG; with real data it fills the disk first. The error is swallowed, no undo entry is recorded, and a 4 KB-deep directory chain is left behind.

**Trigger.** Ctrl+C on a folder, open that folder (or any subfolder of it), Ctrl+V.

```rust
self.pending_paste = Some(crate::conflict::PendingPaste::new(mode, dest, sources));
self.advance_paste();
```

**Skeptic's check.** valid_dest exists only in wayland_actions/drag_drop.rs and is called only there; paste() (121-155), reached from key.rs:721 and context_menu.rs:598, has no check that dest is a source or inside one. With dest = A the target is A/A, which does not exist, so no dialog; the worker runs copy_dir_recursive(A, A/A), which creates the destination and then lists the source that now contains it. Reproduced with rustc on this exact function: a folder with 5 files ended in Err(File name too long) with 3396 entries created. Cancel is only checked between top-level pairs (ops.rs:163), so the progress strip's cancel does nothing. Cut is harmless (rename into itself fails with EINVAL and is ignored).

**Fix.** In advance_paste, before resolving each source, skip it when paste.dest.starts_with(&src) (canonicalise both to cover symlinked paths); that covers paste and drag-drop in one place. Local and mechanical; tell the user why nothing was pasted. Root mode is already protected by cp's own 'cannot copy a directory into itself'.

### fileops-7: "Replace" deletes the destination immediately on the UI thread, before the deferred copy runs - cancel or failure leaves nothing

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:220` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): file_ops.rs: resolving a conflict only records OpItem{src,target,replace}; nothing is deleted on the UI thread any more. ops/worker.rs: a copy is written under a hidden staging name next to the target (copy_tree::temp_sibling), verified, then the old item is moved to the Trash and the staging item renamed into place (RENAME_NOREPLACE); if the rename fails the old item is restored from the Trash; a failed or cancelled copy removes the staging item and leaves the old one untouched. cancel_paste now changes nothing on disk and hands the clipboard back.

**What is wrong.** In Copy mode (and cross-device Cut) the actual copy is deferred to the worker after the whole queue is resolved, but the existing destination is removed during resolution. If the user then cancels the conflict dialog for a later item (cancel_paste drops resolved_pairs/xdev_pairs/perm_fails without running them - PendingPaste.created is never populated, so its 'finalize whatever already happened' comment is false for Copy), clicks the progress cancel button, declines the sudo prompt, or the copy fails (disk full, I/O error, unreadable source), the destination files are already gone and no replacement arrives.

**Trigger.** Paste two files that both exist in the destination. Dialog 1: Replace (no apply-to-all). Dialog 2: Esc/Cancel. Destination file 1 has been deleted and nothing was copied. Or: 'Replace, apply to all' on 100 files -> all 100 destination files are deleted up front; press the red cancel button after 3 files -> 97 files are simply gone from the destination.

```rust
Some(ConflictAction::Replace) => {
    let _ = if target.is_dir() {
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&target)
    } else {
        std::fs::remove_file(&target)
    };
    (target.clone(), false)
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Both resolution sites delete the existing destination on the UI thread at resolution time (220-227 for apply-to-all, 456-462 for single), while in Copy mode the pair is only queued in resolved_pairs and copied by the worker after the whole queue is resolved. cancel_paste (478-503), reached from Esc (key.rs:169) and the Cancel All button/scrim (click.rs:82), takes pending_paste and drops resolved_pairs and xdev_pairs unexecuted; PendingPaste.created is never written anywhere (grep), so the 'finalize whatever already happened' branch is dead for Copy. Result: Replace on item 1, cancel on item 2 leaves item 1's destination deleted and nothing copied. The same holds for a worker cancel, a failed copy, or a declined sudo prompt.

**Fix.** Do not delete during resolution. Record replace intent on the pair and let the worker copy to a temp name in the destination directory and rename over the target on success (for directories: move the old one aside, copy, then remove the aside or restore it on failure). For inline Cut, rename already replaces files atomically, so only directory targets need the aside dance. Alters overwrite behaviour and the worker protocol. Must also guard src == target (see extra finding).

### dialogs-5: Replace on a folder permanently deletes the whole existing folder (no merge, no trash) while the dialog shows it as "Folder · 0 MB"

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:457` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): Replace on a folder moves the whole old folder to the Trash (worker, after the new one is complete), never remove_dir_all, never a merge. dialogs.rs draw_conflict_dialog: title says 'A folder named X already exists', the Existing line shows 'Folder · N items' (ConflictMeta.items), and a line states what Replace does ('moves the whole existing folder to the Trash. The two folders are not merged.'). key.rs: Enter no longer triggers Replace. On a device with no Trash the Replace button is shown disabled with the reason.

**What is wrong.** When the conflicting name is a directory, Replace removes the entire existing tree before copying the incoming one. Files that exist only in the destination folder are lost with no undo (UndoAction::Copy only removes what was created). The dialog gives no hint: ConflictMeta::read uses the directory inode's len() and dialogs.rs formats it with the drive formatter, so both sides read "Folder · 0 MB". Also applies to renaming a file onto an existing folder's name, and to the Enter-key default.

**Trigger.** Copy a folder Photos (10 files) into a place that already has Photos with 500 different files. Dialog: "Photos already exists", both sides "Folder · 0 MB". Press Enter or Replace: the 500 files are gone, only the 10 remain.

```rust
let _ = if target.is_dir() {
    std::fs::remove_dir_all(&target)
} else {
    std::fs::remove_file(&target)
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** All three Replace sites (220-226, 388-392, 457-461) run remove_dir_all on a directory target before the incoming copy; there is no merge path in file_ops.rs or ops.rs and nothing goes to Trash. The dialog text is as claimed: ConflictMeta::read takes metadata().len() of the directory inode and format_meta_line (dialogs.rs:1142-1145) prints it through fs::format_size, which only has MB and GB, so both sides read `Folder · 0 MB`. Whole-folder replace is a legitimate design, but here it is permanent, unannounced and the Enter default, and files that exist only in the destination are lost.

**Fix.** Owner decision. Options in order of effort: move the replaced target to Trash instead of remove_dir_all; label the button and body for folders (`Replace folder, N items will be deleted`); offer Merge for directory-versus-directory. Independently, stop binding Enter to Replace. None of these is mechanical.

### dialogs-4: Replace deletes the destination at click time; cancelling the rest of the paste (or a failed/cancelled copy) leaves nothing in its place

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:478` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): Same change as fileops-7 (conflict.rs PendingPaste now holds resolved OpItems; file_ops.rs advance_paste/apply_single_conflict_action/cancel_paste). Also the rename-conflict Replace (file_ops.rs rename_replacing): checks the source still exists, moves the old target to the Trash, renames with no-replace, restores the old item if the rename fails, and pushes Trash + Rename undo entries.

**What is wrong.** In Copy mode the conflict handler removes the existing target immediately but only records (src, target) in resolved_pairs; the copy itself runs later in the worker, after every conflict is answered. cancel_paste (Esc, Cancel All, or a click on the scrim) takes pending_paste and drops resolved_pairs (paste.created is never filled in this flow), so targets already approved for Replace stay deleted and are never written. The same early delete means a copy that later fails (source on a phone that disconnected, disk full, user hits the progress cancel) also leaves the destination without the file. Replace on a rename also deletes pending.to without checking pending.from still exists.

**Trigger.** Copy a.txt and b.txt into a folder that already has both. First dialog (a.txt): Replace. Second dialog (b.txt): Esc or Cancel All. Result: the destination's a.txt is gone and no new a.txt was copied.

```rust
pub fn cancel_paste(&mut self) {
    self.conflict_dialog = None;
    if let Some(paste) = self.pending_paste.take() {
        ...
        PasteMode::Copy => {
            if !paste.created.is_empty() {
```

**Skeptic's check.** In Copy mode apply_single_conflict_action deletes the target at decision time (456-462) and only pushes (src, target) into resolved_pairs; the copy runs later in finalize_paste -> spawn_copy_worker. cancel_paste (478-503) takes pending_paste and drops resolved_pairs; paste.created is declared in PendingPaste but never pushed to in this flow, so the Copy arm does nothing but re-arm the clipboard. Result after Replace on item 1 then Esc/Cancel All/scrim click on item 2: destination's item 1 is deleted and never rewritten. The same early delete means a later copy failure (worker swallows errors) leaves the destination empty-handed.

**Fix.** Do not delete on the UI thread at decision time. Carry a replace flag on the resolved pair and have the worker copy to a temporary sibling and rename over the target (or remove immediately before its own copy). With that, cancel_paste can keep dropping resolved pairs with targets untouched. This moves deletion onto the worker and changes overwrite timing, so it needs review.

### fileops-14: Move to Trash silently does nothing for anything not on the home filesystem (USB sticks, other partitions, /tmp, phone)

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:621` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): New src/trash/ (mod.rs, info.rs, locate.rs, registry.rs): XDG Trash spec. Home trash for items on its filesystem; elsewhere $top/.Trash/$uid when a valid sticky non-symlink .Trash exists, else $top/.Trash-$uid created on demand (0700, must be a real dir owned by the user); top dir found by walking st_dev (a mount point also ends the walk); Path= relative to $top for volume trashes, percent-encoded through file_ops::trashinfo_contents; names reserved with O_EXCL on the .trashinfo; DeletionDate in local time. trash::trash(path) -> Result<Trashed, TrashError> is the one call; restore/retrash take the Trashed. New src/trash_ops.rs holds the App side: trash_selected/trash_paths (anything that cannot be trashed raises a 'can't be moved to the Trash: <reason>. Delete permanently?' dialog instead of doing nothing), restore_selected (works per trash, reports failures), empty_trash (every trash, on the worker), delete_selected (worker). fs::list_directory merges every mounted drive's trash into the Trash view; in_trash() recognises any trash by path shape. A paste or drop INTO the Trash now is a real Move to Trash. undo.rs Trash undo/redo go through trash::restore / trash::retrash.

**What is wrong.** trash_selected only ever renames into ~/.local/share/Trash/files. Across filesystems (or btrfs subvolumes) rename fails with EXDEV, which lands in this catch-all: the .trashinfo is removed and nothing else happens - no fallback to a per-volume trash, no copy+delete, no offer to delete permanently, no message. Outside the Trash view the context menu only offers 'Move to Trash' and the Delete key only calls trash_selected, so there is no way to delete such files from Fox at all.

**Trigger.** Open a USB stick (or /tmp, a second drive, the phone), select a file, press Delete or right-click -> Move to Trash. Nothing happens.

```rust
Err(_) => {
    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&info_path);
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** trash_selected only ever renames into ~/.local/share/Trash/files. Across filesystems rename returns EXDEV, which is neither Ok nor PermissionDenied, so it lands in the catch-all at 621-623: the .trashinfo is removed and nothing else happens, no message. I checked the other entry points: KEY_DELETE calls trash_selected (key.rs:751), CTX_TRASH calls delete_selected only when in_trash() (context_menu.rs:604-610), and there is no Shift+Delete. So outside the home filesystem (USB sticks, other partitions, /tmp, the phone) Fox cannot delete anything.

**Fix.** Match ErrorKind::CrossesDevices explicitly. Proper fix is the XDG per-volume trash ($topdir/.Trash-$uid/{files,info}) with a confirmed permanent delete as fallback when the mount is read-only or has no topdir. Minimum acceptable change is surfacing a message instead of silence. Changes delete semantics, needs a decision.

### fileops-16: Trash on PermissionDenied escalates to permanent `sudo rm -rf`, with no confirmation when the sudo ticket is cached

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:635` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C3): A privileged permanent delete can no longer run on a cached sudo ticket without a question. `PendingPrivOp::Remove` reports `asks_first()` (src/sudo.rs) and the new modal in src/priv_ops.rs starts it in a Confirm phase: the dialog (src/priv_dialog.rs) is titled 'Delete ... permanently as administrator?', lists the exact paths, says nothing goes to the Trash, and only the 'Delete Permanently' button starts sudo (Enter does nothing, Esc/Cancel/click outside cancels). Only after that is the cached ticket tried, then the password asked for. The Move-to-Trash permission path is now: Trash fails -> C1's question naming the items (delete_question in src/op_dialogs.rs now lists every name, not only a count) -> unprivileged delete on the worker -> for what still fails with permission denied, the root question above -> password if needed. Permission failures on phones/network mounts are reported instead of being handed to sudo, where `rm -rf` as root would 'succeed' on a path root cannot see (offer_root_delete, src/file_ops.rs). Empty Trash now also asks first, with count and names (empty_trash_question / confirm_empty_trash in src/op_dialogs.rs, src/trash_ops.rs), and 'Delete Permanently' on several trashed items names them. `rm -rf` gets `--` before the paths.
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-9

**What is wrong.** A recoverable action (Move to Trash) is converted into an irreversible one. priv_run first tries `sudo -n`, so within the ticket lifetime (or with NOPASSWD) the delete happens instantly with no dialog at all. PermissionDenied is not limited to root-owned items: renaming a directory the user owns but that lacks the write bit to another parent returns EACCES (verified), as do other users' files in sticky dirs.

**Trigger.** Press Delete on a read-only folder you own (Go module cache under ~/go/pkg/mod, a tree extracted with r-x permissions) shortly after any earlier Fox sudo authentication: the folder is removed permanently, bypassing the Trash, with no prompt.

```rust
if !permission_failures.is_empty() {
    self.priv_run(crate::sudo::PendingPrivOp::Remove(permission_failures));
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** trash_selected (617-636) collects PermissionDenied failures and calls priv_run(Remove). priv_run (1034-1047) first calls sudo::try_cached, i.e. `sudo -n -- rm -rf <paths>`; on CachedResult::Ok it just reloads. The modal's description line is the only confirmation and that modal is skipped. sudo.rs's own header says the ticket cache is relied on, and with no tty sudoers falls back to per-parent-pid tickets, so an earlier password entry in the same Fox instance (or a NOPASSWD rule) satisfies it. I verified the wider trigger: renaming a mode-0555 directory I own to another parent fails with EACCES (errno 13), so read-only trees in the home directory qualify, not just root-owned items. Trash, a recoverable action, becomes an irreversible recursive delete as root with no undo entry. I could not read /etc/sudoers to see the timeout on this machine.

**Fix.** Never take the cached path for a Remove that originated from Trash: always show an explicit `cannot be moved to Trash, delete permanently?` confirmation regardless of ticket state. The alternative (root `mv` into Trash followed by chown) keeps the action recoverable but is more work. Owner decision either way.

### fileops-11: Extract Here extracts into an existing same-named folder and overwrites its files

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:921` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): file_ops.rs extract_selected uses fresh_dir(): create_dir on 'stem', 'stem (2)', ... so an existing folder is never reused; unzip runs without -o and with stdin closed; an empty folder left by a failed extraction is removed.

**What is wrong.** The extraction folder is always `<archive stem>` with no uniqueness check. If that folder already exists the archive is unpacked on top of it: tar overwrites by default and unzip is explicitly called with `-o` (overwrite without prompting, line 969). Current files are replaced by the archived versions.

**Trigger.** project.zip sits next to the working folder project/ (the usual state after compressing a folder and continuing to work in it). Right-click project.zip -> Extract Here to look at the old version: files in project/ are silently overwritten with the archived ones.

```rust
let extract_dir = dir.join(&stem);
let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(&extract_dir);
```

**Skeptic's check.** extract_selected derives extract_dir = dir.join(stem) and calls create_dir_all (921-922) with no existence or uniqueness check; tar overwrites by default and unzip is passed `-o` explicitly (970). So an archive whose members sit at its root is unpacked over a same-named folder and silently replaces files there. Realistic case: extract photos.zip, edit files in photos/, extract the zip again. The finder's own example is weaker than stated: an archive made by Fox's Compress contains a top-level `project/` and lands in project/project/. compress_selected has a collision loop, extract has none.

**Fix.** Choose a free directory name first (`stem`, `stem (2)`, ...) and use create_dir so an existing folder is never reused; also drop `-o` from unzip. Small and local, but it changes overwrite behaviour (some users extract on top deliberately), so I am not marking it safe.

### fileops-13: Root mode is invisible and sticky: survives tab switch / Back / Forward, and "Open as Root" on a file just toggles it

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:1008` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C3): Same scoping and badge as app-3. 'Open as Root' is now offered only on folder rows (src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs build_item_menu), and never in a file chooser, the Trash, or on a phone/network mount. `open_as_root` (src/row_actions.rs) does nothing for a file: the toggle-on-file arm is gone and `root_folder` returns only a folder target. Root mode for the folder already shown is a separate, honestly named item in the empty-area menu: 'Root Mode Here' / 'Leave Root Mode' (CTX_ROOT_MODE, src/main.rs). `enter_root_mode` refuses (with a notice) on slow mounts, in the Trash and in pick mode.

**What is wrong.** root_mode is a single App flag that is cleared only in navigate_to when the path changes (app/nav.rs:88). switch_tab, new_tab, close_tab, go_back, go_forward, focus_pane and pick_tree_navigate never clear it, and nothing renders it (render.rs never reads root_mode; window_title is dead code). The 'Open as Root' menu entry is offered on files too, where it does not open anything - it silently flips the flag. While the flag is set, Delete/Move to Trash becomes `sudo rm -rf` (trash_selected line 566 -> delete_selected), paste becomes `sudo cp/mv` with no conflict dialog, rename skips the conflict check, etc. - in whatever folder the user is now looking at.

**Trigger.** Right-click a file -> Open as Root (nothing visibly happens). Or enable root mode in /etc, then switch to another tab / press Back into ~/Documents. Press Delete on a file: with a cached sudo ticket it is permanently removed instantly instead of going to Trash; otherwise a sudo modal appears for a plain user-owned file.

```rust
} else {
    // No folder selected — just toggle root mode for current dir
    self.root_mode = !self.root_mode;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Grep of every root_mode use: the only place it is cleared is navigate_to when the path changes (nav.rs:88). go_back, go_forward (nav.rs:254-278), switch_tab/new_tab/close_tab via sync_from_tab, focus_pane and pick_tree_navigate all change current_dir without clearing it. Nothing draws it: the only reader for display is window_title, which is #[allow(dead_code)]. build_item_menu pushes `Open as Root` unconditionally (context_menu.rs:48), and open_as_root on a non-folder falls into the else arm and just flips the flag (1006-1009). While set, trash_selected becomes delete_selected -> sudo rm -rf (566-568, 699-703), paste becomes sudo cp/mv with no conflict dialog (131-147) and rename skips the conflict check (edit.rs:43). The realistic path: do root work in /etc (which caches a sudo ticket), press Back, press Delete on an ordinary file: permanent delete, no prompt.

**Fix.** Clear root_mode wherever current_dir changes (go_back, go_forward, sync_from_tab, sync_from_right_tab, pick_tree_navigate); that part is mechanical and matches navigate_to. Offer `Open as Root` only for directories and remove the toggle-on-file arm. A visible ROOT indicator in the path bar or status bar is needed too and is a UI decision, so the whole fix is not marked safe.

### fs-2: Symlinks to directories are listed as files: cannot be entered, open a new window, and vanish from the file picker

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:99` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): FileEntry has a new is_symlink field; for a link, is_dir/size/modified now describe the target (new FileEntry::listed in src/links.rs, used by fs::read_directory and the search worker). A link to a folder is entered on click, sorts with folders, expands in Tree view, stays in a filtered file picker and can be a favourite. Its path stays the link's, and every destructive path was re-checked to act on the link via symlink_metadata (trash, delete worker, replace, copy_tree recreating links, undo). Search still recurses by the entry's own kind, so it cannot loop. A link is marked with an arrow emblem at the icon's bottom-left corner in grid, list and tree (new src/sections/emblem.rs, drawn on the overlay layer so thumbnails do not cover it) and the preview pane says 'Link to folder' / 'Link to <type>'. Slow-mount rule kept: src/links.rs resolves a link component by component on local disks only and never stats into a phone or share from the listing; such a link is remembered so fs::is_slow_path is true for it and everything behind it (listing, thumbnails, probes, git take their slow paths), its kind is asked on a 'fox-link-probe' thread, and the folder is re-listed when the answer lands (hook in src/housekeeping.rs). Folder xattrs are not read through such a link.

**What is wrong.** DirEntry::metadata() is an lstat and does not follow symlinks (confirmed with a rustc test: link_to_dir gives is_dir=false, len=length of the link text). So every symlink to a directory gets is_dir=false. It sorts among files, shows the link length as its size, on click goes down the file branch of on_item_click (app/select.rs:65) to open_selected, where the empty extension falls through to `xdg-open <path>`, which launches the inode/directory handler (a second file-manager window) instead of navigating. In pick mode apply_listing (app/nav.rs:195) keeps only `e.is_dir || matches_filter`, so symlinked folders are removed from the file chooser entirely whenever a filter is active, and tree view cannot expand them.

**Trigger.** Have any directory symlink, for example ~/Games -> /mnt/storage/Games. Open the parent folder in Fox: the entry sits in the files group with a tiny size; clicking it spawns xdg-open rather than entering it. Open a file dialog from another app (Fox --pick with a filter): the symlinked folder is not shown at all.

```rust
let metadata = entry.metadata().ok();
let is_dir = metadata.as_ref().map_or(false, |m| m.is_dir());
```

**Skeptic's check.** list_directory uses DirEntry::metadata() (99-102), which does not follow symlinks. Scratchpad test: a symlink to a directory reports is_dir=false, len=7. Nothing corrects it later: on_item_click (select.rs:23, 38) and open_selected (file_ops.rs:717) trust FileEntry.is_dir, so the link sorts among files, shows the link text length as its size, and a click goes to open_selected where the empty extension makes default_app_for_extension return None (desktop.rs:19-22) and `xdg-open <path>` is spawned instead of navigating. apply_listing's pick filter keeps only `e.is_dir || matches_filter` (nav.rs:195-196), so symlinked folders vanish from the file chooser when a filter is active, and build_tree_recursive never expands them. Directory symlinks in a home folder are common.

**Fix.** For entries whose file_type is a symlink, do one std::fs::metadata(&path) and take is_dir (and size/mtime) from the target; a broken link stays a file. Keep search_recursive's recursion decision on the lstat result so search cannot loop. Things to re-check after the change: I confirmed remove_dir_all on a symlink removes only the link, so delete is fine; copy already dereferences via src.is_dir(); copy_dir_recursive fails on a nested symlink-to-directory (see extra finding). On a slow mount the extra stat is one more round-trip per link, but that listing is already off-thread.

### fs-3: Mounted drives whose mount point contains a space disappear from the sidebar

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:369` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Mount points from /proc/mounts are unescaped before statvfs and comparisons.

**What is wrong.** /proc/mounts escapes spaces, tabs and backslashes in mount points as octal (`\040`). detect_mounted_drives passes the raw escaped field to statvfs, which fails with ENOENT, and the `?` silently drops the drive. The lsblk path does not rescue it because collect_unmounted returns early when `node.mountpoint.is_some()` (line 477). The file already has unescape_mount (line 1017) but only the slow-mount table uses it. is_path_mounted (line 692) and the mount_drive fallback (line 800) compare or return the raw escaped field too.

**Trigger.** Plug in a USB stick labelled with a space ("Samsung USB", "My Passport", "Ubuntu 24.04 LTS amd64"). It shows as 'Tap to mount'. Click it: udisks mounts it at /run/media/alva/Samsung USB and Fox navigates there, but on the refresh the drive is gone from the sidebar, so there is no usage bar and no way to Eject it from Fox.

```rust
let mount = parts[1];
...
.filter_map(|(device, (mount, fstype))| {
    let stat = statvfs(&mount)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** detect_mounted_drives takes parts[1] of /proc/mounts raw (345) and passes it to statvfs (369), which builds a CString unchanged (972). A mount point with a space appears as `\040`, statvfs fails with ENOENT and the `?` drops the drive. The lsblk path cannot rescue it: collect_unmounted returns as soon as node.mountpoint is Some (477-479). unescape_mount exists (1017) and is used only by slow_mount_roots. So a stick labelled `USB DISK`, `NO NAME` or `Samsung USB` disappears from the sidebar the moment it is mounted: no usage bar, no Eject. mount_drive's primary path parses udisksctl stdout and returns the real path, so navigation right after mounting works, which makes the disappearance look random. The /proc/mounts fallback in mount_drive (796-804) and is_path_mounted (692) have the same raw-field problem. Not run on hardware; this machine has no udisksctl.

**Fix.** Apply unescape_mount to the mount field in detect_mounted_drives (and derive the display name and PathBuf from the unescaped value), in is_path_mounted, and in the mount_drive fallback. Local and mechanical.

### media-2: Icon cache key does two getxattr syscalls per folder, three times per frame, on the render thread (stalls on MTP)

- **Where:** `src/icons.rs:220` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category freeze; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. xattr reads are skipped on slow mounts (covers the icon key and every other caller).

**What is wrong.** cache_key() is called from get_or_load(), has_icon() and get(), i.e. three times per visible entry per frame (render.rs:141/147/1099 and the split-pane equivalents). For a directory each call runs getxattr() twice, so every visible folder costs 6 path-resolving syscalls per frame on the render thread, with no caching and no slow-mount guard. On local disks that is wasted work (100 visible folders at 60 fps is 36,000 syscalls/s whenever anything animates). On a FUSE mount the path walk must revalidate the dentry once its entry timeout (1 s by default) has passed, which is a blocking FUSE_LOOKUP to the daemon; on jmtpfs that waits on the global device lock. The loop renders continuously in exactly the situations where that lock is held: icon_cache.has_pending() (slow-lane photo thumbnails downloading) and app.op_progress (copying from the phone) both set needs_anim. This is the same bug class the code already fixed for stat (see the comment at preview.rs:123-124 and wayland_loop.rs:1289-1291), but the xattr reads were left in.

**Trigger.** Mount the phone and open a folder that contains both sub-folders and photos (or start copying a large file off the phone while a phone folder with sub-folders is showing). While jmtpfs is downloading, the window freezes in multi-second chunks (for the length of each file download) because each frame's getxattr path walk blocks behind the transfer.

```rust
let icon = get_folder_icon(&entry.path).unwrap_or_default();
let color = get_folder_color(&entry.path).unwrap_or_default();
format!("dir:{}:{}:{}", entry.name.to_lowercase(), icon, color)
```

**Skeptic's check.** Certain from the code: cache_key runs two getxattr calls for every directory entry (220-221) and is called from get_or_load, has_icon and get (render.rs:141/147 and the List/Tree/split equivalents), so each visible folder costs six path-resolving syscalls per rendered frame on the main loop, with no memoisation and no is_slow_path guard (the guard at icons.rs:133 covers files only). On a cache miss load_folder_icon adds more. What I could not establish: that this blocks on an MTP mount. The mechanism is the same one the code already guards against for stat (wayland_loop.rs:1289-1291): resolving a path on a FUSE mount revalidates the dentry through the daemon once the entry timeout lapses, and jmtpfs serialises behind its device lock during a download, which is exactly when frames run continuously (has_pending, op_progress). No phone was available to confirm the stall; severity is what it would be if it does.

**Fix.** Minimal: skip the xattr reads when fs::is_slow_path(&entry.path) in cache_key, folder_icon_embedded and folder_icon_path (MTP has no user xattrs). Better follow-up: memoise (icon, color) per path inside IconCache, cleared in ensure_dir and invalidate, so cache_key does no syscalls at all. The minimal guard is local and cannot change what is drawn on local disks.

### sections-2: File-item zone ids run into the tree-item range at index 4000: clicks and right-clicks hit the wrong entry (or nothing)

- **Where:** `src/main.rs:74` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Item zone id bases moved a million apart (file 1M, tree 2M, P2 file 3M, P2 tree 4M) with closed ranges; tree rows are cleared when leaving Tree view.

**What is wrong.** Grid/List rows register ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE + index (render.rs:675, 750; the unfocused pane's ZONE_P2_FILE_BASE + i is translated to the same value in click.rs:554). For index >= 4000 the id is >= 5000, and both click.rs and context_menu.rs test the tree range first with no upper bound, so the press is decoded as tree row (index - 4000). app.tree_entries is never cleared when leaving Tree view (the view toggle cycles Grid -> List -> Tree -> Grid, so it is normally populated and stale). Result: left click arms pending_tree_open and on release expands or LAUNCHES tree_entries[index-4000]; right click builds the context menu for that other entry (select_item on it, so Move to Trash / Delete Permanently act on a file the user never clicked). If tree_entries is empty the item simply cannot be selected, opened or right-clicked. Same family of collision: ZONE_TAB_BASE 500 vs ZONE_TAB_CLOSE_BASE 550 (tab 50's zone closes tab 0) and ZONE_PROPS_ICON_BASE 2000 sits inside the file range.

**Trigger.** Open a folder with more than 4000 visible entries in Grid or List view (photo dump, node_modules, a big Trash) after having cycled through Tree view once. Scroll to entry #4010 and click it: Fox opens entry #10 instead. Right-click it and choose Move to Trash: entry #10 is trashed. Without a prior Tree visit: the click just clears the selection and the right-click shows the empty-area menu.

```rust
pub const ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE: u32 = 1000;
pub const ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE: u32 = 5000;
// click.rs:300   id if id >= ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE => { let idx = (id - ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE) as usize; ...
// context_menu.rs:338   Some(zone) if zone >= crate::ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE => { ... app.tree_entries.get(ti)
```

**Skeptic's check.** Grid and List register ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE + index for visible rows (render.rs:675, 750), so entry 4000 and above produce ids >= 5000 = ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE. click.rs:300 tests `id >= ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE` before the file arm with no upper bound and arms pending_tree_open; the release path (wayland_loop.rs:955-978) then expands or launches tree_entries[index-4000] without checking the view mode. context_menu.rs:338 does the same and its file arm is explicitly bounded `< ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE` (352), so entries past 4000 can never be right-clicked as themselves. tree_entries is not cleared when leaving Tree view: cycle_view_mode only rebuilds on entering Tree and apply_listing only rebuilds in Tree mode. With a stale tree a left click opens entry #(i-4000) and a right click selects that other entry, so Move to Trash acts on a file the user never clicked; with an empty tree the item cannot be selected at all. Folders with more than 4000 entries (camera dumps) are ordinary. The secondary collisions (tab 50 vs tab-close base, props icon base inside the file range) are real but far less reachable.

**Fix.** Give each family a disjoint range by construction (for example FILE 0x0100_0000, TREE 0x0200_0000, P2_FILE 0x0300_0000, P2_TREE 0x0400_0000) and match each as a bounded range in click.rs, context_menu.rs and translate_split_zone; move ZONE_PROPS_ICON_BASE out of the file range. Also clear tree_entries when leaving Tree view. Behaviour-preserving below 4000 entries, but it touches ordered match arms in three files, so it should be reviewed rather than applied blind.

### fileops-8: Copy failures are swallowed: truncated/partial destinations are left behind with no error shown

- **Where:** `src/ops.rs:196` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): ops.rs OpOutcome carries failures (path + reason) and perm_fails; file_ops.rs finish_op shows them in a notice dialog (new src/op_dialogs.rs, 'N items could not be copied' with up to 8 lines) and logs them to stderr. copy_tree creates every destination exclusively and removes whatever it created when an item fails or is cancelled; nothing that existed before is touched. Moves that fail (the old queue_cut Err(_) => {}) are reported the same way since all moves now run on the worker.

**What is wrong.** Every non-permission error from fs::copy / copy_dir_recursive is dropped. std::fs::copy does not remove a partially written destination, so ENOSPC, EFBIG (FAT32 4 GB limit), EIO, EINVAL for names illegal on the target filesystem etc. leave a truncated file or half-copied folder carrying the real name. The progress strip just disappears as if the copy succeeded; the partial item is not in `created`, so undo will not clean it up either. `cancelled` is also discarded (`_cancelled`). queue_cut has the same `Err(_) => {}` (file_ops.rs:87).

**Trigger.** Copy a 6 GB video onto a FAT32 USB stick, or a large folder onto a nearly full disk. The bar finishes, the file/folder is present in the destination but truncated/incomplete, and nothing says it failed. The user later deletes the original trusting the copy.

```rust
Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied => {
    perm_fails.push(src);
}
Err(_) => {}
```

**Skeptic's check.** ops.rs:193-196: only PermissionDenied is recorded, `Err(_) => {}` drops everything else. fs::copy leaves a partially written destination on ENOSPC/EFBIG/EIO, and copy_dir_recursive aborts at the first `?` leaving a half-copied tree. OpProgress::Done carries only created/perm_fails/cancelled, and poll_op_progress (file_ops.rs:333) binds `_cancelled` and has no error path, so the strip simply disappears. The partial target is not in `created`, so undo does not remove it. queue_cut has the same `Err(_) => {}` (file_ops.rs:87). The source is not deleted on error, so there is no direct loss, but a silently truncated copy that looks complete is a wrong result in a common flow (full disk, >4 GB onto FAT32).

**Fix.** Add a failures Vec<(PathBuf, String)> to Done and surface it (dialog/status) in poll_op_progress; same for queue_cut. Removing the partial target is right but must only remove what this op created: for a Replace-resolved conflict the old target was already deleted in advance_paste, and for a directory merge remove_dir_all would be wrong, so track per-item whether the target pre-existed. Changes what is left on disk after a failure, so not a mechanical fix.

### fileops-12: Cross-device move deletes a source FOLDER with no verification that the copy landed

- **Where:** `src/ops.rs:218` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): copy_tree/moved.rs remove_moved_source: after the copy has landed, a source file is deleted only when its copy exists with the same size, a link only when its copy is a link, and a directory only with remove_dir once empty (bottom-up). Anything unverified stays at the source and is reported. Each file copy is additionally checked at write time (close() result plus size on disk). Covered by a real cross-filesystem test (tmpfs to disk).

**What is wrong.** The function's own comment explains that fs::copy can return Ok on a FUSE destination whose commit-on-close error is swallowed, which is why files get a size check. For directories it returns true unconditionally and 'trusts the recursive copy's error propagation' - but copy_dir_recursive is built on the very same fs::copy calls, so it propagates nothing in that case. remove_dir_all(src) then runs. Folder moves are therefore strictly less protected than single-file moves.

**Trigger.** Cut a folder on disk and paste it onto the phone (jmtpfs) or another FUSE/network mount where an upload fails at close (storage full, unsupported name, cable pulled): every fs::copy reports Ok, the source folder is deleted, the destination is incomplete.

```rust
fn copy_landed(src: &std::path::Path, target: &std::path::Path) -> bool {
    if src.is_dir() {
        return true;
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** copy_landed returns true unconditionally for directories (218-220) and run_copy then calls remove_dir_all(src) (184-186). copy_dir_recursive is built on the same fs::copy that the function's own comment says can return Ok on a FUSE target whose commit-on-close error is swallowed, so the directory case has strictly less protection than the single-file case. That part is certain from the code. Unknown: whether jmtpfs (or sshfs/NFS) really reports Ok for a failed upload; I did not reproduce that and am relying on the comment at ops.rs:212-216. If it does, a folder move onto the phone deletes the source with an incomplete destination.

**Fix.** Before remove_dir_all, walk the source and require every regular file to exist in the target with equal length (metadata only); on any mismatch keep the source and report. Better: delete each source file right after its own size check and remove directories bottom-up only when empty, so a mid-tree failure never loses files. This changes when sources are deleted, so it needs review. On slow mounts the extra stat pass costs a round trip per file but runs on the worker thread.

### media-1: Quick Look stats the file on the UI thread and has no slow-mount guard: phone files freeze the app

- **Where:** `src/quick_look.rs:65` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): QuickLook::open (src/quick_look.rs) takes the FileEntry and uses its size; no metadata() call. On a slow mount nothing is loaded: the overlay shows a note and, if the view already has one, the listing thumbnail (cache lookup only, src/render.rs). Loaders are single-flight: one at a time, and a loader that is no longer the newest returns without reading.
- **Also reported as:** input-21

**What is wrong.** QuickLook::open() is called from handle_key on the render thread (key.rs:693 and :711). It does a blocking std::fs::metadata() there, and then unconditionally starts a loader thread that reads the file (ffmpeg for videos, image decode, or a 256 KB text read). Nothing checks fs::is_slow_path(). On a jmtpfs mount the first read downloads the WHOLE file under the device's global lock, so Space on a phone video starts a multi-GB download that cannot be cancelled (Esc only drops the QuickLook struct; the thread and ffmpeg child keep going). Every later path syscall on the phone waits on that lock, and the very next one comes from the UI thread: pressing Left/Right calls QuickLook::open() again, whose metadata() blocks until the download finishes. icons.rs and file_info.rs both refuse video/audio work on slow paths for exactly this reason; Quick Look was missed. Loader threads are also unbounded: every arrow press spawns a new thread (and ffmpeg) while the previous ones keep decoding.

**Trigger.** Mount the phone, open a folder with videos, select a 2 GB video, press Space (download starts in the background), then press Right arrow. The window stops responding until the whole video has been pulled over USB (minutes). A milder version: press Space on a phone photo while slow-lane thumbnails are still downloading; the UI hitches for the rest of the in-flight download.

```rust
std::thread::spawn(move || {
    let loaded = load(&bg_path);
    *slot.lock().unwrap() = Some(loaded);
});
Self {
    path: path.to_path_buf(),
    file_size: std::fs::metadata(path).map(|m| m.len()).unwrap_or(0),
```

**Skeptic's check.** QuickLook::open is called from handle_key at key.rs:693 and 711 on the main loop. It unconditionally spawns a loader that reads the file (image decode, ffmpeg for video, 256 KB text read) and does std::fs::metadata(path) synchronously; there is no is_slow_path check in quick_look.rs or the key handler. On jmtpfs the loader's first read downloads the whole file under the global lock (the project's own established behaviour), and the next Left/Right calls open() again whose metadata() queues behind it. Esc only drops the struct; the thread and ffmpeg child continue, and each arrow press adds another loader. Space-then-arrows through phone photos is the natural use, so this is a hang in a likely flow. The size is already in FileEntry.

**Fix.** Mechanical part: pass entry.size into open() and delete the metadata() call (both call sites have the FileEntry). Policy part: on is_slow_path allow only images and set an error string for video/other without spawning; that mirrors icons.rs:139-147 but is a behaviour choice. Also worth a generation counter or single-flight so stale loaders are not stacked.

### render-1: File-item zone IDs run into the tree-item range: clicks on entry #4000+ in Grid/List are handled as tree rows (nothing happens, or the wrong file is launched/dragged/cut)

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:675` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Item zone id bases moved a million apart (file 1M, tree 2M, P2 file 3M, P2 tree 4M) with closed ranges; tree rows are cleared when leaving Tree view.

**What is wrong.** Grid and List register one zone per entry as 1000 + index with no upper bound, but the tree family starts at 5000 and both decoders test `>= ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE` first (click.rs:300, context_menu.rs:338). Entry index 4000+k therefore decodes as tree row k. `app.tree_entries` is only rebuilt while the view mode is Tree and is never cleared when leaving it (tabs.rs:12-17, nav.rs:203), and the view toggle cycles Grid -> List -> Tree -> Grid, so anyone who has cycled views holds a stale tree of whatever directory was current at that time. With an empty tree the press is swallowed (no select, no open, a rubber band starts instead). With a stale tree the press arms `pending_tree_open = Some(k)` and release (wayland_loop.rs:955-976) launches `tree_entries[k]` with its default app on a SINGLE click (or toggles a folder expansion); moving >5px drags that stale path (wayland_loop.rs:410-427) so a drop moves/copies the wrong file; right-click builds the menu for `ClickedRow::NestedPath(stale path)` so Open / Open With / Cut / Copy / Copy Path act on a file the user never clicked. The same happens through the split-pane mapping (click.rs:553-554 maps P2 file i to 1000+i). Related overflow in the same scheme: ZONE_TAB_BASE=500 / ZONE_TAB_CLOSE_BASE=550 / ZONE_TAB_NEW=599 means the 51st tab's zone is tab 0's close button, and ZONE_PROPS_ICON_BASE=2000 overlaps file index 1000+.

**Trigger.** Open /usr/share/man/man3 (12021 entries on this machine) in Grid or List view, scroll past the 4000th item and click one: it highlights on hover but the click does not select it and double-click does not open it. If the view toggle was cycled through Tree earlier in the session (in any directory), the single click instead opens entry k of that old tree listing, dragging the item moves that other file, and the right-click menu targets it.

```rust
let zone_id = ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE + i as u32;   // render.rs:675 (grid) and :750 (list); ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE = 1000, ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE = 5000 (main.rs:74-75)
// click.rs:300  id if id >= ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE => { let idx = (id - ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE) as usize; if idx < app.tree_entries.len() { ... app.pending_tree_open = Some(idx);
// click.rs:401  id if id >= ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE => { ... }   (never reached for id >= 5000)
```

**Skeptic's check.** Grid (675) and List (750) register 1000 + index with no upper bound; ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE is 5000. click.rs:300 matches `id >= ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE` before the file arm at 401, so entry 4000+k is handled as tree row k. tree_entries is only rebuilt while in Tree mode (tabs.rs:14, nav.rs:202) and never cleared on leaving it, and the toggle cycles Grid -> List -> Tree -> Grid, so anyone who has cycled views holds a stale tree. With idx < tree_entries.len() the press arms pending_tree_open, and the release handler (wayland_loop.rs:955-977) launches tree_entries[k] on a single click or toggles a folder; a >5 px move drags that stale path (410-427). context_menu.rs:338 likewise catches 5000+ before the bounded file arm at 352, so Cut/Trash/Open act on a different file. With an empty tree the click is swallowed and a rubber band starts. translate_split_zone maps P2 file i to 1000+i, so the unfocused pane has the same hole. Needs a folder with more than 4000 entries (photo dumps, man3, node_modules), but then the wrong file is opened or trashed.

**Fix.** Give each family a disjoint bounded range and decode with explicit range arms in click.rs, context_menu.rs and translate_split_zone; also clear tree_entries when leaving Tree mode. Check every other consumer of these ids (drag_drop.rs, wayland_actions/mod.rs:159, split.rs) and the secondary overlaps: ZONE_PROPS_ICON_BASE 2000 and the tab ranges 500/550/599. Several files, so review needed.

### fileops-5: Undo of Rename / Trash / Move silently overwrites a newer file at the original path

- **Where:** `src/undo.rs:93` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C2): Undo and redo of Rename, Move and Trash go through a no-replace rename (copy_tree::rename_noreplace / trash::restore) in src/undo/steps.rs (shift, untrash, retrash). When a newer item has the original name the step is refused with a message and the item stays where it is (renamed name, or in the Trash); nothing is overwritten. Redo of Trash no longer reuses the old slot: it trashes afresh under a new record (trash::retrash removed from src/trash/mod.rs). Both directions also check the Stamp, so a stranger that took the name is neither moved nor trashed. The stack (src/undo.rs UndoStack::finish) moves only the items that were really reversed to the other stack; failed items are dropped and reported. Tests: undo_of_rename_trash_and_move_never_overwrites_a_newer_item, redo_follows_the_same_rules.

**What is wrong.** All three reversals use fs::rename straight onto the original path (line 82 rename, line 93 trash, line 128 move). rename(2) atomically replaces an existing file (verified with a standalone test), so anything created at the original path after the operation is destroyed without warning. restore_selected uses pick_restore_dest to avoid exactly this, undo does not. Redo of Trash (line 180) likewise overwrites whatever now occupies the recorded trash slot and its .trashinfo.

**Trigger.** Trash notes.txt with Delete. An app recreates/saves a new notes.txt (editor still open, config regenerated). Ctrl+Z in Fox: the old trashed copy replaces the new file. Same with rename a.txt -> b.txt, new a.txt appears, Ctrl+Z.

```rust
let ok = std::fs::rename(trash_file, original).is_ok();
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read execute_reverse and execute_forward in full: lines 82, 93 and 128 call fs::rename straight onto the original path with no existence check, and rename(2) replaces an existing file. Ctrl+Z reaches this from key.rs:722-728 with the result discarded. Trash a file, let anything recreate that name, press Ctrl+Z: the newer file is replaced. restore_selected() uses pick_restore_dest to avoid exactly this; undo does not. Redo of Trash (line 180) overwrites whatever now sits in the recorded trash slot the same way.

**Fix.** Check the destination with symlink_metadata before each reverse rename and skip (and report) that entry if it exists, or restore under a unique name via pick_restore_dest. Either choice changes overwrite behaviour, so Alva should pick. An atomic version needs renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE) through libc. The description string should be surfaced, and undo() should not push to redo when the reversal failed.

### fileops-6: Redo of a paste pairs the wrong sources with the wrong targets after any skip or failure

- **Where:** `src/undo.rs:231` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Undo/redo of a copy stores (source, target) pairs for the items that landed; redo will not overwrite an existing destination.

**What is wrong.** poll_op_progress records `Copy { sources: handle.originals.clone(), created }` (file_ops.rs:337-340): `sources` is every original source, `created` is only the targets that were actually copied. If any item was skipped in the conflict dialog, failed, hit PermissionDenied or the copy was cancelled, the two lists are no longer index-aligned, and redo copies source[i] onto created[i] - one file's content under another file's name, with fs::copy overwriting whatever is there.

**Trigger.** Copy a.txt, b.txt, c.txt into a folder that already has a.txt; choose Skip for a.txt. Ctrl+Z (removes b, c). Ctrl+Shift+Z: dest/b.txt now contains a.txt's content, dest/c.txt contains b.txt's content, c.txt's content is not copied.

```rust
for (src, dst) in sources.iter().zip(created.iter()) {
```

**Skeptic's check.** conflict.rs PendingPaste::new sets originals = every source; ops.rs run_copy pushes to `created` only on Ok; file_ops.rs:337 stores Copy { sources: handle.originals, created }. After a Skip, a failed item, a PermissionDenied or a cancel the lists are different lengths, and redo zips them by index, so source[i] is copied onto created[i] with fs::copy overwriting. Skip a.txt out of a,b,c, undo, redo: dest/b.txt gets a.txt's content and dest/c.txt gets b.txt's. cancel_paste (file_ops.rs:487) has the same shape. duplicate_selected is 1:1 and fine.

**Fix.** Store aligned pairs. Smallest change: keep the resolved (src, target) pairs on OpHandle and, when Done arrives, keep only pairs whose target is in `created`; store UndoAction::Copy as Vec<(PathBuf, PathBuf)> and iterate it in both directions. Touches ops.rs, file_ops.rs and undo.rs but is mechanical. Redo should also refuse to overwrite an existing destination.

### cloud-net-5: Every Fox process, including file-picker and desktop instances, starts its own sync engine: full list per launch, racing reconcilers, killed mid-pass at exit

- **Where:** `src/wayland.rs:441` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category race; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1, C9): Inside the cloud lane: one engine per machine (owner.rs flock), no full list per launch (see cloud-core-7), no racing reconcilers, state files crash-consistent when the owner is killed mid-pass, SyncHandle::stop()/shutdown(wait)/Drop stop the thread between two files. src/wayland.rs calls app.start_cloud(pick.is_none()): a picker (--pick, --pick-save, --pick-directory, --pick-any) loads CloudState so its Cloud entry still opens ~/Cloud, but never calls SyncHandle::spawn and never polls. Normal windows and the --desktop daemon spawn. CloudUi::new() defaults to no engine, so unit tests that build an App never start one either.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-5, wayland-9

**What is wrong.** init_cloud() runs unconditionally, before the pick/desktop branches. lntrn-portal's file chooser, lntrn-notepad open/save, and command-center notes/bluetooth all spawn `lntrn-file-manager --pick...`, and every extra window is another process. Each one spawns a 'fox-cloud-sync' thread that (a) immediately does a FULL list_all (currently 4370 docs = 4370 reads; about 12 launches exhaust the free daily quota; the log shows many separate 'GET ... 429 -> paused 600s' lines minutes apart, each a fresh process), (b) polls every 30 s on its own, and (c) runs reconcile concurrently with every other Fox process against the same ~/Cloud, the same manifest.json and remote-index.json, with no lock. Consequences: manifest lost updates (each process loads, mutates and rewrites the whole file; the last saver wins), which is the likely source of the 168 stale manifest entries; duplicate work (log shows 47 '× remote' pairs for the same path 0-2 s apart); both processes writing the same `<name>.fox-tmp`; and in resolve_conflict a slower process can rename the freshly downloaded remote version over the conflict copy the faster process just made, destroying the user's local edits. The picker then exits (return or process::exit(1)) seconds later, killing its thread wherever it is.

**Trigger.** Open any 'Open file' / 'Save as' dialog from a browser or Notepad while signed in: that picker process does a 4370-read full list and a full reconcile, then dies mid-pass. Open two Fox windows and delete or edit files in ~/Cloud: both sync threads act on the same change simultaneously.

```rust
app.init_cloud();
if let Some(ref p) = pick {
```

**Skeptic's check.** app.init_cloud() runs before the pick/desktop branch. nav.rs init_cloud only guards on cloud_sync.is_some() within the process. cloud/sync.rs run_loop starts with sync_pass(.., full = true) -> firestore::list_all, then polls every 30 s with a recursive watcher on ~/Cloud. No flock or lock file anywhere in src/cloud; manifest.rs:54 and remote_index.rs:54 save with plain fs::write. lntrn-portal filechooser.rs:228, lntrn-notepad actions.rs:112/150/173 and command-center notes export and bluetooth all spawn `lntrn-file-manager --pick*`. fox-cloud.log has 3714 lines mentioning 429. Not traced by me: the exact reconcile outcome when two processes resolve the same conflict (the claimed clobbering of a conflict copy), and the per-day counts quoted from the log.

**Fix.** Skipping the sync thread in pick mode is the obvious first step, but a bare `if pick.is_none()` breaks the picker's Cloud entry: open_cloud_or_login checks self.cloud and would show the login dialog. Load CloudState without spawning SyncHandle in pick/desktop mode. Single-owner sync across windows (flock on a file under ~/.lantern/cloud taken in run_loop) and skipping the startup full list when the persisted index is fresh both change sync semantics and need Alva's sign-off.

### input-6: File zone ids run into the tree zone range at 4000 entries: clicks and right-clicks past the 4000th item hit the wrong target

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/click.rs:300` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Item zone id bases moved a million apart (file 1M, tree 2M, P2 file 3M, P2 tree 4M) with closed ranges; tree rows are cleared when leaving Tree view.
- **Also reported as:** app-11

**What is wrong.** render.rs registers ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE + i (1000 + i) for every visible grid/list row and for search results, with no cap. ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE is 5000, and both click.rs:300 and context_menu.rs:338 test the tree range first. So entry index 4000+k arrives as tree row k. app.tree_entries is not cleared when leaving Tree view (cycle_view_mode passes through Tree on every List to Grid cycle), so it is usually a stale listing. Left click: launches or expands tree_entries[k] instead of the clicked file, or does nothing if the tree is empty. Right click: resolves tree_entries[k] to ClickedRow::Item(k) (when the stale tree is this same folder) and selects entry k, far off-screen, so Move to Trash, Rename, Delete Permanently act on entry k instead of entry 4000+k. With an empty tree the right-click gives the empty-area menu and the item cannot be opened or selected at all.

**Trigger.** Open a folder with more than 4000 visible entries (phone DCIM, /usr/bin, a sample library), after having cycled the view button through Tree once. Scroll past item 4000 and right-click a file, Move to Trash: a different file near the top of the folder is trashed. Left-click instead opens an unrelated file. Search with more than 4000 results behaves the same.

```rust
id if id >= ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE => {            // 5000
    let idx = (id - ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE) as usize;
    if idx < app.tree_entries.len() {
...
id if id >= ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE => {            // 1000, checked second
```

**Skeptic's check.** main.rs: ZONE_FILE_ITEM_BASE = 1000, ZONE_TREE_ITEM_BASE = 5000. render.rs:675 and :750 register 1000 + i with no cap; click.rs:300 and context_menu.rs:338 test the tree range first. app.tree_entries is only rebuilt when entering or reloading in Tree view and is never cleared on leaving it, and the view button cycles Grid -> List -> Tree -> Grid. For entry 4000+k: left click arms pending_tree_open and the release handler (wayland_loop.rs:955-978) launches or expands tree_entries[k]; right click resolves tree_entries[k] to entries index k, selects it and builds its menu, so Move to Trash / Rename act on entry k. With an empty tree the click is swallowed. translate_split_zone maps P2 file ids into the same range. No search result cap exists, so search lists overflow the same way.

**Fix.** Move the bases far apart (file 1_000_000, tree 2_000_000, P2 file 3_000_000, P2 tree 4_000_000) and make the arms closed ranges; update translate_split_zone and the range tests in context_menu.rs:228-234 and :313-360. Constants-only change with no intended behaviour difference. ZONE_PROPS_ICON_BASE = 2000 then no longer sits inside the file range. Also clear tree_entries when leaving Tree view.

### input-8: File picker: double-click sets the result but never closes the dialog, and closing the window later returns that stale selection

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/click.rs:393` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. A confirmed pick ends the dialog (the loop exits on a Selected result); confirm_pick resets the result first, and double-click only confirms a file in Open/Mixed pickers.
- **Also reported as:** sections-4

**What is wrong.** confirm_pick() only stores app.pick_result. Nothing in the loop looks at pick_result; the picker exits only through ClickAction::Close (the Open/Cancel buttons), Enter/Esc, or a window close. So the double-click paths at click.rs:393 (Tree, which pick mode forces) and select.rs:71 (Grid/List) do nothing visible. The stored result then survives: closing the picker with the title-bar X, the context-menu close button (CTX_CLOSE_WINDOW) or the compositor (xdg_toplevel Close) sets running = false without cancel_pick(), and wayland.rs:541 prints the stale path with exit code 0. confirm_pick in Open/Mixed mode also leaves an old result in place when the current selection is empty, so pressing Open with nothing selected returns the earlier double-clicked file.

**Trigger.** In any app's Open dialog (lntrn-file-manager --pick), double-click a file: nothing happens. Close the dialog with Super+Q or the X: the calling app receives that file as if it had been chosen. In Save mode a double-click stores current_dir/<name box>, so closing the window makes the caller write that file.

```rust
if is_double {
    app.confirm_pick();
}
// app/select.rs:66-71
if is_double && is_pick { ... self.confirm_pick(); }
// wayland.rs:541
match app.pick_result.take() { Some(PickResult::Selected(paths)) => { for p in &paths { println!(...) } return Ok(()); }
```

**Skeptic's check.** grep for pick_result: written only in app/select.rs, read only at wayland.rs:541. click.rs:392-394 (Tree, which pick mode forces) and select.rs:71 (Grid/List) call confirm_pick() and fall through with ClickAction::None, so a double-click in any picker does nothing visible. The latched result survives: ZONE_CLOSE -> ClickAction::Close, CTX_CLOSE_WINDOW (context_menu.rs:535) and xdg_toplevel Close (wayland_dispatch.rs:207-209) set running = false without cancel_pick(), and wayland.rs:541 then prints the paths and exits 0. confirm_pick in Open/Mixed mode also leaves an earlier result in place when the current selection is empty.

**Fix.** Stop the loop when a pick produced Selected: return ClickAction::Close from click.rs:393 when pick_result is Some, and check app.pick_result after on_item_click in the release handler. Reset pick_result to None at the start of confirm_pick, and treat every non-confirm exit as cancelled. One caveat: in Directory mode a double-click on a FILE currently makes confirm_pick return current_dir; do not close on that (only confirm when the double-clicked item is eligible for the mode).

### input-9: Right-click path override survives a dismissed menu, so later Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, Open With and drags act on the old tree row

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:403` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The path override is cleared whenever the context menu is no longer open.

**What is wrong.** Right-clicking a nested tree row stores its path in context_override_paths, which makes selected_paths() ignore the real selection. It is cleared only when a menu action fires (835), on the next content right-click outside search mode (302) and in focus_pane. Dismissing the menu with Esc (key.rs:384-390), a click outside (wayland_loop.rs:671-677) or a compositor popup_done leaves it set, and it survives navigation and view-mode changes. From then on copy_selected(), cut_selected(), Open With and the drag-source computation (wayland_loop.rs:393-400 and 921-927, which take `selected_paths()` whenever the grabbed item is selected) use the stale path.

**Trigger.** Tree view, expand a folder, right-click a file inside it, press Esc.
