Now select other files (or switch to Grid, go to another folder, select something) and press Ctrl+X, then Ctrl+V elsewhere: the old nested file is moved, the selected files are untouched. In Grid/List, dragging any item onto a folder and choosing Move also moves the old nested file (the modal shows its name).

```rust
app.clear_selection();
app.context_override_paths = vec![path.clone()];
...
// only cleared here (line 835), inside MenuEvent::Action:
app.context_override_paths.clear();
// app/select.rs:230
if !self.context_override_paths.is_empty() { return self.context_override_paths.clone(); }
```

**Skeptic's check.** grep context_override_paths: set at context_menu.rs:403, cleared only at :302 (next content right-click, and not reached when that click lands on the sidebar or in search mode, which return earlier), :835 (after a menu action) and split.rs:193. Esc (key.rs:384-390), click-outside (wayland_loop.rs:671-677) and compositor popup_done (:1032-1038) close the menu without clearing it. selected_paths() (select.rs:230-233) then returns the stale path indefinitely, so Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X use it, and both drag sites (wayland_loop.rs:393-400 and :921-927) take selected_paths() whenever the grabbed item is selected, which the press itself guarantees in Grid/List. Every later drag or cut carries the old nested file instead of what is selected.

**Fix.** Simplest: at the top of each loop iteration, before input handling, `if !context_menu.is_open() { app.context_override_paths.clear(); }`. The action path still sees the override because handle_ctx_event runs in the same iteration as the Action event. Longer term, stop overriding selected_paths() and pass the target path to the menu actions explicitly.

### input-5: Dropping a file on its own folder's tab, sidebar place or favorite is not treated as a no-op and leads to the self-Replace deletion

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/drag_drop.rs:40` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. valid_dest treats a drop onto the items' own folder as a no-op for tab, sidebar and favorite targets too.

**What is wrong.** Only the other-split-pane branch (lines 148-151) checks `same_dir`. The tab branch (38-50), favorite branch (62-75) and sidebar-place branch (77-91) accept a destination equal to the sources' parent. valid_dest only rejects dest == source or dest inside a source. The result is a pointless 'Move or Copy?' modal followed by the conflict dialog, where Replace deletes the dragged file (see the paste finding).

**Trigger.** Viewing ~/Downloads, drag a file onto the Downloads entry in the sidebar, or onto the current tab. Choose Move (or Copy), then Replace in the conflict dialog: the file is permanently deleted.

```rust
if tab_idx < app.tabs.len() {
    let dest_dir = app.tabs[tab_idx].path.clone();
    if valid_dest(&dest_dir, &sources) {
        app.pending_drop = Some(PendingDrop { sources, dest_dir, reload_tab: Some(tab_idx) });
```

**Skeptic's check.** valid_dest only rejects a destination that is or sits inside a source. The tab (:38-50), favorite (:62-75) and sidebar place (:77-91) branches accept a destination equal to the sources' own parent; only the other-pane branch has the same_dir test (:148-151). Traced the rest: Move or Copy in the drop modal -> start_drag_drop -> advance_paste computes target = dest.join(name), which IS the source, target.exists() opens the conflict dialog, and Replace (also the Enter default, key.rs:172) runs remove_file / remove_dir_all on it in apply_single_conflict_action (file_ops.rs:456-462). The following rename or copy then fails silently. The dragged file or folder is permanently gone.

**Fix.** Apply the same `sources.iter().all(|s| s.parent() == Some(dest_dir))` no-op test in the three zone branches (fold it into one helper). That removes this entry point only. The root cause is in advance_paste / apply_single_conflict_action, where a source whose resolved target is itself must never be offered Replace; see the extra finding, which also covers plain copy-paste into the same folder.

### app-4: Inline rename (and Save-name field) moves a byte cursor one byte at a time: Backspace/Delete/typing next to a non-ASCII character panics

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:559` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category panic; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Rename and save-name editing step on char boundaries; the text widget gets char offsets.
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-6, input-7, sections-3

**What is wrong.** edit.rs defines rename_cursor / rename_selection as BYTE offsets (start_rename uses rfind('.') and len()). The key handler moves the cursor by exactly 1 byte for Left/Right/Backspace and calls String::remove / String::insert at that offset. rename_buf is seeded from the real file name, which can contain multi-byte UTF-8, so the offset lands inside a character and String::remove/insert panic ("byte index N is not a char boundary" / is_char_boundary assertion). There is no panic hook or catch_unwind, so Fox dies, killing any in-flight copy/move worker. The identical pattern exists for save_name_buf (key.rs:626-628, 636, 665), which is seeded from --save-name. Related cosmetic defect: lntrn-ui TextInput treats cursor_pos/selection as CHAR offsets, so the caret and highlight are drawn in the wrong place for such names.

**Trigger.** Have a folder named `Beyoncé` (or a file `Sigur Rós.mp3`). Press F2, press End (or Right) to drop the selection, press Backspace -> crash. Any Backspace that reaches a non-ASCII character, or Left/Right followed by typing, does the same. In a file picker launched with --save-name "Résumé" click the name field and backspace -> the picker crashes.

```rust
KEY_BACKSPACE => {
    if !app.rename_delete_selection() && app.rename_cursor > 0 {
        app.rename_cursor -= 1;
        app.rename_buf.remove(app.rename_cursor);
    }
...
app.rename_buf.insert(app.rename_cursor, ch);
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced key.rs:558-600 and app/edit.rs. start_rename seeds rename_buf from the real file name and uses byte offsets (rfind('.'), len()). Backspace does `rename_cursor -= 1; rename_buf.remove(rename_cursor)`, Left/Right step one byte, typing does insert(rename_cursor, ch). rename_delete_selection clamps only to len. Confirmed with a rustc experiment in the scratchpad: for "Café.txt" (F2 -> cursor at byte 5, Right, Backspace -> remove(4)) and for "Beyoncé" (End, Backspace -> remove(7)) String::remove panics. No catch_unwind / set_hook / panic=abort override anywhere, so the UI thread panic exits Fox and kills any running copy worker. Same code for save_name_buf at 625-668, seeded from --save-name (wayland.rs:454) with cursor = len on click (click.rs:517), so a suggested name ending in a multi-byte char crashes the picker on the first Backspace. lntrn-ui TextInput documents cursor_pos/selection as char offsets (text_input.rs:150) while render.rs:717/794/892 and pick_bar.rs:92 pass byte offsets, so the caret is also misplaced.

**Fix.** Keep byte offsets but step on char boundaries: Backspace/Left move to buf[..cursor].char_indices().next_back() start, Delete/Right advance by buf[cursor..].chars().next().len_utf8(); apply to both rename and save-name branches. Convert to char counts (buf[..off].chars().count()) when calling TextInput::cursor_pos/selection. No behaviour change for ASCII names. Selection endpoints from start_rename (rfind('.'), len) are already on boundaries.

### wayland-1: Closing the window while a background copy/move is running kills the worker mid-file and leaves a truncated destination

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:805` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): wayland_loop.rs: the main loop no longer exits on running=false while an operation runs; App::intercept_close raises a dialog 'Keep Open / Stop and Close'. Stop cancels (the item in flight is rolled back, staging removed), shows 'Stopping…' and the loop exits when the worker is idle. wayland.rs calls app.before_exit() after the loop in every case (cancel + join the worker). Because copies are staged, a real name never holds a truncated file even if the process dies.

**What is wrong.** Every close path just clears `running`. run_loop returns, wayland::run saves settings and returns, main returns, and the process exits. The copy worker is a plain thread in this process (ops.rs `fox-copy`), writing straight to the final destination name with `std::fs::copy(&src, &target)`. Nothing checks `app.op_progress`, joins the worker, or asks the user. The file being written at that moment is left truncated under its real name, the remaining items are never copied, and nothing is reported. ops.rs's own comment says it avoids mid-file cancel because 'we don't want partially-written destinations sitting around'; quitting produces exactly that.

**Trigger.** Paste or drag-copy a large file/folder (progress strip visible), then close Fox with the X button, Super+Q, or the context-menu Close. The window disappears; the destination holds a short file with the correct name and the rest of the batch is missing. For a cross-device move the source survives (delete only happens after copy_landed), but the partial target remains.

```rust
ClickAction::Close => {
    state.running = false;
}
// wayland_dispatch.rs:207  xdg_toplevel::Event::Close => { ... state.running = false; }
// context_menu.rs:535      CTX_CLOSE_WINDOW => *running = false,
```

**Skeptic's check.** All close paths only clear running: ClickAction::Close (805), xdg_toplevel Close (wayland_dispatch.rs:207-210, which is what Super+Q sends), CTX_CLOSE_WINDOW (context_menu.rs:535). run_loop returns, wayland::run saves settings and returns (wayland.rs:537-595), main returns and the process exits. The copy/move worker is a detached thread (ops.rs:128-133, no JoinHandle kept) writing straight to the final name with std::fs::copy / copy_dir_recursive (ops.rs:177-181). Nothing checks app.op_progress or waits. The file in flight is left truncated under its real name and the rest of the batch is silently skipped. For a cross-device move the source survives because the delete happens only after copy_landed, but the partial target remains.

**Fix.** Options: refuse/confirm Close while app.op_progress is Some; or after run_loop returns call request_cancel() and block on the handle's rx until Done (cancel is per item, so the current file completes; a multi-GB file means the process lingers with no window); or copy to a temp name and rename on success so an interrupted copy never looks complete. All change exit or copy semantics, so this needs Alva's call. Cloud sync threads deserve the same look.

## Medium (100)

### app-17: Every reload spawns a fresh listing thread for a slow unfocused pane without checking one is already in flight: thread pile-up and MTP hammering during a copy

- **Where:** `src/app/dir_load.rs:43` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Flights in src/app/dir_load.rs: a directory is never listed twice at once. A refresh asked for while its listing runs is remembered and run once afterwards, after a 2-second rest. A view with nothing to show skips the rest. A 'show hidden' switch during a load is re-requested when it lands.
- **Also reported as:** fs-8

**What is wrong.** The module comment says each target keeps at most one load in flight, but retain() only drops the older Receiver; the older thread keeps running its full readdir+stat pass. reload() calls reload_inactive_pane() unconditionally, which calls spawn_dir_load(Inactive) whenever the unfocused pane is on a slow mount. With the local pane focused, the inotify watcher fires a debounced reload roughly every 120-140 ms while files are being written into it, so a copy from the phone pane into the focused local pane spawns ~7 listing threads per second, each blocking on jmtpfs's global lock behind the very download that is causing the events, then each performing a full redundant MTP listing between files. Minutes of copying means thousands of threads and a much slower transfer.

**Trigger.** Split view: phone folder in one pane, local folder in the other. Ctrl+C files in the phone pane, click the local pane, Ctrl+V. While the copy runs, `ps -L` shows fox-dir-load threads accumulating and the transfer stalls between files.

```rust
// Supersede any older load for the same target; its result would
// only be overwritten by this one anyway.
self.dir_loads.retain(|l| l.target != target);
let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
... std::thread::Builder::new().name("fox-dir-load".into()).spawn(move || {
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read spawn_dir_load, reload() and reload_inactive_pane(). retain() at dir_load.rs:43 only drops the old Receiver; the old thread still runs its whole list_directory and its send just fails. reload() calls reload_inactive_pane() on both branches (nav.rs:149/159), and that spawns a new Inactive load whenever the unfocused pane is on a slow mount (split.rs:294-297) with no in-flight check. The watcher only stands down when the FOCUSED dir is slow (wayland_loop.rs:1292-1298), and op_progress keeps needs_anim true, so a copy into the focused local pane reloads about every 120-140 ms. Caveat on scale: std::fs::copy opens the phone file first (the download, no local events) and only then writes locally, so the spawns cluster in the write phase when the device lock is mostly free. 'Thousands of queued threads' is likely overstated; a storm of redundant MTP listings per copied file, of which only the last is used, is real. A second effect the finder missed: while reloads arrive faster than a listing completes, every result is superseded and the inactive pane never updates.

**Fix.** In spawn_dir_load, if a load with the same target and dir is already pending, do not spawn; mark it 'rerun once on landing' so a change that happened after the in-flight listing started is not lost. Without the rerun flag the fix would trade the storm for a stale pane. This changes loader threading semantics, so not mechanical. Compare sort and show_hidden too, or a sort change during a load is dropped.

### fs-9: Slow-mount listing is discarded if the user switches pane or tab while it loads; that pane then stays empty

- **Where:** `src/app/dir_load.rs:87` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): A slow-mount load belongs to its directory, not to 'the focused pane' (src/app/dir_load.rs). When it lands it is handed to every tab, pane and tree that shows that directory then, each with its own sort. Switching tab or pane, closing or reordering tabs cannot lose or misdeliver it.

**What is wrong.** A load is tagged 'Focused', not with the pane or tab it belongs to. focus_pane() and switch_tab() change current_dir without reloading (they only sync_to/sync_from the parked tab), so when the result arrives its directory no longer matches and it is dropped. The pane that requested it keeps its cleared entries. Nothing retries: switch_tab back just copies the empty tab.entries, and because no load is in flight the pane shows a plain empty folder ("0 folders, 0 files"), not "Loading…". The mirror case exists for Inactive (focus swap) and Tab(idx) (index shifts after close_tab or pinned-tab reorder).

**Trigger.** Click a phone folder (listing takes a few seconds). Before it appears, click the other split pane, or switch to another tab and back. The phone folder shows as empty until you navigate away and back.

```rust
DirLoadTarget::Focused => {
    if load.dir != self.current_dir {
        return false;
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced: reload() on a slow dir clears entries and the tab's entries when stale (nav.rs:138-141) and spawns DirLoadTarget::Focused. focus_pane (split.rs:169-209) and switch_tab (tabs.rs:68-76) change current_dir through sync_from_* without reloading or retargeting. poll_dir_loads removes the DirLoad before apply_dir_load, which returns false when load.dir != current_dir, so the result is gone. Coming back copies the empty tab.entries; no load is pending so dir_loading() is false and the pane shows a plain empty folder. Nothing retries: the watcher and mtime poll both stand down on slow dirs. Partial healing exists only in split view, where the next reload() of the other pane re-lists the slow inactive pane; the tab case never heals until the user re-navigates. Same symptom as the second half of app-6 but a different root cause (load identified by focus instead of by owner).

**Fix.** On a Focused mismatch, install the entries into whichever tab or split right_tab has path == load.dir (via keep_selection) instead of dropping. Better long term: give DirectoryTab a stable id and store it in DirLoad, which also fixes Tab(idx) going stale after close_tab or pinned reorder. If two tabs show the same slow dir, fill both. Changes load routing, so needs review.

### dialogs-19: Root-mode rename skips the conflict check and pkexec mv overwrites an existing target

- **Where:** `src/app/edit.rs:43` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C3): commit_rename (src/app/edit.rs) raises the shared conflict dialog in root mode too (the `!self.root_mode` exception is gone; the test is now lstat-based so a dangling link counts as taken). Replace in root mode (rename_replacing, src/file_ops.rs) claims a Trash slot for the existing item and runs C2's move script through sudo: old item to the Trash, rename, old item back if the rename fails. Keep Both and the plain case run `mv -T` behind a refuse-if-exists check, so an existing file is never replaced and the item is never moved inside an existing folder (test: privileged_rename_never_lands_on_another_item). An unprivileged rename that fails now shows a notice instead of only logging.

**What is wrong.** In root mode an existing target does not raise the dialog; perform_rename spawns `pkexec mv -- from to`. mv replaces an existing file without asking (or moves the item inside it if the target is a directory). The rename is also pushed on the undo stack regardless of outcome.

**Trigger.** Open as Root in /etc/foo, rename a.conf to b.conf where b.conf exists: b.conf is replaced with no prompt.

```rust
if new_path.exists() && !self.root_mode {
```

**Skeptic's check.** 'new_path.exists() && !self.root_mode' skips the dialog in root mode, and perform_rename spawns 'pkexec mv -- from to' (edit.rs:74-83). mv without -n/-i replaces an existing file, or moves the item inside the target if it is a directory. The undo entry is pushed regardless of the detached thread's result. Reachable only in root mode (Open as Root) and behind the polkit prompt, so an uncommon flow; note app-3 makes root mode stickier and invisible, which widens it.

**Fix.** Raise the same dialog in root mode. Simply deleting '&& !self.root_mode' is not enough: Replace would run a non-privileged remove (fails or partially deletes a root-owned dir) and then mv would move INTO a surviving directory. Use 'mv -T' (or 'mv -n -T' for the non-replace path) and do the removal through the privileged runner. Touches privileged semantics, so needs review.

### wayland-10: Exiting any Fox process unmounts every connected phone, including when a picker has just returned a path on that phone

- **Where:** `src/app/mod.rs:480` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Drop for App is gone. App::release_phones (called from before_exit) unmounts only mounts this process owns, only when no other running Fox has a tab or pane inside (per-process markers kept by the watcher thread), and never when a picker's answer lies on the phone. The unmount is non-lazy and bounded to 2 s.
- **Also reported as:** app-22, fs-14

**What is wrong.** `self.phones` is the list of all detected MTP devices, not the ones this process mounted. On normal exit and on a successful pick (`return Ok(())` in wayland::run), App is dropped and `fusermount -u` is run for each mounted phone. The cancel path uses process::exit and skips Drop. So closing one Fox window unmounts the phone another window is browsing, and a picker that returns a file on the phone unmounts it right after printing the path, leaving the caller with a path that no longer exists (unless the mount happens to be busy).

**Trigger.** Mount the phone in window A. Open a second Fox window, or a `--pick` dialog from Command Center, and close/confirm it: window A's phone folder is now an empty mount point. Or pick a photo from the phone in the Bluetooth-send picker: the returned path is gone by the time obex tries to read it.

```rust
impl Drop for App {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        for phone in &self.phones {
            fs::unmount_phone(phone);
        }
    }
}
// wayland.rs:546  return Ok(());   (pick success → App dropped)
```

**Skeptic's check.** Drop for App (mod.rs:480-486) runs fs::unmount_phone for every entry of self.phones, which is detect_phones() output refreshed every 2 s, not a list of mounts this process made. unmount_phone only checks is_path_mounted, then runs 'fusermount -u'. mount_phone returns early when already mounted, so ownership is never recorded. main.rs has no single-instance logic, so each window and each --pick dialog is its own process. App is a local in wayland::run: normal exit and the successful-pick 'return Ok(())' (wayland.rs:546) both drop it; only the cancelled pick uses process::exit. Strongest scenario: a picker in which the user opens the phone and picks a file prints the path and then unmounts the phone, so the caller receives a path that no longer exists unless another process happens to hold a file open. fusermount -u refusing a busy mount was not tested (no phone attached), but a window that is merely browsing holds no open fd.

**Fix.** Never unmount when self.pick is Some. For normal windows, tracking 'mounted by this process' fixes the picker and second-window cases but not the reverse one (the owning window closes while another window is browsing), so the real choice is between leaving the phone mounted until an explicit eject and some shared refcount. That is a behaviour decision for Alva, not a mechanical edit.

### app-13: Search cancellation only aborts one recursion level: every superseded search thread keeps walking the whole tree

- **Where:** `src/app/mod.rs:501` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Search cancellation treats a disconnected cancel channel as cancelled, so every recursion level stops.
- **Also reported as:** input-23

**What is wrong.** cancel_search() sends one () and drops the Sender. The first try_recv() in the worker consumes it and returns from the innermost search_recursive call only. Back in the parent's loop the next try_recv() yields Err(Disconnected), `.is_ok()` is false, and the walk continues through every remaining sibling and ancestor directory. The result channel being closed does not stop it either (tx.send failing also returns from just one level, and only on a name match). run_search() is invoked on every keystroke, so typing an N-letter query from ~ or / leaves N threads each doing readdir + stat over the entire subtree to completion. On a phone (jmtpfs) each stat is an MTP round-trip behind the global device lock, so a few keystrokes saturate the device for minutes.

**Trigger.** Navigate to ~ (or the phone's storage root), open search, type `report`. Six fox search threads run a full recursive walk concurrently; closing the search or navigating away does not stop them. Visible as sustained CPU/disk (or a phone that stops responding to listing/copy).

```rust
for entry in entries {
    // Check cancellation
    if cancel.try_recv().is_ok() {
        return;
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** cancel_search (search.rs:15-21) takes the Sender, sends one () and drops it. Verified with rustc: after one message plus sender drop, try_recv is Ok(()) once and Err(Disconnected) afterwards. search_recursive returns () so the frame that consumed the message returns alone; every parent frame then sees Disconnected, '.is_ok()' is false, and the walk continues to the end. A failed tx.send also returns from one frame only, and only on a name match. run_search is called on every typed character and every Backspace (key.rs:401, 420), so each keystroke leaves a full recursive readdir+stat walk running. Hidden directories are skipped, which shortens but does not bound it.

**Fix.** One-line fix is enough: 'if !matches!(cancel.try_recv(), Err(std::sync::mpsc::TryRecvError::Empty)) { return; }'. The Sender lives in App.search_tx for the whole search and is only dropped by cancel_search, so Disconnected always means cancelled, and every parent frame hits the check at its next iteration, so no bool needs propagating. An Arc<AtomicBool> is equivalent. Debouncing run_search and capping results are separate improvements.

### cloud-net-11: Sign-in runs a blocking HTTPS request on the UI thread with no read timeout

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:45` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): submit_cloud_login (src/app/background.rs) runs CloudConfig::load and cloud::auth::sign_in on a worker; the dialog shows 'Signing in...' and frames keep coming. While the request is out the dialog cannot be dismissed (Cancel greyed out, Esc and scrim ignored); the wait is bounded by the cloud HTTP timeouts. No file under src/cloud was edited.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-19, dialogs-15

**What is wrong.** submit_cloud_login is called straight from the key and click handlers on the Wayland/render thread. sign_in uses ureq with the default agent: DNS has no timeout, connect waits up to 30 s, and the response read has no timeout at all. While it blocks, the loop neither reads the Wayland socket nor renders, so the window is frozen (the 'Signing in...' label set via dlg.submitting is never drawn) and compositor events pile up in the socket buffer. The doc comment says 'typically <2s', which only holds on a healthy network.

**Trigger.** Open the Cloud login dialog on a flaky or captive network (or with the network cable out but DNS cached), type credentials, press Enter. The whole Fox window hangs for 30 s or indefinitely; moving the mouse over it queues events the client never reads.

```rust
match crate::cloud::auth::sign_in(&cfg, &email, &password) {
```

**Skeptic's check.** submit_cloud_login is called directly from handle_key (key.rs:267) and handle_click (click.rs:120), both inside run_loop. It calls auth::sign_in, which is 'ureq::post(&url).send_json(body)' on the default agent (auth.rs:80). Checked ureq 2.12.1 in the cargo registry (agent.rs:256-259): timeout_connect is 30 s, timeout_read, timeout_write and the overall timeout are None. No frame is drawn between setting dlg.submitting and the call, so the 'Signing in' state is never visible, and the loop reads no Wayland events while blocked. The function's own doc comment acknowledges the block.

**Fix.** Run sign_in on a worker thread and deliver the Result through a slot or channel polled in the loop (the pattern AudioEdit::save already uses), keep submitting = true meanwhile, and add that state to needs_anim so the loop keeps polling. Also build the request from an Agent with connect and overall timeouts. A timeout alone is a small safe mitigation but still freezes the window for its duration. Changes threading, so not mechanical.

### fs-10: A superseded slow-mount load keeps dir_loading() true: local folders show "Loading…" and the loop renders at 60 fps

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:152` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. A fast reload drops the focused pane's in-flight slow-mount load, so Loading and the 60 fps polling stop.

**What is wrong.** The fast branch of reload() never removes a pending DirLoadTarget::Focused entry from dir_loads; only another spawn_dir_load or thread completion does. While the old worker is blocked (behind a jmtpfs download, or forever on a dead sshfs), dir_loading() stays true. That feeds needs_anim (wayland_loop.rs:1372), so Fox redraws continuously, and render.rs:941 and sections/status.rs:85 show "Loading…" for any empty local folder.

**Trigger.** Click a phone folder while a large copy from the phone is running (the listing blocks behind it), then click Home or an empty local folder. The empty folder says "Loading…" and Fox renders at 60 fps until the phone listing returns; with a hung network mount that is forever.

```rust
let entries = fs::list_directory(
    &self.current_dir,
    self.show_hidden,
    self.sort_by,
    self.sort_dir,
);
self.apply_listing(entries);
```

**Skeptic's check.** The fast branch of reload() (nav.rs:152-159) never touches dir_loads; entries are removed only by a new spawn_dir_load for the same target or by poll_dir_loads when the thread answers. dir_loading() is 'any load with target Focused', so a superseded phone or sshfs listing keeps it true after the user has navigated to a local folder. That feeds needs_anim (wayland_loop.rs:1372), so the loop renders at 60 fps, and both render.rs:941 and sections/status.rs:85 print 'Loading…' for an empty local folder. With a hung network mount the worker never returns, so this lasts until exit.

**Fix.** Add 'self.dir_loads.retain(|l| l.target != DirLoadTarget::Focused);' at the top of the non-slow branch of reload(). The dropped result would have been discarded by the dir mismatch anyway, including in the split-view case where it belonged to the other pane, so nothing regresses. The worker thread itself still runs to completion, which is unavoidable without a cancellable listing.

### app-25: Selection carry-over on reload is O(n²) path comparisons on the UI thread

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:185` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Selection carry-over uses a HashSet at all three sites.
- **Also reported as:** fs-19

**What is wrong.** selected_paths is a Vec<PathBuf>; every entry of the new listing does a linear contains(). After Select All this is n² PathBuf comparisons per reload. Measured with a standalone rustc -O benchmark using realistic paths: 5,000 entries = 0.39 s, 10,000 = 1.6 s, 20,000 = 6.2 s per reload. Reloads fire on every debounced fs-event burst (~120 ms), so anything that keeps writing into the folder while all is selected freezes the UI continuously. The same pattern is in reload_inactive_pane (split.rs:306) and keep_selection (dir_load.rs:119).

**Trigger.** Open a folder with ~10k files, Ctrl+A, right-click -> Compress (tar writes the archive into the same folder, generating modify events for its whole run): Fox is unresponsive until tar finishes. Changing sort with everything selected stalls for seconds.

```rust
if !selected_paths.is_empty() {
    for e in &mut self.entries {
        e.selected = selected_paths.contains(&e.path);
    }
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** selected_paths is a Vec<PathBuf> and each fresh entry calls contains() (nav.rs:185-188); the same pattern is in keep_selection (dir_load.rs:119-125) and reload_inactive_pane (split.rs:299-308, which does not even skip the empty case). I re-ran the pattern with rustc -O on realistic paths: 2,000 all-selected entries took 70 ms, 10,000 took 1.63 s per reload, on the render thread. Needs a large folder with a large selection plus reload churn, so an uncommon flow, but then every watcher burst repeats the stall.

**Fix.** Collect the selected paths into a HashSet<PathBuf> (or HashSet<&Path> where the borrow allows) and test membership against it at all three sites. No behaviour change.

### app-5: Tab switch / new tab / close tab does not rebuild the tree: Tree view keeps showing (and acting on) the previous tab's directory

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:221` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Tab switch / new tab / close tab rebuild the tree and reset the range-select anchor.

**What is wrong.** Tree view renders app.tree_entries, which is only rebuilt by apply_listing/cycle_view_mode/toggle_tree_expand. switch_tab, new_tab and close_tab only call sync_from_tab, which swaps current_dir/entries/scroll and nothing else. After the switch the path bar and current_dir describe the new tab while every tree row (and its click, drag, right-click, Open) still belongs to the old tab's directory. Paste / New Folder go to the new current_dir, so what the user sees and where operations land disagree. (Split focus swap is fine because tree_entries is in PaneView; tabs have no equivalent.)

**Trigger.** Set view to Tree. Open two tabs on different folders. Click the other tab: the tab highlight and breadcrumb change but the rows are still the first folder's files. Dragging a row moves a file from the first folder; pasting lands in the second.

```rust
pub(super) fn sync_from_tab(&mut self) {
    let tab = &self.tabs[self.current_tab];
    self.current_dir = tab.path.clone();
    self.entries = tab.entries.clone();
    self.scroll_offset = tab.scroll_offset;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** sync_from_tab copies only current_dir, entries and scroll_offset. tree_entries is rebuilt only by rebuild_tree, whose callers are apply_listing, cycle_view_mode, toggle_tree_expand and the pick setup in wayland.rs:453. switch_tab, new_tab and close_tab call sync_from_tab and nothing else. view_mode and tree_entries are App-wide, and the Tree branch of render.rs (804-869) draws app.tree_entries, so after a tab switch in Tree view the rows, their click zones and drags still belong to the previous tab while current_dir, the path bar and paste target are the new one. The loop does not repair it: DirWatcher::watch on a new dir clears pending events and the mtime tracker is reset without a reload (wayland_loop.rs:1327-1337).

**Fix.** After sync_from_tab in switch_tab, new_tab and close_tab add 'if self.view_mode == ViewMode::Tree { self.rebuild_tree(); }'. Put it in those three callers, not inside sync_from_tab: focus_pane also calls sync_from_tab right before a click is re-dispatched by row index, and rebuilding there could shift the row under the click. rebuild_tree lists synchronously, which on a slow-mount tab is the existing fs-4 problem. If app-6 is fixed by calling reload() on tab switch, that covers local dirs, but a slow dir would still show the old tree until the load lands unless tree_entries is cleared first.

### app-23: Eject, Format, mount and cloud sign-in run blocking subprocess / HTTP calls on the render thread

- **Where:** `src/app/places.rs:137` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Mount, eject, format, open-phone and cloud sign-in no longer run on the render thread. New primitive src/bg.rs (Task::spawn / poll, plus one eventfd that wakes the main loop when a result lands). src/app/device_ops.rs starts a DeviceJob per click and finishes it when the result arrives (navigate, status note or queued notice); the sidebar row shows Mounting/Ejecting/Formatting/Opening with moving dots (src/sections/sidebar.rs). Sign-in runs in src/app/background.rs. The blocking udisksctl/busctl functions moved from fs.rs to src/drive_ops.rs, jmtpfs to src/phone_mounts.rs. The 2-second lsblk poll moved to its own thread (src/devices.rs).

**What is wrong.** eject_drive -> `udisksctl unmount` waits for all dirty pages to be flushed to the stick; confirm_drive_format -> unmount of every partition plus a synchronous UDisks2 Format call (mkfs.ext4) via busctl; on_drive_click -> `udisksctl mount`; on_phone_click -> jmtpfs plus up to 20x50 ms sleeps; submit_cloud_login -> ureq POST with default 30 s connect timeout and no read timeout. All are called directly from click/key handlers inside run_loop, so no frames are drawn and no Wayland events are answered until they return (the 'submitting' flags are set but can never be rendered). The device poll also runs `lsblk` via .output() on this thread every 2 s of activity.

**Trigger.** Copy a few GB to a USB stick, then right-click it -> Eject: Fox is frozen for the whole write-back (tens of seconds). Format a large stick: frozen for the duration of mkfs. Submit the Cloud login while Wi-Fi is down: frozen up to the 30 s connect timeout.

```rust
if let Err(msg) = fs::unmount_drive(&drive) {
...
match fs::format_drive_ext4(&drive, "") {
...
match crate::cloud::auth::sign_in(&cfg, &email, &password) {   // nav.rs:45
```

**Skeptic's check.** All on the loop thread, read in fs.rs: eject_drive calls unmount_drive, which is 'udisksctl unmount' via Command::output() and waits for the stick's write-back; on_drive_click calls mount_drive (udisksctl mount, output()); on_phone_click calls mount_phone (jmtpfs .status() plus up to 20 sleeps of 50 ms); confirm_drive_format calls format_drive_ext4 (several udisksctl unmounts, then busctl ... Format with output()). They are reached from handle_click and handle_ctx_event inside run_loop, so no frame is drawn and the Wayland socket is not read until they return. refresh_drives also runs lsblk via output() on this thread every 2 s of rendered frames. The sign-in part is the same defect as cloud-net-11 and the format part overlaps dialogs-14.

**Fix.** Move mount, unmount, eject and format to a worker with a result slot polled by the loop, show a 'working' state in the dialog or sidebar row, and include that state in needs_anim so the loop keeps polling. Deliver Ok/Err, then refresh drives and navigate. Children are already reaped (output()/status()). Threading change, so not mechanical.

### dialogs-13: Format confirmation acts on a device name captured when the dialog opened, never re-validated

- **Where:** `src/app/places.rs:174` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Format re-detects drives and refuses if device, parent disk, name or whole-disk size changed since the dialog opened.

**What is wrong.** DriveDialog::ConfirmFormat stores a clone of the Drive (name, /dev/sdX, parent_disk). refresh_drives runs every 2 s but only updates app.drives; the dialog's copy is never compared against it. format_drive_ext4 formats whatever block device currently has that kernel name (the whole parent disk), so if the stick is swapped while the dialog is open the wrong device is erased while the title still names the old one. Enter confirms.

**Trigger.** Right-click USB stick A, Format to ext4…; while the dialog is open unplug A and plug in stick B, which takes the freed /dev/sdb; click Format (or press Enter): B is wiped, the dialog still says "Format A?".

```rust
let Some(crate::dialogs::DriveDialog::ConfirmFormat { drive, .. }) =
    self.drive_dialog.clone()
else {
    return;
};
match fs::format_drive_ext4(&drive, "") {
```

**Skeptic's check.** The defect is in the code: DriveDialog::ConfirmFormat holds a clone of the Drive taken when the dialog opened; refresh_drives replaces app.drives every 2 s but never touches or re-checks the dialog's copy; confirm_drive_format passes it to format_drive_ext4, which formats the block device currently named parent_disk (only validating that the basename has no '/'). Enter confirms. What I could not establish: that a second stick really takes the freed kernel name (usual, not guaranteed, not tested here), and how likely it is that someone swaps sticks while the confirm dialog is open. Impact if it happens is erasing the wrong removable disk under a title naming the old one.

**Fix.** In confirm_drive_format, re-run fs::detect_drives() and require an entry with the same device and parent_disk that is still removable, plus matching friendly name and whole-disk size; otherwise put an error in the dialog and do not format. This only adds a guard. Compare the whole-disk size (disk_size_bytes of parent_disk), not total_bytes, which differs between mounted and unmounted states. Two identical sticks would still pass; a serial or /dev/disk/by-id identity is the complete fix.

### dialogs-14: Format button blocks the UI thread for the whole mkfs, and busctl's 25 s call timeout can report failure while the format is still running

- **Where:** `src/app/places.rs:179` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Format runs on a worker (src/app/device_ops.rs). DriveDialog::ConfirmFormat has a `working` state drawn by the new src/format_dialog.rs: title 'Formatting <name>...', 'Keep the drive plugged in', Format greyed out, Cancel becomes Hide. The 'is it still the same disk' check (which runs lsblk) moved into the worker. The result closes the dialog or fills its error; if the dialog was hidden it arrives as a status note or notice.

**What is wrong.** confirm_drive_format calls format_drive_ext4 inline: several udisksctl unmounts and then `busctl call ... Block Format` with .output(), all on the render thread. The window is frozen with no progress for the duration. busctl call waits 25 s by default; a large or slow stick can exceed that, at which point the dialog shows an error although UDisks is still formatting, and a retry hits a busy device. Clicks made during the freeze are delivered afterwards to whatever is under the cursor once the dialog is gone.

**Trigger.** Format a large USB 2.0 stick: Fox stops repainting until mkfs returns; past 25 s the dialog shows "Error: format: ... timed out" while the stick is in fact being formatted.

```rust
match fs::format_drive_ext4(&drive, "") {
    Ok(()) => {
        self.drive_dialog = None;
```

**Skeptic's check.** confirm_drive_format calls fs::format_drive_ext4 inline; that runs unmount_all_on_disk (one blocking udisksctl per mounted partition) and then 'busctl call ... Block Format' with Command::output(), all on the render thread, so the window is frozen for the whole format. This part is traced and overlaps app-23. The second claim rests on busctl's documented 25 s default for 'call' (no --timeout is passed, and busctl is at /usr/bin/busctl on this machine): on a slow or large stick the call can time out and the dialog would show an error while UDisks keeps formatting. I did not measure a format, so treat that half as likely rather than proven.

**Fix.** Run the format on a worker thread with a result slot polled by the loop, show a 'Formatting…' state with the buttons disabled, and pass an explicit generous --timeout to busctl. Input events queued during the current freeze are replayed afterwards against whatever is under the cursor, which the threaded version also removes. Threading change.

### wayland-11: search_rx is never cleared when a search finishes, so needs_anim stays true and Fox renders at 60 fps for as long as results are shown

- **Where:** `src/app/search.rs:59` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. poll_search drops the receiver when the worker finishes, so the loop goes idle.

**What is wrong.** poll_search treats Empty and Disconnected the same and leaves `search_rx = Some(..)` after the worker thread has exited. search_rx is only reset by cancel_search (closing the search or typing again). needs_anim therefore stays true after every completed search, forcing a full frame every 16 ms.

**Trigger.** Open search, type a query, wait for the results, and leave the window alone. lntrn-file-manager keeps redrawing continuously until the search bar is closed.

```rust
|| app.search_rx.is_some()
// app/search.rs poll_search:
match rx.try_recv() {
    Ok(entry) => self.search_results.push(entry),
    Err(_) => break,
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** app/search.rs:53-63: poll_search breaks on any Err, treating Empty and Disconnected alike, and never clears search_rx. grep shows search_rx is set to None only in cancel_search (closing search or typing again). The worker closure owns result_tx and drops it when search_recursive returns, so Disconnected is observable. wayland_loop.rs:1364 keeps needs_anim true while search_rx.is_some(), so after every completed search Fox forces a full frame every 16 ms for as long as the results stay on screen. Root cause is in search.rs, the symptom line is wayland_loop.rs:1364.

**Fix.** In poll_search match Err(TryRecvError::Disconnected) and set search_rx = None and search_tx = None after draining. The last batch is still painted because that iteration had already requested a frame callback while needs_anim was true. Returning a bool and setting frame_done from it is a harmless extra.

### app-12: context_override_paths outlives a dismissed context menu: later Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X / drag / Open With act on the previously right-clicked nested tree row instead of the selection

- **Where:** `src/app/select.rs:231` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The path override is cleared whenever the context menu is no longer open.

**What is wrong.** Right-clicking a nested Tree row sets context_override_paths = [that path] so the next menu action targets it. It is cleared only after a MenuEvent::Action, at the start of the next content-area right-click, or in focus_pane. If the menu is closed without choosing anything (click outside, Esc, compositor popup_done), the override stays. selected_paths() is used by copy_selected, cut_selected, the drag source list (wayland_loop.rs:394 and 922) and Open With, so all of those silently operate on the stale nested path while the UI shows a different selection. Trash/Delete read entries directly and are unaffected.

**Trigger.** Tree view: expand a folder, right-click a file inside it, press Esc. Now click-select other files and press Ctrl+X, go elsewhere, Ctrl+V: the nested file is moved, not the files that were highlighted. Dragging a selected file likewise drags the nested one.

```rust
pub fn selected_paths(&self) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    if !self.context_override_paths.is_empty() {
        return self.context_override_paths.clone();
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** context_override_paths is set at context_menu.rs:403 for a nested tree row and cleared only at :302 (a later content-area right-click, after the sidebar and search early returns), :835 (after a MenuEvent::Action) and split.rs:193. The three dismissal paths do not clear it: click outside (wayland_loop.rs:671-677), Esc (key.rs:384-390) and compositor popup_closed (wayland_loop.rs:1032-1038). selected_paths() returns the override whenever it is non-empty, and it feeds copy_selected, cut_selected, the drag source lists (wayland_loop.rs:394, 922, 421, 931) and Open With. The nested right-click also clears the entries selection, so the user's next visible selection and the override disagree: Ctrl+X then acts on the stale nested path. Tree view only.

**Fix.** Clear app.context_override_paths at the three dismissal sites (click-outside close, Esc close, popup_closed). Safe ordering-wise: a click on a menu item is routed to the popup branch, not the click-outside branch, and popup_done is only sent for dismissals. Cleaner still is to stop consulting the override in selected_paths() and pass it explicitly inside handle_ctx_event.

### app-16: toggle_split, restore_split, pinned-tab restore and favourites restore stat/list directories synchronously with no slow-mount check

- **Where:** `src/app/split.rs:106` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): toggle_split seeds the right pane from the listing in hand; restore_split skips is_dir on slow paths and loads through reload_inactive_pane (src/app/split.rs). Pinned tabs on slow paths are restored unchecked and listed through reload_tab (src/wayland.rs). Favourites on slow paths skip exists() (src/app/places.rs). The mount table is invalidated after every mount. Also: fs::read_directory no longer stats an entry that is itself a slow mount point (an sshfs folder in ~).
- **Also reported as:** fs-20

**What is wrong.** toggle_split lists the focused directory again on the UI thread to seed the right pane even when it is a slow mount (it could clone self.entries). restore_split does right_path.is_dir() + list_directory at startup (and toggle_split then lists current_dir a second time, the result being thrown away). wayland.rs:480-485 does path.is_dir() + list_directory for every pinned tab, and places.rs:24 does p.exists() for every favourite, all before the first frame. Any of these paths living on an unreachable sshfs/NFS mount or a busy MTP mount blocks startup or the split toggle for the mount's timeout. Additionally fs::is_slow_path caches /proc/mounts for 2 s, so the first navigations right after on_phone_click mounts the phone are still classified fast and listed synchronously.

**Trigger.** Browse a phone folder and click the split toggle while a copy from the phone runs: UI freezes. Or pin a tab / add a favourite on an sshfs/NAS mount and start Fox while the host is unreachable: blank frozen window until the FUSE/NFS timeout.

```rust
let mut right_tab = DirectoryTab::new(self.current_dir.clone());
right_tab.entries = fs::list_directory(
    &right_tab.path, self.show_hidden, self.sort_by, self.sort_dir,
);
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read each site: toggle_split lists current_dir with fs::list_directory and no is_slow_path check (split.rs:105-111); restore_split does right_path.is_dir() plus a listing, then calls toggle_split, which lists current_dir again and throws the result away (split.rs:144-158); wayland.rs:478-487 does is_dir() plus a listing for every pinned tab; load_favorites_from calls exists() per favorite. The last three run before the first frame, so a pinned tab or favorite on an unreachable sshfs/NFS mount blocks startup, and toggling split on a phone folder blocks behind the MTP lock. The cache claim also holds: is_slow_path trusts /proc/mounts for 2 s (fs.rs:1003), so a navigate_to issued right after mount_phone returns can classify the new mount as fast and list it synchronously, depending on when the cache was last refreshed.

**Fix.** toggle_split: seed right_tab.entries from self.entries.clone() with the selected flags cleared (same directory, same sort, same show_hidden), which is safe by itself. restore_split and pinned tabs: when is_slow_path(path), skip the is_dir probe, leave entries empty and load through spawn_dir_load (Inactive / Tab(idx)). Favorites: skip exists() for slow paths. Invalidate MOUNT_TABLE after a successful mount_phone or mount_drive. Several sites and async loading, so review as a whole.

### sections-10: Unfocused split pane in Tree view keeps a stale tree: reload_inactive_pane refreshes entries but nothing rebuilds the parked tree_entries

- **Where:** `src/app/split.rs:305` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): App::rebuild_parked_tree (src/app/tree.rs) builds the unfocused pane's tree from its parked state with the same builder. It is called from install_inactive_listing (reload_inactive_pane and off-thread deliveries) and when a slow expanded folder of that tree lands.
- **Also reported as:** app-18

**What is wrong.** For a pane parked in Tree mode, render_inactive_pane draws split.parked_view.tree_entries, drag_drop.rs:134 hit-tests drops against it, and wayland_loop uses its length for scrolling. reload_inactive_pane and apply_dir_load(Inactive) only replace tab.entries. focus_pane swaps the stale tree_entries into the flat fields and copies entries, without calling rebuild_tree; the watcher/mtime trackers are merely re-pointed, so no reload follows. The stale tree persists until the user navigates or an unrelated fs event arrives.

**Trigger.** Split view; focus the right pane and switch it to Tree; click the left pane. Drag a file from the left pane into the right pane and choose Move. The file disappears from the left pane and does not appear in the right one, even after clicking the right pane to focus it. Renames/deletes of folders shown in the parked tree likewise leave dead rows that still accept drops (dest_dir is the stale path).

```rust
tab.entries = fs::list_directory(&tab.path, self.show_hidden, sort_by, sort_dir);
for e in &mut tab.entries {
    e.selected = selected.contains(&e.path);
}
// sections/split.rs:389   let tree_entries = &view.tree_entries;   (what the pane actually draws)
```

**Skeptic's check.** sections/split.rs:389 draws 'view.tree_entries' for a Tree-mode unfocused pane, drag_drop.rs:134 hit-tests drops against the same list, and wayland_loop.rs:1458 sizes scrolling from it. grep shows the parked tree_entries is written only by swap_view_with_parked; reload_inactive_pane (split.rs:285-309) and apply_dir_load(Inactive) replace tab.entries only. focus_pane swaps the stale tree back into the flat fields and does not rebuild, and the loop does not reload on a focus change. toggle_split and restore_split start the right pane in List, but a user can switch the focused right pane to Tree and then click the left pane, which parks it as Tree. After that, a drop or any file change in that pane's directory is not shown, and stale folder rows still accept drops with their old paths.

**Fix.** Factor the tree builder into a function taking (root, expanded set, sort, show_hidden) so it can run against the parked state, and call it from reload_inactive_pane and apply_dir_load(Inactive) when parked_view.view_mode is Tree. Do not simply call rebuild_tree() at the end of focus_pane: translate_split_zone focuses the pane and then re-dispatches the click by tree row index, so rebuilding there can move a different row under the click; and it is a synchronous listing (see fs-4 for slow mounts).

### sections-5: Picker file-type filter is never applied in Tree view, which pick mode forces

- **Where:** `src/app/tabs.rs:38` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Tree view applies the picker's active file-type filter.

**What is wrong.** The active filter is applied only to self.entries in apply_listing (app/nav.rs:190-198). Pick mode forces ViewMode::Tree (wayland.rs:449) and the tree is rebuilt straight from fs::list_directory with no filtering, so the rows the user sees and selects ignore the filter entirely. cycle_filter() reloads but the visible list does not change (only the Save extension swaps). Also the very first listing is unfiltered even for Grid/List because navigate_to runs before app.pick is set (wayland.rs:443-448).

**Trigger.** Any app opens the picker with filters (e.g. '--filters "Images:*.png,*.jpg"' via the portal). Every file type is listed; clicking the filter button changes its label and nothing else; the user can pick a .zip and the caller receives a file type it did not ask for.

```rust
fn build_tree_recursive(&mut self, dir: &PathBuf, depth: usize) {
    let entries = fs::list_directory(dir, self.show_hidden, self.sort_by, self.sort_dir);
    for entry in entries {
        ...
        self.tree_entries.push(TreeEntry { entry, depth, is_expanded });
```

**Skeptic's check.** matches_filter is applied in exactly one place, apply_listing on self.entries (nav.rs:190-198); grep finds no other use. Pick mode forces ViewMode::Tree, and the Tree branch of render.rs (804-869) draws app.tree_entries, which build_tree_recursive fills straight from list_directory with no filtering. So the picker's default view shows every file whatever the filter; cycle_filter reloads but only the flat list (not shown) and the Save extension change. A top-level file excluded from entries still gets clicked through the tree path: entry_idx is None, it goes into pick_tree_selection, and confirm_pick returns it. Also confirmed: wayland.rs:443-448 calls navigate_to before app.pick is set, so the first flat listing is unfiltered too. Impact is 'filter does nothing', not data loss.

**Fix.** In build_tree_recursive, when self.pick has filters, skip non-directory entries that fail matches_filter(&entry.name, &patterns) with the active filter, the same rule as apply_listing; and set app.pick before the first navigate_to in wayland.rs. Cloning the active patterns once per rebuild avoids borrowing self across the recursion. Local and mirrors existing behaviour.

### app-6: Switching tab or split pane installs a stale listing snapshot and never reloads it; an in-flight slow-mount load for the old pane is dropped

- **Where:** `src/app/tabs.rs:68` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): switch_tab, new_tab and close_tab reload the tab that comes into view (src/app/tabs.rs). A pane focus change sets a flag and the main loop reloads after the click has been handled. The unfocused pane's folder has its own inotify watch (src/wayland_loop.rs). Watcher reloads are now processed on every wake-up, not only in drawn frames, so an idle window refreshes too.

**What is wrong.** A background tab's entries are whatever was cloned into it the last time it was active (only a drop onto the tab refreshes it via reload_tab). switch_tab / focus_pane copy that snapshot into the flat fields and do not call reload(). The loop does not compensate: dir_watcher.watch(new_dir) discards pending events ('Navigation reloads anyway') and the mtime poll resets its tracker without reloading when current_dir changes. So the listing stays as old as the last visit until some later fs event in that folder. Also, the unfocused split pane's directory is never watched at all; it is only re-listed when the focused pane happens to reload. Worse on slow mounts: reload() clears entries and spawns DirLoadTarget::Focused; if the user switches tab/pane before it lands, apply_dir_load drops the result (load.dir != current_dir) and the original tab/pane is left permanently empty with no 'Loading…' state.

**Trigger.** Tab A on ~/Downloads, switch to tab B, let a download finish (or delete files from a terminal), switch back to A: the new file is missing / deleted files are still shown and clicking them silently fails. Or: open a phone folder in tab A, click tab B before the listing arrives, return to A: empty folder forever.

```rust
pub fn switch_tab(&mut self, index: usize) {
    self.focus_pane(super::PaneSide::Left);
    if index >= self.tabs.len() || index == self.current_tab { return; }
    self.sync_to_tab();
    self.current_tab = index;
    self.sync_from_tab();
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** switch_tab, new_tab and close_tab install tab.entries, a clone taken when that tab was last active; reload_tab is called only from the paste and drop finalisers. Nothing reloads afterwards: DirWatcher::watch on a different dir clears dirty and pending_since (dir_watch.rs:78-80), and the mtime poll's 'directory changed' branch resets its tracker without reloading (wayland_loop.rs:1327-1337). So a background tab shows its old listing until a later fs event in that folder, and deleted files remain clickable. focus_pane behaves the same, and the unfocused pane's directory is never watched; it is only re-listed as a side effect of the focused pane reloading. The dropped slow-mount load in the last sentence is fs-9's defect; a reload on switch would re-request it, but the root causes differ, so both are kept.

**Fix.** Call self.reload() at the end of switch_tab, new_tab and close_tab; that is cheap on local dirs and goes async on slow ones, and it also rebuilds the tree (app-5). Do not add it to focus_pane as proposed: translate_split_zone focuses the pane and then re-dispatches the same click by entry index, so a reload there can change which item the click lands on. For pane focus, reload after the click has been handled, or watch both panes' directories. The startup 'app.switch_tab(0)' in wayland.rs would then list once more, which is harmless. Behaviour and timing change, so review.

### audio-9: Saving an MP3 with an ID3v2.2 tag deletes every frame except the nine Fox edits

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/id3.rs:151` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (B): src/audio_tags/id3.rs rewritten around a version-preserving model, parsing moved to new src/audio_tags/id3/parse.rs, encodings/pictures to new src/audio_tags/id3/text.rs. A tag is written back in the version it was read in (v2.2 stays v2.2, v2.3 stays v2.3, v2.4 stays v2.4). Frame is now { id, flags, data-as-stored }; every frame the user did not edit is emitted byte for byte, whatever its id. For v2.2 that means all 3-character frames (TP2, TCM, TPA, COM, ULT, unknown ones) survive; Fox's own fields map to TT2/TP1/TAL/TYE/TCO/TRK/TBP/TKE and artwork to PIC (read and written). No frame is converted or dropped, so no id table and no refusal for unknown v2.2 frames is needed.

**What is wrong.** parse_v22 keeps only TT2/TP1/TAL/TYE/TCO/TRK/TBP/TKE/PIC. Everything else (TP2 album artist, TCM composer, TPA disc number, TCP compilation, COM comments incl. iTunNORM/gapless data, ULT lyrics, TEN, ...) is discarded at parse time, so the rebuilt v2.3 tag no longer has them. The comment is wrong: nearly all v2.2 ids have a direct v2.3 equivalent. Reproduced: a v2.2 tag with TT2, TP2, TCM, TPA, COM comes back with only TIT2 after one save. This contradicts the module's promise that unknown frames survive a round-trip.

**Trigger.** MP3 ripped/tagged by old iTunes (v2.2 was its default for years). Properties, fix a typo in Title, Save. Album artist, composer, disc number, comments and lyrics are gone.

```rust
_ => continue, // 3-char ids have no v2.3 home — dropped
```

**Skeptic's check.** parse_v22 maps nine ids (TT2, TP1, TAL, TYE, TCO, TRK, TBP, TKE, PIC) and hits '_ => continue' for everything else, so those frames never enter tag.frames. mp3::write does parse, apply, build and writes only tag.frames (as v2.3), and wav::write goes through the same parse. So saving any edit to a v2.2-tagged file removes album artist (TP2), composer (TCM), disc (TPA), comments (COM), lyrics (ULT) and the rest. The module header's promise that unknown frames survive a round trip does not hold for v2.2, and the code comment is wrong: most v2.2 ids have a direct v2.3 counterpart. Only affects files still carrying v2.2 tags (old iTunes rips).

**Fix.** Add the standard v2.2 to v2.3 id table for frames whose body layout is identical (all T?? text frames, TXX, WXX and W?? URLs, COM, ULT, UFI, CNT, POP, GEO, MCI), and for any id with no layout-compatible mapping (LNK, CRM and similar) refuse to write with a clear error rather than drop it. The table needs care per frame, and refusing to save is a visible behaviour change, so not mechanical.

### audio-11: When ID3 parsing stops early, the save silently discards every frame after that point (old iTunes v2.4 sizes, compressed frames)

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/id3.rs:190` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (B): id3/parse.rs: the frame walk reports completeness. It ends cleanly only at the exact end of the tag or on padding that is zeros to the end; otherwise the tag gets a `blocker` (invalid frame id, frame overrunning the tag, non-zero bytes after the last frame, tag cut short, unknown header flags, v2.2 compression bit, extended header, an unsynchronisation flag that does not match the bytes, v2.4 'all frames unsynchronised' over a frame that says otherwise). mp3::write and wav::write refuse a tag with a blocker, and mp3::write also refuses an 'ID3' header of unknown version/size instead of stacking a new tag in front of it. Things that used to abort the walk and are now handled: old-iTunes v2.4 frame sizes written as plain 32-bit numbers (detected per frame by checking which reading lands on a frame/padding/end; written back sync-safe), compressed/encrypted/grouped frames and frames with unknown flags (carried through untouched with their flags), zero-length frames, NUL/space-padded 3-char ids in v2.3/v2.4, tag-level unsynchronisation in v2.2/v2.3 (decoded after validating it), v2.4 frame-level unsync + data-length indicator (decoded for reading, stored bytes kept), v2.4 footer (dropped, tag rewritten in place). AudioMeta gained `write_blocker`, and the Properties dialog shows it as a notice when the file is opened.

**What is wrong.** parse_v23 breaks on the first frame it cannot walk (non-syncsafe v2.4 size, invalid id such as a NUL-padded 3-char id, size overrun) and skips compressed/encrypted frames (line 218). The caller cannot tell a clean end (padding) from an abort, so write() rebuilds the tag from the partial frame list and overwrites the original. Old iTunes wrote v2.4 frame sizes as plain big-endian (mutagen, TagLib and ffmpeg all carry a workaround). Reproduced with a v2.4 tag whose APIC size is plain BE: Fox reads title only, artist and album come back empty, artwork is cut to 180 of 308 bytes; a save would write that truncated picture and drop TPE1/TALB.

**Trigger.** MP3 tagged by old iTunes as ID3v2.4 with cover art larger than 127 bytes. Properties shows Title but empty Artist/Album and no art; type anything and Save: the remaining frames and the intact cover are permanently replaced by the truncated version.

```rust
let size = if v24 {
    match syncsafe(&body[pos + 4..pos + 8]) {
        Some(s) => s,
        None => break,
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** parse_v23 stops at the first frame it cannot walk: a v2.4 size that is not syncsafe ('None => break', line 190-193), an id failing valid_id, or a size overrunning the body; compressed or encrypted frames are skipped with 'continue' (line 218-220). Id3Tag carries no 'partial' marker, and mp3::write / wav::write rebuild the tag from whatever frames were collected and write it over the original (in place when it fits the old padding). So every frame at and after the abort point, and every skipped frame, is deleted by a save. The plain-big-endian v2.4 size case follows directly from syncsafe(): any byte with the high bit makes it None, and without high bits the size is mis-read and the next 'id' is garbage. I did not build such a file; how many of Alva's files are affected is unknown, but the code path is unconditional for any tag the parser does not fully understand.

**Fix.** Have the parser report completeness: true only when the loop ended on padding (id[0] == 0) or exactly at the end of the body, and false if any frame was skipped. Refuse to write when it is false, with an error the dialog can show. Optionally add the usual fallback for v2.4: if the syncsafe size does not land on a valid frame id or padding but the plain big-endian size does, use the plain size. Changes the parse API and adds a refusal path, so not mechanical.

### audio-10: Any save collapses all pictures to a single retyped front cover, even when the artwork was not touched

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/id3.rs:488` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. apply() leaves APIC frames alone when the artwork is unchanged (regression test added).

**What is wrong.** AudioEdit::current_tags() always passes `meta.tags.artwork.clone()` when the user did not change the art, and apply() -> set_artwork() removes every APIC frame and writes one new frame built by build_apic (type 3, empty description). Additional pictures (back cover, booklet, artist) are deleted, the surviving picture loses its type and description, and a picture that parse_apic split wrongly is re-serialised in its damaged form. Reproduced: tag with Front and Back APIC -> after changing only the title one APIC remains, description gone.

**Trigger.** MP3 with front + back cover. Properties, edit Title, Save. The back cover is gone from the file.

```rust
self.set_artwork(t.artwork.as_ref());
```

**Skeptic's check.** current_tags() passes 'self.meta.tags.artwork.clone()' when art_change is None (properties_audio/mod.rs:180-183). apply() always calls set_artwork, which removes every APIC frame and pushes one built by build_apic: picture type 3, empty description (id3.rs:443-451, 386-394). So a save that only changed the title deletes all additional pictures and rewrites the survivor's type and description; a picture that parse_apic split imperfectly is re-serialised in that form. wav::write uses the same apply(). Files with more than one embedded picture are uncommon, but the loss is silent.

**Fix.** In apply(), skip the artwork step when it is unchanged: 'if t.artwork != self.artwork() { self.set_artwork(t.artwork.as_ref()); }'. Artwork derives PartialEq, and both sides come from the same parse_apic path (including the mime sniff), so an untouched picture compares equal and all APIC frames stay byte-identical. Behaviour when the user really replaces or removes the art is unchanged.

### audio-13: Clearing Track does not stick: stale ID3v1 track byte (MP3) or IPRT / acid tempo (WAV) refill the field after save

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/id3v1.rs:68` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. ID3v1 track byte is written whenever the block is v1.1; WAV IPRT follows ITRK when present. (The acid-chunk tempo fallback is unchanged.)

**What is wrong.** build() starts from the existing trailer and only writes the track byte when the new track is non-zero, so a cleared (or >255, or non-numeric) track leaves the old number in ID3v1. mp3::read then fill_missing()s the empty v2 track from v1. WAV has the same shape: write sets ITRK only, read falls back to IPRT (wav.rs:266-274, ffmpeg writes track as IPRT), and an `acid` chunk tempo refills an emptied BPM (wav.rs:275). Reproduced for both: track "5" (v1) and "7" (IPRT) are still there after clearing and saving.

**Trigger.** MP3 with an ID3v1.1 trailer carrying a track number. Properties, empty the Track field, Save: "Saved", and the old number is back in the field.

```rust
if track > 0 {
    out[125] = 0;
    out[126] = track;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** id3v1::build copies the existing 128 bytes and writes the track only inside 'if track > 0' (id3v1.rs:68-71), so an emptied, non-numeric or over-255 track leaves the old byte 126 in place. mp3::read then calls fill_missing, which copies the v1 track into the now-empty v2 field, so the number comes back after 'Saved'. The other v1 fields are fine because put() zeroes them and a cleared genre becomes 255. WAV has the same shape: write sets ITRK only, read falls back to IPRT when ITRK is empty (wav.rs:266-274), and an 'acid' chunk tempo refills an emptied BPM (wav.rs:275-277).

**Fix.** id3v1: write the track byte whenever the block is v1.1 or a track is set: 'if track > 0 || out[125] == 0 { out[125] = 0; out[126] = track; }'. That leaves a v1.0 30-byte comment alone. WAV: when IPRT already exists in the INFO list, update or remove it together with ITRK; do not start writing IPRT into files that never had it. The acid tempo is audio data, not a tag, so leave the chunk and either accept it as a read-only fallback or skip the fill when an id3 chunk is present; that last point is a choice, the first two are mechanical.

### audio-7: Saving through a symlink replaces the link with a private copy and leaves the real file untouched

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/mod.rs:184` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. replace_file resolves symlinks and replaces the real file (regression test added).

**What is wrong.** replace_file() opens `path` (following the link) to read, but renames the temp file onto `path` itself, which replaces the symlink rather than its target. Reproduced: after write() on a symlinked MP3 the link is a regular file with the new tag and the real file has no tag. Whether this happens depends on an invisible detail (MP3 with enough padding / WAV with trailing tags go in place and write through the link correctly), so behaviour is inconsistent. Hard links are split the same way, and ownership is reset to the current user.

**Trigger.** A playlist/crate folder of symlinks into the real library. Properties on a linked MP3 that has no ID3v2 tag yet, set Title, Save. The library file is unchanged, the link silently became a full duplicate of the audio.

```rust
std::fs::set_permissions(&tmp, perms)?;
std::fs::rename(&tmp, path)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** replace_file opens 'path' for reading (follows the link) but renames the temp file onto 'path' itself. Verified with rustc: rename onto a symlink replaces the link with the regular file and leaves the target's content unchanged. So when the rewrite path is taken (an MP3 gaining its first tag or outgrowing its padding, or a WAV with metadata before the audio), a symlinked song becomes a private full copy and the library file is untouched, while the in-place paths (write_all_at on an opened path) do write through the link. Hard links are split the same way and the new file is owned by the current user. Needs a symlinked or hard-linked library, so an uncommon setup.

**Fix.** At the top of replace_file, resolve the real file with std::fs::canonicalize(path) and derive the temp directory, temp name and rename destination from the resolved path. The temp then lives beside the real file, which also keeps the rename on one filesystem. No one benefits from the current link-replacing behaviour. Hard links cannot be preserved with a rename-based rewrite; that is an accepted limitation.

### audio-3: WAV whose final chunk is odd-sized with no pad byte: new tags are written one byte misaligned and cannot be read back

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/wav.rs:327` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. A missing pad byte after an odd final chunk is supplied in both write paths, so new tags land on the even boundary (regression test added).

**What is wrong.** Chunk::end() includes the RIFF pad byte. When the last kept chunk has an odd size and the writer omitted the trailing pad (very common for the final chunk), end() is len+1 and `.min(len)` silently drops the pad, so the tail is written at an odd offset. Every reader, including walk() itself, then looks for the next chunk one byte later and sees "IST?" instead of "LIST". Reproduced with odd data sizes 1001/1003/1005/1041 and no pad: after each save read-back title is "" and the file grows by ~1.1 KB per save (three saves in a row never recover). The rewrite branch has the same miscount (`total` adds a pad byte that io::copy never copies, so the RIFF size is off by one and the tail is misaligned).

**Trigger.** 8-bit mono or 24-bit mono WAV with an odd number of data bytes and no trailing pad byte. Properties, edit Title, Enter: "Saved", then the fields blank out; repeated saves keep bloating the file.

```rust
let keep_end = keep
    .iter()
    .map(|c| c.end())
    .max()
    .unwrap_or(12)
    .min(len);
```

**Skeptic's check.** Reproduced: odd data size 1001, no pad byte. In-place branch: save 1 -> 2,129 bytes, read-back title "" (tail written at odd offset 1045 while walk() looks at 1046). In my run the second save happened to recover because the leftover 'IST'+size byte was not printable (title 'Hello', LIST size 0x12) and the walk stopped; with realistic tags the LIST size low byte is 0x20-0x7E, 'IST?' parses as a chunk clamped to EOF, becomes a kept chunk, and every save appends another tail (the finder's never-recovers case). Rewrite branch also reproduced (LIST before data, odd data): read-back "", RIFF size counts a pad byte that io::copy never copied.

**Fix.** When the unclamped max end() is len+1, materialise the pad: in-place branch set_len(len+1) (zero-extends) and use len+1 as keep_end; rewrite branch write one 0 byte after a kept chunk whose copy came up one byte short (or copy exactly body+size and emit the pad explicitly for every odd chunk). Note a clamped chunk (audio-2) can also end up odd, so apply the same rule after clamping. Add tests with odd data sizes for both branches.

### audio-4: WAV in-place save drops the old tags before the new ones are safely written, and validates the 4 GB limit after mutating

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/wav.rs:341` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The 4 GB check happens before the file is touched, and the tail is written before the file is trimmed. (Extra-27 is the same.)

**What is wrong.** The in-place branch is not crash- or error-safe: the existing LIST/id3 chunks are truncated away first, then the new tail is written. If write_all_at fails (ENOSPC is realistic: the new tail carries 1 KiB padding plus up to 3 MB of art), or Fox exits while the detached save thread is between the two calls, the file is left with no tags at all (including the preserved Serato/rekordbox frames) and a stale RIFF size. The 4 GB check runs after both mutations, so that error path also leaves a modified file with a stale RIFF size. The MP3 in-place branch (mp3.rs:281) is a single pwrite, so it only risks a torn tag.

**Trigger.** Tagged WAV on a nearly full disk: add cover art, Save -> "Save failed: No space left on device" and all previous tags are gone. Or a WAV a few MB under 4 GiB: add art -> "Save failed: WAV would exceed 4 GB" but the old tags were already replaced and the RIFF size is now wrong.

```rust
f.set_len(keep_end).map_err(io_err)?;
f.write_all_at(&tail, keep_end).map_err(io_err)?;
let new_len = keep_end + tail.len() as u64;
if new_len - 8 > u32::MAX as u64 {
    return Err("WAV would exceed 4 GB".into());
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Code order in the in-place branch is set_len(keep_end) -> write_all_at(tail) -> 4 GB check -> RIFF size write. Case (3) of my audio-1 run shows the error path leaving a mutated file with a stale RIFF size. A write_all_at failure (ENOSPC, EIO, or the detached save thread dying with the process) leaves the file with the old LIST/id3 chunks already truncated away. Trigger is rare (full disk, WAV within a few MB of 4 GiB, exit mid-save) and the loss is tags/analysis frames, not audio.

**Fix.** Safe part: compute new_len and do the `> u32::MAX` check before opening the file for write. The finder's 'grow first with set_len so ENOSPC surfaces' does not work: ftruncate creates a hole and reserves nothing (and btrfs CoW allocates on overwrite anyway); it would need fallocate. Real crash safety is not achievable for an O(1) in-place tail rewrite, since the new tail overwrites the old chunks; at most write the tail first and trim with set_len(new_len) afterwards so a failed write leaves the old length rather than a truncated file. Only the check reordering is what I consider mechanical.

### fileops-35: Checksum thread cannot be cancelled and never ends on endless/blocking sources; result does not trigger a redraw

- **Where:** `src/checksums.rs:21` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
