- **Status:** FIXED. Checksum job has a cancel flag set on drop, refuses non-regular files, and keeps frames coming while it runs.
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-11

**What is wrong.** Dropping the ChecksumJob (closing Properties) leaves the detached thread reading the whole file; reopening and expanding the section starts another one each time. For a FIFO the open blocks forever, and for an endless character device the read loop (lines 39-45) never terminates. On an MTP path the read pulls the whole file through jmtpfs under its global lock for as long as the thread lives. The header says the dialog polls get() every frame, but nothing in needs_anim (wayland_loop.rs:1356-1379) covers a running checksum, so with an idle pointer 'Computing…' stays on screen after the hash is done.

**Trigger.** Properties on a 50 GB image, open Checksum, close the dialog, repeat: several threads each hash 50 GB in the background. Open Checksum and leave the mouse still: the hash never appears until an input event arrives.

```rust
std::thread::spawn(move || {
    let hex = compute_sha256(&path).unwrap_or_else(|| "Unreadable".to_string());
    *slot.lock().unwrap() = Some(hex);
});
```

**Skeptic's check.** ChecksumJob holds only the result Arc, has no Drop and no cancel flag; compute_sha256 loops until EOF. properties.rs:1121-1122 spawns whenever the section is open and checksum_job is None, gated only on !props.is_dir, so a FIFO (open blocks forever) or /dev/zero (never EOF) is accepted and there is no is_slow_path check (MTP read pulls the whole file under jmtpfs's lock). Each new Properties instance starts another reader. Redraw part also confirmed: the needs_anim expression at wayland_loop.rs:1356-1379 has no checksum term and rendering is gated on state.frame_done, which the idle 500 ms poll does not set, so 'Computing…' stays until an input event.

**Fix.** Arc<AtomicBool> cancel flag set in Drop for ChecksumJob and checked once per 4 MiB chunk; refuse non-regular files via metadata().file_type().is_file() before opening; add `|| app.properties.as_ref().is_some_and(|p| p.checksum_job.as_ref().is_some_and(|j| j.get().is_none()))` (or an is_running()) to needs_anim. For slow paths either refuse or keep it (it is explicitly user-requested) but make it cancellable. A read blocked inside jmtpfs cannot be interrupted by the flag, only prevented from continuing.

### cloud-net-10: Token refresh deletes the cached session on ANY HTTP error status, and a real revocation leaves the app stuck with no way to sign in again

- **Where:** `src/cloud/auth.rs:117` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1, C9): src/cloud/auth.rs: a real revocation returns a typed AuthStop::Revoked (found again with auth::stop_reason through any error context) and removes the session file only if it still holds the rejected refresh token. src/cloud/engine.rs / sync.rs: the engine stops on it instead of re-posting the dead token every 30 s; SyncHandle::needs_sign_in() becomes true and problem() is SignInRequired. The stuck-signed-out state is gone. poll_cloud checks SyncHandle::needs_sign_in(); when true, cloud_ended() drops app.cloud and app.cloud_sync, closes cloud dialogs and calls init_cloud() (which starts sync for another account if one signed in). Otherwise the pill says 'Cloud sync stopped: sign in again' (revoked: loud, every folder) or 'Sign in to sync' (signed out: inside ~/Cloud); clicking it, the Cloud button or the sidebar entry opens the login dialog with the last email filled in. After a successful sign-in poll_signin (src/app/background.rs) calls cloud_signed_in(), which starts the engine again. A session that still carries the rejected refresh token is never restarted (dead_token guard), so a file that could not be removed cannot cause a respawn loop. A window that is not signed in looks for a session file every 2 s, so a sign-in from another window or --cloud-login starts sync there too.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-24

**What is wrong.** The status code is ignored: a 500/503 from securetoken, a 429 (QUOTA_EXCEEDED on the token endpoint), or a 403 for an API-key restriction all delete ~/.lantern/config/fox-cloud-session.json. A long-lived window re-creates the file on its next successful refresh, but a short-lived picker process that hits a transient error deletes the session and exits, so the user is signed out on the next launch. This is the '429 means quota, not disconnect' class again. In the genuinely revoked case (password changed, account disabled) the opposite problem appears: only the file is removed; App.cloud stays Some and the thread keeps its in-memory session, so open_cloud_or_login() keeps navigating to ~/Cloud instead of showing the login dialog, and the sync thread re-POSTs the dead refresh token every 30 s forever, logging a failure each time. Only quitting and relaunching Fox brings the login dialog back.

**Trigger.** (a) securetoken returns a 5xx/429 at the moment a file-picker Fox starts with an id_token older than about 58 minutes: next Fox launch shows the login dialog. (b) Change the account password elsewhere: Fox shows 'Sync error' permanently, the Cloud button never offers sign-in, and fox-cloud.log grows by a line every 30 s.

```rust
Err(ureq::Error::Status(_code, r)) => {
    let body = r.into_string().unwrap_or_default();
    // Refresh token revoked / invalid — drop the cached session.
    Session::forget();
    anyhow::bail!("token refresh failed: {}", map_err_body(&body));
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** refresh() binds the status as _code and calls Session::forget() for every HTTP error (117-121). Both callers (ensure_fresh via Authed::token, force_refresh after a 401) propagate only the error. Since init_cloud() runs in every Fox process including pick mode and desktop mode (wayland.rs:441), a short-lived picker that hits a 5xx/429 from securetoken deletes the session file and exits; a long-lived process re-saves it on its next successful refresh (auth.rs:131). Revoked case also traced: only the file is removed; App.cloud/cloud_sync are set only in init_cloud and never cleared (grep), open_cloud_or_login checks self.cloud.is_some(), and the thread keeps the expired in-memory session so every 30 s poll re-POSTs the refresh token and logs a failure.

**Fix.** Minimal: forget only when status is 400/401 and the Firebase message is one of TOKEN_EXPIRED, INVALID_REFRESH_TOKEN, USER_DISABLED, USER_NOT_FOUND, INVALID_GRANT; everything else is transient (and 429 should feed the existing backoff). That part is local. The revoked-case repair (a SignedOut status that stops run_loop and makes the UI clear cloud/cloud_sync so the login dialog opens) touches the sync thread and UI state, so the whole fix is not mechanical.

### cloud-net-12: Delta sync depends on client wall clocks: one future-stamped write blinds delta pulls on both machines

- **Where:** `src/cloud/firestore.rs:72` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): src/cloud/firestore.rs: every doc write is now a documents:commit whose updated_at is set by a server-side field transform (REQUEST_TIME); the client never sends a stamp. updated_at is read back as a Firestore timestamp, kept as microseconds (new src/cloud/stamp.rs: RFC 3339 <-> micros). src/cloud/remote_index.rs: the cursor is the server's readTime of the last pull, not a doc stamp and not this machine's clock. The machines' wall clocks no longer take part in what is written or asked; they are only used locally (when the next full list is due, retry waits, the held-deletions answer TTL).

**What is wrong.** The file header says updated_at is a server-side timestamp, but it is the uploader's local clock in ms, and the cursor is the maximum stamp ever seen (it only increases and is persisted). If one machine writes while its clock is ahead by more than OVERLAP_MS (5 min) — RTC in local time on a dual-boot, a boot before NTP has synced, a dead RTC battery — the cursor on every machine jumps to that future value. From then on `updated_at > cursor - 5 min` excludes every correctly stamped write until real time catches up with the bad stamp, so changes from the other machine are only picked up by the 6-hourly full list or a Fox restart. During that window the reconciler works from a stale index, which also widens the lost-update race in put(). A full list does not repair it because seed_full re-derives the same maximum.

**Trigger.** Boot the laptop with its clock an hour fast, save one file in ~/Cloud before NTP corrects it. For the next hour edits made on the PC take up to 6 hours to appear on the laptop, and vice versa, with the pill showing 'Synced'.

```rust
let stamp = doc.updated_at.unwrap_or_else(now_ms);
...
"updated_at": { "integerValue": stamp.to_string() },
```

**Skeptic's check.** to_fields stamps updated_at with the local now_ms() (72); the header comment at line 12 calling it server-side is wrong. RemoteIndex::bump_cursor keeps the max stamp ever seen with no clamp to local time, seed_full re-derives the same max, and sync_pass queries updated_at > cursor - 300,000 ms. A write stamped more than 5 min ahead therefore hides all correctly stamped later writes from delta pulls on every machine until wall time catches up; only the 6-hourly/start-up full list sees them. The mirror case also holds: a machine whose clock is more than 5 min behind writes stamps below an active peer's cursor and is missed the same way. Traced in code; reachable only with a clock off by more than 5 minutes at write time (no evidence of that on these machines: stored cursor is in the past), hence medium not high.

**Fix.** Either let the server stamp the field (documents:commit with a fieldTransform setToServerValue REQUEST_TIME, query on that timestamp) or stop deriving the cursor from document stamps and use a local pull watermark; at minimum clamp cursor_ms to now_ms() in bump_cursor and when loading the index. All of these change sync semantics and the on-wire schema both machines must agree on.

### cloud-e2e-9: Remote writes are unconditional PATCHes against a snapshot taken at pass start: concurrent edits are silently overwritten and a delete beats a newer edit

- **Where:** `src/cloud/firestore.rs:115` (Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category race; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): firestore::put / firestore::delete send a currentDocument precondition (updateTime of the version the mirror holds, or exists=false). FileDoc gained `version` (Firestore updateTime, verbatim). src/cloud/actions.rs put_doc: on a refusal the doc is fetched afresh into the mirror (RemoteIndex::refresh) and the path is reported as Done::RemoteMoved (counted as deferred, not as a failure); the engine looks again after the debounce time. A tombstone is conditional on the live version the plan saw, so a delete cannot beat a newer edit; an upload over a changed doc becomes a conflict copy on the next pass. A refusal with an unchanged doc is turned into a visible per-path failure instead of a silent loop. Tests: pass_tests/conflicts.rs (7), engine_more.rs::an_edit_made_by_the_other_machine_mid_pass_ends_as_a_conflict_copy_not_a_lost_edit.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-net-13

**What is wrong.** Scenario 1. reconcile_with decides from index.docs as fetched before the local scan and before all earlier uploads/downloads of the same pass; put() carries no currentDocument precondition. If the other machine uploads H2 for a file after this machine's fetch, this machine still sees R == M and takes arm 3b (upload H1) or 4a (tombstone), replacing the H2 doc. The other machine then sees L == M == H2 with R changed and takes 3c (downloads H1 over its edit) or 5a (deletes its freshly edited file). No conflict copy is made in either direction. The window is the whole pass, i.e. minutes when a large file (there is a 538 MB one) or a backlog is being transferred.

**Trigger.** While the PC is in a long pass (first sync, big upload, backlog after being offline), edit file F on the laptop and also have a pending edit or delete of F on the PC. When the PC reaches F its stale snapshot wins; 30 s later the laptop's version of F is replaced or removed with no "(conflict from ...)" file.

```rust
pub fn put(authed: &Authed, doc: &FileDoc) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let url = doc_url(authed, &doc.path);
    let body = serde_json::json!({ "fields": to_fields(doc) });
    authed.patch_json(&url, body)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** put() is a bare PATCH (no currentDocument precondition, no updateMask; patch_json adds only the bearer). reconcile_with decides every path from the index snapshot fetched before the local scan and never re-fetches. If the other machine writes H2 after this machine's fetch, arm 3b (upload) or 4a (tombstone) still fires on R==M and replaces the doc; the other machine then has L==M==H2 with R changed and takes 3c (download over its edit) or 5a (delete). No conflict copy on either side. Window is the whole pass (minutes during bulk transfers; the index has a 538 MB file). Needs edits to the same file on both machines inside that window, so uncommon for one user, but the code path is as described.

**Fix.** Keep each doc's updateTime from list/runQuery in FileDoc and PATCH with currentDocument.updateTime=<seen> (currentDocument.exists=false for new docs); treat 409/412 as 'remote changed', re-pull that doc and re-run the decision next pass. Changes sync semantics and the FileDoc/index schema, and failed-precondition must not be counted as a pass failure (see the full_due retry loop in extra findings).

### cloud-e2e-15: Manifest and remote index are not scoped to the signed-in account, logout does not stop running sync threads, and a token refresh re-saves the forgotten session

- **Where:** `src/cloud/manifest.rs:39` (Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1): src/cloud/manifest.rs and remote_index.rs carry the uid; a state file of another uid (or without one) is renamed to manifest.json.other-account / remote-index.json.other-account, never reused, and that pass holds deletions in both directions. src/cloud/session.rs: SessionWatch — owner and follower threads check the session file every loop turn (250 ms / 500 ms) and before every file of a pass, and stop when it is gone or names another account. Session::save_refreshed only writes over the session it refreshed, under a short flock shared with Session::forget, so a refresh that finishes after a sign-out cannot bring the file back; auth::refresh does not even contact securetoken once the file is gone.

**What is wrong.** Scenario 10. Signing out only deletes fox-cloud-session.json. Running Fox processes keep their in-memory Session, keep syncing, and at the next hourly refresh auth::refresh writes the session file back, so the logout is undone. manifest.json and remote-index.json (including cursor_ms) survive a logout and carry no uid. When a different account signs in on the same ~/Cloud, account A's pivots are applied to account B's remote: a path that exists in both with different content hits arm 3c (local == manifest, remote != manifest) and B's version overwrites the local file with no conflict copy; a tombstone in B at a path A had synced hits 5a and the local file is deleted; files A had in its manifest but that are absent locally and present in B with the same sha are tombstoned in B.

**Trigger.** Run `lntrn-file-manager --cloud-logout`, then `--cloud-login --email other@x` (or the login dialog) with the old ~/Cloud contents still present, and start Fox. Local files whose paths also exist in the second account are replaced or deleted without a conflict file. If a Fox window was open during the logout, the first account is signed in again within the hour.

```rust
home.join(".lantern/cloud/manifest.json")      // same file for every uid
// main.rs:358
crate::cloud::Session::forget();
// auth.rs:131
session.save()?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** --cloud-logout (main.rs:357-360) only removes the session file. Running processes keep their in-memory Session; SyncHandle._stop is constructed and never stored to (grep), and auth::refresh ends with session.save() (auth.rs:131), so any live Fox (including the daemonised --desktop instance, which also runs init_cloud) rewrites the file at its next hourly refresh. manifest.json and remote-index.json contain no uid and are not removed, so a different account signing in inherits account A's pivots and cursor; arms 3c/5a then overwrite or delete local files without a conflict copy. Account switching is rare for a single user; the logout-being-undone part is the one that will actually be seen.

**Fix.** Store uid in manifest.json and remote-index.json and discard both on mismatch at load; have --cloud-logout remove them too. In auth::refresh skip save (and signal the thread to stop) when the session file is gone or holds a different uid. Touches state-file formats and thread lifetime.

### cloud-core-9: Manifest is written non-atomically, a corrupt one is silently replaced by an empty one, and progress is only persisted at pass end

- **Where:** `src/cloud/manifest.rs:54` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1): Remaining half. src/cloud/manifest.rs load_from(): an unreadable or unparsable manifest is renamed to manifest.json.corrupt, logged, and replaced by an empty one for this uid; reconcile.rs runs that pass with every deletion held (remote tombstones held, local deletes deferred). Progress is now also saved every 5 s during a pass, not only at the end.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-14, cloud-net-14

**What is wrong.** std::fs::write truncates then writes, twice per pass, every 30 s, in every Fox process, with no fsync or rename. A kill, power loss, full disk or concurrent writer during that window leaves an empty or broken file. load() then returns an empty manifest without logging, and the pass start (reconcile.rs:306) overwrites the damaged file. With no pivots: every file that differs between the machines becomes a conflict (local renamed to a "(conflict from ...)" copy and the remote version pulled into its place), files deleted on the other machine but still present locally are re-uploaded, and files deleted locally are re-downloaded. Because the manifest is only saved at the end of a pass (line 437), closing Fox during a long pass discards all recorded progress, which is how the 168 stale entries above came about. remote_index.rs:44 and :54 have the same pattern (less harmful, a full list reseeds it).

**Trigger.** Power loss, kill or disk-full while Fox is running (the write happens at least every 30 s), or two Fox processes saving different-length manifests at once. On next start deleted files reappear on both machines and edited files sprout conflict copies.

```rust
Ok(s) => serde_json::from_str(&s).unwrap_or_default(),
...
std::fs::write(&path, serde_json::to_string_pretty(self)?)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** Manifest::save is create_dir_all + std::fs::write (truncate then write), called at reconcile.rs:306 and :437 on every pass; load() maps any parse error to Manifest::default() with no log (line 44). RemoteIndex has the same pattern (remote_index.rs:44, :54) and Session::save too. Aggravating fact I verified: init_cloud() is called unconditionally in wayland::run (wayland.rs:441), so every Fox process (windows, file pickers, the --desktop daemon) runs its own sync thread against the same files, each loading at pass start and saving twice per pass; a load that lands between another process's truncate and write, or a kill/power loss in that window, yields an empty manifest. With no pivots the table takes 2b (re-upload over tombstones: the index has 4,198 of them), the keep-both conflict arm for any differing file, and arm 2 re-downloads. The exposure per pass is small, the mechanism is certain.

**Fix.** Safe, mechanical part: write to a temp file in the same directory, fsync, rename over the target, for manifest.json, remote-index.json and the session file (create the session temp with mode 0600). Use a per-process temp name (include the pid): several Fox processes save concurrently and a shared 'manifest.json.tmp' would interleave. Not mechanical and worth a separate decision: on parse failure keep the bad file as .corrupt, log, and run that pass without tombstone-revive/conflict-rename; saving progress during the pass; and a cross-process lock so only one Fox syncs (see extra findings).

### cloud-core-10: Local scan errors are treated as deletions: an unreadable directory or file is tombstoned remotely and deleted on the other machine

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:49` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1, C9): Same change as cloud-e2e-1 (src/cloud/scan.rs, reconcile.rs action loop): read_dir errors, mid-listing entry errors and stat/hash failures mark the path or the whole subtree unknown instead of absent; the action loop skips unknown paths; a pass with any read failure propagates no deletion unasked. Each distinct scan problem is logged once (not every 30 s), and the count is in SyncReport::unreadable. Same dialog and pill as cloud-e2e-1 cover the unreadable-folder cases: HoldReason::ScanFailed is worded 'Part of the folder could not be read, so sync cannot be sure what was deleted'; SyncProblem::FolderUnreadable / FolderMissing give a loud pill in every folder ('Cloud folder cannot be read, sync paused' / 'Cloud folder is missing, sync paused') whose click opens a details dialog saying nothing was deleted; SyncProblem::Unreadable shows 'N items could not be read' on the pill inside ~/Cloud with the names in the details dialog. App::new (src/app/mod.rs) and open_cloud_or_login no longer re-create a missing ~/Cloud while a sign-in exists; the details dialog offers 'Create folder' instead (which then leads to the held question, never to deletions).

**What is wrong.** A failed read_dir returns silently (not even logged), rd.flatten() drops mid-listing errors, and a failed stat or hash skips the file. All of these leave the path out of `locals`, which the decision table reads as "user deleted it". If the path is in the manifest and unchanged remotely, case 4a tombstones it and the other machine removes its copy with remove_file. The origin keeps its files and re-uploads them once readable again (case 2b), so this is a destructive round trip rather than total loss, but while the directory stays unreadable the other machine's copies are gone. Permission problems inside ~/Cloud are real here: the log has 42,050 "hash ... failed: Permission denied" lines.

**Trigger.** A synced subfolder of ~/Cloud becomes unreadable to the user (root-owned after a sudo copy or chown, chmod 700 by another user, I/O error). Next pass logs "× remote <every file under it>"; the laptop deletes those files.

```rust
let Ok(rd) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) else {
    return;
};
for entry in rd.flatten() {
...
Err(e) => {
    super::log_line(&format!("hash {} failed: {e}", abs.display()));
    continue;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** walk(): a failed read_dir returns silently (49-51), rd.flatten() drops per-entry errors, and file_type/metadata/hash failures `continue`. Anything missing from `locals` that has a manifest entry and an unchanged live remote doc matches arm 4a -> tombstone_remote; the other machine then has L==M with a tombstone and takes 5a -> remove_file. So any read error on a directory (EACCES after a sudo copy, EIO, EMFILE in a process that also runs thumbnails and watchers) is published as a deletion. The origin keeps its files and re-uploads via 2b once readable, so it is a destructive round trip rather than permanent loss, unless the origin's copy is the one that is failing. Same class: reconcile_with does create_dir_all(root) first, so a moved/renamed ~/Cloud becomes an empty root and tombstones every synced file. No unreadable directory exists in ~/Cloud right now (checked); the log shows permission failures do occur there.

**Fix.** Make walk return a Result and abort the pass when read_dir (or a mid-listing entry) fails; for per-file stat/hash failures record the path in an `unknown` set and skip it in the action loop instead of treating it as absent. A perpetually unreadable directory then blocks all sync until fixed, which is a behaviour choice (alternative: mark the whole prefix unknown and continue elsewhere), and aborted passes must not feed the full_due retry loop. Consider a guard that refuses to tombstone more than a fraction of the manifest in one pass.

### cloud-core-18: The .git ignore only matches at the root of ~/Cloud: a repository in a subfolder has its .git internals synced

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:106` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Ignore rules match .git/.syncthing at any depth, and ignored paths are skipped in the action loop so they are never tombstoned.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-25

**What is wrong.** rel is relative to ~/Cloud, so starts_with(".git/") only matches ~/Cloud/.git. For ~/Cloud/projects/foo/.git/... nothing is ignored: index, refs, lock files and objects are uploaded on every git operation (and every git status that refreshes the index), and the two machines' differing index and refs are merged file by file, producing "(conflict from ...)" files inside .git and an inconsistent repository on both sides.

**Trigger.** Put or clone a git repository in a subfolder of ~/Cloud and use it on both machines. Git reports a corrupt index or missing refs, and conflict copies appear under .git/.

```rust
const IGNORE_PREFIXES: &[&str] = &[".git/", ".syncthing/"];
...
if IGNORE_PREFIXES.iter().any(|p| rel.starts_with(p)) {
```

**Skeptic's check.** should_ignore tests `rel.starts_with(".git/")` where rel is relative to ~/Cloud, so only ~/Cloud/.git is skipped; projects/foo/.git/** is scanned, hashed, uploaded and merged file by file, including index/HEAD/refs and lock files, with conflict copies possible inside .git. No other .git check exists. Latent today: the index has 0 paths containing '/.git/'.

**Fix.** Match on components: `rel.split('/').any(|c| c == ".git" || c == ".syncthing")`. Hazard that applies to every new ignore rule (also the .fox-tmp one): should_ignore is only consulted in the local scan, so a path that is already in the manifest/remote index and becomes ignored is read as 'locally deleted' and tombstoned via 4a, deleting it on the other machine. Add `if should_ignore(path) { continue; }` at the top of the action loop over all_paths so ignored paths are inert on all three sides. Nothing matches in this machine's index today.

### cloud-core-13: Upload sends bytes re-read from disk under the scan-time hash, downloads are not verified, and the debounce does not wait for writes to settle

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:172` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): New src/cloud/transfer.rs. Upload: BlobSource/VerifiedReader re-hash the file while it is streamed and hold the last bytes back until the whole file has hashed to the scan's sha; on a mismatch the reader fails, the request ends short of its Content-Length, nothing is stored and the path is deferred to the next pass. Download: streamed into the temp file through HashingWriter, compared with doc.sha256 before the rename; a mismatch removes the temp file and is a failure (then backed off). Debounce: A1's settle logic kept; in addition src/cloud/scan.rs does not hash a file written to within the last second (Timing::busy_window), treats it as unknown for that pass, and the engine looks again with a growing wait.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-11

**What is wrong.** local.sha was computed during the scan; upload_local reads the file again later and stores whatever it finds under that hash, with the scan-time size in the doc. If the file changed in between, the content-addressed blob holds bytes that do not match its name. download_remote writes the blob and records doc.sha256 in the manifest and stat cache without hashing the bytes, so the other machine considers the wrong content in sync. This is made common by the loop at sync.rs:146-154: the comment says it waits for events to settle, but it sleeps 1.5 s once and proceeds even if events are still arriving. A large file still being copied or downloaded into ~/Cloud is hashed, read whole and uploaded as a partial file on each pass until it stops changing; each partial blob stays in Storage forever.

**Trigger.** Copy a multi-GB file into ~/Cloud or let a browser download into it. About 2 s after the first byte Fox uploads a truncated version, the other machine downloads it, and this repeats until the copy finishes. Storage fills with partial blobs.

```rust
let bytes = std::fs::read(&local.abs)?;
let mime = mime_for(&local.rel);
storage::upload_blob(authed, &local.sha, &bytes, &mime)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** local.sha/size/mtime come from walk(); upload_local re-reads the file later (172) and stores those bytes at blobs/{scan sha} with the scan-time size, with no hash of what was read. download_remote writes whatever arrives and records doc.sha256 in manifest and stat cache unverified (193-215). The debounce in sync.rs:146-154 is a single 1.5 s sleep followed by a drain, not a wait-until-quiet loop, so a file still being copied into ~/Cloud is hashed mid-write and uploaded as a different partial snapshot. The state converges once the file stops changing (a final pass hashes and reads the same bytes), so the lasting damage is transient wrong content on the other machine, wasted transfers and orphan/mislabelled blobs in Storage (the log has 1,376 Storage uploads rejected with 402).

**Fix.** Hash the bytes actually read in upload_local (ideally while streaming) and skip the file this pass on mismatch; hash downloaded bytes before the rename and reject a mismatch; make the debounce loop until no events arrive for the window, with an upper bound. Not marked safe: verify-on-download turns any blob already stored under a wrong sha (which this bug can have produced) into a permanently failing path, and a permanently failing pass currently keeps status at Error and triggers the full_due retry loop, so it needs a recovery rule (e.g. ask the uploader to re-put, or treat as missing blob).

### cloud-core-17: Whole files are held in memory for upload and download, and a failing upload re-sends the entire blob on every retry

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:172` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): src/cloud/http.rs: Authed::post_stream sends from a reader with an explicit Content-Length (re-opened for the 401 retry); src/cloud/storage.rs upload_blob / download_blob stream in both directions (Store::upload_blob takes a BlobSource, Store::download_blob takes a writer). No file is held in memory any more. Re-sending on every retry is bounded by the per-path back-off (cloud-net-7) and the existing stop-on-quota.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-20

**What is wrong.** hash.rs streams, but upload_local reads the full file into a Vec and storage::download_blob does read_to_end into a Vec before writing. Fox's memory grows by the file size (the largest file in ~/Cloud today is 513 MiB) inside the file-manager process. upload_local also always uploads the blob before the Firestore PATCH, with no check that the blob already exists and no early abort of the pass on a quota error. When the PATCH or the upload keeps failing (the log has 1376 files failing with status code 402 in one pass), every file is read and POSTed again in full on each retry, every 30 s for non-429 errors.

**Trigger.** Drop a multi-GB video into ~/Cloud: Fox allocates that much RAM during the upload and again on the other machine during download; on a small-memory laptop it is OOM-killed. With Storage over quota, Fox re-uploads everything pending every 30 s.

```rust
let bytes = std::fs::read(&local.abs)?;
...
let bytes = storage::download_blob(authed, &doc.sha256)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** upload_local does std::fs::read of the whole file (172) and put_bytes sends the slice; storage::download_blob does read_to_end into a Vec (storage.rs:31-32) before download_remote writes it. No size check exists in src/cloud (the 1 GiB limit lives only in storage.rules). The sync thread is in the file-manager process, so RSS grows by the file size (largest doc today: 538,409,526 bytes) and an allocation failure or OOM kill takes Fox down. The retry part overlaps cloud-net-7.

**Fix.** Stream both ways: upload with a File reader and explicit Content-Length (hashing while reading, which also fixes cloud-core-13), download with io::copy from into_reader() into the temp file while hashing. The 401-retry wrapper in put_bytes has to reopen/seek the reader for the second attempt. Changes the transport layer, so not mechanical.

### cloud-e2e-13: Failed downloads leave *.fox-tmp files that are then uploaded as real files, and a failing pass re-triggers itself through its own inotify events

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:198` (Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1): New src/cloud/watch.rs: a watcher event only marks the tree dirty when at least one of its paths is not ignored by sync (download temp files, *.lntrn-tmp, editor backups, .git internals) and it is not a read-only access event. src/cloud/engine.rs: a pass that ended in an error sets a not-before time (30 s doubling to 5 min; quota errors keep their 10 min to 2 h pause); local changes are remembered and synced when it lifts. The debounce now waits until the tree has been quiet for 1.5 s (at most 20 s).
- **Also reported as:** cloud-net-15, cloud-core-14

**What is wrong.** The temp file lives inside the watched, scanned tree and should_ignore() does not exclude it. If write fails part-way (ENOSPC), rename fails (the target is a directory because the other machine replaced a folder by a same-named file, or an empty folder left behind by an earlier remote delete is in the way), or the process is killed between the two calls (pick-mode Fox exiting), the temp file stays and the next scan uploads it as a brand-new file that the other machine downloads. Worse, writing the temp file generates inotify events on ~/Cloud; the loop in sync.rs:146-154 turns them into pending_dirty right after the Outcome::Err arm cleared it, so the same failing download is retried about every 2 s: one delta query plus one full blob download per iteration, indefinitely.

**Trigger.** On the PC delete folder ~/Cloud/notes and create a file named notes. On the laptop (where the emptied directory notes/ still exists, since directories are never removed) each pass downloads the blob, writes "notes..fox-tmp", fails the rename with EISDIR, uploads the temp file, and immediately starts over; status flickers between "Syncing..." and "Sync error" and Storage egress and Firestore reads are consumed continuously. A full disk on the downloading machine triggers the same loop.

```rust
let tmp = abs.with_extension(format!(
    "{}.fox-tmp",
    abs.extension().and_then(|e| e.to_str()).unwrap_or("")
));
std::fs::write(&tmp, &bytes)?;
std::fs::rename(&tmp, &abs)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** download_remote writes `<name>.<ext>.fox-tmp` (or `<name>..fox-tmp` for extension-less names; confirmed with rustc) inside ~/Cloud, then renames; neither error path removes it and should_ignore has no rule for it, so a leftover is uploaded by arm 1 on the next scan. rename(file, existing directory) returns EISDIR (rustc test), and delete_local never removes directories, so a folder replaced by a same-named file on the other machine fails permanently. Retrigger loop traced in sync.rs: the temp write emits inotify events during the pass; Outcome::Err clears pending_dirty (193), but the queued events are read by the next recv_timeout, set pending_dirty again, and after the 1.5 s debounce the same failing download (delta query + full blob GET) runs again, indefinitely. Pick-mode Fox processes also run sync and can be killed between write and rename. No .fox-tmp files or docs exist on this machine now.

**Fix.** Remove the temp on every error path; add the suffix to should_ignore together with the action-loop guard described under cloud-core-18 (an already-synced *.fox-tmp doc would otherwise be tombstoned); use a hidden per-process temp name. Ignoring the name alone does not stop the loop: the watcher still sees the write. Either filter watcher events whose paths are all temp files, or add a not-before time after Outcome::Err. Do not simply drain fs_rx after a pass, that discards genuine user changes made during the pass until the next poll. Loop-logic change, so not mechanical.

### cloud-e2e-10: download_remote and delete_local act on a scan snapshot without re-checking the file: local edits made during a pass are overwritten or deleted

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:203` (Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1): src/cloud/scan.rs unchanged_since_scan(): compares size, mtime in ns and inode with the scan snapshot, plus a re-hash for files up to 8 MiB. src/cloud/reconcile.rs: download_remote checks before the transfer and again right before the rename; delete_local checks before moving the file to the Trash; resolve_conflict and upload_local check before using the scan's hash; tombstone_remote and the stale-pivot cleanup check that no file appeared. A mismatch is a non-error 'deferred' outcome: nothing is touched, the stat-cache entry is dropped, the next pass decides from a fresh scan. A folder sitting where a file must be downloaded is reported as a failure instead (it would defer forever).
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-11

**What is wrong.** Arms 3c (remote changed only) and 5a (remote tombstone, local clean) are chosen from `locals`, hashed at the start of the pass. All paths are then processed sequentially with network I/O in between, so the decision can be minutes old when it is executed. Neither function re-stats the file, so a save made by the user in that window is replaced by the remote version or unlinked, with no conflict copy.

**Trigger.** Laptop comes online with a backlog (dozens of remote changes or large downloads). While the pass runs, open and save one of the not-yet-processed files in an editor. When the pass reaches it the saved content is overwritten by the remote version (or the file is removed if the remote is a tombstone).

```rust
std::fs::write(&tmp, &bytes)?;
std::fs::rename(&tmp, &abs)?;
...
if abs.exists() {
    std::fs::remove_file(&abs)?;      // delete_local, 222-224
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Arms 2, 3c, 4b call download_remote and 5a calls delete_local based on `locals` built once at the start of reconcile_with. All paths are then processed sequentially with network I/O in between; download_remote renames over whatever is at the path now (203) and delete_local removes it if it exists (222-224). There is no re-stat, re-hash or lock between scan and action, so a save made after the scan is replaced or unlinked with no conflict copy. In steady state a pass is short; with a backlog or large transfers the window is minutes.

**Fix.** Pass the LocalFile snapshot (or None) into both functions and re-stat immediately before rename/remove; if size or mtime (compare in ns) differ, or a file now exists where the snapshot had none, skip the path so the next scan sees it as changed. Low risk because it only makes the code act less often, but it changes when overwrites/deletes happen, so I am not flagging it as mechanical. The skip must be a non-error outcome or it will keep the pass in Error.

### cloud-net-16: Tombstone docs and old blobs are never removed: 96% of the Firestore collection is tombstones and every full list pays for them

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:246` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): New src/cloud/gc.rs, run at the end of a complete pass that started from a full list, on the server's clock. Tombstones older than 30 days are deleted with a version precondition, and only when this machine has no file and no pivot left at the path. Blobs: listed (storage::list_blobs), and deleted only if no live doc in the fresh mirror names them AND the blob was last written more than 24 h ago AND a Firestore query run at that moment finds no doc with that sha. A 403 on delete is logged once per process and is not an error state. storage.rules: comments updated (the rule text already split create/update from delete).
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-26, cloud-e2e-17

**What is wrong.** Deletes write a tombstone doc that lives forever, and blobs are content-addressed with no delete path at all (storage.rs has only upload and download; storage.rules would in fact deny a delete because the write rule dereferences request.resource.size, which is null on delete). The current remote index holds 4370 docs of which 4198 are tombstones for 172 live files. list_all is billed per doc, so every Fox start and every 6-hour heal costs 4370 reads instead of 172, remote-index.json is 1.3 MB, and the numbers only grow. Each saved revision of a file also leaves its previous blob in Storage permanently.

**Trigger.** Normal use: copy a project folder into ~/Cloud and delete it again (as happened with 'Ember Nights (copy)', +1205 tombstones on 2026-09-02). Every later Fox launch pays for those docs.

```rust
deleted: true,
updated_at: None, // stamped with now_ms() by firestore::put
};
firestore::put(authed, &doc)?;
manifest.remove(rel);
```

**Skeptic's check.** tombstone_remote writes a deleted:true doc and nothing ever removes it; http.rs has get/post/patch/put_bytes only, no DELETE. I counted the on-disk ~/.lantern/cloud/remote-index.json read-only: 4370 docs, 4198 tombstones, 172 live, file is 1.3 MB. list_all (firestore.rs:163) pages the whole collection, so every full list bills all 4370. The storage.rules claim was not checked (no rules file read) but is not needed for the verdict.

**Fix.** Needs an owner decision on retention: a tombstone purged before an offline machine has seen it makes that machine re-upload the file (arm 6 / arm 1). Reasonable design: purge tombstones older than N days during the periodic full pass on one machine, plus blob GC for hashes no doc references with a grace period. Cheaper interim step that changes no semantics: stop paying for them on startup (see cloud-core-7).

### cloud-e2e-8: Delta cursor depends on client clocks and never decreases: a stamp more than 5 min ahead blinds delta polls, so own uploads are reverted, own deletes resurrected, new files re-uploaded every pass

- **Where:** `src/cloud/remote_index.rs:58` (Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): Same change as cloud-net-12, plus: own writes go into the mirror with the stamp and version the server returned (firestore::put returns Put::Written(doc), actions.rs put_doc records it), so nothing depends on reading an own write back. seed_full sets the cursor from the server time read before the list instead of max-ing with the old value. The mirror file carries a format number; an older-format file is not read (re-list).
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-16

**What is wrong.** updated_at is stamped with the writer's wall clock (firestore.rs:72). The in-memory index is not updated after this machine's own put; correctness relies on the next delta query returning that write. If cursor_ms (set by the other machine's clock, or by one bogus stamp) is more than OVERLAP_MS ahead of this machine's clock, own writes are stamped below `cursor - 5 min` and are never returned. Next pass sees a stale R: after an edit, L == M == H1 but index R == H0, so arm 3c "remote changed only" downloads the OLD version over the file just saved; after a delete, (None, Some(live), None) hits arm 2 and re-downloads the file; a new file hits arm 6 and is re-uploaded (blob + doc write) every 30 s. The other machine does not see the changes either until a 6-hourly full list. cursor_ms is persisted and only ever max()ed, including across seed_full and across an account switch, so one future stamp wedges both machines until remote-index.json is deleted by hand.

**Trigger.** Let the two machines' clocks differ by more than 5 minutes, then save a file in ~/Cloud on the machine that is behind: 30 s later the file content flips back to the previous version. This PC runs no time-sync service (no chronyd/ntpd in any OpenRC runlevel) and its clock was 56 s ahead of an HTTP Date header during this audit, so it is drifting toward the threshold.

```rust
fn bump_cursor(&mut self, doc: &FileDoc) {
    if let Some(ts) = doc.updated_at {
        self.cursor_ms = self.cursor_ms.max(ts);
    }
}
pub fn seed_full(&mut self, docs: Vec<FileDoc>) {
    self.docs.clear();          // cursor_ms is not reset
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced: firestore::to_fields stamps updated_at with the writer's now_ms() (firestore.rs:72). reconcile_with takes remotes immutably, so upload_local/tombstone_remote never update the index; the next pass depends on query_changed_since(cursor - 5 min) returning the own write. cursor_ms is max(updated_at) ever seen, persisted, and not reset by seed_full (65-71). If the cursor is more than 5 min ahead of this machine's clock, the own write is not returned, the index keeps the old sha, and with L == M == new sha arm 3c downloads the old blob over the just-saved file; arm 2 re-downloads a deleted file; arm 6 does not apply (remote exists) but new files are fine. It heals at the next full list (6 h or restart), so it is a temporary revert, not permanent loss. Precondition is clock skew over 5 min between machines or one future-dated stamp; I could not measure the laptop's clock, but no chronyd/ntpd/timesyncd is running on this PC, so drift is unbounded. Rated medium for that precondition.

**Fix.** Correct fix: pass the index mutably into reconcile_with and upsert the exact FileDoc (with its stamp, generated once by the caller rather than inside to_fields) after each successful firestore::put, so local decisions never depend on re-reading own writes. Also recompute cursor_ms from the listed docs in seed_full instead of max-ing with the old value. Touches reconcile/sync signatures and sync behaviour, so not mechanical. Server-side REQUEST_TIME stamps are the long-term answer.

### cloud-net-9: Rate-limit detection is a substring search for "429" in error text that embeds URLs, hashes and file paths

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:244` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Rate-limit detection matches ureq's exact 'status code 429'; quota failures are sorted first in the summary.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-8, cloud-e2e-16

**What is wrong.** http.rs formats errors as `"GET {url}: {e}"` and ureq's Display for both Status and Transport errors prints the URL again (error.rs:214, 229). Those URLs contain the 64-hex blob sha (about a 1.5% chance of containing '429'), the percent-encoded file path (IMG_4290.jpg, 2024-29, ...), the uid and the API key; reconcile_with then concatenates every failing path and error into one string. Any unrelated failure whose text happens to contain '429' is treated as quota exhaustion: sync is paused 10 minutes, doubling to 2 hours, and the pill says 'Sync paused (quota)'. Confirmed in fox-cloud.log: the line 'reconcile failed: 1376 file(s) failed to sync' contains 1376 x 'status code 402', zero 'status code 429', and '429' 35 times inside hashes and paths, and is immediately followed by 'quota exhausted (429) — sync paused 7200s'. The current remote index has 33 paths and 3 hashes containing '429'.

**Trigger.** Any upload/download failure (network blip, 403, 402, 5xx) for a file whose name or sha256 contains '429', or a pass with a few dozen failures. Sync silently stops for 10 minutes to 2 hours and reports a quota problem that does not exist.

```rust
if format!("{e:#}").contains("429") {
    *status.lock().unwrap() = SyncStatus::RateLimited;
    Outcome::RateLimited
```

**Skeptic's check.** classify_failure tests format!("{e:#}").contains("429"). Read ureq 2.12.1 error.rs: Status displays as "<url>: status code N" and Transport prefixes the url too; http.rs wraps with "GET {url}: "; storage URLs carry the 64-hex sha and Firestore doc URLs the encoded path; reconcile_with joins every failure into one string (444-448). In the log, exactly one of the 862 'sync paused' lines follows a 'reconcile failed' line containing no 'status code 429' (the 1376-file 402 batch), confirming a false quota pause. On-disk index: 33 paths and 3 hashes contain '429'.

**Fix.** Minimal mechanical fix: test for the exact ureq text "status code 429" instead of "429". Cleaner: a small typed error from Authed carrying Option<u16> and a downcast in classify_failure, with reconcile_with recording whether any per-file failure was a 429; that touches more code but no behaviour beyond classification.

### fs-17: Exec lines are split on whitespace: quoted paths with spaces, quoted or embedded field codes and flatpak @@ markers launch the wrong command

- **Where:** `src/desktop.rs:215` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): New src/desktop/exec.rs parses Exec per the Desktop Entry spec: value escapes first, then double-quote rules (plus shell-style single quotes as GLib accepts), %f %F %u %U expanded in place with correct argument splitting (a list code makes each file its own argument, embedded codes are filled where they stand, flatpak @@u %U @@ keeps files between the markers), %i -> --icon <Icon>, %c, %k, deprecated codes dropped, %% literal, unknown codes refused. DesktopApp (src/desktop.rs) now keeps the raw Exec plus Terminal, Icon, Path and the file path; clean_exec and the whitespace split are gone. New src/desktop/launch.rs builds the argv and passes it to Command with no shell. All launch sites go through it (src/app/select.rs, src/file_ops.rs, src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs). A launcher that cannot be run (bad Exec, program not found) now shows a message instead of only printing to stderr. Checked against all 112 Exec lines installed on this machine: all parse.

**What is wrong.** clean_exec ignores the Desktop Entry quoting rules and launch_app (line 237) splits the result on whitespace again. `Exec="/opt/My App/app" %U` becomes program `/opt/My` with argument `App/app`, which fails to spawn. `Exec=sh -c "viewer %f"` keeps a literal `%f`. `--file=%f` keeps a literal `%f`. Flatpak exports (`--file-forwarding app @@u %U @@`) keep `@@u @@` with the path appended after the closing marker, so the file is not forwarded into the sandbox. The file is always appended at the end rather than substituted. The failure is only printed to stderr. No injection is possible because the path is passed as its own argv entry.

**Trigger.** Install an AppImage or game whose .desktop has a quoted Exec path containing a space and a MimeType, right-click a matching file, Open With, choose it: nothing happens. This machine already has such Exec lines ("/home/alva/Ember Nights/…/run.sh"), though none currently declares a MimeType that Fox maps.

```rust
for token in exec.split_whitespace() {
    if token.starts_with('%') && token.len() <= 2 {
        continue; // skip %f, %F, %u, %U, etc.
    }
    let t = token.trim_matches('"');
    parts.push(t.to_string());
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** clean_exec (213-226) splits on whitespace, drops tokens that are exactly a field code, trims quotes per token and re-joins; launch_app splits on whitespace again (237) and appends the path. So `"/opt/My App/app" %U` runs /opt/My with argument App/app; `--file=%f` and `sh -c "viewer %f"` keep a literal %f; flatpak @@u ... @@ markers stay with the path outside them. Both the Open With menu and the default-open path (click.rs:418, wayland_loop.rs:969, file_ops.rs:729) go through it. No shell is involved, so no injection. Failure is only an eprintln.

**Fix.** Keep the raw Exec in DesktopApp and tokenise once at launch following the spec (double quotes, backslash escapes inside quotes, %% literal), substituting %f/%u with the path and %F/%U with the list, dropping %i/%c/%k, appending the path only when no file code was present; pass a Vec to Command. Changes DesktopApp and all five call sites.

### fileops-28: Deferred copy targets are not reserved: "Keep Both" name can collide with a later source in the same paste

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:229` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Deferred targets are reserved per paste; a second item mapping to a claimed name gets a Keep Both name.

**What is wrong.** In Copy mode and for cross-device Cut the copies run later, so uniqueness is checked against a filesystem that does not yet contain earlier resolutions. If the batch contains both 'a.txt' and 'a (2).txt' and the destination has only 'a.txt', Keep Both maps a.txt -> 'a (2).txt' and the second source maps to the same 'a (2).txt' with no conflict detected. The worker writes one over the other; for a cross-device move both sources pass the size check and are deleted, so one file is lost outright.

**Trigger.** Cut 'a.txt' and 'a (2).txt' on a USB stick/phone and paste into a folder on disk that already holds 'a.txt'; choose Keep Both. Only one of the two files survives.

```rust
Some(ConflictAction::KeepBoth) => {
    (crate::conflict::unique_keep_both_path(&target), false)
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Conflict detection is `target.exists()` (217) and unique_keep_both_path (conflict.rs:116-136) probes the filesystem only. In Copy mode and for cross-device Cut the writes are deferred, so an earlier resolution is invisible: with sources 'a.txt' and 'a (2).txt' and a destination holding 'a.txt', Keep Both maps the first to 'a (2).txt' and the second resolves to the same path with no conflict. run_copy then writes one over the other, and for a cross-device move copy_landed passes for both, so both sources are deleted and one file is gone. Narrow trigger, real loss on the move path.

**Fix.** Keep a set of targets already assigned in this PendingPaste (resolved_pairs plus xdev_pairs) and treat membership as 'exists' both in the conflict test and inside unique_keep_both_path (pass the set in). Local to file_ops.rs and conflict.rs; only changes the colliding case.

### fileops-18: Starting a second paste/drop while a copy is running overwrites op_progress: the first op is never finalized and cannot be cancelled

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:317` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): App.op_progress (single slot) replaced by App.ops: ops::OpQueue. Copies and cross-device moves run one at a time, later ones wait; same-filesystem moves and local deletes run beside a running copy. Every operation is polled and finalised on its own (undo, sudo fallback, failures, reload). Names an unfinished operation will create count as taken in conflict resolution (OpQueue::is_reserved). sections/status.rs: the strip shows the current operation plus '+N more'; its cancel button stops the one shown.

**What is wrong.** paste(), start_drag_drop and finalize_paste (also line 291 for cross-device moves) never check whether a worker is already active. The old OpHandle is dropped: its thread keeps copying, but its Done event is lost, so no undo entry is pushed, PermissionDenied sources are never sent to sudo, the clipboard is not re-armed, the view is not reloaded, and the cancel button now targets only the new op.

**Trigger.** Paste a large folder; while the strip shows 'Copying', paste or drag-drop something else.

```rust
handle.reload_tab = reload_tab;
self.op_progress = Some(handle);
```

**Skeptic's check.** The only writes to op_progress are finalize_paste lines 291 and 317, both unconditional; grep shows no entry point (key.rs, click.rs, context_menu.rs, wayland_loop.rs:614-624) testing op_progress before starting a paste or drop. Assigning a new handle drops the old one: its thread keeps copying (send errors are ignored), but its Done is never received, so no undo entry, no sudo fallback for perm_fails, no reload_tab, and its cancel flag is unreachable. The status strip shows only the new op.

**Fix.** Either queue handles (VecDeque<OpHandle>, finalise each independently in poll_op_progress) or refuse/defer a new paste while one is active. Both change user-visible behaviour and the progress UI, so not mechanical.

### fileops-19: A finished (or cancelled) copy overwrites the clipboard with its own sources - including for drag-drop copies

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:342` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Clipboard is handed back only after a clipboard paste and only if it is still empty.

**What is wrong.** The 're-arm' is unconditional. Anything the user copied or cut while the worker was running is replaced when Done arrives, so the next Ctrl+V pastes the previous batch (and a pending Cut is silently dropped). Because drag-drop goes through the same PendingPaste path, a drag-drop Copy that never touched the clipboard also replaces it with the dropped paths (also lines 303 and 492).

**Trigger.** Start a long copy. While it runs, select other files and press Ctrl+C (or Ctrl+X). When the copy finishes, Ctrl+V in the target folder pastes the files from the first copy instead.

```rust
self.clipboard = Some(ClipboardOp::Copy(handle.originals));
```

**Skeptic's check.** paste() empties the clipboard with take() (122) and poll_op_progress unconditionally writes ClipboardOp::Copy(handle.originals) when a Copy worker finishes (342); the same unconditional re-arm sits at 303 and 492. Anything copied or cut while the worker ran is replaced, and a pending Cut becomes a Copy of other files. start_drag_drop goes through the same PendingPaste/worker path, so a drag-drop copy that never used the clipboard overwrites it with the dropped paths.

**Fix.** Carry a from_clipboard flag on PendingPaste/OpHandle (true only when created by paste()) and re-arm only when it is set and self.clipboard.is_none(), at all three sites. Local and mechanical. Keep the take() in paste(): it is what currently stops the same clipboard being pasted twice while the worker runs.

### dialogs-20: Keep Both is ignored by the sudo fallback: the privileged copy writes to the original name and overwrites the file the user chose to keep

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:343` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): Same change as fileops-4. The privileged command also refuses an existing target unless the conflict was resolved as Replace, and then the old item is moved to a reserved slot in the home Trash first and moved back if the new one does not arrive (sudo.rs copy_argv / move_argv, tested by running the built scripts without sudo).

**What is wrong.** run_copy records only the source of a pair that failed with PermissionDenied, dropping the resolved target (which for Keep Both is "name (2).ext"). The fallback builds `cp -r -- <src> <dest>/<src file name>` (sudo.rs build_argv), i.e. the original conflicting name, and cp as root overwrites it.

**Trigger.** Copy report.txt into a root-owned directory that already contains report.txt, choose Keep Both. The unprivileged copy to "report (2).txt" fails with EACCES, the sudo path runs (prompt or cached ticket) and replaces the existing report.txt.

```rust
if !perm_fails.is_empty() {
    self.priv_run(crate::sudo::PendingPrivOp::Copy {
        sources: perm_fails,
        dest: handle.dest,
    });
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced: ops::run_copy (ops.rs:193-195) pushes only `src` on PermissionDenied, dropping the resolved target. poll_op_progress (file_ops.rs:343-348) builds PendingPrivOp::Copy{sources, dest}, and sudo::build_argv (sudo.rs:206-219) always emits `cp -r -- <src> <dest>/<src file name>`. So a Keep Both target of `report (2).txt` that failed with EACCES is re-run as root against `report.txt`, the file the user chose to keep. Same shape for Cut: queue_cut (file_ops.rs:81-83) pushes src only and the Move argv is `mv <src> <dest>/<name>`. No guard anywhere. Needs a non-writable destination that already holds the name, so an uncommon flow, but the outcome is the opposite of what was chosen. Not executed, read only.

**Fix.** Carry (src, resolved target) pairs through perm_fails, OpProgress::Done and PendingPrivOp::Copy/Move so the privileged command uses the target the dialog resolved. Proposed fix is right. Adding `-n` is only correct when the user did not pick Replace, so the pair needs a replace flag too. Changes the PendingPrivOp shape and overwrite behaviour, so not a mechanical edit.

### fileops-20: Trash/Duplicate/Compress/Extract/Restore from a nested tree row's context menu do nothing

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:578` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C2): Trash, Delete Permanently and Restore (src/trash_ops.rs), Duplicate and Compress (new src/row_actions.rs) and Extract (src/file_ops.rs) now start from App::selected_paths(), the same source Copy and Cut use, so they act on the nested row the menu was opened on. Duplicate lands next to the item, Compress runs tar in the item's own folder and puts the archive there, Extract unpacks next to the archive. Compress and Extract set a flag when their thread ends so the view re-lists (the watcher does not see inside expanded tree folders). The stale-override hazard named in the finding was already closed in wayland_loop.rs (override cleared whenever the menu is not open, before the keyboard is handled); I checked the ordering and left it.
- **Also reported as:** render-10

**What is wrong.** Right-clicking a nested tree row clears the entries selection and sets context_override_paths (context_menu.rs:399-404), whose doc comment says it exists 'so cut/copy/trash/etc. operate on the clicked path'. Only selected_paths() honours it. trash_selected, delete_selected, restore_selected, duplicate_selected, compress_selected and extract_selected all iterate self.entries directly, find nothing selected, and return without doing anything, although the menu offers all of those actions for the row.

**Trigger.** Tree view, expand a folder, right-click a file inside it -> Move to Trash (or Duplicate / Compress / Extract Here). Nothing happens.

```rust
for entry in &self.entries {
    if !entry.selected {
        continue;
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** handle_right_click's NestedPath branch (context_menu.rs:399-404) calls clear_selection() and stores the row only in context_override_paths. selected_paths() (select.rs:230-239) is the only reader. trash_selected, delete_selected, restore_selected, duplicate_selected, compress_selected and extract_selected all iterate self.entries filtered by .selected (file_ops.rs:520, 578, 676, 788, 841, 888), find nothing, and return after a reload. build_item_menu still offers every one of those actions for the nested row.

**Fix.** Have the six functions start from selected_paths(). Hazard the proposal misses: context_override_paths is only cleared on the next right-click, after a menu Action, or on focus_pane; if the menu is dismissed with Esc or a click outside, the override stays set. Today that already makes Ctrl+C/Ctrl+X act on the stale row (see extra finding). Once trash honours the override, the Delete key would trash that stale path. So clear the override whenever the context menu closes without an action, in the same change. duplicate needs is_dir from metadata and compress must run in the item's parent directory, as the finder says.

### fileops-24: Duplicate copies files synchronously on the UI thread and records an undo entry even when the copy failed

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:830` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** partly fixed. Batch 2 (C1): file_ops.rs duplicate_selected builds OpItems and runs through the worker (label 'Duplicating'): progress, cancel, and the Copy undo entry is pushed at the end for exactly what was created. Root mode duplicates through sudo with exact targets instead of pkexec. Of the other inline I/O the finding lists: the Replace remove_dir_all is gone, delete_selected and empty_trash now run on the worker. REMAINS: The destination probes in advance_paste (lstat/canonicalize of the target, ConflictMeta) and the free-name probe in duplicate_selected still run on the UI thread, also on slow mounts. Moving them off-thread means making the conflict walk asynchronous; that was outside this package's direction and I did not start it.

**What is wrong.** Folders are duplicated on a thread but plain files are copied inline. Duplicating a multi-GB file blocks the window until it finishes; on an MTP mount this is a whole-file download plus upload under jmtpfs's global lock. The result is ignored and the Copy undo entry is pushed regardless. The same thread also runs other unbounded I/O inline: remove_dir_all for Replace (220-226, 456-462), delete_selected (686-697) and empty_trash (653-665), plus exists()/metadata probes of destination paths on slow mounts in advance_paste.

**Trigger.** Right-click a 20 GB video -> Duplicate: Fox freezes (compositor may flag it unresponsive) until the copy completes. Empty a Trash holding a huge source tree: same.

```rust
} else {
    let _ = std::fs::copy(&path, &dest);
}
self.undo_stack.push(crate::undo::UndoAction::Copy {
```

**Skeptic's check.** duplicate_selected threads folder copies (823-828) but copies plain files inline with std::fs::copy on the caller's thread (830), which is the main loop (context menu handler). The result is discarded and the Copy undo entry is pushed unconditionally (832-835), also for the threaded and pkexec branches that may fail later. No is_slow_path check, so on an MTP mount this is a whole-file download and upload on the UI thread. delete_selected, empty_trash and the Replace remove_dir_all calls are likewise inline.

**Fix.** Route duplicate through spawn_copy_worker with (path, dest) pairs so progress, cancel and a truthful undo entry come for free. Note op_progress is a single slot: starting a worker while another runs drops the first handle (see extra finding), so this needs a queue or a guard first. Threading change.

### fileops-26: Root-mode rename/duplicate/compress/extract/undo use pkexec, which has no auth agent in Lantern and changes cwd to /root

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:872` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C3): No pkexec is left in the crate (grep clean). Root-mode rename (src/app/edit.rs perform_rename), Compress (src/row_actions.rs) and Extract Here (src/file_ops.rs extract_as_root) now build `PendingPrivOp::Rename` / `Archive` / `Extract` and go through the same sudo flow as paste and delete. The commands are in new src/sudo/tools.rs: fixed `sh -c` scripts with every path a positional argument, tar told its folder with `-C` and given an absolute archive path (nothing depends on a working directory), the archive written under a staging name and renamed into place, extraction only into a folder `mkdir` has just made, `--` before member names, stdin of the tools closed, `--no-same-owner` for tar as root. A case-only rename on a case-insensitive drive goes through a temporary name and refuses any other item under the new name. Duplicate and undo/redo had already been moved to the sudo flow by C1/C2 (verified; duplicate now uses `root_covers`). The undo entry of a root rename is pushed only once the rename is seen to have happened (record_root_rename, src/priv_ops.rs).

**What is wrong.** Two problems. (1) pkexec chdirs to the target user's home before exec (the binary contains 'Error changing to home directory'), so .current_dir(&dir) is discarded and `tar czf name.tar.gz <relative names>` runs in /root: it fails or archives same-named files from /root and writes the archive there. (2) No polkit authentication agent exists in the Lantern DE tree and none is running on this machine (only polkitd), so pkexec from a DE-launched Fox cannot authenticate: root-mode rename (app/edit.rs:78), duplicate (817), compress, extract (985) and undo/redo (undo.rs) silently do nothing - while perform_rename still pushes a Rename undo entry. Paste/delete/new in root mode use the in-app sudo modal and do work.

**Trigger.** Open as Root on a root-owned folder, F2-rename a file or choose Duplicate/Compress: nothing happens and nothing is reported.

```rust
let _ = std::process::Command::new("pkexec")
    .args(&args)
    .current_dir(&dir)
    .status();
```

**Skeptic's check.** Root-mode rename (edit.rs:74-83), duplicate (file_ops.rs:813-822), compress (869-875), extract (984-987) and undo/redo (undo.rs) all shell out to pkexec. (1) `strings /usr/bin/pkexec` shows `Error changing to home directory %s: %s`: pkexec chdirs to the target user's home before exec, so `.current_dir(&dir)` is discarded and `tar czf <relative archive> <relative names>` runs in /root. That part is certain. (2) On this machine only polkitd is running, no authentication agent process, the binary carries `No authentication agent found`, and a grep of the Lantern-DE tree finds no agent implementation. I could not read /etc/polkit-1/rules.d, so a rule granting passwordless access cannot be excluded. Either way perform_rename pushes a Rename undo entry without knowing the outcome.

**Fix.** Route these through PendingPrivOp/priv_run like paste, delete and new-folder already do (add a Rename op, reuse Copy for duplicate, add Archive/Extract ops), and give tar `-C <dir>` plus an absolute archive path instead of relying on cwd. Push undo entries only after success. Not mechanical.

### fileops-17: Compress passes selected file names to tar without `--`: a file name starting with '-' is executed as a tar option

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:877` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. tar gets -- before the file names.

**What is wrong.** file_args are bare file names (p.file_name()), and GNU tar permutes options, so names such as `--checkpoint=1` and `--checkpoint-action=exec=...` run arbitrary commands, and `--remove-files` deletes the inputs after archiving. Reproduced in the scratchpad: the injected command ran. The root-mode branch (lines 870-875) passes the same list to `pkexec tar`.

**Trigger.** A folder (downloaded/extracted archive, shared USB stick) contains files named '--checkpoint=1' and '--checkpoint-action=exec=sh x.sh'. Select all -> Compress: the command runs as the user.

```rust
let _ = std::process::Command::new("tar")
    .arg("czf")
    .arg(&archive_name)
    .args(&file_args)
    .current_dir(&dir)
    .status();
```

**Skeptic's check.** compress_selected passes bare file names (p.file_name()) straight after the archive name with no `--` (865-882), in both the tar and pkexec tar branches. Reproduced in the scratchpad with the same argv shape: files named `--checkpoint=1` and `--checkpoint-action=exec=touch INJECTED` caused tar to run the command; with `--` inserted the two names were archived as files and nothing ran. Needs the user to compress a folder containing attacker-named files (extracted archive, shared stick), so medium.

**Fix.** Insert `--` before the file arguments in both branches: `tar czf <archive> -- <names>`. Verified it works with the old-style `czf` option bundle. No behaviour change for normal names.

### dialogs-8: Submitting the sudo prompt (and the cached-ticket path) runs the whole privileged command synchronously on the UI thread

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:1065` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C3): sudo never runs on the UI thread any more. `App::priv_run` (moved from file_ops.rs to new src/priv_ops.rs) returns at once; `sudo::run` executes on a 'fox-sudo' thread and the result is polled next to poll_op_progress (`poll_priv`, src/wayland_loop.rs; `priv_busy` keeps the loop animating). This covers the cached-ticket attempt and the password submit. The modal has three phases (Confirm / Password / Working): while commands run it shows 'Working as administrator...' with the operation named (after 250 ms for a cached-ticket run, immediately after a question or password), a wrong password comes back into the password field as 'Incorrect password', and a command failure is reported in a notice dialog naming the operation and sudo's message. The old synchronous SudoPrompt and its drawing were removed from src/dialogs.rs. Also fixed on the way: a second privileged op arriving while one is open used to replace it (leaking its Trash slots); they now queue (`App::priv_queue`). An op of several commands continues where it stopped if the ticket runs out half way instead of repeating finished items, and one failing item no longer stops the rest.
- **Also reported as:** fileops-23

**What is wrong.** run_with_password / try_cached spawn sudo and wait_with_output() for every sub-command (one cp -r or mv per source, or rm -rf) before returning. The loop cannot dispatch or render meanwhile, so the "Authenticating..." state set just above is never drawn, there is no progress and no cancel. priv_run (line 1036) does the same from poll_op_progress when a ticket is cached. A wrong password also stalls for sudo's fail delay.

**Trigger.** Paste a multi-GB folder into a root-owned directory such as /opt. The copy worker reports permission failures, the sudo prompt opens, type the password, Enter: the window is frozen (compositor sees an unresponsive client) for the entire sudo cp -r, possibly minutes.

```rust
if let Some(p) = self.sudo_prompt.as_mut() {
    p.submitting = true;
}
match crate::sudo::run_with_password(&op, &password) {
```

**Skeptic's check.** run_with_sudo (sudo.rs:115-171) spawns sudo per sub-command and blocks in wait_with_output. submit_sudo_prompt (1052-1079) calls run_with_password inline, and priv_run (1034-1047) calls try_cached inline; every caller (trash_selected, delete_selected, finalize_paste, poll_op_progress, new_folder_or_prompt, key and click handlers) is on the main loop. `p.submitting = true` is set at 1063 but no frame can be drawn before the blocking call, so `Authenticating...` never shows. A root cp -r or rm -rf of a large tree freezes the window for its duration with no dispatch. Root operations are an uncommon flow, so medium rather than high.

**Fix.** Run run_with_sudo on a worker thread, return CachedResult over a channel polled next to poll_op_progress, keep the modal in the submitting state and add the pending job to needs_anim so the loop keeps polling. Threading change.

### fs-11: Sidebar drive order is random for same-named drives and drive actions use a stale index: Eject/Format can hit the wrong drive

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:216` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Drive order has a device tie-break; drive context actions carry the device path instead of a list index.

**What is wrong.** detect_drives builds its list from two HashMaps (by_device, by_disk) whose iteration order differs per instance (confirmed: two keys flip order across 1000 fresh maps, 578/422), and the final sort key is only (mounted class, name). Two removable disks of the same vendor/model get the same friendly name, so their relative order is whatever the HashMap produced and changes on the 2 s refresh. Separately, the context menu stores ContextTarget::Drive(i) (context_menu.rs:210) and resolves it only when the item is chosen (lines 741, 746), while refresh_drives keeps replacing app.drives every 2 s because an open menu sets needs_anim. Any reorder, plug or unplug in between makes the index refer to a different drive.

**Trigger.** Plug in two identical USB sticks. Their sidebar rows swap at random every couple of seconds. Right-click one, wait a moment, choose Eject or 'Format to ext4…': the action (and the confirm dialog, which shows the identical name) may be for the other stick. With different drives the same happens if one is plugged or unplugged while the menu is open.

```rust
drives.sort_by(|a, b| {
    ...
    ord(a).cmp(&ord(b)).then_with(|| a.name.cmp(&b.name))
});
```

**Skeptic's check.** detect_mounted_drives collects from a HashMap (by_device) and group_removable_by_disk iterates another (by_disk); the final sort key is only (mounted class, name) (216-228). Removable disks are renamed to vendor+model, so two identical sticks tie and their order is whatever the freshly seeded HashMaps produced on that refresh. Separately, the menu stores ContextTarget::Drive(i) (context_menu.rs:210) and resolves the index only when the item is chosen (741, 746, 751) through app.drives.get(index) (places.rs:134, 151, 161), while refresh_drives replaces app.drives every 2 s whenever frames render, and an open menu sets needs_anim. Any reorder, plug or unplug in between retargets Eject or Format. The confirm dialog clones the Drive, so with identical sticks it shows the same name for the wrong device.

**Fix.** Add `.then_with(|| a.device.cmp(&b.device))` to the sort, and make ContextTarget::Drive carry the device path (or a cloned Drive) and resolve with `drives.iter().find(|d| d.device == dev)` at the three use sites. Both are mechanical and nobody wants the index behaviour. Left-click zone indices have the same weakness but only mount or navigate.

### fs-15: Phone unplugged while mounted leaves a dead jmtpfs mount that blocks reopening it

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:724` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): src/phone_mounts.rs records the USB address a phone had when mounted. A click re-reads the address in the worker: a mount made for another address is detached lazily and remounted; otherwise the mount root is probed (1.5 s; a definite error means dead). The watcher thread detaches jmtpfs mounts whose phone is gone or re-plugged, only after two passes in a row. Not tested with a phone.

**What is wrong.** Nothing unmounts a phone's FUSE mount when the device disappears from detect_phones; Drop only iterates the phones currently plugged in.
