After a re-plug the phone gets the same slug and mount point, is_path_mounted is still true for the stale jmtpfs instance (bound to the old USB handle), so mount_phone returns Ok without mounting and Fox navigates into a mount that returns errors. list_directory turns the error into an empty listing.

**Trigger.** Open the phone in Fox, pull the cable, plug it back in, click the phone again. The sidebar says "Connected" but the folder is empty, and stays that way until `fusermount -u` is run by hand.

```rust
if is_path_mounted(&phone.mount_point) {
    return Ok(());
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** The code is as described: mount_phone returns Ok(()) as soon as is_path_mounted is true (724-726); refresh_phones just replaces the list (places.rs:129-131); Drop only unmounts phones currently in self.phones (app/mod.rs:480-486), so a phone that vanished is never unmounted, and a re-plug gets the same slug and mount point. What is unknown: whether jmtpfs keeps its FUSE mount alive after the USB device disappears and whether that stale mount returns errors or an empty listing. That is jmtpfs's usual behaviour, but it was not tested with hardware.

**Fix.** In refresh_phones, diff old and new lists and lazily unmount (`fusermount -uz`) mount points of phones that vanished; in mount_phone, treat an existing mount whose root read_dir fails as stale, unmount and remount. Both probes must run off the UI thread (a dead FUSE mount can hang on stat). Needs a hardware test.

### fs-13: Format reports failure after busctl's 25 s default timeout while UDisks keeps formatting

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:858` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): src/drive_ops.rs passes --timeout=7200 to busctl, so the call waits for UDisks to finish. If busctl still reports a timeout, format_failure() says the drive is still being formatted instead of 'failed'. I checked that this machine's busctl accepts --timeout on a harmless call; a real format was not run (udisksctl is not installed here).

**What is wrong.** busctl gives a method call 25 seconds unless --timeout is passed. UDisks2 Block.Format only replies when mkfs.ext4 has finished; on a large or slow stick (journal of 256 MB to 1 GB written over USB 2.0) that takes longer. busctl then exits non-zero with a timeout error, Fox shows it in the dialog as a format failure, but the format is still running in udisksd.

**Trigger.** Format a 64 GB USB 2.0 stick from the sidebar. After a 25 s freeze the dialog shows "format: … timed out". The user retries (rejected as busy) or pulls the stick while mkfs is still writing, leaving a broken filesystem.

```rust
let output = std::process::Command::new("busctl")
    .args(&args)
    .output()
    .map_err(|e| format!("spawn busctl: {e}"))?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** The args vector (837-856) has no --timeout, and busctl's documented default for `call` is 25 s. UDisks2 Block.Format replies only when mkfs finishes. On a failed exit Fox stores the stderr as a format error (862-865, places.rs:184-190) while udisksd keeps formatting. What is unknown: whether mkfs.ext4 through UDisks actually exceeds 25 s on the sticks in use (depends on journal size and write speed). Not reproduced; udisksctl is not even installed on this machine.

**Fix.** Pass `--timeout=600` (or similar). One argument, but the call blocks the UI thread (fs-12), so a longer timeout means a longer freeze; do it together with moving format off the main loop, and show progress rather than an error-or-nothing dialog.

### fs-12: Mount, eject and format run synchronously on the render thread

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:925` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Same change as app-23: on_drive_click, on_phone_click, eject_drive and confirm_drive_format (src/app/device_ops.rs) only start a worker task; results are collected in App::idle_tick via src/app/background.rs. Clicks on a busy row are ignored. Errors arrive as queued notices so they cannot replace a dialog that is open by then.

**What is wrong.** eject_drive, on_drive_click, confirm_drive_format and on_phone_click (app/places.rs:137, 111, 179, 201) call unmount_drive, mount_drive, format_drive_ext4 and mount_phone directly from click handlers, each of which waits for a child process. Unmount blocks until the kernel has flushed dirty data to the stick; mount can wait on a polkit prompt or an NTFS check; jmtpfs waits on libmtp opening the device (long USB timeouts when the phone is locked); format waits for mkfs. Fox draws nothing and answers no input for that whole time.

**Trigger.** Copy a few GB to a slow USB stick, then right-click it and choose Eject: the window freezes until the flush completes (tens of seconds to minutes). Clicking a locked phone freezes for the libmtp timeout.

```rust
let output = std::process::Command::new("udisksctl")
    .args(["unmount", "-b", &drive.device])
    .output()
```

**Skeptic's check.** All four call sites are on the main loop with no thread in between: eject_drive -> unmount_drive (places.rs:137), on_drive_click -> mount_drive (111), confirm_drive_format -> format_drive_ext4 (179), on_phone_click -> mount_phone (201). Each waits on a child (`.output()`/`.status()`), and mount_phone additionally sleeps up to 20 x 50 ms (fs.rs:753-758). Unmount blocks until dirty data is flushed, format until mkfs ends, jmtpfs until libmtp opens the device.

**Fix.** Run the four operations on a worker and deliver the Result through a channel polled beside poll_op_progress, with a busy state on the sidebar row; apply the existing follow-ups (refresh_drives, navigate_to, show_message) when the result lands. Threading change.

### fs-18: Every watcher burst triggers a full reload plus a new git scan every ~120 ms, with no cap and no in-flight guard

- **Where:** `src/git_status.rs:58` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): src/dir_watch.rs: new ReloadGate (pure, unit-tested): first reload 120 ms after the first event, then at most one per second, and always one after the last event. The watcher thread only writes the eventfd on the first unprocessed event, so a file being written wakes the loop about 3 times a second instead of once per event. src/git_status.rs rewritten around bg::Task: one scan at a time, a request made meanwhile is remembered (one follow-up, started when the scan lands), the follow-up waits as long as the last scan took (max 5 s) so a slow repository is not scanned back to back; navigation skips that wait. The periodic 5 s rescan goes through the same guard. Not done on purpose: skipping the git scan for pure Modify events (a modified tracked file is exactly what changes its badge).

**What is wrong.** take_due_reload fires 120 ms after the first event and then again for the next event, so a file being written continuously (download, copy, log) reloads the directory about 7 times a second for its whole duration. Each reload re-lists the focused pane, re-lists the split pane, rebuilds the tree in Tree view, and calls git.refresh (wayland_loop.rs:1306), which unconditionally starts a thread running up to three git processes. If `git status` takes longer than the interval (large repo, $HOME as a dotfiles repo), scans overlap without bound; the 5 s timer adds more. Stale results are discarded by generation, but the processes still run to completion.

**Trigger.** View a folder inside a large git repo (or ~/Downloads) while a multi-GB download or copy writes into it. `ps` shows a growing set of concurrent `git status` processes and Fox plus git hold a core busy for the duration.

```rust
std::thread::spawn(move || {
    let (branch, marks) = scan(&dir);
    let _ = tx.send(GitResult {
```

**Skeptic's check.** take_due_reload (dir_watch.rs:109-120) fires 120 ms after the first event of a burst and re-arms on the next event, so sustained writes give a reload about every 120 ms. Each one runs app.reload() (focused pane, split pane, tree rebuild) and git.refresh (wayland_loop.rs:1299-1307). refresh() (git_status.rs:53-66) has no in-flight guard: it bumps the generation and spawns a thread running up to three git processes; stale results are discarded but the processes run to completion, so scans overlap when git status is slower than the interval. The 5 s timer adds more. Only happens while frames render, but reload_pending keeps needs_anim true during a burst.

**Fix.** Keep in_flight and rescan_wanted flags in GitStatus: refresh() spawns only when idle, otherwise marks rescan_wanted, and poll() starts the follow-up when the current scan lands. Cap watcher-driven reloads during a sustained burst (for example one per 500 ms) and skip git.refresh for pure Modify events. Changes scheduling, needs care.

### fs-7: git status runs without GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0: rewrites .git/index and can break the user's own git command

- **Where:** `src/git_status.rs:159` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category race; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. git runs with GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0.

**What is wrong.** Background `git status` takes .git/index.lock and rewrites the index whenever stat data is stale (verified: index mtime changed after Fox's exact command, unchanged with GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0). Fox fires it 120 ms after any file event in the viewed folder and every 5 s. While it holds the lock a foreground git command fails with "Unable to create '.git/index.lock': File exists". The classic watcher feedback loop is not reachable here: the watch is non-recursive so .git/index writes are not seen from the worktree, and inside .git itself `rev-parse --show-toplevel` fails (verified) so no status runs.

**Trigger.** View a repo folder in Fox and run a multi-step git operation in a terminal (rebase, stash pop, checkout of many files). The files changing trigger Fox's reload and `git status` mid-operation; intermittently a rebase step aborts with the index.lock error.

```rust
let out = std::process::Command::new("git")
    .arg("-C")
    .arg(dir)
    .args(args)
    .output()
    .ok()?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** git_stdout sets no environment. Scratchpad check with Fox's status argv: after touching a tracked file, the index mtime was unchanged with GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 and changed without it, so the background status takes index.lock and rewrites .git/index. Fox runs it 120 ms after any file event in the viewed folder and every 5 s in a repo, which is exactly when a terminal rebase or checkout is touching files, so a foreground git step can fail on index.lock. I agree with the finder that the classic feedback loop is not reachable here: the watch is non-recursive on the viewed directory, so .git/index writes are not seen from the worktree, and inside .git itself rev-parse --show-toplevel fails so no status runs.

**Fix.** Add `.env("GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS", "0")` to the Command in git_stdout. One line, no downside for a read-only badge scan.

### media-4: Thumbnail GPU cache is unbounded while the user stays in one directory; the 512 cap is only checked on navigation

- **Where:** `src/icons.rs:56` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C8): New src/icon_store.rs: TextureStore<T>, the map behind IconCache, with a per-texture last-used frame stamp (set by every get / get_or_load through a Cell, so the draw lists' shared borrows still work). The 512-thumbnail cap is enforced when a thumbnail is inserted: least-recently-used thumbnails go, down to 7/8 of the cap, and never one that was looked up in the frame being drawn or the previous drawn frame (so what is on screen is never evicted, even if a window shows more than 512). Inserting a thumbnail for a path removes any other thumbnail key of the same path, so a file rewritten on disk no longer leaks one texture per change. src/icons.rs: IconCache::ensure_dir is replaced by begin_frame([focused dir, other pane's dir]); a split-view focus swap shows the same set of folders and is no longer treated as navigation (no purge, no retry of every failed file); on a real navigation only failures outside the folders now shown are forgotten. Folder icons / SVGs are not counted and never evicted. src/render.rs calls begin_frame.

**What is wrong.** THUMB_RAM_CAP is documented as a ~75 MB ceiling, but the check only runs when current_dir changes. Inside one directory every thumbnail ever scrolled past stays on the GPU. In addition the key includes size+mtime, so each time a visible image/video file changes on disk the old texture is orphaned under its old key and a new one is uploaded; nothing removes the old key. Because the focus-swap split view swaps current_dir, every click on the other pane also counts as navigation: with more than 512 thumbnails cached both panes lose all thumbnails and reload them, and failed is cleared so every failed file is retried.

**Trigger.** Open a folder of 10,000 photos in grid view and scroll to the bottom: up to 10,000 x 110-147 KB (1.1-1.5 GB) of textures stay allocated until the user navigates elsewhere; on a small-VRAM GPU texture creation eventually fails and wgpu aborts. Second trigger: leave Fox on a folder where a script rewrites plot.png every second (or a recorder is writing an .mkv): one orphaned texture per directory reload, never released while the folder stays open.

```rust
if self.cached_dir != dir {
    let thumb_count = self.cache.keys().filter(|k| k.starts_with("thumb:")).count();
    if thumb_count > THUMB_RAM_CAP {
        self.cache.retain(|k, _| !k.starts_with("thumb:"));
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** The cap is only evaluated inside ensure_dir when cached_dir changes (50-64). poll_thumbs inserts every finished thumbnail with no size check (69-82), so within one directory every thumbnail ever scrolled past stays on the GPU; at up to 192x192 RGBA that is about 147 KB each, so several thousand photos reach hundreds of MB to over 1 GB. cache_key embeds size and mtime (228-233) and nothing removes the previous key when a file changes, so a repeatedly rewritten image leaks one texture per change while the folder stays open. render_frame calls ensure_dir(&app.current_dir) every frame (render.rs:61) and a split-pane focus swap changes current_dir, so each swap counts as navigation: queue cleared, `failed` cleared, and a full purge once over 512.

**Fix.** Enforce the cap at insert time with a per-key last-used frame counter updated in get/get_or_load, evicting least-recently-used `thumb:` keys not requested this frame, and drop any other `thumb:{path}:` key when inserting for a path. Track both pane directories instead of treating a focus swap as navigation. Design work, not mechanical.

### media-3: Folder icons are keyed by folder NAME: every distinct folder name gets its own synchronous SVG rasterization and its own GPU texture, never freed

- **Where:** `src/icons.rs:222` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Folder icon cache key uses the name only for the seven standard folders.
- **Also reported as:** sections-14

**What is wrong.** The comment says file-type icons are shared, but the directory key embeds the folder's own name. Only seven names map to a special icon; every other folder renders the identical yellow SVG, yet each distinct name is a cache miss. A miss runs load_folder_icon() on the render thread: usvg parse plus resvg render of a 256x256 SVG with gradients and two feDropShadow filters, then a 192x192 RGBA upload (about 147 KB of VRAM). These dir: entries are never evicted (ensure_dir only purges keys starting with thumb:). So scrolling a directory with many sub-folders stutters as each newly visible row re-rasterizes the same picture, and VRAM grows by 147 KB per folder name seen for the life of the process.

**Trigger.** Open a directory with a couple of thousand sub-folders (node_modules, ~/.cargo/registry/src/..., /usr/share/doc) and scroll through it: the first frame and every scroll step hitch while rows rasterize, and about 300 MB of GPU memory is allocated for 2,000 identical folder icons and kept until Fox exits. Repeating this across a long session can exhaust VRAM, which wgpu reports as a fatal error.

```rust
format!("dir:{}:{}:{}", entry.name.to_lowercase(), icon, color)
...
if entry.is_dir {
    match load_folder_icon(entry, gpu, tex) {
        Some(texture) => {
            self.cache.insert(key.clone(), texture);
```

**Skeptic's check.** The directory key is `dir:{name}:{icon}:{color}` (222). folder_icon_embedded (250-271) maps only seven names to special artwork; every other name resolves to the same yellow SVG. Each distinct name is still a cache miss in get_or_load (118-131), which runs load_folder_icon synchronously on the main loop (usvg parse, resvg render, 192x192 upload). ensure_dir purges only keys starting with `thumb:` (51-58) and invalidate only keys containing a path, so `dir:` textures live for the whole process. Scrolling a folder with thousands of sub-folders rasterises the same picture per newly visible row and keeps about 147 KB of VRAM per name. I did not measure the per-icon raster time.

**Fix.** Smallest correct change: keep the key shape but replace the name component with the standard name when it is one of the seven and an empty string otherwise, so all plain folders share one texture while the icon/color precedence stays untouched. The finder's four-way key works too but must mirror the fallback in folder_icon_path when a custom icon path no longer exists. Local, no visual change.

### app-21: set_wallpaper writes the image path into the shared lantern.toml unescaped: a quote, backslash or newline in the file name corrupts or injects into the compositor config

- **Where:** `src/lantern_config.rs:24` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. set_wallpaper escapes backslash, quote and control characters for the TOML string.

**What is wrong.** The path is interpolated into a TOML basic string with no escaping. A file name containing `"` or `\` produces a line that is not valid TOML, so the next read of ~/.lantern/config/lantern.toml (compositor live-reload, every Lantern app reading theme/scale/keybinds) fails for the whole file. A file name containing a newline lets the name terminate the string and append arbitrary keys/sections ([keybinds], autostart-style entries) to the compositor config, i.e. a downloaded/extracted file with a crafted name becomes config injection when the user picks 'Set as Wallpaper'. The patch is also written with a truncating std::fs::write on a file the compositor polls by mtime, and section detection is by exact string, so a header like `[appearance] # comment` or a multi-line array line starting with `[` makes it append a second [appearance] table (duplicate-table parse error).

**Trigger.** Right-click an image named `my "best" shot.png` (or one copied from Windows containing a backslash) -> Set as Wallpaper. lantern.toml now contains `wallpaper = "/…/my "best" shot.png"`; the compositor's reload fails and the DE config is unparseable until hand-fixed.

```rust
let new_line = format!("wallpaper = \"{}\"", image.display());
```

**Skeptic's check.** patch_wallpaper_toml interpolates image.display() into a TOML basic string with no escaping (24). The caller passes any entry whose name ends in a raster extension (context_menu.rs:649-662, icons.rs:440-446). A `"` or `\` in the name yields invalid TOML for the whole shared ~/.lantern/config/lantern.toml, which the compositor live-reloads and every Lantern app reads; a newline in the name closes the string and lets the remainder of the name add keys or sections. The write is a truncating std::fs::write (20), and section detection is an exact string match on `[appearance]`, so a header with a trailing comment gets a second table appended. I did not check how the compositor reacts to a parse failure.

**Fix.** Escape before formatting: `\` -> `\\`, `"` -> `\"`, and control characters (including newline and tab) as \uXXXX, or refuse names containing control characters. That is local and changes nothing for ordinary paths. Follow-ups that are not part of the safe change: write via temp file + rename, and compare section headers after stripping trailing comments.

### sections-8: Split divider is clamped by ratio only: at narrow pane widths the right-aligned nav buttons overlap Back/Forward/Up and steal their clicks

- **Where:** `src/layout.rs:330` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): layout.rs: split_pane_cols clamps each pane to SPLIT_PANE_MIN_W (400 logical px, scaled); a window too narrow for two such panes is shared evenly; split_ratio_at() gives the divider drag (wayland_loop.rs) the same clamp. New src/layout/pane_nav.rs places all of a pane's nav buttons in one go so nothing can overlap: path strip goes first, then Sort, then Search, then left buttons; the right pane always keeps its split toggle; the forced 40px path minimum that poked across the divider is gone. Strip::Typing / Strip::Badge guarantee room for the search box/path field and for the ROOT badge even in a cramped pane. render.rs, sections/nav.rs, sections/split.rs and wayland_actions/click.rs use it; dropped buttons get no zone and are not drawn.

**What is wrong.** A pane needs at least 244*s (left) or 286*s (right, which also hosts the split-close button) for its left-aligned and right-aligned button rows not to overlap, but the only limit is 20% of the available width. InteractionContext resolves overlaps as last-registered-wins, and render.rs registers sort, search and the split toggle after back/forward/up, so the right-aligned buttons win. pane_path_rect's forced 40*s minimum also pokes out of a narrow left pane across the divider into the right pane's view-toggle button.

**Trigger.** Window about 1100 logical px wide (or the laptop's whole screen), split view on, drag the divider fully to one side so a pane is ~170 px. In that pane: clicking Back hits the split toggle and closes split view (the right pane's location and history are discarded); Forward opens the Sort menu; Up toggles search. At the default 1580 px width the right pane at the 0.8 limit is 266 px and the split toggle already covers 20 px of Up.

```rust
let lw = avail * ratio.clamp(SPLIT_RATIO_MIN, SPLIT_RATIO_MAX);
// pane_back_rect: pane_x + 48*s .. +84*s      pane_split_toggle_rect: pane_x + pane_w - 126*s .. -90*s
// pane_forward: +86..+122   pane_sort: pane_w-84..-48      pane_up: +124..+160   pane_search: pane_w-42..-6
```

**Skeptic's check.** split_pane_cols clamps only the ratio (330) and the divider drag does the same (wayland_loop.rs:360-363). Left-aligned buttons sit at pane_x+48..160 and right-aligned ones at pane_w-126..-6, so a pane needs about 244*s (286*s for the right pane, which also hosts the split toggle) to keep them apart. At ratio 0.2 a pane is 0.2 x (window - 248) logical px, so any window narrower than about 1470 (left) or 1680 (right) overlaps at the limit. InteractionContext resolves overlaps last-registered-wins (lntrn-ui input.rs:107-115, 128-134), and both render.rs (288-291 then 390-391, 405) and sections/split.rs (183-188, 269) register sort, search and the split toggle after back/forward/up. So in a narrow pane a click on Up or Back can close split view or open the sort menu. Reached by dragging the divider to an extreme.

**Fix.** Clamp in pixels as well in both split_pane_cols and the divider drag (each pane at least about 300*s, falling back to the ratio clamp only when the window cannot provide it), and when a pane is still too narrow, drop the path strip and collapse the right-aligned row rather than letting zones overlap. The fallback layout is a design choice.

### fileops-29: A FIFO anywhere in a copied tree blocks the worker forever; cancel cannot interrupt it

- **Where:** `src/ops.rs:180` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): copy_tree: FIFOs, sockets and device nodes are never opened; they are skipped and reported. Regular files are opened O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK and re-checked with fstat, so a swapped-in FIFO cannot block. The cancel flag is read between entries inside a folder and between chunks of a file (copy_file_range in 32 MiB chunks, read/write fallback), so cancel takes effect mid-tree and mid-file and the item is rolled back.

**What is wrong.** fs::copy opens the source before checking its type; opening a FIFO for reading blocks until a writer appears (reproduced: the call never returned). copy_dir_recursive sends every non-directory, including fifos/sockets/devices, to fs::copy. The cancel flag is only read between top-level items (line 163), so the op never finishes: op_progress stays Some, needs_anim stays true (wayland_loop.rs:1368) and the window repaints at 60 Hz indefinitely with a stuck progress strip; the thread and its handle leak.

**Trigger.** Copy or cross-device move a folder that contains a named pipe (application runtime dirs, some dev trees, ~/.config of certain apps).

```rust
std::fs::copy(&src, &target).map(|_| ())
```

**Skeptic's check.** run_copy sends every non-directory to std::fs::copy (ops.rs:180) and copy_dir_recursive does the same for every non-dir child (file_ops.rs:65-68). std's copy opens the source before checking it is a regular file; I reproduced it: fs::copy on a mkfifo'd path never returned (timeout 124). cancel is only read at the top of the per-item loop (ops.rs:163), so Done is never sent, op_progress stays Some, and wayland_loop.rs:1368 keeps needs_anim true -> permanent 60 Hz repaint with a stuck strip. A symlink to a fifo has the same effect. FIFOs inside user folders are uncommon, so medium.

**Fix.** Branch on symlink_metadata().file_type() in both run_copy and copy_dir_recursive: copy only regular files, skip-and-report fifo/socket/char/block. Whether symlinks should be recreated instead of followed is a behaviour decision (today they are followed), so keep that separate. Checking cancel inside the recursion is cheap; a chunked copy loop for mid-file cancel changes the documented 'no partial files' policy at ops.rs:8-10 and must then delete the partial target.

### media-11: Preview pane shows Modified/Created in UTC, not local time

- **Where:** `src/preview.rs:211` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. All dates shown go through one localtime_r helper (preview, list view, Properties, conflict dialog).
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-21

**What is wrong.** format_date() splits seconds-since-epoch directly into a calendar date and a 12-hour clock time with no timezone conversion, and labels nothing as UTC. The machine runs on US Eastern time, so every time shown is 4 or 5 hours ahead, and for anything saved in the evening the date is the next day. The same arithmetic is duplicated in views.rs:552, properties.rs:330, dialogs.rs:1155, file_ops.rs:14 and undo.rs:251, so the whole app agrees with itself but not with the clock.

**Trigger.** Save a file at 9:15 PM on Oct 1 local time, select it with the preview pane open: it reads "Oct 2, 2026 · 01:15 AM".

```rust
let secs = dur.as_secs();
let days = secs / 86400;
let tod = secs % 86400;
let hh = (tod / 3600) as u32;
let mm = ((tod % 3600) / 60) as u32;
```

**Skeptic's check.** format_date splits epoch seconds directly into date and hh:mm with no zone conversion. Same arithmetic confirmed in views.rs:552 (list date column), properties.rs:330 (Created/Modified/Accessed), dialogs.rs:1147 (conflict dialog 'Modified:' used to pick Existing vs Replacement), plus file_ops.rs:9 and undo.rs:246 for timestamps. grep finds no localtime/gmtoff in src. This machine is EDT (`date` 05:27 vs `date -u` 09:27), so every displayed time is 4 h ahead and the date flips at 8 PM local.

**Fix.** One shared helper using libc::localtime_r (libc is already a dependency) returning y/m/d/h/min, with a NULL check that yields "—" (this also fixes the far-future loop in media-17). Use it from preview.rs, views.rs, properties.rs and dialogs.rs. Do not blindly switch file_ops.rs chrono_now: the Trash spec wants local time for DeletionDate, but check Fox's own parser/sort of .trashinfo and undo.rs before changing those two.

### dialogs-7: Opening Properties does blocking file I/O on the UI thread with no slow-mount guard (on an MTP phone the tag read downloads the whole file)

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:223` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Properties opens at once from the listing entry and shows '...' until a worker (src/props_load.rs) has done the stat, readlink, folder count, statvfs, owner/group lookup and folder attributes. On a slow mount no file content is read: no tag read, and the Checksum row says it is not computed there. FileProperties::from_path is gone; App::open_properties / FileProperties::open replace it. Then (lead, after batch 2): props_load.rs now carries the AudioMeta its worker read to the dialog, and AudioEdit::from_meta builds the editor from it: the render thread no longer reads the file at all (AudioEdit::load is gone).
- **Also reported as:** audio-5

**What is wrong.** FileProperties::from_path runs on the render thread from the context-menu handler (src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:731). It does symlink_metadata, metadata, read_dir(path).count() for folders (line 187), statvfs, and for .mp3/.wav AudioEdit::load, which calls audio_tags::read: File::open plus reads. Nothing checks crate::fs::is_slow_path, although the listing, thumbnails, file_info and the watcher all stand down on slow mounts for exactly this reason. Under jmtpfs the first read downloads the entire file under the global lock, and even the stat calls queue behind any download already running.

**Trigger.** Browse the phone, right-click an MP3 or WAV, Properties: the window freezes until the whole track has been pulled over MTP. Right-click any phone file, Properties, while a copy from the phone is running: frozen until that download finishes. Properties on a folder with a very large number of entries stalls on the count.

```rust
let audio = if is_dir {
    None
} else {
    crate::properties_audio::AudioEdit::load(path)
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** The handler at wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:730 calls FileProperties::from_path on the main loop. It does symlink_metadata, metadata, read_dir(path).count() for folders (187), statvfs, and AudioEdit::load -> audio_tags::read (properties_audio/mod.rs:114), a File::open plus reads. No is_slow_path check exists in properties.rs or properties_audio (icons.rs:133 and file_info.rs:59 both have one for exactly this). Per the project's own established jmtpfs behaviour, the first read of a phone MP3/WAV downloads the whole file under the global lock, so the window is frozen for that download. Properties on phone audio is not an everyday flow, so medium rather than high.

**Fix.** Minimal: if crate::fs::is_slow_path(path) skip AudioEdit::load and show "—" instead of the read_dir count. That removes the tag editor for phone files (sensible, a tag save there is a full re-upload) but it is a behaviour decision. The stat calls still queue behind an in-flight download; the full fix is to build FileProperties on a worker thread and open the dialog with a placeholder, which changes threading.

### dialogs-16: Properties content is taller than the panel on short windows and there is no scrolling or clipping; the icon picker's height depends on which sections were collapsed

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:598` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): Properties: header (icon, name, subtitle, close button) is fixed, the rows below scroll inside a clipped viewport with an always-visible scrollbar (ZONE_PROPS_SCROLLBAR, draggable) and the wheel is routed to the dialog while it is open. New src/props_scroll.rs (panel_height, begin_scroll/end_scroll, visible_part, texture_clip, mask_outside_viewport). properties.rs: exact header height (the 9px shortfall is gone), section-header and checksum zones intersected with the viewport, cover zones over everything outside the viewport so the audio editor's own zones cannot be hit through the header, row values cut by measured width. Icon picker moved to new src/props_picker.rs: its height is derived from the window (five rows wanted), tabs and Reset/Back keep their places, the grid scrolls and its cell zones/thumbnails are clipped. render.rs clips cover art and picker thumbnails to the viewport; icons.rs/wayland_loop.rs request a redraw when a picker thumbnail was just rasterised (rows scrolled into view were blank until the pointer moved). props_load.rs/props_click.rs: new picker_scroll/scroll_view fields, picker scroll reset on open and tab change.

**What is wrong.** The panel is clamped to the window but the rows are drawn at their natural positions with no clip and no scroll (FileProperties::scroll_offset exists and is never used). With default sections a plain file needs about 810 logical px, an image about 872, more with Checksum open. On a window shorter than that the lower rows are painted below the panel over the dimmed backdrop and past the window edge, and the Checksum header cannot be reached without first folding sections. The same dialog_h (computed from the normal body) is handed to the icon picker (line 739): if the user folded sections first the picker gets grid_h = 80 px, shows one row of the 24 icons, and its Reset/Back buttons are drawn below the panel.

**Trigger.** On the laptop (1080p at scale 1.25-1.4, at most ~860 logical px tall) or any window under ~900 px tall, open Properties on an image: Permissions/Mode rows hang out of the panel bottom and Checksum is off-screen. On a folder, fold General/Disk/System/Permissions, click the icon: one row of icons, footer buttons outside the panel.

```rust
let dialog_h = content_h.min(screen_h - 40.0 * s);
```

**Skeptic's check.** dialog_h = content_h.min(screen_h - 40*s) only clamps the panel; rows are drawn at natural positions, Fox has no push_clip anywhere in src, and scroll_offset is only declared (75) and initialised (271). Summing the constants gives 810 logical px for a plain file and 872 for an image, so a window under about 850-910 logical px tall (laptop 1080p at 1.25-1.4) paints rows past the panel and off-window. The picker gets `dialog_y + dialog_h - cy - pad` (739): with the four sections folded on a folder content_h is 310, the picker gets 127 px, grid_h falls to the 80*s floor (one row) and the footer is drawn below the panel. Also noticed the header actually consumes 159*s while content_h budgets 150*s, so bottom padding is 9*s short.

**Fix.** Needs a clipped, scrollable body (painter+text push_clip, wheel routed to props.scroll_offset while the dialog is open, zones intersected with the clip) and the picker height derived from the window, not from the info body. New interaction, not mechanical.

### dialogs-10: Checksum row stays on "Computing…" until some input event arrives: the result is only polled when a frame is drawn

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:1124` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Checksum job has a cancel flag set on drop, refuses non-regular files, and keeps frames coming while it runs.

**What is wrong.** The hash thread writes into a Mutex slot and nothing wakes the loop. wayland_loop.rs only renders when state.frame_done is set by an input/configure event or when needs_anim is true, and the needs_anim expression (wayland_loop.rs:1356-1379) includes the audio editor's busy() but not a pending checksum job. For any file that takes longer to hash than the click itself, the finished result is not shown.

**Trigger.** Properties on a file of a few hundred MB or more, click the Checksum header, keep the mouse still: the row reads "Computing…" indefinitely. Nudging the pointer makes the hash appear.

```rust
match props.checksum_job.as_ref().and_then(|j| j.get()) {
    Some(hash) => {
```

**Skeptic's check.** ChecksumJob (checksums.rs) writes into a Mutex slot and wakes nothing. The loop only reaches render when state.frame_done is set (wayland_loop.rs:161) and the needs_anim expression (1356-1379) has no checksum term, so after the header click no frame is scheduled and 'Computing…' stays until an input event. Same family as the stale cloud pill and (see extra findings) the dropped directory-watcher wake.

**Fix.** OR a pending check into needs_anim: app.properties.as_ref().is_some_and(|p| p.section_open[6] && p.checksum_job.as_ref().is_some_and(|j| j.get().is_none())). Cost: 60 fps repaint while a large file hashes; a cheaper design is an eventfd the worker pokes, added to the poll set. Also consider cancelling the hash thread when the dialog closes.

### render-2: Every frame does getxattr on each visible folder (6 syscalls per folder), with no slow-mount guard: UI freezes behind a jmtpfs transfer

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:141` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it high; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): FileEntry carries folder_icon / folder_color, read once in fs::read_directory (on the loader thread for slow mounts, so they show there again). icons::cache_key and the icon loaders use the entry; drawing does no syscall. Icon changes go through App::apply_folder_icon (written and read back on a worker, then every copy of the entry is updated); the two picker threads raise the refresh flag.

**What is wrong.** `get_or_load`, `has_icon` and `get` each call `cache_key`, and for a directory `cache_key` performs two `libc::getxattr` calls on the entry's path (icons.rs:352-370). render_frame calls all three for every visible entry on every frame (plus the same again for the unfocused split pane in sections/split.rs:316-318 and render.rs:1189), so each visible folder costs 6 path-based syscalls per frame on the render thread. `get_or_load` computes the key before it looks at `fs::is_slow_path`, so there is no slow-mount exemption. On an MTP/sshfs mount each getxattr needs a kernel path walk; once the FUSE entry timeout (1s by default) expires that walk sends LOOKUP to the daemon, and jmtpfs serves it under the same global lock it holds while downloading a file. The rest of the codebase already treats exactly this as forbidden on the render thread (wayland_loop.rs:1289-1292: 'every stat below is a device round-trip that can block for minutes behind a jmtpfs download').

**Trigger.** Mount a phone, browse a phone folder that shows sub-folders (the storage root, DCIM, Pictures) and start copying a large file or folder off it (or let an image thumbnail download on the slow lane). `op_progress` keeps `needs_anim` true, so frames run at 60fps; within a second a frame's getxattr on a visible phone folder blocks behind the in-flight download and the whole window (including the progress strip) freezes until that file finishes. On local disks the same code is just ~600 wasted syscalls per frame for 100 visible folders.

```rust
icon_cache.get_or_load(&entries[i], ctx, tex_pass);            // render.rs:141 / 161 / 179
ir.intersect(&content).is_some() && icon_cache.has_icon(&entries[i])  // render.rs:147
let tex = icon_cache.get(&entries[i])?;                          // render.rs:1099 / 1126 / 1150
// icons.rs:217-222
fn cache_key(entry: &FileEntry) -> String {
    if entry.is_dir {
        let icon = get_folder_icon(&entry.path).unwrap_or_default();
        let color = get_folder_color(&entry.path).unwrap_or_default();
```

**Skeptic's check.** icons.rs:217-222: cache_key for a directory calls get_folder_icon and get_folder_color, each a libc::getxattr on the path (352-370). get_or_load (118), has_icon (92) and get (106) each recompute the key, and render.rs calls all three for every visible entry every frame (141/147/1099 and the list/tree equivalents, plus split.rs:396-398 and render.rs:1189 for the unfocused pane). The is_slow_path test at icons.rs:133 is after the key and only gates thumbnails. So 6 path syscalls per visible folder per frame on the render thread, with no slow-mount exemption: that part is certain. The freeze is inferred, not reproduced: each getxattr needs a path walk, FUSE re-validates dentries after its 1 s entry timeout with a LOOKUP to the daemon, and jmtpfs serves that under the lock it holds during a download (the same reasoning as the comment at wayland_loop.rs:1289-1291). It needs phone folders on screen while a transfer runs, i.e. split view or a drag onto a tab/favourite. If it does block, this is high.

**Fix.** Cache (icon, color) per directory path in IconCache (HashMap<PathBuf,(String,String)>), cleared in invalidate() for that path and wholesale in ensure_dir on directory change, and return empty strings without a syscall when is_slow_path(path). Careful with invalidation: xattrs set by another Fox window or the sudo NewFolder path will not show until the directory is re-entered; cache_key is a free fn used by &self methods, so the cache needs interior mutability or key computation hoisted into get_or_load.

### render-9: More than 256 icon draws in a frame are silently dropped (TexturePass instance cap), leaving blank cells with no fallback icon

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:1516` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Texture draws are submitted in batches of 256 with a flush between them (both layers).
- **Also reported as:** sections-15, media-9

**What is wrong.** render_frame collects every visible icon of the focused pane, then the unfocused split pane's icons, then the drag ghost and the preview thumbnail into one `tex_draws` vector and hands it to a pass whose instance buffer holds 256 entries; anything past index 255 is truncated without a warning. Because `has_icon[i]` is true for those entries, the procedural fallback icon is also suppressed, so the cell shows only its label. The first casualties are the items appended last: drag ghost, preview thumbnail and the unfocused pane's icons.

**Trigger.** On the 4K monitor at scale 1.0-1.1 with the window maximized, Grid view at the smallest icon zoom: cells are 152px, giving 23 columns x 14 visible rows = 322 icons, so the bottom rows render without icons (and a dragged item has no ghost). Split view with two Grid panes hits it the same way.

```rust
tex_pass.render_pass(ctx, frame.encoder_mut(), &view, &tex_draws, None);
// lntrn-render/tex/src/lib.rs:71, 418
const MAX_TEX_INSTANCES: usize = 256;
let count = draws.len().min(MAX_TEX_INSTANCES);
```

**Skeptic's check.** lntrn-render tex/src/lib.rs:71 and 418: MAX_TEX_INSTANCES = 256 and `count = draws.len().min(MAX_TEX_INSTANCES)`, silently truncating. render_frame builds one tex_draws (focused pane, then unfocused pane, then drag ghost, then preview thumbnail) and never checks its length. has_icon stays true for the dropped entries, and grid.rs:70 then skips the procedural icon, so those cells are blank. Layout check: at zoom 0 a cell is 152*s; a maximised 3840x2160 window at scale 1.0 gives 23 columns x about 14 rows = 322. At the default zoom or scale 1.2+ the count stays under 256, so it needs big-monitor/low-zoom or two Grid panes.

**Fix.** In render.rs: for chunk in tex_draws.chunks(256) { tex_pass.render_pass(..chunk..); frame.flush(ctx); } before text.render_layer(0). The flush is required because each render_pass rewrites the shared instance buffer at offset 0 (queue.write_buffer is applied at the next submit). The pass uses LoadOp::Load, so chunks compose. The cleaner fix (growable instance buffer) is in lntrn-render and needs approval.

### sections-7: Breadcrumb overflow can skip every segment, leaving only '... /' with no current folder

- **Where:** `src/sections/nav.rs:240` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): New src/sections/crumbs.rs: one measured breadcrumb layout. The current folder is never skipped: when nothing else fits it stays, cut with an ellipsis; the leading ellipsis yields when the name would get less than ~3 em. A strip at the bar's right end (and the leading ellipsis) is always a ZONE_PATH_INPUT so the bar can be clicked into edit mode. render.rs builds zones from it, sections/nav.rs draws from it, sections/split.rs uses it for the unfocused pane (which used to cut the tail of the path off).

**What is wrong.** When even the last segment plus the ellipsis does not fit, skip ends at segments.len(): the draw loop then skips every segment and only '...' and a separator are painted (outside the bar if it is narrow). render.rs:320-345 runs the same loop for zones, so no breadcrumb zone is registered either, and when the bar is narrower than the ellipsis the ZONE_PATH_INPUT strip is not registered, so the bar cannot be clicked into edit mode. Widths use name.len() (bytes) * 9.9*s, so non-ASCII names hit this far sooner. Verified the loop with a standalone rustc run: w=166 gives skip=4 of 4.

**Trigger.** Split view at the default 1580 px window: the focused pane's path bar is about 364-406 px, so any folder whose name is longer than roughly 30-34 ASCII characters (or ~12 CJK characters) shows a blank path bar. Drag the divider so a pane is ~400 px wide and any name over ~7 characters does it. Single pane: a 50-character folder name in an 1100 px window.

```rust
for (i, _) in segments.iter().enumerate() {
    let remaining: f32 = segments[i..] ... .sum();
    if ellipsis_w + remaining <= path_rect.w {
        break;
    }
    skip = i + 1;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** nav.rs:237-257 and render.rs:320-340: `skip = i + 1` runs for every i where ellipsis + remaining does not fit, so when even the last segment does not fit skip == segments.len(), every segment is skipped in both the draw loop and the zone loop, and only '... /' is painted. pane_path_rect guarantees only 40*s (layout.rs:407). With the estimate 9.9*s per byte + 12*s and a 55.7*s ellipsis, a 364-406 px split-pane bar blanks for a final folder name over about 30 ASCII bytes (about 10 CJK characters); single pane needs a much longer name.

**Fix.** Clamp skip to segments.len() - 1 in both places (ideally one shared helper, since nav.rs says 'must match render.rs exactly' and click.rs uses app.breadcrumb_skip). The clamp alone makes the last segment draw past the bar over the sort/search buttons, because the label has no max_width, so it must come with truncate_to_width for that label and a zone rect clamped to the bar. Do it together with render-14.

### app-20: Settings file is written non-atomically and any parse failure silently resets everything; the next save makes the loss permanent

- **Where:** `src/settings.rs:146` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): Writes are atomic (temporary file in the same folder, fsync, rename, folder fsync; permissions kept; a symlinked config stays a symlink). A file that is not a JSON object is renamed to file-manager.json.broken-<unix time> at load (or at save if it broke meanwhile) and is never written over; if it cannot be moved aside or cannot be read at all, nothing is written. Struct-level serde default means a missing key no longer resets everything, and a value of the wrong type costs only that key (per-key lenient load, the odd value is left in the file). Every failure is logged. The old-path fallback (~/.config/lantern/fox.json) is read and the first save writes the new path. The aside in the finding: the exit save now runs before a dead-compositor error can return, pins are written when made, the hidden-files toggle is written at once and icon zoom is written once the loop is idle (src/wayland_loop.rs, src/wayland.rs, Settings::store_session). Split layout and pinned-tab order are still written only at exit, so a SIGKILL/SIGTERM or panic loses those two.

**What is wrong.** std::fs::write truncates the file and then writes. A crash, power loss or ENOSPC in between, or two instances saving at the same moment (all Fox windows save simultaneously when the compositor exits or restarts and their loops break) can leave an empty or spliced file (shorter content followed by the tail of the longer one). load() maps every error, including a single missing required field (icon_zoom, window_width, window_height have no #[serde(default)]) or one bad value, to Settings::default() with no log line, and the exit save then overwrites the file, permanently dropping favourites and pinned tabs. The image viewer reads the same file on every folder scan and can observe the truncated state. Separately, pinned tabs and icon zoom are only persisted on a clean loop exit, so SIGTERM at logout or any panic loses them.

**Trigger.** Have two Fox windows open and restart/quit the compositor (both hit 'flush error', break, and save concurrently), or fill the disk and close Fox. Next launch: default sort/zoom, no favourites, no pinned tabs.

```rust
std::fs::read_to_string(&path)
    .ok()
    .and_then(|s| serde_json::from_str(&s).ok())
    .unwrap_or_default()
```

**Skeptic's check.** save() is `let _ = std::fs::write(&path, json)` (truncate then write, result ignored); load() maps any read or parse error to Settings::default() with no log (146-151); icon_zoom, window_width and window_height have no #[serde(default)]. So an ENOSPC or a kill between truncate and write leaves an empty file, the next launch silently loads defaults, and the next save (any sidebar click, or exit at wayland.rs:593) makes the loss of favourites and pinned tabs permanent. The two-instance splice is possible but needs a microsecond race; ENOSPC is the deterministic trigger. pinned_tabs and icon_zoom are only written in the exit block, which is skipped if run_loop returns Err.

**Fix.** Write to file-manager.json.tmp in the same directory, fsync, rename; on parse error log it and copy the bad file aside instead of defaulting silently; add serde defaults to the three bare fields. Low risk, but it does change how the file is overwritten (a symlinked config would be replaced by a regular file, and the image viewer reads this file), so I am not marking it mechanical.

### app-19: Settings are whole-struct last-writer-wins across instances: a favourite/pin/sort changed in one window is erased when an older window (or picker) saves

- **Where:** `src/settings.rs:154` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): Settings::save (src/settings.rs + new src/settings/store.rs) is now read-modify-write: it remembers every key as last read or written, re-reads the file under a short flock, and writes only the keys this process changed; everything else, including keys this version does not know, stays as another window left it. favorites and pinned_tabs are merged three ways (this window's order wins, what it removed stays removed, what another window added is appended, what another window removed is dropped), and a list changed here is handed back merged so the sidebar shows the other window's favourite too. Pickers write only what they changed.

**What is wrong.** Each process loads Settings once at startup (wayland.rs:267) and every save() serialises its entire in-memory copy. Normal windows save on exit (wayland.rs:593) and on many clicks; pick-mode instances also save from the loop when a sidebar section is collapsed, a favourite is added, or the sort changes. No instance re-reads the file before writing, so any field another instance changed in the meantime (favorites, pinned_tabs, show_hidden, sort, split state) is silently reverted to the saver's startup snapshot. pinned_tabs is only ever written at exit, which makes it the easiest to lose.

**Trigger.** Open Fox window 1, then window 2. In window 2 add a folder to Favorites (saved immediately) and pin a tab, close window 2. Close window 1: its exit save rewrites favorites and pinned_tabs from its own stale copy. Relaunch: the favourite and the pin are gone.

```rust
pub fn save(&self) {
    let path = Self::config_path();
    ...
    if let Ok(json) = serde_json::to_string_pretty(self) {
        let _ = std::fs::write(&path, json);
```

**Skeptic's check.** Settings is loaded once (wayland.rs:267) and every save() serialises the whole in-memory struct; there is no reload-before-save or lock. Normal windows save on many clicks and at exit (wayland.rs:593, which also rewrites pinned_tabs, sort, view, split state); pick-mode instances skip the exit save but do save from the loop (wayland_loop.rs:769 etc.). So a favourite or pin added in window 2 is overwritten when window 1, holding its startup snapshot, next saves or exits.

**Fix.** Read-modify-write: reload the file, apply only the fields this instance changed, write atomically (combine with app-20). This changes persistence semantics (which window's sort/view wins at exit), so it needs a decision on per-field ownership, not a mechanical edit.

### fileops-22: sudo cp/mv are built without -T: an existing directory target makes the item land inside it, and files are overwritten with no conflict dialog

- **Where:** `src/sudo.rs:206` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C1): Root-mode paste and drop no longer bypass the conflict flow: PendingPaste.privileged sends the resolved items to sudo at the end. sudo.rs builds 'cp -rT' / 'mv -T' with '--', positional arguments in a fixed sh script, a guard that refuses an existing target, and (copy) a staging name so a failed copy leaves nothing under the real name. A cached-ticket failure is now shown in a notice instead of only stderr.

**What is wrong.** paste()/start_drag_drop skip conflict handling in root mode on the assumption that 'cp -r / mv handle overwrites natively' (file_ops.rs:127-131). They do not prompt at all (stdin is a pipe) and, when dest/name already exists as a directory, `cp -r src dest/name` and `mv src dest/name` put the source INSIDE it (dest/name/name) instead of replacing or merging. Same for the PermissionDenied fallback. Existing files with the same name are silently replaced.

**Trigger.** Root mode (or paste into a root-owned folder): paste folder 'conf' into a directory that already has 'conf' -> result is conf/conf. Paste a file with an existing name -> overwritten without any Replace/Keep Both/Skip dialog.

```rust
Some(vec![
    "cp".into(),
    "-r".into(),
    "--".into(),
    s.as_os_str().to_owned(),
    target.into_os_string(),
])
```

**Skeptic's check.** build_argv emits `cp -r -- src dest/name` and `mv src dest/name` (194-219). paste() and start_drag_drop bypass conflict resolution entirely in root mode (file_ops.rs:127-147, 171-187) on the stated assumption that cp/mv 'handle overwrites natively'. With GNU coreutils, when dest/name already exists as a directory both commands place the source inside it (dest/name/name), and existing files are replaced without any prompt since stdin is a pipe. The PermissionDenied fallback in poll_op_progress has the same shape and additionally ignores a Keep Both rename, because build_argv recomputes the target from the source name. Also seen while here: these sudo commands run synchronously on the UI thread (see extra findings).

**Fix.** Run the normal conflict flow in root mode and pass resolved (src, target) pairs into PendingPrivOp so Keep Both/Skip are honoured, then use `cp -rT --` / `mv -T --`. -T alone changes behaviour: cp -rT merges into an existing directory, mv -T fails on a non-empty one. Overwrite semantics change, so this needs a decision.

### media-5: Thumbnail queue is FIFO with no cancellation: entries scrolled out of view are still decoded ahead of what is on screen

- **Where:** `src/thumbs.rs:126` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C8): The worker pool moved to new src/thumbs/pool.rs and now owns the pending set. Every frame each visible row re-asks for its thumbnail (ThumbPool::want from IconCache::get_or_load); IconCache::end_requests (called in src/render.rs after both panes and the preview pane have asked) reduces both queues (main and slow/MTP lane) to exactly the keys asked for this frame, ordered as asked (top row first). Jobs for rows that left the screen are dropped and their keys leave pending, so they are requested afresh when the row returns; jobs a worker already started finish normally. The clear-on-navigation is gone because the per-frame cut covers it. has_pending now reads the pool's set, so the 60 fps polling stops as soon as nothing on screen is waiting.

**What is wrong.** get_or_load() submits a job for every entry that is visible in any frame, and a submitted job stays queued until a worker reaches it. clear_queue() only runs when the directory changes. Scrolling inside one large folder therefore queues every row that was ever on screen, and the workers process them oldest first, so the rows the user is actually looking at are served last. Every one of those off-screen results is also uploaded to the GPU (feeding the unbounded cache above).

**Trigger.** Open a folder with several thousand photos for the first time (cold disk cache) and drag the scrollbar to the bottom. The visible rows keep their placeholder icons for minutes while 2-4 workers decode thousands of photos that are no longer on screen (at roughly 50-100 ms per multi-megapixel JPEG). The app also renders at 60 fps the whole time because has_pending() stays true.

```rust
lock.lock().unwrap().push_back(job);
...
if let Some(job) = q.pop_front() {
    break job;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read thumbs.rs worker_loop (pop_front, FIFO) and icons.rs get_or_load/ensure_dir, plus render.rs:136-191 where get_or_load is called for every entry visible in a frame. A key enters `pending` once and the job stays queued until a worker reaches it; clear_queue() is only called from ensure_dir() when cached_dir changes (render.rs:61). So scrolling inside one big folder queues every row that was ever on screen and serves them oldest first, while has_pending() keeps needs_anim true (wayland_loop.rs:1370). No visibility or generation check exists anywhere.

**Fix.** Switching pop_front to pop_back is a safe one-line win (newest request first). The full fix (bounding the queue and handing dropped keys back so IconCache removes them from `pending`) changes pool bookkeeping and needs a new return path, so it is not purely mechanical. Dropped keys must leave `pending` or they are never re-requested. The THUMB_RAM_CAP purge also only runs on navigation, so off-screen results still pile up on the GPU.

### media-6: A thumbnail job that never returns leaves its key in pending forever: permanent 60 fps redraw, and the pool dies after 2-4 such jobs

- **Where:** `src/thumbs.rs:132` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Thumbnail workers catch panics and always send a result; non-regular files are refused; lock poisoning is tolerated.

**What is wrong.** worker_loop has no catch_unwind and no timeout. If generate() panics (the image, resvg and usvg crates, plus the in-house audio tag parser, all run on untrusted file contents; the workspace does not set panic=abort so only the thread dies) or blocks forever (opening a FIFO, a hung NFS/CIFS mount on the single slow-lane worker, an ffmpeg child that never exits), no ThumbResult is sent. The key is only removed from IconCache::pending when a result arrives or when the job is still queued at clear_queue() time, so an in-flight key stays forever. has_pending() then stays true for the rest of the process, wayland_loop.rs:1370 keeps needs_anim set, and Fox re-renders a full frame every 16 ms forever (the idle spin the loop comments say melted the laptop). Each such job also permanently removes one of the 2-4 workers (or the only slow-lane worker); once they are all gone no thumbnail is produced again and every new request is added to pending.

**Trigger.** Concrete non-panic trigger: run mkfifo ~/Downloads/x.png and open Downloads in Fox. ImageReader::open blocks in open(2) forever, the entry never gets a thumbnail, and Fox renders at 60 fps until it is closed, including after navigating away. Two to four such files (or one hung network file on the slow lane) stop all thumbnails for the session. Any file that makes a decoder panic has the same effect.

```rust
let rgba = generate(&job.path, job.kind);
if tx.send(ThumbResult { key: job.key, rgba }).is_err() {
    return; // IconCache dropped — shutting down
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced the FIFO trigger: fs::list_directory lists a fifo named x.png as a plain file, thumb_kind() classifies by name only, cache_file() only stats, then decode_image_limited calls ImageReader::open -> File::open, which blocks on a FIFO until a writer appears. No ThumbResult is sent, the key never leaves IconCache::pending (only removed on result or while still queued), so has_pending() stays true and the loop renders every 16 ms forever, and that worker is gone. No catch_unwind in the crate and ../Cargo.toml [profile.release] sets no panic=abort, so a decoder panic kills only the worker with the same effect. ffmpeg .output() has no timeout. I did not find a concrete file that panics a decoder; the FIFO path is enough.

**Fix.** Wrap generate() in std::panic::catch_unwind(...).unwrap_or(None) so a result is always sent, and skip non-regular files with fs::metadata(path).is_file() at the top of generate() (this also covers the /dev/zero symlink in media-7). Both are local. An ffmpeg deadline and a per-key pending timeout are worth adding but are design work. icons.rs load_folder_icon calls generate() synchronously on the render thread for custom folder icons, so the regular-file check matters there too.

### media-10: EXIF orientation is ignored: portrait phone photos are shown sideways in thumbnails and Quick Look

- **Where:** `src/thumbs.rs:213` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Thumbnails and Quick Look apply EXIF orientation (limits re-checked by hand); cache generation bumped.

**What is wrong.** image 0.25's ImageReader::decode() returns the pixels as stored and does not apply the EXIF Orientation tag; the caller has to read decoder.orientation() and call DynamicImage::apply_orientation(). Neither thumbs.rs (decode_limited) nor quick_look.rs:139 (load_image) does, and there is no other orientation handling in the crate. Any JPEG/TIFF/WebP whose orientation tag is not 1 is drawn rotated or mirrored, and the wrong thumbnail is then persisted in the disk cache.

**Trigger.** Copy portrait photos from a phone camera that records rotation in EXIF (Orientation = 6 or 8) into a folder and open it: the thumbnails, the preview pane image and the Quick Look image are all rotated 90 degrees compared with the image viewer.

```rust
let img = reader.decode().ok()?;
Some(img.thumbnail(THUMB_SIZE, THUMB_SIZE).to_rgba8())
```

**Skeptic's check.** Cargo.lock pins image 0.25.9. Read io/image_reader_type.rs: decode() calls DynamicImage::from_decoder with no orientation handling; orientation() exists on the JPEG, WebP and TIFF decoders and apply_orientation exists on DynamicImage. grep -i 'orientation|exif' over src returns nothing, so thumbs.rs decode_limited and quick_look.rs:139 both show EXIF-rotated photos sideways, and the wrong thumbnail is persisted to the disk cache. Phone cameras that record rotation in EXIF hit this on every portrait shot.

**Fix.** The proposed fix has one error: limits do NOT fully carry over. ImageReader::decode() does limits.reserve(decoder.total_bytes()) before decoding; into_decoder() only calls set_limits, so the output-buffer check is lost. Replicate it by hand (reject when decoder.total_bytes() > MAX_DECODE_BYTES) before DynamicImage::from_decoder. Then read dec.orientation() and apply_orientation. In quick_look take src_w/src_h after rotating. Add a version byte to the cache_file hash so cached sideways thumbnails regenerate (old files become orphans, see media-8).

### media-7: SVG paths bypass every decode limit: whole-file read with no size cap and unlimited embedded-raster decode (symlink to /dev/zero kills Fox)

- **Where:** `src/thumbs.rs:228` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. SVG files are read through one helper that requires a regular file of at most 16 MB. (Embedded rasters inside an SVG are still uncapped.)

**What is wrong.** Raster images go through decode_limited (16384 px, 256 MB), but anything named .svg is read fully into memory with std::fs::read and handed to usvg/resvg with default options. Three holes: (1) no file-size check and symlinks are followed, so x.svg -> /dev/zero is read until allocation fails (process abort) or the OOM killer fires; a multi-GB real SVG does the same on up to four workers at once. (2) resvg decodes rasters embedded in the SVG (PNG via tiny_skia::Pixmap::decode_png, JPEG via zune-jpeg, GIF, WebP) at their full pixel size with no cap, so a sub-100 KB SVG embedding a 40000x40000 PNG forces multi-GB allocations. (3) The same pattern exists in quick_look.rs:162 (load_svg) and icons.rs:423 (rasterize_svg), and the icons.rs path runs on the render thread for a custom folder icon whose path comes from the user.lantern.folder_icon xattr of the directory being listed, which travels with ext4/btrfs removable media.

**Trigger.** In any folder run ln -s /dev/zero pic.svg (or extract an archive / clone a repo containing such a link) and open the folder in Fox: a thumbnail worker reads /dev/zero until memory is exhausted and Fox is killed, and it happens again every time the folder is opened because failures are not cached on disk. Pressing Space on the file does the same through Quick Look.

```rust
let data = std::fs::read(path).ok()?;
let tree = resvg::usvg::Tree::from_data(&data, &resvg::usvg::Options::default()).ok()?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** fs::list_directory uses DirEntry::metadata (lstat), so a symlink pic.svg -> /dev/zero is listed as a file and thumb_kind() classifies it by name. rasterize_svg_file does std::fs::read(path), which follows the link and reads until allocation fails. Same unbounded read in quick_look.rs:162 and icons.rs:423 (the latter on the render thread). resvg-0.47.0 src/image.rs decodes embedded PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP with no size limit, confirmed by reading it. The xattr-on-removable-media route for icons.rs was not exercised.

**Fix.** One shared helper for the three SVG call sites: open, require metadata.is_file(), reject len over a cap (16 MB is generous), read through file.take(cap). That part is local and mechanical. Capping embedded rasters needs a usvg image_href_resolver or a tree walk and is a separate, less mechanical step. Moving custom folder icon loads off the render thread changes threading and should be its own change.

### fileops-27: Redo of New File / New Folder guesses the type from the name and truncates existing files

- **Where:** `src/undo.rs:197` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Create records file-vs-folder; redo uses create_dir / create_new.

**What is wrong.** UndoAction::Create does not record whether the item was a file or a directory. Redo treats 'no extension' as a folder, so the default 'New File' comes back as a DIRECTORY, and a folder named like 'v1.2' comes back as an empty file. The file branch uses fs::write(path, ""), which truncates whatever exists at that path at redo time.

**Trigger.** Right-click -> New File, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z: a folder named 'New File' appears.

```rust
if path.extension().is_none() || path.to_string_lossy().ends_with('/') {
    if std::fs::create_dir_all(path).is_ok() {
        created += 1;
    }
} else {
    if std::fs::write(path, "").is_ok() {
```

**Skeptic's check.** execute_forward's Create branch decides file vs folder from path.extension().is_none(). new_file_or_prompt pushes Create([current_dir/"New File"]), which has no extension, so New File -> Ctrl+Z -> Ctrl+Shift+Z runs create_dir_all and produces a directory. A folder named like v1.2 comes back as an empty file. The file branch uses fs::write(path, ""), which truncates anything that appeared at that path in between.

**Fix.** Record the kind in the action (Create(Vec<(PathBuf, bool)>)) at the two push sites and recreate with create_dir / OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true). Small and mechanical; the only callers are context_menu.rs:870 and :895.

### fileops-25: Undo/redo execute on the UI thread, including waiting on pkexec and recursive re-copies

- **Where:** `src/undo.rs:233` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C2): Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z (src/wayland_actions/key.rs) now only call App::request_history (new src/undo_ops.rs). The work runs on the ops worker: OpKind::History + OpQueue::push_history in src/ops.rs, glue in new src/ops/history.rs, execution in src/undo/exec.rs. Progress shows in the existing status strip ("Undoing • name (1/3)"), cancel works, and the result arrives through App::finish_history (status-bar note, failure notice, reload). Jobs run strictly one at a time in the order pressed. pkexec is gone from undo: a rename or move refused for lack of permission is handed to the existing sudo modal as PendingPrivOp::Move (its script refuses an existing target) and is recorded on the redo/undo stack only once it is seen to have happened (UndoStack::settle_root, called from after_priv_op in src/file_ops.rs). The redo re-copy fallback uses the worker's own staged copy path with cancel.

**What is wrong.** key.rs:722-728 calls undo_stack.undo/redo directly from the key handler. Redo of a paste re-copies whole trees inline, undo of Copy/Create runs remove_dir_all inline, and in root mode each entry blocks on `pkexec mv ... .status()` (lines 74-80, 120-126, 158-164, 213-219) while an authentication dialog would be open. The returned description is discarded, so the user also gets no feedback about what was undone. undo() additionally pushes the action to redo even when the reversal failed.

**Trigger.** Paste a 10 GB folder, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z: the window freezes for the entire re-copy.

```rust
let ok = if src.is_dir() {
    crate::file_ops::copy_dir_recursive(src, dst).is_ok()
} else {
    std::fs::copy(src, dst).is_ok()
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** key.rs:722-728 calls undo_stack.undo/redo inline in the key handler. Redo of Copy runs copy_dir_recursive / fs::copy on the UI thread, undo of Copy/Create runs remove_dir_all inline, and the root-mode branches block on `pkexec mv ... .status()`. The returned description is discarded and undo() pushes the action onto redo even when execute_reverse failed (undo.rs:54-56).

**Fix.** Move execution onto a worker (the ops worker already exists for copies), poll for the result, show the description, and only move the action between stacks on success. That is a threading change. Note the rest of the app uses the sudo modal while undo uses pkexec; worth unifying when this is reworked.

### render-4: List view: file names disappear entirely when the content column is narrower than 356*m*s (name column width goes negative)

- **Where:** `src/views.rs:307` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): New src/layout/list.rs list_columns(): the name column has a minimum (180 * zoom multiplier * scale); the Modified column is dropped first, then Size, when the width cannot hold them. views.rs header, rows and list_row_hit_rect all use the same ListCols, so they stay in step.

**What is wrong.** `list_columns` reserves fixed widths for the size (110*m*s) and date (180*m*s) columns plus paddings regardless of how wide the content is, so the name column is `content.w - 356*m*s` with m = 0.8 + 1.4*zoom (534 logical px at the default zoom 0.5, 783 at zoom 1.0). When that is <= 0, `truncate_to_width` returns an empty string (sections/mod.rs:187) and the row is drawn with no name at all; `list_row_hit_rect` shrinks to the icon. The size column can also land left of the name column and draw over the icon. Only a selected row shows its (untruncated) name.

**Trigger.** Any of: (a) split view with a List pane in the default 1580px window at icon zoom 1.0 (the zoom saved on this machine): pane is ~666px, name column is -117px, every row is blank; (b) List view with the preview pane open in a window narrower than about 1000 logical px at default zoom; (c) List view in a half-width window on the laptop at scale 1.4 (about 685 logical px). The user sees rows with only an icon, a size and a date.

```rust
let date_x = content_rect.x + content_rect.w - right_pad - date_w;
let size_x = date_x - size_w;
let name_x = content_rect.x + 42.0 * m * s;
...
let max_name_w = size_x - name_x - 12.0 * m * s;   // = content.w - 356*m*s
```

**Skeptic's check.** list_columns reserves 12+180+110 on the right and 42 on the left, and max_name_w subtracts another 12, all times m*s, so the name column is content.w - 356*m*s. truncate_to_width (sections/mod.rs) returns an empty string when max_w <= 0. With the saved icon_zoom 1.0 on this machine m is 2.2, so 783 logical px are consumed; split view in the default 1580 window gives each pane about 666 px (1580 - 240 sidebar - 8 handle, halved), so every unselected row is blank. Minimum window size is 400x300, far below the threshold at any zoom.

**Fix.** Give the name column a guaranteed minimum inside list_columns and drop the date column, then the size column, when the width cannot hold them, so header, rows and list_row_hit_rect stay in step. The header draw, the row draw and the hit rect all need the same answer; this is a layout decision that needs a visual pass.

### render-6: Modified dates are rendered in UTC, not local time: evening files show tomorrow's date

- **Where:** `src/views.rs:559` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. All dates shown go through one localtime_r helper (preview, list view, Properties, conflict dialog).

**What is wrong.** `format_date` converts the mtime to a calendar date by dividing seconds since the epoch by 86400 with no timezone offset; nothing in the crate calls localtime (grep for localtime/tm_gmtoff finds nothing). The List view 'Modified' column is therefore a UTC date. preview.rs:204-267 has the same routine and additionally prints the UTC clock time. I compiled the function standalone: an mtime of 2026-10-01 21:30 EDT prints 'Oct 2, 2026'.

**Trigger.** On this machine (America/New_York), save a file after 8 PM (7 PM in winter) and look at it in List view: the Modified column shows the next day. Sorting by date still orders correctly, but the displayed day (and the preview pane's time) is wrong for 4-5 hours every day.

```rust
let secs = dur.as_secs();
let days = secs / 86400;
```

**Skeptic's check.** format_date divides seconds since the epoch by 86400 with no offset, and grep for localtime/tm_gmtoff/utc_offset over src finds nothing. preview.rs:204-267 is the same routine and also prints the UTC hour and minute as if local, so the preview pane time is wrong by the UTC offset at all times, and the List date is a day ahead for files saved in the local evening.

**Fix.** Convert with libc::localtime_r (libc is already a dependency) and format from the tm fields in both views.rs and preview.rs; return "--" when it returns null. This also removes the per-year loop (render-7). properties.rs format_time should be checked for the same routine.

### render-7: format_date walks one year per loop iteration: a file with a far-future mtime hangs the List view

- **Where:** `src/views.rs:563` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The per-year loops are gone; out-of-range stamps show a dash.

**What is wrong.** The year is found by subtracting 365/366 days in a loop, so the cost is proportional to the year. It is called for every visible row on every frame (views.rs:335), and the identical loop is in preview.rs:218. btrfs (the filesystem of /home here) stores 64-bit timestamps: I verified `touch -d @4000000000000000` is accepted and stat returns year 126756924. Measured with a standalone -O build: that mtime costs 100 ms per call, and 4e17 seconds costs 10.2 s per call.

**Trigger.** A file whose mtime is garbage or deliberately huge (`touch -d @400000000000000000 x`, a corrupt archive entry, a buggy sync tool) sits in a folder; open that folder in List view (or select the file with the preview pane open). Each frame spends 10 s per such visible row inside format_date, so the window is effectively frozen and must be killed.

```rust
let mut y = 1970u64;
let mut remaining = days;
loop {
    let leap = y % 4 == 0 && (y % 100 != 0 || y % 400 == 0);
    let year_days = if leap { 366 } else { 365 };
    if remaining < year_days {
        break;
    }
    remaining -= year_days;
    y += 1;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** The year is found by subtracting one year per iteration, called per visible row per frame (views.rs:335) with nothing cached; preview.rs:218 has the same loop. I confirmed in the scratchpad (btrfs) that `touch -d @4000000000000000` is accepted and stat reports year 126756924, which is 126 million iterations per call. Only pre-1970 times are guarded. The trigger needs a garbage or deliberate mtime, which is rare, but the result is a frozen window every time that folder is opened in List view.

**Fix.** Same fix as render-6: libc::localtime_r, returning "--" on failure (it fails cleanly on overflow). If the hand-rolled path is kept, use constant-time civil-from-days arithmetic and clamp absurd years.

### wayland-5: Pinned tabs restored at startup: switch_tab(0) is a no-op, so the first pinned tab shows the start directory and is then overwritten by it

- **Where:** `src/wayland.rs:493` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Restored pinned tabs: the start tab's index is set correctly before switching, and an explicit start directory stays active.
- **Also reported as:** app-7

**What is wrong.** navigate_to(home/start_dir) has already filled the flat fields (current_dir, entries) for the original tab. The pinned tabs are then prepended, `current_tab` is set to 0 and switch_tab(0) is called, but switch_tab returns early when `index == self.current_tab`, so sync_from_tab never runs.
