Result: current_tab points at the first pinned tab while the flat fields still hold the start directory. The tab bar highlights the pinned tab, the view and path bar show Home. The first later sync_to_tab() (any tab switch, new tab, close tab) writes Home's path and entries into tabs[0], so the pinned folder is unreachable for the session. pinned_path survives, so it is re-saved and the same thing happens on the next launch.

**Trigger.** Pin a tab on e.g. ~/Projects (double-click the tab), quit, relaunch. The 'Projects' tab is highlighted but Home is listed. Click the trailing home tab and then the pinned tab again: it is now labelled and listing Home. (Not currently visible on this machine because file-manager.json has `pinned_tabs: []`.)

```rust
pinned.append(&mut app.tabs);
app.tabs = pinned;
app.current_tab = 0;
app.switch_tab(0);
```

**Skeptic's check.** app/tabs.rs switch_tab returns when index == self.current_tab, and wayland.rs sets current_tab = 0 immediately before calling switch_tab(0), so sync_from_tab never runs. The flat fields still describe the home/start-dir tab while current_tab points at the first pinned tab. The next sync_to_tab (any tab switch, new tab, close tab) writes the home path and entries into tabs[0]; DirectoryTab::label() comes from path, so the tab is relabelled. pinned_path survives and is re-saved on exit, so it repeats every launch. Latent here only because pinned_tabs is empty in file-manager.json.

**Fix.** `let n = pinned.len(); pinned.append(&mut app.tabs); app.tabs = pinned; app.current_tab = n; app.switch_tab(0);` With current_tab = n, switch_tab(0) parks the home state in the home tab and loads the pinned tab. When an explicit start_dir was passed, skip the switch_tab so the requested directory stays active. In Tree view the tree must also be rebuilt after the switch (see the extra finding on tab switches).

### input-15: selection_anchor is never reset: Shift+click after navigating or re-sorting selects a range from a stale index

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/click.rs:447` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. selection_anchor and last_click_idx are reset on navigation/tab change and remapped by path on every re-list.

**What is wrong.** The anchor is an index that is only ever overwritten by clicks. navigate_to, go_back, tab switches, sort changes, reloads and clear_selection leave it alone. In single-click mode the click that enters a folder sets the anchor to that folder's index in the parent listing, so the first Shift+click in the new folder selects everything between the clicked row and that unrelated index (select_range clamps to the end of the list). Much of that range can be off-screen.

**Trigger.** Click the 40th folder to enter it. In the new folder Shift+click item 3: items 3 through 40 become selected (or 3 through the end if the folder is shorter). Press Delete: all of them go to the trash, including rows scrolled out of view.

```rust
} else if !shift {
    app.select_item(idx);
    app.selection_anchor = Some(idx);
}
// wayland_loop.rs:985
let anchor = app.selection_anchor.unwrap_or(idx);
app.select_range(anchor, idx);
```

**Skeptic's check.** grep selection_anchor: written at click.rs:352/365/386/444/447 and wayland_loop.rs:987, swapped in split.rs, never cleared. navigate_to, go_back, go_forward, sync_from_tab, sort changes and reloads leave it. The click that enters folder #40 sets the anchor to 40 at press; in the new folder a Shift+click on item 3 runs select_range(40, 3) on release (wayland_loop.rs:985-986), selecting 3 through 40, clamped to the list end, much of it possibly off screen.

**Fix.** Set selection_anchor = None and last_click_idx = None in navigate_to, go_back, go_forward and sync_from_tab, and when the sort order changes. A watcher reload can still shift indices under the anchor; storing the anchor as a path and resolving it at use is the complete fix.

### sections-9: Drive context-menu actions resolve a stale list index, and same-named drives are re-ordered randomly every 2 s

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:210` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it high; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Drive order has a device tie-break; drive context actions carry the device path instead of a list index.
- **Also reported as:** app-10

**What is wrong.** The right-click stores an index into app.drives; Eject / Format / Properties look that index up again when the menu item is chosen. In between, wayland_loop.rs:1350-1354 replaces app.drives wholesale every 2 s (frames render continuously while a menu is open). detect_drives builds the list out of HashMap iteration (by_device, by_disk) and then sorts stably by (rank, name) only, so drives with equal names keep HashMap order, which differs per HashMap instance: a rustc experiment showed the order flipping in 23 of 40 rebuilds. Two identical USB sticks both get the vendor+model name from disk_friendly_name, so their rows swap at random. Left-click zones (ZONE_DRIVE_ITEM_BASE + i) have the same index-vs-refreshed-list problem.

**Trigger.** Plug in two USB sticks of the same model. The two Devices rows swap places every couple of seconds. Right-click stick A, choose 'Format to ext4...' a moment later: half the time the confirm dialog (which shows the same name for both) is for stick B, and confirming wipes B's whole disk. Eject acts on the wrong stick with no confirmation. Any hot-plug or mount-state change while the menu is open shifts the index the same way for differently-named drives.

```rust
app.context_target = Some(ContextTarget::Drive(i));
// later, context_menu.rs:739-746
CTX_DRIVE_EJECT => { if let Some(ContextTarget::Drive(idx)) = ... { app.eject_drive(idx); } }
CTX_DRIVE_FORMAT => { ... app.open_drive_format_dialog(idx); }
// fs.rs:227   ord(a).cmp(&ord(b)).then_with(|| a.name.cmp(&b.name))
```

**Skeptic's check.** The right-click stores ContextTarget::Drive(i); Eject, Format and Properties resolve that index later through app/places.rs, which only bounds-checks. wayland_loop.rs:1350-1354 replaces app.drives every 2 s regardless of an open menu. fs.rs group_removable_by_disk iterates a HashMap and detect_drives then sorts stably by (rank, name) only, so two removable disks with the same vendor+model name and the same mount state keep HashMap order, which differs per instance. A hot-plug or mount-state change while the menu is open shifts indices for differently named drives too. I rate it medium rather than high: it needs identical sticks or a device change inside the menu window, and the Format dialog shows Device and Size, though that does not help tell identical sticks apart.

**Fix.** Carry the device string in ContextTarget::Drive and resolve with drives.iter().find(|d| d.device == dev) at action time, doing nothing if it is gone; add .then_with(|| a.device.cmp(&b.device)) to the sort so the order is deterministic. on_drive_click has the same index-versus-refreshed-list shape with a one-frame window.

### input-14: Context-menu target is a raw index: a reload while the menu is open makes Rename, Copy Path, Properties, Set as Wallpaper hit another file

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:599` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Context-menu item target is remapped by path on every re-list (None if the file is gone).
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-18

**What is wrong.** ContextTarget::Item(idx) is captured when the menu opens and applied when an action is chosen. The loop keeps running while the popup is open, so the directory watcher can reload and re-sort the listing in between. Index-based actions (Rename, Copy Path/Name, Set as Wallpaper, Properties, Add/Remove Favorite) then address a different entry. Selection-based actions are fine because selection is remapped by path. ContextTarget::Drive(idx) has the same shape against the 2-second drive refresh (wrong drive in the Format dialog after a hot-plug).

**Trigger.** Right-click a file in a folder that is changing (download completing, build output). While the menu is open a file sorted earlier appears or disappears. Click Rename: the rename box opens on the neighbouring file with its name preselected, and typing plus Enter renames that one.

```rust
app.context_target = Some(ContextTarget::Item(idx));   // line 367, at menu open
...
CTX_RENAME => {
    if let Some(ContextTarget::Item(idx)) = app.context_target {
        app.start_rename(idx);
```

**Skeptic's check.** ContextTarget::Item(idx) is stored at context_menu.rs:367 and used by Rename (:600), Copy Path/Name, Set as Wallpaper, Change Icon, Properties (:709) and the favorite helpers, all bounds-checked but none identity-checked. The loop keeps running while the popup is open, and the watcher reload (wayland_loop.rs:1299) and mtime poll (:1345) call app.reload(); nav.rs apply_listing remaps selection and an active rename by path but not context_target. Selection-based actions are unaffected. The window is only as long as the menu stays open in a changing folder.

**Fix.** Local option: in apply_listing, capture the path of a ContextTarget::Item before replacing entries and re-resolve it afterwards (None if gone), exactly as is done for `renaming`. Alternative: store the path in the target and resolve with position() at action time.

### input-11: Trash, Duplicate, Compress, Extract and Open as Root from a nested tree row do nothing or act on the wrong thing

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:608` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C2): Same change as fileops-20 for Trash, Duplicate, Compress and Extract. Open as Root (src/row_actions.rs open_as_root + root_folder) now uses the menu's target: a folder row opens that folder with root mode on; a file row in an expanded subfolder opens the folder the file is in with root mode on; only when the target is in the folder already shown does it toggle root mode for it, as before. It no longer flips the current folder into root mode when a nested folder was clicked. Folder-ness comes from the listing, not from a stat. Test: row_actions_work_from_the_row_not_from_the_shown_folder.

**What is wrong.** For a nested tree row the menu is built with every action, the selection is cleared and the target lives only in context_override_paths. But trash_selected, delete_selected, duplicate_selected, compress_selected, extract_selected, restore_selected and open_as_root all iterate `self.entries` filtered by `selected`, not selected_paths(). So those items silently do nothing. Open as Root is worse: with no selected folder it falls into `self.root_mode = !self.root_mode`, switching the CURRENT directory into root mode, where Delete and Move to Trash become permanent sudo deletes.

**Trigger.** Tree view, expand a folder, right-click a file inside it, Move to Trash: nothing happens. Right-click a nested folder, Open as Root: the folder does not open, but the current directory is now in root mode; the next Delete key press permanently deletes (silently if a sudo ticket is cached).

```rust
CTX_TRASH => {
    if app.in_trash() { app.delete_selected(); } else { app.trash_selected(); }
}
...
CTX_DUPLICATE => app.duplicate_selected(),
CTX_COMPRESS => app.compress_selected(),
CTX_EXTRACT => app.extract_selected(),
CTX_OPEN_AS_ROOT => app.open_as_root(),
```

**Skeptic's check.** For a nested tree row handle_right_click clears the selection (:402) and builds the full item menu with allow_rename = false only. trash_selected, delete_selected, restore_selected, duplicate_selected, compress_selected, extract_selected and open_as_root (file_ops.rs) all filter self.entries by `selected`, not selected_paths(), so those items do nothing. open_as_root with no selected folder falls into `self.root_mode = !self.root_mode` (file_ops.rs:1008), switching the current directory into root mode, where trash_selected becomes delete_selected via sudo. Root mode has no on-screen indicator: the only reference outside file ops is the dead window_title().

**Fix.** Either make those methods take their targets from selected_paths() (and have open_as_root use the context target instead of toggling when one exists), or hide the unsupported items for nested rows as Rename already is. The first changes what gets trashed or deleted, so it is Alva's call. Root mode needs a visible indicator either way.

### input-22: Properties from the context menu reads audio tags and stats on the UI thread with no slow-mount guard

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:731` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Same change as dialogs-7: the context-menu handler (src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs) calls App::open_properties, which does no I/O. Then (lead, after batch 2): props_load.rs now carries the AudioMeta its worker read to the dialog, and AudioEdit::from_meta builds the editor from it: the render thread no longer reads the file at all (AudioEdit::load is gone).

**What is wrong.** FileProperties::from_path (properties.rs:151-224) does symlink_metadata, metadata, read_dir().count() for folders, statvfs and, for WAV/MP3, AudioEdit::load -> audio_tags::read, all synchronously in the menu action handler. On jmtpfs the first read of a file downloads all of it under the global lock, so the app is frozen for the whole transfer. Thumbnails and file_info already skip slow paths; this path does not.

**Trigger.** Browse a phone, right-click an .mp3 (or any file while another transfer holds the lock), Properties: the window stops responding until the file has been pulled off the phone.

```rust
if let Some(mut props) = crate::properties::FileProperties::from_path(&path) {
    props.populate_media_info(file_info);
    app.properties = Some(props);
```

**Skeptic's check.** FileProperties::from_path (properties.rs:151-224) runs symlink_metadata, metadata, read_dir().count() for folders, disk_usage and, for WAV/MP3, AudioEdit::load -> audio_tags::read, all synchronously inside the menu action handler. grep shows no is_slow_path in properties.rs or properties_audio. On jmtpfs the tag read pulls the whole file and even the stats wait on the device lock. I did not read wav::read / mp3::read line by line; any read on that mount triggers the download.

**Fix.** On slow paths build the dialog from the FileEntry already in memory and skip AudioEdit::load, or load on a worker and fill the section in when ready. Skipping the tag load alone is small, but the stat calls still block, so the real fix moves work off the UI thread.

### input-27: "New Folder" silently does nothing when a folder named "New Folder" already exists

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:865` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. New Folder picks a free name (New Folder 2, 3, ...).
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-17

**What is wrong.** The name is fixed and AlreadyExists falls into the ignore-all arm. If the user dismissed the rename box (Esc, or clicked away without typing) the folder keeps the default name, and every later New Folder click in that directory, including the colour swatches, is a no-op with no feedback.

**Trigger.** Right-click, New Folder, press Esc. Right-click, New Folder again: nothing is created.

```rust
let target = app.current_dir.join("New Folder");
...
match std::fs::create_dir(&target) {
    Ok(()) => { ... }
    Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied => { ... }
    Err(_) => {}
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Both New Folder handlers pass current_dir/"New Folder" and new_folder_or_prompt calls fs::create_dir; AlreadyExists falls into `Err(_) => {}` at :884. Cancelling the rename (Esc, or committing the unchanged name) leaves the default name in place, after which every New Folder in that directory, including the colour swatches, is a silent no-op.

**Fix.** Pick a free name first (New Folder, New Folder 2, ...) and then continue with the existing select + start_rename path. Apply the same naming to the root-mode / sudo path, which passes the same fixed target.

### input-20: Dropping files on the Trash sidebar entry moves them into Trash/files with no trashinfo

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/drag_drop.rs:80` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C2): C1 had already made a Move into a trash folder call trash_paths in start_paste. On top of that, src/wayland_actions/drag_drop.rs now has queue_drop: a drop whose destination is inside any trash (sidebar Trash, a favourite, tab or split pane showing it, a folder inside it) calls the new App::drop_on_trash directly, with restore records and one undo entry, and no Move/Copy question. drop_on_trash filters out items that are already in a trash; start_paste uses it too, so cut+paste inside the Trash no longer raises a delete question.

**What is wrong.** The Trash place is an ordinary Place whose path is ~/.local/share/Trash/files. A drop there shows the generic Move/Copy modal and then renames (or copies) the items straight into that directory. No info/<name>.trashinfo is written, so restore_selected() later logs 'missing or unreadable' and skips them, and a name collision raises the Replace dialog against an older trashed item. Copy puts a duplicate in the trash.

**Trigger.** Drag a file onto Trash in the sidebar, choose Move. It shows up in Trash, but Restore on it does nothing.

```rust
if place_idx < places.len() {
    let dest_dir = places[place_idx].path.clone();
    if valid_dest(&dest_dir, &sources) {
        app.pending_drop = Some(PendingDrop { sources, dest_dir, reload_tab: None });
```

**Skeptic's check.** app/mod.rs:349/386 builds Trash as an ordinary Place whose path is ~/.local/share/Trash/files. A drop on it goes through the generic PendingDrop -> Move/Copy modal -> rename or copy straight into that directory. No info/<name>.trashinfo is written, so restore_selected() (file_ops.rs:533-540) logs 'missing or unreadable' and skips the item, a name collision raises Replace against an older trashed item, and Copy leaves a duplicate in the trash. Dragging onto Trash is a natural gesture.

**Fix.** Special-case the Trash place in handle_drop and call a path-based trash routine (trash_selected currently works only on the entries selection, so it needs a trash_paths(&[PathBuf]) variant) instead of creating a PendingDrop. This changes what the drop does, so it is a behaviour decision.

### input-12: Delete key inside the Trash re-trashes the item instead of deleting it, and breaks its restore info

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:751` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C2): src/trash_ops.rs trash_selected now decides per item: anything already inside a trash (any volume's) is a permanent delete that asks first (new App::confirm_delete_permanently and permanent_delete_question in src/op_dialogs.rs, reusing the ConfirmDelete dialog: Cancel / Delete Permanently, Enter never confirms); on confirm the ops worker deletes the item and trash::forget removes its .trashinfo. Everything else goes to the Trash as before. The Delete key and the menu's Move to Trash / Delete Permanently both call this one function (src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs), so the key and the menu can no longer disagree.
- **Also reported as:** fileops-21

**What is wrong.** The context menu branches on app.in_trash() (context_menu.rs:604-610) but the key does not. trash_selected() run inside ~/.local/share/Trash/files finds the name already present (it is the item itself), picks `name.1.ext`, writes a new `name.1.ext.trashinfo` whose Path= points into the trash, and renames the item. The original trashinfo is orphaned. Restore then 'restores' the item back into the trash under its old name.

**Trigger.** Open Trash, select foo.txt, press Delete. Instead of being deleted it becomes foo.1.txt; each further press adds another suffix. Restore on it does not return it to its original folder.

```rust
KEY_DELETE if app.pick.is_none() => app.trash_selected(),
```

**Skeptic's check.** key.rs:751 calls app.trash_selected() with no in_trash test, while context_menu.rs:604-609 branches to delete_selected. trash_selected (file_ops.rs:565-638) only special-cases root_mode. Inside Trash/files the entry's own name already exists, so the loop picks foo.1.txt, writes foo.1.txt.trashinfo with Path= pointing into the trash, renames the item and pushes an undo entry; the original foo.txt.trashinfo is orphaned. Restore then moves it back to Trash/files/foo.txt (where the old trashinfo happens to match again, so a second Restore works). Nothing is lost, but Delete in Trash never deletes and mangles names.

**Fix.** Two options and it is a product decision: (a) mirror the menu, `if app.in_trash() { app.delete_selected() } else { app.trash_selected() }`, which makes the Delete key permanently delete with no confirmation; (b) make trash_selected return early when in_trash() so the key is a no-op there. Either way add the in_trash guard inside trash_selected itself. Not marked safe because (a) changes what a key press deletes.

### wayland-12: wl_keyboard keymap is ignored, so the compositor's synthetic-keymap text injection (Command Center emoji picker) is read as Escape or digits

- **Where:** `src/wayland_dispatch.rs:353` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): New src/keyboard.rs (xkbcommon 0.9 added to Cargo.toml). wl_keyboard Keymap events are now handled (src/wayland_dispatch.rs): the keymap is mmapped (with a read-at-offset-0 fallback) and every key press is translated at dispatch time with the keymap and modifiers current at that moment, so the compositor's text-injection batch (keymap swap, taps, swap back) is read as the characters it carries. A key that only types text gets no command code, whatever its position: the injected key on evdev 1 is no longer Escape. Unit test builds the same keymap text_inject.rs builds and checks a thumbs-up plus a two-scalar heart arrive as three characters.

**What is wrong.** Fox interprets `key` as a raw evdev code on a fixed US layout and never looks at the Keymap event. lntrn-compositor/src/text_inject.rs focuses the last toplevel, swaps in a throwaway keymap whose first key is xkb keycode 9, taps one key per Unicode scalar, and swaps back. xkb 9 arrives as wl_keyboard key 1, which Fox's key.rs treats as KEY_ESC; the next scalars arrive as 2, 3, ... which are '1', '2', ... Because all taps arrive in one batch and key_pressed is a single slot, only the last one is acted on.

**Trigger.** Fox is the focused window. Open the Command Center emoji picker and click a single-scalar emoji such as 👍. Fox receives Escape: an in-progress rename is cancelled, search closes, a conflict/paste dialog is cancelled, and in `--pick` mode the picker exits as cancelled. An emoji with a variation selector (❤️, two scalars) types '1' into the rename field instead.

```rust
wl_keyboard::Event::Key { key, state: key_state, .. } => {
    if key_state == WEnum::Value(wl_keyboard::KeyState::Pressed) {
        state.key_pressed = Some(key);
...
_ => {}   // Keymap event dropped
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read /home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-compositor/src/text_inject.rs in full: it focuses the focused toplevel, swaps in a throwaway keymap with FIRST_KEYCODE = 9, calls input_forward press+release per scalar, restores the keymap and focus, all in one turn. Fox's wl_keyboard Dispatch (wayland_dispatch.rs:352-377) matches only Key and Modifiers; Keymap falls to `_ => {}`. xkb keycode 9 is wire key 1 = KEY_ESC in key.rs; later scalars map to digits. Since key_pressed is one slot, only the last tap is acted on. So with Fox focused, a single-scalar emoji is read as Escape (cancels rename / search / dialogs; in --pick with no field focused it cancels the picker). I did not run it, and I did not verify which CC actions actually call type_text_into_focused_window, hence PLAUSIBLE rather than CONFIRMED.

**Fix.** Proper fix is decoding keys through the delivered keymap, which is a real piece of work without an xkb crate. Minimal stopgap: handle wl_keyboard::Event::Keymap, remember the first keymap (size + hash of the mmapped fd), and drop Key events while a different keymap is active. That needs mmap of the fd and changes key handling, so not mechanical. Could mis-fire if the compositor ever legitimately changes layout at runtime.

### input-18: Only one pending key press is kept per frame: fast typing or a slow frame drops characters

- **Where:** `src/wayland_dispatch.rs:359` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): src/keyboard.rs: presses are pushed to a VecDeque in dispatch order, each carrying the ctrl/shift/logo state of its own moment; src/wayland_loop.rs drains the whole queue each frame through handle_key. State.key_pressed/held_key/repeat_* /ctrl/shift/logo were replaced by State.kbd (src/wayland.rs). Seat capabilities announced again no longer create a second wl_keyboard/wl_pointer (which would now double every key). Mouse buttons are unchanged: still one press and one release flag per frame (not part of the brief for this id).
- **Also reported as:** wayland-13

**What is wrong.** key_pressed is a single Option consumed once per rendered frame (wayland_loop.rs:499). All events that arrive during the 16 ms frame sleep, a render, or any blocking call on the UI thread (directory reload, xdg-mime query, sudo, sign-in) are dispatched together, and each press overwrites the previous one. Modifier state is likewise only the latest value, so a Shift released in the same batch lower-cases the earlier letter. left_pressed/left_released are booleans with the same collapse for two clicks in one batch.

**Trigger.** Type quickly in the search box, rename box or sudo password field, or type while a large folder is reloading: letters go missing; a missing letter in the sudo prompt reads as a wrong password.

```rust
if key_state == WEnum::Value(wl_keyboard::KeyState::Pressed) {
    state.key_pressed = Some(key);
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland_dispatch.rs:358-359 overwrites state.key_pressed on every press; wayland_loop.rs:499 takes it once per rendered frame and passes the current state.ctrl/shift. Loop order is poll/read/dispatch, then sleep to the 16 ms budget (134-138), then the frame, so every press that arrives during sleep + render (and during any blocking call such as submit_cloud_login, a sync reload, sudo) is dispatched in one batch and only the last survives. Modifiers are sampled at frame time, so a Shift release in the same batch lower-cases the letter. Mechanism is certain; frequency depends on typing speed and frame time (two presses within roughly 16-25 ms, or any typing during a UI stall).

**Fix.** Replace key_pressed with a VecDeque<(u32 key, bool ctrl, bool shift, bool logo)> pushed in the Key handler (capturing modifier state at press time) and drain it in the keyboard section. Keep held_key/repeat as is but re-arm the repeat deadline after draining. Local to State, wayland_dispatch.rs and the keyboard block, but it is a small refactor that makes several handle_key calls per frame, so review rather than auto-apply.

### wayland-3: held_key is never cleared on keyboard focus loss, so Alt+Tab (or any shortcut that moves focus) leaves Fox rendering at 60 fps forever

- **Where:** `src/wayland_dispatch.rs:364` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Keyboard Leave clears held_key and modifiers.
- **Also reported as:** input-17

**What is wrong.** held_key is set on every key press, including bare modifiers, and only cleared by the matching release. wl_keyboard Leave is not handled. The Lantern compositor forwards a bare Alt/Super press to the focused client (input/keyboard.rs falls through to FilterResult::Forward), intercepts Tab, and intercepts the Alt release while the switcher is active; for Super+Return / Super+<n> the release goes to the newly focused surface. Fox therefore keeps held_key = Some(Alt/Super) indefinitely. needs_anim includes `state.held_key.is_some()`, so the loop forces a full render every 16 ms (plus 4 config-file reads and a new wl_callback per frame) until some key is pressed and released in Fox again. This is the idle spin the loop comments say was removed. If Fox is on a hidden workspace or minimized, the per-frame `surface.frame()` callbacks are never answered and pile up on both sides (not verified end to end). Secondary effect: if the stuck key is a character and a text mode is active, the repeat block re-sends it every 30 ms.

**Trigger.** Focus Fox, press Alt+Tab to another window (or Super+Return to open a terminal, or Super+2 to switch workspace). Fox now redraws continuously in the background: visible as constant GPU/CPU use from lntrn-file-manager until you click into Fox and tap any key.

```rust
} else if key_state == WEnum::Value(wl_keyboard::KeyState::Released) {
    if state.held_key == Some(key) {
        state.held_key = None;
    }
}
...
_ => {}   // wl_keyboard::Event::Leave is ignored
// wayland_loop.rs:1363   || state.held_key.is_some()
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland_dispatch.rs:358-368 sets held_key on any press and clears it only on the matching release; Leave is not handled. wayland_loop.rs:1363 puts held_key.is_some() in needs_anim, and line 145 then forces frame_done every 16 ms. Read /home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-compositor/src/input/keyboard.rs: a bare Alt press reaches the final FilterResult::Forward (614), Alt+Tab press is Intercepted (204-216), and the Alt release while the switcher is active is Intercepted (240-251). So after any Alt+Tab from Fox, Fox holds held_key = Some(56) forever and renders at ~60 fps (plus the 4 config reads per frame) until a key is pressed and released inside Fox again. Super+Return and similar focus-moving chords do the same because the release goes to the new focus. Each such frame also requests a wl_callback, which piles up if the window is not being shown (not verified end to end).

**Fix.** Handle wl_keyboard::Event::Leave: held_key = None, repeat_started = false, clear ctrl/shift/logo (Smithay re-sends modifiers on enter), set frame_done. Do NOT clear key_pressed there as the finder suggests: a press and a leave can arrive in the same batch and the press was legitimately for Fox. Optionally also only count held_key toward needs_anim when a text mode is active.

### wayland-7: A drag that leaves the window becomes a Wayland DnD that Fox itself can never receive: dropping back into Fox does nothing (dnd_drop_on_self is never read)

- **Where:** `src/wayland_dispatch.rs:449` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** partly fixed. Batch 2 (C5): New src/dnd_in.rs: Fox is now a drop target. On wl_data_device enter/motion the folder under the pointer is resolved with the same function an in-window drop uses (src/wayland_actions/drag_drop.rs was split into drop_target_at + apply_drop; handle_drop keeps its behaviour), the offer is accepted or refused per position (accept + set_actions), and a hint pill at the bottom of the window says where the drop goes. On drop the text/uri-list offer is read through a pipe on a bg::Task (10 s timeout, 8 MiB cap), parsed (local file: URIs only, absolute, no '..'), then finish/destroy and the paths go through apply_drop: the same Move/Copy/Cancel dialog (or Trash / pin to Favorites). Works for a Fox drag that left the window and came back, for another Fox window and for other programs. Drops are refused in picker mode and while any modal is open. Dead fields dnd_over_self/dnd_drop_on_self/dnd_cursor_* removed; offers carry their MIME list as user data. New src/dnd_out.rs: the data source's Send is written on a thread so a slow receiver cannot stall the window. REMAINS: The inverse gap from the finding is still there: a drag only becomes a compositor drag when the pointer leaves Fox's rectangle, so dropping on another window that overlaps Fox is still handled as an in-window drop at that position. Closing it means handing every drag to the compositor at drag start and drawing the ghost, row dimming and tab/sidebar highlights from data-device motion instead of pointer motion; that is a redesign of the in-window drag visuals that I did not want to make without being able to run the app. Also cosmetic: after the hand-off there is no drag picture (start_drag is still called without an icon surface); only the hint pill shows where a drop would go.
- **Also reported as:** input-19

**What is wrong.** The moment the cursor crosses the window edge by one pixel, the internal drag is cancelled and the compositor owns the drag. Fox as a target never calls offer.accept()/set_actions/receive, and the fields the handlers fill in (dnd_over_self, dnd_cursor_x/y, dnd_drop_on_self) are not read anywhere outside wayland_dispatch.rs. So a drop back on Fox is rejected and the source gets Cancelled. The inverse gap exists too: a drag is only handed to Wayland when the pointer leaves Fox's rectangle, so dropping onto another window that overlaps Fox is treated as an internal drop at that position (possibly onto a folder hidden under the other window).

**Trigger.** Floating Fox window: drag a file toward a sidebar favourite or a tab near the window edge and overshoot the edge slightly, then come back and drop on the folder. The drag ghost vanished at the edge and the drop does nothing. Or: with a small window floating over a maximized Fox, drag a file onto it; the other app never sees a drop.

```rust
wl_data_device::Event::Drop => {
    if state.dnd_over_self {
        state.dnd_drop_on_self = true;
        state.frame_done = true;
    }
}
// wayland_loop.rs:464
if raw_cx < 0.0 || raw_cy < 0.0 || raw_cx > logical_w || raw_cy > logical_h {
    ... dd.start_drag(Some(&source), surf, None, state.dnd_serial);
    state.dnd_active = true;
    app.drag_item = None; app.drag_tree_item = None; app.drag_pos = None;
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland_loop.rs:456-485 starts a wl_data_device drag as soon as the surface-local cursor leaves [0,w]x[0,h] during an internal drag (coordinates keep flowing under the implicit grab) and clears drag_item/drag_tree_item/drag_pos. As a target Fox does nothing: Dispatch<WlDataOffer> is empty, no accept/set_actions/receive anywhere, and grep shows dnd_over_self / dnd_cursor_x/y / dnd_drop_on_self are only written in wayland_dispatch.rs and initialised in wayland.rs, never read. So re-entering and dropping on a Fox folder ends in Cancelled and nothing happens. The inverse claim also holds: until the pointer leaves Fox's rectangle the drag stays internal, so a drop on a window overlapping Fox is handled by handle_drop at that position.

**Fix.** Needs a design choice: either implement self-drop (accept text/uri-list on Enter over our own surface, on Drop run handle_drop with state.dnd_paths at dnd_cursor_x/y scaled by s, then finish/destroy the offer), or keep the internal drag alive and only hand off when the pointer actually enters another surface. Either touches DnD protocol handling. Also destroy the wl_data_source on DndFinished/Cancelled.

### wayland-8: text/uri-list for outgoing drags is not percent-encoded

- **Where:** `src/wayland_dispatch.rs:479` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. uri-list entries are percent-encoded from raw bytes; text/plain writes raw bytes.
- **Also reported as:** input-35

**What is wrong.** The raw path is pasted after `file://`. Characters that are significant in URIs (`#`, `%`, `?`), spaces and non-ASCII bytes are sent unescaped, and non-UTF-8 names go through the lossy display(). Receivers that parse URIs strictly either reject the entry or resolve a different path: `#` starts a fragment (GLib's g_filename_from_uri refuses URIs containing '#'), and a literal `%41` in a name is decoded to `A`. The write also happens synchronously inside dispatch on the UI thread, so a receiver that never reads a payload larger than the 64 KiB pipe buffer blocks Fox.

**Trigger.** Drag `Track #3.mp3`, `50%off.png` or `a%20b.txt` from Fox into a browser, a GTK app or another file manager. The drop fails or, for `a%20b.txt`, the receiver opens `a b.txt` instead.

```rust
let uri = format!("file://{}\r\n", path.display());
let _ = file.write_all(uri.as_bytes());
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland_dispatch.rs:477-481 writes `file://{path.display()}\r\n` with no escaping, and dnd_paths is filled raw at wayland_loop.rs:393-401/420-426. I checked Lantern's own receivers: lntrn-terminal/src/dnd.rs:291-311 and lntrn-image-viewer/src/dnd.rs:153 percent-decode what they receive, so even inside the ecosystem a file named `a%20b.txt` or `50%41.png` arrives as a different path, and `#`/`?` break strict URI parsers elsewhere. display() is also lossy for non-UTF-8 names. The write is synchronous inside dispatch, so a receiver that does not read a payload larger than the pipe buffer blocks the UI thread.

**Fix.** Percent-encode the raw bytes of each path (OsStrExt::as_bytes) leaving unreserved chars and '/' alone; urlencoding is already a dependency (use encode_binary per component, or a 10-line local encoder). Keep text/plain as the plain path. Lantern receivers already decode, so this cannot break them. Moving the write to a thread is a separate, threading-changing step and not part of the safe fix.

### wayland-2: Idle window never runs its background polls: inotify wake-up is drained and then discarded, so the listing does not refresh until an input event arrives

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:161` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): New src/housekeeping.rs (Housekeeping::tick). Everything that used to sit behind the frame gate in src/wayland_loop.rs now runs on every wake-up of the loop, before the gate, and again right after a frame: watcher re-pointing and debounced reloads (focused and unfocused pane), the 3 s mtime fallback, git poll / follow / periodic rescan, poll_search, poll_op_progress, poll_priv, file_info.poll, expire_root_mode, take_focus_refresh. tick() returns true only when something visible changed; only then is a frame drawn. The poll() timeout is now: 0 when a frame is already owed (a redraw requested after a render used to wait up to 500 ms), 16 ms while animating, otherwise min(500 ms, time until the next scheduled thing) via Housekeeping::wake_at (reload debounce/interval, git cooldown) and the key-repeat deadline. The 16 ms frame-cap sleep moved behind the gate so wake-ups that draw nothing do not sleep. A held key no longer forces 60 fps (it is a timer now). The mtime fallback now also requests a frame when it reloads (it did not before). src/app/search.rs poll_search returns whether results changed.
- **Also reported as:** fs-6, app-24, input-24, render-8

**What is wrong.** Everything after the `continue` is gated on a rendered frame: dir_watcher.take_due_reload(), the 3 s mtime fallback, git.poll(), the 2 s drive/phone poll, poll_dir_loads() and the needs_anim recomputation. The watcher eventfd is polled specifically 'so a file landing in the current directory wakes the loop immediately', but the wake only calls drain_fd(); it never sets frame_done, and `dir_watcher.reload_pending()` is only evaluated at the end of a rendered frame. With no pointer/keyboard events the loop wakes, drains, hits `continue`, and sleeps another 500 ms forever. Same starvation applies to git scan results, hot-plugged drives, and dir loads targeting the unfocused split pane or a background tab (dir_loading() only counts DirLoadTarget::Focused).

**Trigger.** Open Fox on ~/Downloads, move the pointer to another window (or just leave it still), and let a download finish or run `touch ~/Downloads/x` in a terminal. Fox keeps showing the old listing indefinitely. Moving the pointer over Fox renders one frame, after which the debounced reload finally happens. Likewise plug in a USB stick with Fox idle: the sidebar does not update until the mouse moves.

```rust
if ret > 0 && pfds[1].revents & libc::POLLIN != 0 {
    dir_watcher.drain_fd();
}
...
if !state.frame_done {
    continue;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Traced the loop in /home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-file-manager/src/wayland_loop.rs. The watcher eventfd wakes poll and line 122-124 only calls drain_fd (dir_watch.rs:100, a bare read). frame_done is set only by dispatch handlers, needs_anim (145), the theme-variant flip (158), key repeat, poll_dir_loads and file_info.poll (the last two are themselves below the gate). At 161 the loop continues, so take_due_reload (1299), the 3 s mtime fallback (1338), git.poll and the 5 s git rescan (1310-1324), the 2 s drive/phone poll (1350) and the needs_anim recomputation (1356) never run while idle. The dirty flag just sits until an input event produces a frame, then the reload fires about 120 ms later. This is the 'work gated behind a frame' class from the brief.

**Fix.** Correct shape: hoist the non-render housekeeping (take_due_reload, mtime poll, device poll, git.poll, poll_dir_loads, poll_op_progress, file_info.poll) above the `if !state.frame_done { continue; }` gate, set frame_done when any of them changed state, and use a short poll timeout while dir_watcher.reload_pending(). The one-line variant (frame_done = true after drain_fd) works but has a cost to weigh: Modify events from a file being written re-arm the 120 ms debounce, so an idle Fox on ~/Downloads would then render at 60 fps, re-list the directory and spawn a git scan about 8 times a second for the whole download (see wayland-27). Pair it with a rate limit or ignore pure data-modify events. Not mechanical.

### wayland-4: pending_open / drag_item are stale indices after a directory reload: unchecked entries[idx] can panic, or the drag silently picks a different file

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:395` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Drag sources come from the snapshot taken at drag start; drag/pending indices are remapped by path on re-list and indexing is checked. (Also covers the unsafe-rated fs-5.)
- **Also reported as:** app-8, input-13, render-3

**What is wrong.** `pending_open` (set at press) and `drag_item` (held for the whole drag) are indices into app.entries. A reload can happen between press and release: the watcher/mtime reload at the end of every frame, poll_op_progress, poll_dir_loads, or keyboard navigation. App::apply_listing remaps `renaming` and the selection by path but leaves pending_open/drag_item untouched. If the listing shrank, `app.entries[idx]` is out of bounds and panics (the tree variants right below are bounds-checked, these are not). If the listing only shifted, entries[idx] is a different, unselected file, so the 'grabbed item not selected' branch drags that other file, and the Move/Copy drop acts on it. With the user's current settings (sort by date, descending) every new file lands at index 0 and shifts all indices by one.

**Trigger.** In ~/Downloads sorted newest-first, press on a file and start dragging it toward a folder while a download completes or any file is created in the directory: the debounced reload runs mid-drag, and on drop a neighbouring file is moved/copied instead of the grabbed one. Panic variant: press and hold on the last item, press Backspace (go up to a shorter directory) or have a file deleted, then move the mouse 5 px: index out of bounds, Fox crashes.

```rust
if selected.is_empty() || !app.entries[idx].selected {
    vec![app.entries[idx].path.clone()]
...
// line 923 (release):
if selected.is_empty() || !app.entries[drag_idx].selected {
    vec![app.entries[drag_idx].path.clone()]
```

**Skeptic's check.** click.rs:434 stores pending_open = Some(idx) (bounds-checked at press only); wayland_loop.rs:387 promotes it to drag_item. apply_listing (app/nav.rs:165-205) remaps only `renaming` and the selected flags by path. While a drag is live needs_anim is true, so every iteration reaches take_due_reload/mtime poll/poll_op_progress and reload() can replace app.entries; Backspace (go_up) can also run while the button is held. Lines 395-396 and 923-924 then index app.entries[idx] unchecked: out of range panics if the list shrank past the index; if it only shifted, the pressed file is still selected by path but entries[drag_idx] is an unselected neighbour, so the `!app.entries[drag_idx].selected` branch makes the neighbour the sole drag source and the Move/Copy acts on it. on_item_click(idx) at 989 has the same staleness (bounds-checked, wrong file). The tree variants are bounds-checked but equally stale. Needs a create/remove in the viewed directory during the press or drag, so uncommon, but the result is a crash or the wrong file being moved. The tab-reorder aside (line 904) is not reachable: close_tab refuses pinned tabs.

**Fix.** Minimal mechanical fix: at release build `sources` from the state.dnd_paths snapshot taken at drag start (std::mem::take) for both the grid/list and tree drags instead of re-indexing, and use app.entries.get(idx) at 395. That removes the panic and the wrong-file move without changing normal behaviour. Follow-up (not mechanical): store the pressed entry's PathBuf, or remap/clear pending_open, pending_tree_open and drag_item in apply_listing the way `renaming` is, so a click released after a reload cannot open the wrong item.

### wayland-6: Maximized window: clicks within 10 px of any edge are swallowed and start an interactive resize

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:707` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. No edge-resize on press while maximized.
- **Also reported as:** input-33

**What is wrong.** The cursor-shape code a few hundred lines up only shows resize arrows when `!state.maximized`, but the press handler has no such check. When Fox is maximized the 10 px border coincides with the screen edges, which is exactly where the pointer ends up when it is thrown at a corner. The click never reaches handle_click, and the compositor's resize_request starts a ResizeSurfaceGrab regardless of maximized state.

**Trigger.** Maximize Fox with the title bar shown, push the pointer to the very top-right of the screen and click Close (or click anything in the top 10 px, the left 10 px of the sidebar, or the bottom 10 px). Nothing is activated, the cursor shows a normal arrow, and the maximized window enters an interactive resize instead.

```rust
if let Some(toplevel) = toplevel {
    if !handled_scrollbar {
        let border = 10.0 * s;
        if let Some(edge) = edge_resize(cx, cy, wf, hf, border) {
            if let Some(seat) = &state.seat {
                toplevel.resize(seat, state.pointer_serial, edge);
            }
            handled_resize = true;
```

**Skeptic's check.** The cursor-shape branch (270) requires !state.maximized, the press branch (707-716) does not: any press within 10*s px of an edge calls toplevel.resize and sets handled_resize, so handle_click is skipped. Read /home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-compositor/src/handlers/xdg_shell.rs:112-160: resize_request has no maximized guard, it sets the Resizing state and starts a ResizeSurfaceGrab. So on a maximized Fox the outer 10 px are dead for clicks (first 6 px of sidebar rows, bottom of the status bar, the top 4 px of nav buttons with the title bar hidden, the screen-corner pixel of the close button with it shown) and a drag from there resizes a maximized window.

**Fix.** Add `&& !state.maximized` to the press-path condition, matching the cursor-shape branch. Only maximized is parsed from the configure states today; fullscreen/tiled could be added the same way later.

### input-16: Clicks on modal dialogs fall through to rubber-band selection, and right-click is not blocked by any modal

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:780` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Modal branches return ClickAction::Consumed; the loop no longer starts a window move or rubber band under a modal.

**What is wrong.** handle_click returns ClickAction::None for every press swallowed by Quick Look, the conflict dialog, the sudo prompt, the cloud login and the drive dialog. The caller treats None as 'clicked empty content': it clears the selection and starts a rubber band under the dialog, which then selects whatever the pointer crosses until release (or drags the window if the press was in the title strip). Separately, handle_right_click (context_menu.rs:121) has no modal checks at all, so a right-click with any of those dialogs, the Properties dialog or the drop modal open still opens the file menu underneath; its actions can overwrite sudo_prompt or pending_paste mid-flow.

**Trigger.** Select a photo, press Space for Quick Look, click to dismiss it: the photo is no longer selected, and if the mouse moved a pixel the item under the pointer is selected instead, so a following Delete trashes that one. Or: with the conflict dialog open, right-click, Copy then Paste: the first paste's queued work is discarded.

```rust
ClickAction::None => {
    ...
    } else if app.pending_open.is_none() && app.pending_tree_open.is_none()
        && app.preview_drag.is_none() && !app.suppress_rubber_band {
        let cr = active_content_rect(app, wf, hf, s);
        if cr.contains(cx, cy) {
            app.clear_selection();
            app.rubber_band_start = Some((cx, cy));
```

**Skeptic's check.** Every modal branch in click.rs:41-138 (Quick Look, conflict, sudo, cloud login, drive dialog) ends in `return ClickAction::None`. The caller (wayland_loop.rs:772-804) treats None as an empty-content press: a window move if cy is in the title strip, otherwise clear_selection() and rubber_band_start when the point is inside the content rect and no pending_open is set. So dismissing Quick Look by clicking its backdrop deselects the previewed file, and any pointer motion before release band-selects whatever is under the pointer; a following Delete then acts on that. The right-click half is the same defect as dialogs-12.

**Fix.** Add a ClickAction::Consumed variant, return it from the modal branches (and from the commit-on-click-outside returns for rename/path edit if desired), and have the loop do nothing for it. Mechanical; the only behaviour removed is the accidental rubber band / window move under a modal.

### app-9: selection_anchor (and last_click_idx) are never reset or remapped on reload, sort, navigation or tab switch: Shift+click selects a range from a stale index

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:985` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. selection_anchor and last_click_idx are reset on navigation/tab change and remapped by path on every re-list.

**What is wrong.** selection_anchor is an index into entries set on click. Nothing clears it in navigate_to, go_back/forward, apply_listing (which carefully remaps renaming and selection by path), apply_sort_selection, or sync_from_tab; it is only swapped between split panes. After the listing changes, the first Shift+click selects from the old index: extra unrelated items get selected (select_range is additive), and a following Delete/Cut acts on them. Same staleness lets `renaming` leak across tabs through the context-menu tab chevrons (CTX_PREV_TAB/CTX_NEXT_TAB -> switch_tab while a rename is open): the rename box then points at index N of the other tab and Enter renames that unrelated file. last_click_idx being stale makes the 400 ms double-click test compare indices from different listings.

**Trigger.** Click item #12 in folder A (anchor=12), click into a subfolder (single-click navigation), Shift+click item #2 there: items 2..12 are selected instead of one. Or click a file, change Sort By, Shift+click another: the range starts at whatever now sits at the old position. With date-desc sorting any new file landing in the folder shifts the anchor by one.

```rust
let anchor = app.selection_anchor.unwrap_or(idx);
app.select_range(anchor, idx);
app.selection_anchor = Some(idx);
```

**Skeptic's check.** grep shows selection_anchor is only assigned in click.rs and wayland_loop.rs:987 and swapped in split.rs; navigate_to/go_back/go_forward/apply_listing/sync_from_tab never clear or remap it (navigate_to only resets last_click_path). Shift+click release (983-987) uses selection_anchor.unwrap_or(idx) and select_range is additive, so after navigating, re-sorting, switching tab or an auto-reload the range starts from a stale index and extra items are selected; a following Delete/Cut acts on them. last_click_idx is stale the same way. The rename-across-tabs claim is also real and worse than described (see extra finding): focus_pane is a no-op without split, so switch_tab does not commit or cancel a rename.

**Fix.** Set selection_anchor = None and last_click_idx = None in navigate_to, go_back, go_forward, sync_from_tab and on sort change; in apply_listing capture the anchor entry's path before the swap and re-resolve it afterwards like renaming_path. Mechanical; worst case a Shift+click after a reload selects one item instead of a range.

### render-5: List view cannot be scrolled to its end: scroll-range helper omits the 32*m*s header that render.rs adds, so the last row stays hidden

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:1399` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. List view scroll range includes the 32px header.

**What is wrong.** render.rs sizes the List ScrollArea as rows + header (rows start at base_y + hdr_h), but `view_content_height` (documented as mirroring render.rs) and the rubber-band auto-scroll use `layout::list_content_height`, which has no header term. Wheel scrolling clamps its target to `total_h - content.h` (wayland_loop.rs:575-578) and scrollbar dragging maps through the same total (wayland_loop.rs:304-307, 689-695), so both stop 32*m*s px short of the real maximum. Rows are 40*m*s tall, so only the top 8*m*s px of the last row is visible and its name is not. The search-results branch and `inactive_view_content_height` both add the header correctly; only the focused pane's normal List mode is wrong. Related mirror mismatches nearby: `apply_icon_zoom` (wayland_loop.rs:1406) always clamps against the GRID height even in List/Tree mode, and the scrollbar-grab path at :688 uses `content_rect(wf, hf, s)` instead of the split/pick-aware `active_content_rect`, so in split view (or pick mode) the grabbed thumb geometry differs from the drawn one and the view jumps on press.

**Trigger.** Open a folder with more files than fit, switch to List view, scroll the wheel (or drag the scrollbar thumb) all the way down: the last file is cut off under the status bar and cannot be brought into view.

```rust
crate::app::ViewMode::List => list_content_height(app.entries.len(), s, zoom),   // wayland_loop.rs:1399 (also :331)
// render.rs:127
entries.len() as f32 * rh + 32.0 * list_zoom_multiplier(zoom) * s // +header
// layout.rs:483
pub fn list_content_height(entry_count: usize, s: f32, zoom: f32) -> f32 { entry_count as f32 * list_row_h(s, zoom) }
```

**Skeptic's check.** render.rs:119-131 sizes the List scroll area as entries*row_h + 32*m*s and draws rows at base_y + hdr_h + i*row_h (157-166, 731-734). view_content_height's List arm (1399) and the rubber-band auto-scroll (331) use layout::list_content_height (layout.rs:483), which has no header term. The wheel target is clamped to total_h - content.h (574-578) and scrollbar dragging maps through the same total (303-307, 689-695); ScrollArea::new in lntrn-ui only clamps downward, it never extends. So the maximum reachable offset is 32*m*s short and, with 40*m*s rows, only the top 8*m*s px of the last row is visible. The search branch (1390-1393) and inactive_view_content_height (1454-1456) include the header, confirming the focused-pane List arm is the odd one out.

**Fix.** Add `+ 32.0 * list_zoom_multiplier(zoom) * s` in the List arm of view_content_height and in the rubber-band block at 331; better, add one layout helper for 'list total height including header' and use it from render.rs, sections/split.rs and wayland_loop.rs. Pure geometry fix.

## Low (99)

### sections-17: Path bar accepts relative and non-normalised paths, leaving current_dir in a state the breadcrumbs and Up button mishandle

- **Where:** `src/app/edit.rs:141` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Path bar input is resolved (~, relative to the shown folder) and normalised lexically before navigating.
- **Also reported as:** app-27

**What is wrong.** The typed string is used verbatim. is_dir() resolves a relative path against the process working directory, and '..' components are kept, so current_dir can become 'Documents' or '/home/alva/../etc'. breadcrumb_segments then shows 'Home / .. / etc' (or a single relative crumb), sidebar active-place matching fails, and can_go_up/go_up use the lexical parent: Up from '/home/alva/..' (which is /home) goes down into /home/alva; Up from 'Documents' navigates to the empty path and shows an empty folder with an empty path bar.

**Trigger.** Click the empty part of the path bar, type 'Documents' (or append '/..' to the current path), press Enter, then press Up or click the '..' crumb.

```rust
let path = std::path::PathBuf::from(&self.path_buf);
if path.is_dir() {
    self.navigate_to(path);
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** commit_path_edit builds PathBuf::from(&path_buf) and navigates if is_dir(); navigate_to stores it unchanged. Verified with rustc that Path::new("Documents").parent() is Some(""), so can_go_up is true and go_up navigates to the empty path, which lists as an empty folder. '..' components are kept, so breadcrumb_segments (sections/mod.rs:116-145) emits a '..' crumb and is_active_place and the is_slow_path/in_trash prefix tests miss. Requires the user to clear the pre-filled absolute path and type a relative one, so a rare edge. '~' is not expanded either (is_dir fails, nothing happens).

**Fix.** In commit_path_edit: expand a leading '~', join relative input onto current_dir (not the process cwd), then normalise '.' and '..' lexically before navigate_to. I would not use std::fs::canonicalize as proposed: it resolves symlinks (the path bar would jump to the real location of a symlinked folder) and is another blocking syscall on a possibly slow mount.

### app-26: Back/Forward in pick mode do not re-anchor tree_root or clear pick_tree_selection: the tree shows one folder while the Save target/path bar is another

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:254` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Back and Forward re-anchor the pick-mode tree and clear pick selection like navigate_to.

**What is wrong.** navigate_to() contains the pick-mode fix-up (`if self.tree_root.is_some() { self.tree_root = Some(self.current_dir.clone()); }`, clear pick_tree_selection, last_click_path). go_back/go_forward duplicate the navigation but not that block (nor the root_mode reset). In a picker (always Tree, anchored at tree_root) pressing Back changes current_dir and the path bar while rebuild_tree keeps listing the previous root, and nested selections from the other folder stay in pick_tree_selection and are returned by confirm_pick.

**Trigger.** In a Save dialog click Documents in the sidebar, then the Back arrow: the tree still shows Documents but the path bar shows the start folder, and Save writes the file into the start folder.

```rust
pub fn go_back(&mut self) {
    let cur = self.current_dir.clone();
    let tab = self.active_nav_tab();
    if let Some(prev) = tab.history_back.pop() {
        ...
        self.current_dir = prev;
        self.scroll_offset = 0.0;
        self.reload();
```

**Skeptic's check.** navigate_to re-anchors tree_root and clears pick_tree_selection and last_click_path (nav.rs:102-106); go_back and go_forward (254-278) duplicate the navigation without that block. In pick mode tree_root is Some, the nav bar and its Back zone are drawn (render.rs:289, 425), and rebuild_tree prefers tree_root, so after sidebar click then Back the tree still lists the previous root while current_dir, the path bar and the Save target are the earlier folder. pick_tree_selection entries from the other folder survive and confirm_pick returns them.

**Fix.** Factor the post-navigation block of navigate_to (re-anchor tree_root when Some, clear pick_tree_selection and last_click_path) into a helper and call it from go_back and go_forward before reload(). Outside pick mode tree_root is None, so nothing changes there.

### fs-32: Favorites on unplugged drives are dropped at startup and then lost on the next favorites save

- **Where:** `src/app/places.rs:24` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): src/app/places.rs: load_favorites_from keeps every favourite; ones whose folder is missing are tracked in App::favorites_offline, drawn dimmed with a stroke through the icon (src/sections/sidebar.rs), re-checked whenever the drive list changes (src/app/background.rs) and on click. Clicking one shows a message instead of navigating; dropping files onto one is refused (src/wayland_actions/drag_drop.rs). All five save sites now go through App::persist_favorites, so the full list is what is written. Same disease fixed for pinned tabs: a tab pinned on an unplugged drive gets no tab but stays in the settings (App::keep_absent_pin / pinned_tab_paths in src/app/tabs.rs, src/wayland.rs).

**What is wrong.** load_favorites_from discards favorites whose path does not exist right now. settings.favorites keeps the full list only until the user adds, removes or reorders any favorite, at which point it is overwritten from the filtered in-memory list (context_menu.rs:786/803, wayland_loop.rs:759/877/947).

**Trigger.** Favorite a folder on a USB drive, NAS or phone. Start Fox while it is not mounted, then add or reorder any other favorite. The removable favorite is gone for good.

```rust
.map(PathBuf::from)
.filter(|p| p.exists())
```

**Skeptic's check.** load_favorites_from filters with p.exists() (places.rs:24). settings.favorites keeps the full list in memory and the exit path in wayland.rs does not reassign it, so nothing is lost just by starting with the drive unplugged. But every add, remove and reorder site assigns settings.favorites = app.favorites_paths() (context_menu.rs:786, 803; wayland_loop.rs:759, 877, 947), which is the filtered list, and saves it. The doc comment shows the filtering is intentional; the permanent loss on the next unrelated edit is the unintended part.

**Fix.** Either keep missing favorites in the list and render them dimmed, or keep the filter for display but merge the hidden (currently missing) entries back in whenever settings.favorites is written. The first changes a deliberate behaviour (no dead links in the sidebar), so it is Alva's call. Note exists() on a favorite living on a dead network mount also blocks startup (see app-16).

### app-14: search_rx is never cleared when the search thread finishes, so Fox renders at 60 fps for as long as a query is open

- **Where:** `src/app/search.rs:57` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. poll_search drops the receiver when the worker finishes, so the loop goes idle.

**What is wrong.** poll_search treats Empty and Disconnected the same and leaves search_rx = Some(..) after the worker has exited. wayland_loop's needs_anim includes `app.search_rx.is_some()`, which forces frame_done every 16 ms. A finished search therefore keeps the full render pass running continuously until the user closes the search bar or clears the query, the exact idle spin the loop comments say was 'melting the laptop'.

**Trigger.** Open search, type a query, wait for results, leave the window alone: GPU/CPU usage stays at a constant 60 fps redraw instead of dropping to idle.

```rust
match rx.try_recv() {
    Ok(entry) => self.search_results.push(entry),
    Err(_) => break,
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** poll_search breaks on any Err, so a Disconnected receiver (worker finished and dropped result_tx) is left as Some. grep shows search_rx is set to None only in cancel_search, and needs_anim includes 'app.search_rx.is_some()' (wayland_loop.rs:1364), which forces frame_done every 16 ms. So once a query has run, Fox renders continuously until the search is closed or the query changes. No other code reads search_rx.

**Fix.** In poll_search, match the error: on TryRecvError::Disconnected (after draining all Ok results) set self.search_rx = None and self.search_tx = None. Nothing else keys off search_rx. Compatible with the app-13 fix, since the thread has already exited when the result channel disconnects.

### sections-12: Single-select picker returns several paths when more than one entry is selected

- **Where:** `src/app/select.rs:128` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C8): A picker started without --pick-multiple can no longer build or return a multi-selection. src/app/select.rs: new App::single_select_only (in pick_confirm.rs), select_all is a no-op there, select_range selects only the clicked item, select_item / on_item_click / new select_only drop the nested-tree marks (pick_tree_selection) so entries[].selected and pick_tree_selection together never hold more than one item, new unselect_others makes a Ctrl+click toggle only its own row. src/wayland_actions/click.rs calls unselect_others in both Ctrl branches (tree rows and grid/list items); src/wayland_loop.rs does not start a rubber band in such a picker (the click on empty space still clears the selection); key.rs Quick Look stepping uses select_only. Backstop in App::finish_pick (pick_confirm.rs): if more than one eligible path is still selected, nothing is returned and a notice says to choose one.

**What is wrong.** pick.multiple is honoured only on plain clicks. Ctrl-click (click.rs:349-360), Shift-click, rubber band and Ctrl+A all build multi-selections regardless, and confirm_pick returns every selected path without consulting pick.multiple.

**Trigger.** Notepad -> Open (plain --pick): Ctrl-click two files, press Open. Fox prints two lines; Notepad treats the whole stdout as one path and silently opens nothing. Through the portal a single-file OpenFile request gets two URIs back.

```rust
PickType::Open => {
    for e in self.entries.iter().filter(|e| e.selected && !e.is_dir) {
        push(e.path.clone(), &mut paths, &mut seen);
    }
    for p in &self.pick_tree_selection { ... }
    if !paths.is_empty() {
        self.pick_result = Some(PickResult::Selected(paths));
```

**Skeptic's check.** pick.multiple is read only in on_item_click and the plain pick-mode tree click (click.rs:379-382). The Ctrl branch (click.rs:349-360) toggles entries[i].selected and inserts into pick_tree_selection regardless, Shift range-select and Ctrl+A (key.rs:718) have no check either, and confirm_pick returns every selected path without consulting multiple. Verified the consequence in lntrn-notepad open (actions.rs:112-117): it trims the whole stdout into one path, so two lines become one bogus path and the open silently does nothing.

**Fix.** Preferred: when !pick.multiple, make Ctrl/Shift clicks, the rubber band and Ctrl+A behave as single-select in pick mode, so what is highlighted is what is returned. Truncating in confirm_pick is simpler but returns an arbitrary one of several highlighted files, which is its own surprise. Either way it is a behaviour choice.

### audio-14: Untouched fields are rewritten lossily on every save (full date cut to a year, Camelot key replaced)

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/id3.rs:481` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (B): Id3Tag::apply_changes(old, new) (id3.rs) only touches the frames of fields whose value differs from what the editor showed; mp3::write / wav::write pass the merged view the user saw (ID3v2 + ID3v1, or ID3 + INFO + acid). So a title edit leaves TDRC 2024-05-17, TKEY 8A, TCON (17), multi-value TPE1 and UTF-8 frames byte-identical. The TDRC/TDRL-deleting code is gone: a changed year is spliced into the stored date (2024-05-17 -> 2025-05-17) in TYER/TDRC, a new year goes into the version's own frame (TYE / TYER / TDRC), a cleared year removes TYER/TDRC/TDRL. Same for WAV INFO (ICRD spliced, entries raw) and for the ID3v1 trailer (new id3v1::update writes only changed fields; the old build() re-truncated every field and reset unknown genres). Key: keys::to_store (keys.rs), used by properties_audio/mod.rs current_tags(), passes an untouched Key buffer through exactly as the file has it and only normalises what the user typed.

**What is wrong.** to_tags() keeps only the first four characters of TDRC/TDRL and apply() deletes both and writes TYER, so a release date like 2024-05-17 becomes 2024 the first time anything is saved (confirmed in the multi-APIC experiment: TDRC 2024-05-17 came back as TYER 2024). Likewise properties_audio/mod.rs:177 normalises the Key buffer on every save, so a file whose TKEY is "8A" (Mixed In Key / rekordbox style) is rewritten to "Am" when the user only edits the title. v2.4 multi-value text frames are flattened to "A / B".

**Trigger.** v2.4-tagged MP3 with TDRC = 2024-05-17 and TKEY = 8A. Properties, change Title, Save. The date is now just 2024 and the key tag reads Am.

```rust
self.frames
    .retain(|f| &f.id != b"TDRC" && &f.id != b"TDRL");
self.set_text(b"TYER", &t.year);
```

**Skeptic's check.** to_tags() takes the first four characters of TYER, TDRC or TDRL; apply() removes TDRC and TDRL and writes TYER from that value (id3.rs:481-483), so a full date becomes a bare year on the first save of anything. apply() rewrites all eight text fields unconditionally, so a v2.4 multi-value frame that decode_text joined as 'A / B' is written back flattened. current_tags() (properties_audio/mod.rs:177-179) normalises the Key buffer on every save, so an untouched TKEY of '8A' is rewritten as 'Am' when only the title changed; is_dirty gates the save as a whole, not per field.

**Fix.** In apply(), compute self.to_tags() first and only call set_text for fields whose new value differs from it; only drop TDRC/TDRL when the year actually changed. Same for the key: only normalise when the buffer differs from the saved value. The TDRC removal is a deliberate 'one year, not two' choice in the code, and leaving a v2.4-only frame inside a v2.3 tag is a compatibility trade-off, so this needs a decision rather than a mechanical edit.

### audio-20: Arithmetic overflow panics in debug builds (Key field with a 20-digit number; WAV fmt with byte_rate 0)

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/keys.rs:60` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category panic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Key numbers outside 1..=12 are rejected before the arithmetic; WAV byte-rate fallback uses saturating maths.

**What is wrong.** normalize() parses any run of digits into usize and passes it to open_to_camelot before range-checking; `n + 7` overflows for n near usize::MAX. Reproduced in a debug build: keys::normalize("18446744073709551615m") panics with 'attempt to add with overflow'. draw_key_chip calls normalize every frame on the UI thread, and the string can also come from a file's TKEY. Same class at wav.rs:243-245: `sample_rate * channels as u32 * (bits / 8)` overflows u32 for hostile fmt chunks with byte_rate 0, on a thumbnail worker. The release profile has no overflow-checks, so deployed builds wrap silently (harmless here); only dev/test builds crash.

**Trigger.** Debug build: open Properties on an MP3 and type 18446744073709551615m into Key (or open a file whose TKEY holds it): Fox panics on the next frame.

```rust
fn open_to_camelot(n: usize) -> usize {
    match (n + 7) % 12 {
```

**Skeptic's check.** normalize() parses the leading digits into usize and calls open_to_camelot(n) before any range check; 'n + 7' overflows only for the seven values within 7 of usize::MAX. The workspace Cargo.toml [profile.release] sets opt-level, strip and incremental only, so overflow checks are off in the deployed build and the add wraps harmlessly (from_camelot then range-checks). A debug or test build panics. Same class at wav.rs:243-245: sample_rate * channels * (bits/8) in u32 for a crafted fmt chunk with byte_rate 0. Real but confined to dev builds and hostile input.

**Fix.** keys.rs: return None when n is outside 1..=12 before calling open_to_camelot. wav.rs: compute the fallback byte rate in u64 or with saturating_mul. Both local, no behaviour change for valid input.

### audio-8: Temp file uses a fixed, predictable name opened with File::create (follows symlinks, no O_EXCL, shared by concurrent saves)

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/mod.rs:179` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category security; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The temp file has a unique per-save name and is created with create_new and the final mode.

**What is wrong.** File::create truncates whatever is at `.NAME.lntrn-tmp`. If that path is a pre-planted symlink (shared or world-writable music directory), the full audio file is written through it over the link's target, the target is then chmod'ed, and the link is renamed over the song. Separately, two saves of the same file can overlap (close the dialog during a slow rewrite, reopen, save again: the first thread is detached): both threads write into the same inode and the first rename publishes a mixed file. The temp is also created with default umask permissions and only chmod'ed at the end, so a 0600 file is briefly world-readable. Names of 245+ bytes fail with ENAMETOOLONG.

**Trigger.** Slow rewrite (large MP3 gaining its first ID3v2 tag on a NAS/USB stick): click outside the dialog while "Saving…" shows, reopen Properties, edit, Save. Second save reports "No such file or directory" and the file may end with leftover bytes from the first writer.

```rust
let tmp: PathBuf = dir.join(format!(".{name}.lntrn-tmp"));
...
let mut dst = File::create(&tmp)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** The code is as described: the temp path is always '.<name>.lntrn-tmp' next to the file and is opened with File::create, which follows a symlink and truncates, with permissions applied only after the copy. The overlap scenario is also possible in code: AudioEdit::save spawns a detached thread, the 'saving' flag lives in the dialog, and both the click-outside path and Esc (when not dirty) close the dialog mid-save, so a reopened dialog can start a second writer on the same temp file. What I could not establish is the outcome and the likelihood: the interleaving was reasoned, not reproduced, and it needs a rewrite slow enough to close, reopen, edit and save inside it. The symlink angle needs another user able to write in the music directory.

**Fix.** Open the temp with OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true).mode(perms.mode()) and a unique suffix (pid plus a counter), retrying on AlreadyExists. Use a unique name rather than create_new on the fixed name, or one leftover temp from a crash would block every later save of that file. Local to replace_file.

### audio-6: audio_tags::read opens FIFOs and other special files named *.mp3 / *.wav and blocks forever

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/mp3.rs:17` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. audio_tags::read and write refuse anything that is not a regular file.

**What is wrong.** Container detection is by extension only and both readers open the path with a plain blocking File::open (mp3.rs:17, wav.rs:205). A named pipe with an audio extension blocks in open() until a writer appears; if a writer exists, the read steals data from the pipeline. Listings do not filter special files (FileEntry has only is_dir), so a thumbnail worker opens it while merely browsing and never returns; its key stays in IconCache.pending, so has_pending() keeps the 16 ms redraw loop running for the rest of the session. Properties on such a file calls the same read on the UI thread and hangs the app.

**Trigger.** `mkfifo ~/Music/live.wav` (common for snapcast/mpd/encoder pipelines), open that folder in Fox: one thumbnail worker is lost per FIFO and the app redraws at 60 fps forever. Right-click it, Properties: Fox hangs permanently.

```rust
let mut f = File::open(path).map_err(io_err)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** container_for decides by extension only, and mp3::read (mp3.rs:17) and wav::read (wav.rs:205) open the path with a plain blocking File::open, which on a FIFO with no writer blocks in open(). list_directory does not filter special files (FileEntry has only is_dir), so browsing the folder submits the entry to the thumbnail pool (icons.rs:155-156, thumbs.rs:220), the worker never returns, its key stays in IconCache.pending, and has_pending() keeps the loop rendering at 60 fps. Properties calls AudioEdit::load, hence audio_tags::read, on the UI thread (properties.rs:223), which hangs the window. Not run against a real FIFO; the blocking open is standard. Rated low: a named pipe with a .wav or .mp3 extension inside a browsed folder is a rare setup.

**Fix.** At the top of audio_tags::read and audio_tags::write: 'if !std::fs::metadata(path).map(|m| m.is_file()).unwrap_or(false) { return Err("Not a regular file".into()); }'. metadata follows symlinks, so a link to a FIFO is rejected too. The image thumbnail path (ImageReader::open) has the same exposure for a FIFO named .png and deserves the same guard in thumbs::generate.

### audio-15: mp3::write reports failure after the file was already rewritten when the ID3v1 sync fails

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/mp3.rs:291` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. mp3::write checks write access first, and an ID3v1 sync failure after the v2 tag is committed is logged, not reported as a failed save.

**What is wrong.** sync_v1 reopens the file read+write and its error becomes the result of the whole write, although the ID3v2 block has already been committed. For a read-only (0444) MP3 in a writable directory the temp+rename path succeeds (replacing a file the user marked read-only), permissions are copied, then sync_v1 fails with EACCES. Reproduced: write() returned Err("Permission denied") and the file now starts with the new ID3 tag. The editor then shows "Save failed" and stays dirty while the disk has the new tags and a stale ID3v1.
