**Trigger.** chmod 444 an MP3 that has no ID3v2 padding, Properties, edit Title, Save: red "Save failed: Permission denied (os error 13)", but reopening Properties shows the new title.

```rust
    sync_v1(path, tags)
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Reproduced in the harness: a 0444 MP3 with no ID3v2 in a writable dir -> write() returned Err("Permission denied (os error 13)") while the file now starts with 'ID3' (replace_file's temp+rename succeeded and copied 0444), then sync_v1's read+write open failed. The save thread in properties_audio/mod.rs:227 turns that into 'Save failed' although the tags are on disk. The in-place branch fails early (write(true) open), only the rewrite branch gets through.

**Fix.** At the top of mp3::write open the file with OpenOptions::new().write(true) and return the error before doing anything, so both branches refuse a read-only file consistently. Additionally make a sync_v1 failure after a committed v2 write non-fatal (log, return Ok) so the reported result matches the disk. The first part does change behaviour for read-only files in writable dirs (currently silently replaced), which is the intended correction.

### audio-19: No cap on chunk count (WAV) or ID3 tag size (MP3): a crafted large file makes thumbnail workers allocate hundreds of MB while browsing

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/wav.rs:50` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. WAV chunk walk is capped at 65,536 chunks; ID3 tags over 64 MiB are refused.

**What is wrong.** walk() issues one pread and pushes one Chunk per 8 bytes for a file made of zero-length chunks ("AAAA\0\0\0\0" repeated): a 1 GB file yields ~134 M syscalls and a ~2 GB Vec on a thumbnail worker, just from opening the folder. mp3::read_head (mp3.rs:51-52) allocates tag_len + 128 KiB with tag_len up to 256 MiB taken from the header (bounded only by file size), and parse() copies frames again, so a few such files across 2-4 workers reach GBs. WAV metadata chunks have a 32 MiB cap; these two paths have none. Allocation is proportional to file size, so this needs a deliberately large hostile file.

**Trigger.** Download or unpack a crafted 1 GB .wav of repeated empty chunks (or several 300 MB .mp3 files whose ID3 header claims 256 MiB) into a folder and open it in Fox: a worker spins for minutes and RAM balloons.

```rust
while pos + 8 <= len {
    let mut ch = [0u8; 8];
    f.read_exact_at(&mut ch, pos).map_err(io_err)?;
    ...
    chunks.push(c);
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** walk() has no bound on chunk count: one pread + one 16-byte Chunk (size confirmed with rustc) per 8 bytes of zero-length printable-id chunks, so ~134M syscalls and ~2 GiB (more with Vec doubling) for a 1 GB file. mp3::read_head allocates (tag_len + 128 KiB).min(file len) with tag_len up to 256 MiB from four syncsafe bytes, and id3::parse copies frames again. Both are reached from thumbs.rs:220 (audio_artwork -> audio_tags::read) on a thumbnail worker just by opening the folder; MAX_META_CHUNK only guards read_chunk. Allocation is bounded by real file size, so it needs a deliberately large hostile file: low. Mechanism read, not timed.

**Fix.** Cap the walk (e.g. error out after 65,536 chunks; 4,096 is fine too, real WAVs have a few dozen). For MP3, cap the tag read (e.g. 64 MiB): in read() return no tags, but in write() return Err rather than treating it as 'no tag' (otherwise a new tag would be prepended in front of the huge one). Thumbnail path could additionally only read the tag region instead of tag+probe.

### wayland-20: Leftover debug logging appends every copied string to world-readable /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log

- **Where:** `src/clipboard.rs:43` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The clipboard debug log (every copied string to /tmp) is gone.

**What is wrong.** set_text (UI thread) and the clipboard thread's dbg() both append the full copied text to a fixed, predictable file in /tmp created with the default umask (it exists now as 0644). /tmp is on the btrfs root here, so it persists and grows without bound. Content is file paths, names and whatever the Properties dialog copies. Symlink/pre-creation attacks are mitigated on this machine by fs.protected_symlinks/protected_regular = 1.

**Trigger.** Use Copy Path / Copy Name or copy from Properties: the text is appended twice to /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log, readable by any local user.

```rust
if let Ok(mut f) = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
    .create(true)
    .append(true)
    .open("/tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log")
{
    let _ = writeln!(f, "set_text called: {text}");
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** set_text (43-50) and the thread-local dbg() (67-76, used at 104 with the full text) both append to /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log. The file exists on this machine: 576 bytes, mode 0644, modified today. Unbounded, world-readable, contains every copied path/name/hash.

**Fix.** Delete both writers (the set_text block and dbg()). If any diagnostics are wanted, eprintln without the copied text. No behaviour depends on the file.

### wayland-21: Clipboard thread polls with a 50 ms timeout forever (20 wake-ups per second per Fox process)

- **Where:** `src/clipboard.rs:129` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The clipboard thread blocks on the Wayland socket plus an eventfd instead of waking every 50 ms.

**What is wrong.** The thread's only way to notice a new copy request is `rx.try_recv()` at the top of the loop, so it wakes every 50 ms for the life of every Fox process (windows and pickers), even though copies are rare.

**Trigger.** Any running Fox: the clipboard-wayland thread shows a steady 20 wake-ups/s in powertop/perf while completely idle.

```rust
let n = unsafe { libc::poll(&mut pfd, 1, 50) };
```

**Skeptic's check.** Loop is try_recv -> flush -> dispatch_pending -> prepare_read -> poll(fd, 50 ms) -> read. It reads the socket correctly, but the fixed 50 ms timeout is the only way to notice a new mpsc message, so the thread wakes 20x/s for the life of every Fox process (windows, pickers, the desktop-mode daemon).

**Fix.** Add an eventfd (or pipe) written by set_text after tx.send, poll [wayland fd, eventfd] with timeout -1, drain the eventfd then try_recv in a loop. Keep the Disconnected exit path working: Clipboard needs a Drop that writes the eventfd, or the thread will not notice the sender going away.

### wayland-19: Data offers are created for every selection change and never destroyed (main data device and the data-control clipboard thread)

- **Where:** `src/clipboard.rs:170` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Clipboard offers are destroyed as they arrive (data-control thread and main data device), the DnD offer on leave/drop, and a cancelled source is destroyed.

**What is wrong.** The data-control device receives a new offer object for every clipboard and primary-selection change system-wide; the wl_data_device receives one per focus gain, selection change and DnD enter. Neither handler keeps or destroys them, and Cancelled on Fox's own sources is ignored too. Each object stays allocated in Fox's object map and in the compositor for the life of the process.

**Trigger.** Leave a Fox window open for days while copying and selecting text in other apps: memory in Fox's clipboard thread and the compositor's per-client resource table grows by one offer (plus its mime list) per selection change.

```rust
event_created_child!(ClipState, zwlr_data_control_device_v1::ZwlrDataControlDeviceV1, [
    0 => (zwlr_data_control_offer_v1::ZwlrDataControlOfferV1, ()),
]);
// wayland_dispatch.rs:459: same for wl_data_offer, Dispatch impl is empty
```

**Skeptic's check.** Both offer types are created via event_created_child and both Dispatch impls are empty (clipboard.rs:175-185, wayland_dispatch.rs:502-512); the device handlers ignore Selection/PrimarySelection, and no .destroy() exists for offers or sources anywhere (grep). In wayland-client 0.31 dropping a proxy sends nothing; an offer lives in the client object map and the compositor's resource table until the client sends destroy. So one object per clipboard/primary change on the data-control thread, plus one per focus/selection/DnD-enter on the main wl_data_device, for the process lifetime. Also the data-control source and the DnD wl_data_source are never destroyed on Cancelled/DndFinished. Growth is small per object (not measured), relevant for the long-lived desktop-mode process.

**Fix.** Fox never reads offers, so the simplest correct handling is: data-control device Selection{id}/PrimarySelection{id} -> if Some(offer) call offer.destroy(); wl_data_device Selection{id: Some(o)} -> o.destroy(), and destroy the DnD offer on Leave and after Drop. Destroy the data-control source on Cancelled and the wl_data_source on Cancelled/DndFinished (clear copied_text only when the cancelled source is the current one).

### cloud-net-19: Firebase API key is written to fox-cloud.log and shown in the login dialog via ureq transport errors

- **Where:** `src/cloud/auth.rs:123` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Transport errors are formatted without the request URL, so the API key stays out of the log and the login dialog.

**What is wrong.** Both auth URLs carry `?key={api_key}` and ureq's Transport Display prints the full URL. Every offline refresh attempt therefore logs the key: fox-cloud.log (mode 0644) currently contains 868 lines with `key=`. config.rs says the key is deliberately kept out of the repo; the log undoes that the first time it is pasted into an issue or chat. The rules let any authenticated account write under its own uid, so with the key (and email sign-up enabled in the project) a stranger can create an account and consume the project's Storage/Firestore quota. In the dialog the same string is drawn on one clipped line, so the user sees a URL rather than the reason.

**Trigger.** Start Fox while offline (or lose DNS) with a token older than about an hour: each poll appends 'token refresh transport error: https://securetoken.googleapis.com/v1/token?key=AIza...' to the log.

```rust
Err(e) => anyhow::bail!("token refresh transport error: {e}"),
...
Err(e) => anyhow::bail!("sign-in transport error: {e}"),   // auth.rs:86
```

**Skeptic's check.** Both auth URLs embed ?key={api_key} (auth.rs:70-73, 105-108). ureq 2.12.1 (version pinned in Cargo.lock) attaches the URL to transport errors (request.rs:150) and Transport's Display prints it first (error.rs:227-231). auth.rs:86 and :123 format {e} into the anyhow message, which classify_failure logs and the login dialog shows (nav.rs:54). Counted 868 lines containing 'key=' in ~/.lantern/log/fox-cloud.log (mode 0644) without printing them. Bearer/refresh tokens travel in headers/body and are not logged. Low: a Firebase web API key is not a credential by itself, but config.rs deliberately keeps it out of the repo.

**Fix.** Match ureq::Error::Transport(t) and format `t.kind()` plus `t.message()` (or strip everything from '?' in the string) in both sign_in and refresh. The existing log still holds the key; rotating or truncating that file is the user's call, not something to do automatically.

### cloud-net-21: device_name() reads /etc/hostname, which does not exist on the Gentoo PC, so every doc and conflict copy is labelled 'device'

- **Where:** `src/cloud/mod.rs:55` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. device_name reads /proc/sys/kernel/hostname first.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-21, cloud-e2e-22

**What is wrong.** OpenRC Gentoo keeps the hostname in /etc/conf.d/hostname; /etc/hostname is absent on this machine (kernel hostname is 'genforge'). The remote index confirms it: 4311 docs carry device='device' and 59 carry 'archbook'. Conflict copies made on the PC are therefore named '<file> (conflict from device).<ext>', which tells the user nothing about which machine produced them and removes the only thing that would keep two machines' conflict names distinct if the laptop ever hit the same fallback.

**Trigger.** Any upload or conflict on the PC. The user sees 'notes (conflict from device).txt'.

```rust
std::fs::read_to_string("/etc/hostname")
    .ok()
    .map(|s| s.trim().to_string())
    .filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
    .unwrap_or_else(|| "device".to_string())
```

**Skeptic's check.** device_name() reads only /etc/hostname. On this machine `ls /etc/hostname` fails and /proc/sys/kernel/hostname is 'genforge'. remote-index.json: 4,311 docs with device 'device', 59 with 'archbook'. conflict_name() uses that string, so conflict copies made here are '(conflict from device)'.

**Fix.** Read /proc/sys/kernel/hostname first (present on both machines), then /etc/hostname, then the literal fallback. Only affects labels on future writes and conflict names.

### cloud-net-22: fox-cloud.log grows without bound and a single failure line can be hundreds of KB

- **Where:** `src/cloud/mod.rs:75` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. fox-cloud.log rotates to .1 past 4 MB and the failure summary is capped to three paths.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-24, cloud-e2e-21

**What is wrong.** log_line appends forever with no rotation or cap. Every failed pass logs at least one line (two per failing file plus an aggregate), and failures repeat every 30 s or faster. reconcile_with's aggregate error joins every failing path with its full error text into one line. The log on this machine is 10.7 MB / 60,811 lines after four months, including one 725,029-byte line (1376 failures) and 3523 repeats of the same one-file failure. Each line also reopens the file.

**Trigger.** Any persistent sync failure or being offline with Fox open; the file grows by 0.5 MB a day or more, and by megabytes per minute in the full_due hot loop.

```rust
if let Ok(mut f) = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
    .create(true)
    .append(true)
    .open(&path)
```

**Skeptic's check.** log_line opens with create+append on every call, no size check or rotation anywhere. Measured read-only: fox-cloud.log is 10,727,113 bytes, 60,811 lines, longest line 725,028 bytes; 42,101 'hash … failed' lines come from 86 distinct messages repeated 1,859 times each. reconcile_with joins every failure with its full error text into one line (reconcile.rs:444-448) after already logging each one.

**Fix.** Rotate at a fixed size (rename to .1 when over a few MB, checked before the append), cap the aggregate error to a count plus the first few paths, and remember the last message per path so an unchanged failure is logged once. With several Fox processes appending, do the rotation by rename so a concurrent writer keeps a valid fd.

### cloud-core-23: Relative paths are built lossily: backslashes are rewritten to '/' and non-UTF-8 bytes to U+FFFD

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:68` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1): src/cloud/scan.rs builds relative paths from the exact file names: backslashes stay, a name that is not UTF-8 (file or folder) is skipped with one log line and never rewritten. The keys an older build would have produced (lossy U+FFFD form, backslash turned into '/') are shielded as 'unknown' unless a real file lives there, so a manifest or remote entry under such a key can never be read as deleted locally. Header comment in src/cloud/firestore.rs corrected.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-27

**What is wrong.** On Linux a backslash is an ordinary filename character. A file named "a\b.txt" is uploaded as "a/b.txt" and created on the other machine as file b.txt inside folder a. Locally every later action goes through abs_for(rel), which points at ~/Cloud/a/b.txt rather than the real file, so a remote change is written to a second file; then both local files map to the same key in `locals` and which one wins depends on read_dir order. Names with invalid UTF-8 behave the same way via the replacement character.

**Trigger.** Put a file whose name contains a backslash (common in files extracted from Windows archives) in ~/Cloud. It appears under a different folder structure on the other machine, and edits made there land in a duplicate file here.

```rust
let rel = rel_pb.to_string_lossy().replace('\\', "/");
```

**Skeptic's check.** Line 68 is `rel_pb.to_string_lossy().replace('\\', "/")`. On Linux '\' is a normal filename byte, so 'a\b.txt' is keyed and uploaded as 'a/b.txt'. upload_local uses local.abs (the real file) but download_remote, delete_local and resolve_conflict go through abs_for(rel), i.e. ~/Cloud/a/b.txt, a different path; after a remote edit both files map to the same HashMap key and read_dir order decides. to_string_lossy does the same for non-UTF-8 names. No such docs exist in the current index (0 keys with a backslash).

**Fix.** Drop the replace (strip_prefix already yields '/'-separated components on Linux) and skip, with a log line, entries whose relative path is not valid UTF-8 (rel_pb.to_str()). Hazard to handle in the same change: a path that was synced under the old key and is now skipped or re-keyed looks 'locally deleted' and would be tombstoned via 4a; none exist in this index, but the laptop's should be checked or the action loop should skip unrepresentable paths too.

### cloud-core-12: Stat cache uses whole-second mtime: a same-size rewrite in the same second is never synced and is later overwritten

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:78` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Stat cache refuses entries for files modified in the last 2 seconds (racy-clean rule), so a same-second same-size rewrite is re-hashed.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-23

**What is wrong.** cached_sha trusts (size, mtime-in-seconds). The stat is taken before hashing, and nothing avoids caching a file whose mtime is the current second. If a file is hashed and then rewritten with the same length within the same second, the cache keeps returning the old sha forever. The change is never uploaded, and when the other machine later changes that file, case 3c (local == manifest) downloads over the unsynced content without a conflict copy.

**Trigger.** A fixed-size file that is rewritten in place in ~/Cloud (SQLite database, a game's cache or save file, a config rewritten with the same length) gets two writes within one second with a pass scanning in between (a 30 s poll or an earlier event's pass). Fox keeps showing Synced; the other machine never receives the second write.

```rust
.map(|d| d.as_secs())
...
let sha = match manifest.cached_sha(&rel, size, mtime) {
    Some(cached) => cached.to_string(),
```

**Skeptic's check.** walk() truncates mtime with as_secs() (74-79) and Manifest::cached_sha (manifest.rs:72-78) trusts size + that second. The stat is taken before hashing and nothing refuses to cache a file whose mtime is the current second; set_meta is recorded at 301-305, in upload_local (scan-time stat) and after download. A same-length rewrite in the same second after the hash therefore keeps the old sha indefinitely: never uploaded, and a later remote change takes 3c and overwrites it. Classic racy-timestamp problem; the window is narrow (same size, same second), hence low.

**Fix.** Store nanosecond mtime in FileMeta (keep serde(default) so old manifests load; they simply miss the cache once and re-hash) and do not record a cache entry when the file's mtime is within ~2 s of the scan time. FileDoc.mtime can stay in seconds. Older builds re-saving the manifest would drop/reset the new field, so update both machines together.

### cloud-net-17: Blob store trusts the scan-time hash on upload and never verifies the hash on download

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:172` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category data-loss; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): Same code as cloud-core-13 (transfer.rs, actions.rs upload_local / fetch_verified). A blob can only be completed with bytes that hash to its name; a download is only accepted if it hashes to the name asked for. The download is also fsynced before the rename. Recovery for a blob that is missing: gc.rs re-uploads it from a machine whose local file has exactly that hash (at the next full pass).

**What is wrong.** upload_local reads the file again after the scan computed (or cache-returned) its sha. If the file changed in between, the new bytes are stored under the old hash, overwriting a correct blob that other identical files may reference. download_blob returns whatever came back and download_remote records doc.sha256 in the manifest plus the stat cache without hashing the bytes, so a poisoned or wrong blob is written to disk and then treated as verified content forever (the stat cache prevents re-hashing). ureq does catch short bodies when Content-Length is present (LimitedRead returns UnexpectedEof), so plain truncation is covered; a mismatched blob is not.

**Trigger.** Save a file twice in quick succession (editor autosave, write-then-rewrite) so the second save lands between the scan's hash and the upload's read, while another file with the first save's exact content exists (a duplicate elsewhere in ~/Cloud). The other machine later downloads the duplicate and gets the wrong bytes, marked as in sync.

```rust
resp.into_reader().read_to_end(&mut buf)?;
Ok(buf)
...
let bytes = std::fs::read(&local.abs)?;                       // reconcile.rs:172
storage::upload_blob(authed, &local.sha, &bytes, &mime)?;     // sha from the earlier scan
```

**Skeptic's check.** upload_local re-reads the file (172) long after scan_local hashed it (or took the sha from the stat cache) and stores the bytes under local.sha; in a long pass the gap can be minutes. A file edited in between is stored under the wrong content address, overwriting a correct blob. For that path it self-heals on the next pass (mtime changed, rehash, arm 3b), so the lasting damage needs another path that shares the old content hash. download_remote (192-218) writes whatever arrives and records doc.sha256 plus the stat cache without hashing. Both halves are as described; real-world impact is rare, hence low. Line corrected from storage.rs:32 to the upload site.

**Fix.** Upload half is safe and should be done: hash the bytes actually read and use that sha for the blob name, the doc and manifest.set, and only call set_meta if bytes.len() and a fresh stat still match. Download half (hash and fail on mismatch) is correct but changes failure behaviour: an already-poisoned blob would then fail every pass, which today feeds the stuck-full-list loop (cloud-net-3), so fix that first.

### cloud-core-22: Directories are never synced or cleaned up: deleting or renaming a folder leaves its empty skeleton on the other machine

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:223` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A2): src/cloud/actions.rs: after a remote deletion moves a file to the Trash, prune_empty_parents removes the now-empty parent folders up to, never including, the sync root (remove_dir only, so a folder holding anything else stays). The comment states that empty directories as such are not synced. reconcile.rs runs local removals before the other actions, so a folder replaced by a file (and the reverse) arrives in one pass. Tests: pass_tests/cleanup.rs (4 folder tests), actions.rs::only_empty_folders_inside_the_root_are_pruned.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-e2e-26

**What is wrong.** Only regular files have docs. delete_local removes the file and nothing else, so after a folder is deleted or renamed on one machine, the other keeps the old directory tree, empty. An empty folder created on one machine never appears on the other. The leftover empty directory is also what makes the folder-replaced-by-file case fail permanently (see the *.fox-tmp finding).

**Trigger.** Rename ~/Cloud/A to ~/Cloud/B on the PC. On the laptop B appears with the files, and A remains as an empty folder tree.

```rust
if abs.exists() {
    std::fs::remove_file(&abs)?;
}
manifest.remove(rel);
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read walk() (lines 48-103): only regular files are inserted, directories are only recursed into. delete_local (220-228) does remove_file and nothing else; grep finds no remove_dir anywhere in src/cloud. So a folder delete/rename on one machine leaves the empty directory tree on the other, and an empty folder never propagates. Purely cosmetic divergence unless a file later needs that path.

**Fix.** Walking up with remove_dir (only succeeds when empty) after a successful delete_local, stopping at cloud_root, is the right minimal fix. Not flagged safe: it deletes directories the sync never created a record for, so a folder the user deliberately keeps empty next to a synced file would vanish when the last file in it is remotely deleted. Each remove_dir also fires inotify and schedules one more pass (harmless, idempotent). Owner decision.

### cloud-net-18: Session file holding the refresh token is created with default permissions and chmod'ed afterwards

- **Where:** `src/cloud/session.rs:47` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Session file is created 0600 from the start and written atomically.

**What is wrong.** On first creation (and after every Session::forget) the file exists as 0644 under the usual umask until set_permissions runs, and the result of set_permissions is discarded, so a failure leaves the long-lived refresh token world-readable. The write is also truncate-in-place. If HOME is unset the path falls back to /tmp/.lantern/config, a world-traversable location. The file on this PC is currently 0600, so steady state is fine; the gap is the creation window and the ignored error.

**Trigger.** Another local user or process reads ~/.lantern/config/fox-cloud-session.json in the window between create and chmod during sign-in or re-creation after a refresh failure.

```rust
std::fs::write(&path, raw)?;
// Tighten perms — this file holds a refresh token.
#[cfg(unix)]
{
    use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
    let _ = std::fs::set_permissions(&path, std::fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600));
```

**Skeptic's check.** Session::save does std::fs::write (0666 & ~umask) then set_permissions(0600) with the result discarded. On first creation after sign_in the file is briefly 0644; /home/alva, ~/.lantern and ~/.lantern/config are all 0755 here, so another local user could read it in that window. Re-saves truncate in place and keep 0600 (current mode is 0600). Single-user desktop, microsecond window: low.

**Fix.** Write a temp file opened with OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true).mode(0o600) in the same directory, then rename over the target; propagate errors. Local and mechanical; also makes the write atomic. Remove a stale temp file first if create_new fails.

### cloud-core-25: The sync thread cannot be stopped, so --cloud-logout does not sign out running instances and the session file comes back

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:41` (Cloud sync core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (A1, C9): src/cloud/sync.rs: the stop flag is real (SyncHandle::stop(), Drop, shutdown(wait) with a bounded join); the thread also stops by itself when the session file disappears (see cloud-e2e-15). The engine checks both before every file of a pass and saves progress before leaving. App::before_exit (src/op_dialogs.rs) now calls cloud_stop() first and cloud_shutdown() last: the owner gets SyncHandle::shutdown(2 s) to finish its file and save, a follower is just dropped. In-app sign-out added (details dialog -> 'Sign out'): Session::forget() (the engine's sign-out signal for every running Fox), handle dropped, cloud state cleared. --cloud-logout from a terminal now also shows up in open windows (pill 'Sign in to sync', Cloud button opens the login dialog). The two #[allow(dead_code)] in src/cloud/sync.rs are removed.

**What is wrong.** The stop flag is never set and nothing joins the thread. `lntrn-file-manager --cloud-logout` only deletes fox-cloud-session.json. Any running Fox keeps syncing with its in-memory session, and at the next token refresh (within the hour) auth::refresh calls session.save(), recreating the file, so the next launch is signed in again. The lack of a join on exit is also why passes are cut off mid-way (see the stale-manifest finding).

**Trigger.** Run --cloud-logout while a Fox window is open. Sync continues, and after up to an hour the session file exists again.

```rust
pub struct SyncHandle {
    pub status: Arc<Mutex<SyncStatus>>,
    _stop: Arc<std::sync::atomic::AtomicBool>,
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** `_stop` is only ever read (sync.rs:140); grep finds no store. main.rs:357-360 handles --cloud-logout by Session::forget() and exit. A running Fox holds the session in memory, keeps syncing, and auth::refresh calls session.save() (auth.rs:131), recreating the file within the token lifetime. There is no in-app sign-out.

**Fix.** Have the loop check Session::path().exists() before each pass and stop without saving when it is gone; make refresh skip save when the file was removed. Setting the flag and joining on exit changes shutdown timing (a pass can be mid-upload), so bound the join. Threading change, not mechanical.

### cloud-e2e-18: Every quiet 30 s poll rewrites the 1.3 MB index, rewrites the manifest twice and rescans the whole tree

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:231` (Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Manifest and remote index carry a dirty flag and are only written when something changed.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-core-20, cloud-net-23

**What is wrong.** apply_delta returns how many docs changed ("0 = quiet poll, nothing to reconcile against") but sync_pass ignores it: after every successful fetch it serialises and writes remote-index.json, then reconcile_with loads the manifest, walks and stats all of ~/Cloud, and calls manifest.save() at lines 306 and 437 whether or not anything changed. With today's files that is about 1.5 MB of writes per pass, 2,880 passes a day, roughly 4.3 GB/day of SSD writes per Fox process, plus a full directory walk every 30 s, for nothing.

**Trigger.** Leave any Fox process running while signed in; watch ~/.lantern/cloud/remote-index.json and manifest.json mtimes advance every 30 seconds with no file activity.

```rust
let _ = index.save();

match super::reconcile::reconcile_with(authed, &index.docs) {
```

**Skeptic's check.** index.save() at sync.rs:231 and manifest.save() at reconcile.rs:306 and 437 are unconditional; neither type has dirty tracking; apply_delta's return value is dropped in the closure at 224-226. On disk remote-index.json is 1,300,842 bytes and manifest.json 100,845 bytes, and a pass runs every 30 s, so about 1.5 MB per pass, roughly 4.3 GB a day per process. Wasteful rather than harmful; low.

**Fix.** Add a dirty flag to Manifest (set in set/remove, and in set_meta only when the value differs) and to RemoteIndex (seed_full, apply_delta when changed > 0 or the cursor moved); save only when dirty. That part is mechanical. Do NOT take the suggested 'skip reconcile_with on a quiet poll': the watcher can fail to arm (sync.rs:101-103 only logs) and the 30 s pass is then the only thing that notices local changes.

### fs-34: A user-level Hidden=true or NoDisplay=true override does not hide the system entry in Open With

- **Where:** `src/desktop.rs:132` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The first .desktop file per id (in priority order) decides, so a user-level Hidden override masks the system entry.

**What is wrong.** apps_for_mime walks the directories in priority order and inserts the first parse result per desktop id. When the higher-priority copy is hidden, parse_desktop_file returns None, nothing is recorded for that id, and the lower-priority system copy is then accepted. The same happens when the user override removes the MimeType line.

**Trigger.** Copy an app's .desktop file to ~/.local/share/applications and set Hidden=true to remove it from menus. It still appears in Fox's Open With list.

```rust
if no_display || hidden {
    return None;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** apps_for_mime (119-141) walks desktop_dirs() in priority order and only records an id when parse_desktop_file returns Some (132-134). A user copy with Hidden=true returns None at 180-182, so nothing is recorded and the system copy of the same id is accepted later. Same when the user copy drops the MimeType line. Line moved to the or_insert site.

**Fix.** Keep a HashSet of desktop ids seen (file name) and insert before parsing; skip any id already seen. Local and mechanical. Side note: per the Desktop Entry spec NoDisplay=true entries are still valid MIME handlers, so filtering them from Open With is itself questionable; Hidden=true is the one that must mask.

### fs-25: Terminal=true is ignored: terminal applications are launched with no terminal

- **Where:** `src/desktop.rs:169` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): Terminal=, NoDisplay= and Hidden= are read with a real key=value reader (spaces around '=' allowed) in src/desktop.rs. A Terminal=true entry is run inside lntrn-terminal (src/desktop/launch.rs). lntrn-terminal takes no command argument (it execs $SHELL with no args), so Fox writes a small 0700 script into ~/.lantern/run/fox-launch/ and starts lntrn-terminal with SHELL pointing at it; the script restores the real SHELL, runs the command once (atomic mkdir marker) and is simply the user's shell for any later tab of that window. Scripts older than a week are swept. The script text is unit-tested and executed under /bin/sh in a test; it was NOT run end to end inside the real terminal (launching apps was out of bounds).

**What is wrong.** Neither parser reads the Terminal key and launch_app runs the Exec with stdin and stdout on /dev/null. A TUI program offered in Open With (or set as the default handler) starts without a tty: nothing appears, and editors such as vim or nvim can linger in the background holding a swap file. NoDisplay/Hidden are also matched only as the exact strings "NoDisplay=true"/"Hidden=true".

**Trigger.** Install neovim (its desktop entry has Terminal=true and text MIME types), right-click a .txt, Open With, Neovim: nothing visible happens and an nvim process is left running. On this machine phoronix-test-suite-launcher.desktop is such an entry.

```rust
} else if let Some(val) = line.strip_prefix("Exec=") {
    exec = Some(val.to_string());
} else if let Some(val) = line.strip_prefix("MimeType=") {
```

**Skeptic's check.** Both parse_desktop_file (144-210) and parse_desktop_unfiltered (44-71) read only Name/Exec (plus MimeType/NoDisplay/Hidden in the former); DesktopApp has no terminal field; launch_app (232-276) spawns with stdin/stdout null. A Terminal=true handler therefore starts with no tty and shows nothing. Whether nvim lingers or exits was not tested; the visible symptom (nothing happens) holds either way.

**Fix.** Parse Terminal= and either wrap the launch in the Lantern terminal or leave such entries out of Open With. I did not check that lntrn-terminal accepts an -e style argument, so the wrapper form needs confirming in that crate (read-only). Adds behaviour, not mechanical.

### fs-35: fox-launch.log collects the stderr of every launched app and is never rotated

- **Where:** `src/desktop.rs:248` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. fox-launch.log is rotated to .1 past 4 MB.

**What is wrong.** All applications started through launch_app write their stderr for their whole lifetime into ~/.lantern/log/fox-launch.log, opened in append mode with no size cap, truncation or rotation.

**Trigger.** Open media or web content with a chatty application (GStreamer, Chromium or Electron apps) over weeks of use; the log grows without limit.

```rust
let stderr_dest = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
    .create(true)
    .append(true)
    .open(&log_path)
```

**Skeptic's check.** launch_app opens ~/.lantern/log/fox-launch.log with create+append and hands it to the child as stderr for its whole lifetime; grep shows no other code touching the file, so no truncation or rotation. It is 2.96 MB on this machine today.

**Fix.** Before opening, if metadata().len() exceeds a few MB, rename to fox-launch.log.1 (replacing). Local and mechanical. Children already running keep writing to the renamed inode, which is fine.

### dialogs-27: Conflict dialog sizes use the drive formatter: anything under about 512 KB shows "0 MB", folders show their inode size

- **Where:** `src/dialogs.rs:1144` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Conflict dialog sizes show B/KB; folders show just 'Folder'.

**What is wrong.** crate::fs::format_size only knows GB and whole MB (it is written for drive capacities). In the Existing/Replacement comparison most documents read "File · 0 MB" on both sides, and a directory reads "Folder · 0 MB" because ConflictMeta::read takes len() of the directory inode. The dialog's purpose is to let the user compare the two.

**Trigger.** Paste a 40 KB file over a 300 KB file of the same name: both lines say "File · 0 MB".

```rust
format!("{} \u{00B7} {}", kind, crate::fs::format_size(meta.size))
```

**Skeptic's check.** format_meta_line calls crate::fs::format_size, whose body (fs.rs:184-193) only produces "{:.1} GB" or "{:.0} MB"; anything under about 524 KB prints "0 MB". ConflictMeta::read (conflict.rs:104-111) takes metadata().len() for directories too, so a folder shows its inode size.

**Fix.** Use a B/KB/MB/GB formatter; several already exist (properties.rs:299 format_size, format_bytes in preview.rs/views.rs/status.rs/quick_look.rs), so hoist one into fs.rs and call it here. For directories print just "Folder" rather than a size (an item count would need a read_dir on the UI thread, avoid on slow mounts).

### fs-36: Watch is not re-armed when the viewed directory is deleted and recreated, and a failing watch is retried every frame

- **Where:** `src/dir_watch.rs:65` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The watch is re-placed after each reload (re-arming on a recreated folder), a failing watch is retried once a second, and queue overflow counts as a change.

**What is wrong.** The inotify watch is on the inode. If the directory is removed and a new one created at the same path, the kernel drops the watch but `watched` still equals the path, so watch() is a no-op forever and the new directory is only covered by the 3 s mtime poll (which itself only runs while frames are drawn). Conversely, when w.watch() fails (unreadable directory, inotify limit, vanished path) `watched` stays None and the add is retried on every rendered frame, each attempt being a round trip to notify's thread. Queue-overflow notifications (EventKind::Other with the rescan flag) are also ignored by the filter at line 41.

**Trigger.** View build/, run `rm -rf build && mkdir build && make`: new files stop appearing instantly. View a directory without read permission: one failed inotify_add_watch per frame.

```rust
if self.watched.as_deref() == Some(dir) {
    return;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** watch() returns early when `watched` equals the path (65-67) and wayland_loop.rs:1297 calls it every frame. notify 6.1.1 inotify.rs removes its own watch on DELETE_SELF/IGNORED (lines 286-299, 359-360) and nothing tells DirWatcher, so after rm -rf + mkdir of the viewed directory the path matches but no kernel watch exists; only the 3 s mtime poll (wayland_loop.rs:1338-1346) remains. Conversely when w.watch fails, `watched` stays None and the add is retried each rendered frame. The callback filter (41-44) drops EventKind::Other, which is how notify reports Q_OVERFLOW with the Rescan flag (inotify.rs:208-209). All three sub-claims hold; impact is small because the mtime poll still refreshes.

**Fix.** Simplest: when take_due_reload returns true, re-issue w.watch(dir) for the current path (inotify_add_watch on an already-watched inode is idempotent, and it re-arms on a recreated one). Remember the last failed path with a timestamp and retry at most once a second. Treat an event with the Rescan flag as dirty. All local to dir_watch.rs.

### fs-26: File info cache is never invalidated, so dimensions and duration stay stale; clear() is dead code

- **Where:** `src/file_info.rs:57` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): The cache moved to new src/file_info/cache.rs and is keyed to the file's state: get(path, (size, mtime)) with the stamp taken from the listing entry (callers in src/sections/status.rs, src/preview.rs, src/properties.rs updated). A changed stamp triggers a re-probe; a growing file is probed at most once every 2 s (the last state is probed when the wait is over, driven by a new wake_at() that src/wayland_loop.rs adds to its sleep deadline), and the old facts stay on screen meanwhile. A probe that failed while the file was still downloading is therefore retried when it changes. The map is capped at 2000 records and the dead clear() is gone. src/props_load.rs now reports the target's stamp for a link so it matches the listing.
- **Also reported as:** media-21

**What is wrong.** Entries are keyed by path only and live for the whole process: clear() is marked dead_code and has no caller. A failed or early probe is cached forever, and later changes to the file are never seen. The map also only grows (one small record per file ever selected or previewed).

**Trigger.** Select a video that is still downloading: ffprobe fails and no duration is cached. When the download completes the status bar and preview still show no duration until Fox restarts. Or resize an image in an editor and reselect it: the old dimensions are shown.

```rust
if !self.cache.contains_key(path) {
    let mut info = placeholder(path);
```

**Skeptic's check.** FileInfoCache::get keys on the path only (57-70); clear() is #[allow(dead_code)] with no caller (grep); the only other insert is poll() storing the worker result. So the first probe's dimensions/duration/created are served for the life of the process, a probe that failed while the file was still being written is never retried, and the map only grows.

**Fix.** Key on (path, size, mtime) taken from the FileEntry the callers already hold (status.rs:138, preview.rs:111, properties.rs:282 need the entry or its stamp passed in). Not mechanical: a file that is growing (download in progress) would then queue a new ffprobe on every size change, so the stamp change needs a debounce or 'only re-probe when the previous result was empty'.

### fs-28: TypeScript .ts files are labelled "MPEG-TS Video" and sent to ffprobe

- **Where:** `src/file_info.rs:206` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. .ts defaults to TypeScript; it is MPEG-TS only when the 0x47 sync byte sits at offsets 0 and 188.

**What is wrong.** type_name_from_ext and file_category both map `ts` to video, with a comment acknowledging the clash. desktop.rs maps the same extension to application/typescript, so the two tables disagree.

**Trigger.** Select any TypeScript source file: the status bar and preview pane say "MPEG-TS Video" and an ffprobe process is run on it.

```rust
"ts" => "MPEG-TS Video",
```

**Skeptic's check.** type_name_from_ext maps "ts" to "MPEG-TS Video" (206, with a comment at 234 acknowledging the clash) and file_category puts "ts" in Video (174), so build_info runs ffprobe on every selected .ts file. desktop.rs:318 maps the same extension to application/typescript.

**Fix.** Default the label to TypeScript and classify as MPEG-TS only when the worker finds the 0x47 sync byte at offsets 0 and 188 (a single 'G' at offset 0 is not enough for source text). Local to file_info.rs; the placeholder shown before the probe lands will say TypeScript. Other tables (icons, thumbnails) may have their own ts mapping; I did not check them.

### fs-27: A probe that never returns stalls the single worker and leaves probing() true forever

- **Where:** `src/file_info.rs:264` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The probe worker skips non-regular files.

**What is wrong.** There is one fox-probe thread and no timeout. Opening a FIFO with an image extension blocks in open() until a writer appears; ffprobe on a FIFO or a stuck device blocks in Command::output(). After that no further probe completes, in_flight never returns to zero, and probing() keeps needs_anim true (wayland_loop.rs:1373), so Fox renders at 60 fps for the rest of the session. A milder version of the same: holding an arrow key through hundreds of media files queues one ffprobe each with no cancellation, and Fox animates until the backlog drains.

**Trigger.** `mkfifo x.png` in a folder and click it in Fox. From then on the status bar never shows dimensions or durations for any file and Fox redraws continuously.

```rust
let mut file = std::fs::File::open(path).ok()?;
let mut buf = [0u8; 32];
file.read(&mut buf).ok()?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** status.rs:137-138 calls file_info.get for any single selected non-directory entry, so a FIFO qualifies. There is one worker (probe_loop) and build_info goes straight to read_image_dimensions, where File::open on a FIFO blocks until a writer appears; probe_media's Command::output() has no timeout either. in_flight is only decremented in poll() when a result arrives, so probing() stays true and wayland_loop.rs:1373 keeps needs_anim set, i.e. continuous redraw, and every later probe is stuck behind it. Trigger (a FIFO or stuck device with a media extension) is rare.

**Fix.** Minimal mechanical guard: at the top of build_info, `if !std::fs::metadata(path).map(|m| m.is_file()).unwrap_or(false) { return placeholder(path) }`. An ffprobe timeout (spawn, wait with deadline, kill) and dropping superseded jobs are good follow-ups but are more than mechanical.

### fileops-30: Cross-device move and copy drop all modification times and directory permissions

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:61` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Copies carry the source's timestamps; copied folders get the source's permissions and times.

**What is wrong.** fs::copy preserves file mode bits only; nothing restores mtime/atime, and directories are created with default umask permissions. A same-filesystem move keeps everything (rename), a cross-device move silently resets every timestamp to 'now' and turns a 0700 folder into 0755.

**Trigger.** Move photos from the phone or a camera card to disk: all files show today's date, so sort-by-date (in Fox and the image viewer that mirrors it) is scrambled. Move a chmod 700 folder across devices: it becomes world-readable.

```rust
std::fs::create_dir_all(dst)?;
```

**Skeptic's check.** copy_dir_recursive uses create_dir_all (umask default mode) and std::fs::copy, which on Linux copies the mode bits but not timestamps; run_copy (ops.rs:177-181) does the same for single files. Nothing calls set_times/set_permissions. A same-filesystem move is a rename and keeps everything, a cross-device move resets every mtime to now and turns a 0700 directory into 0755.

**Fix.** After each file copy call File::set_times with the source's atime/mtime, and after finishing a directory apply the source's permissions and times; ignore errors (FAT, exFAT and MTP destinations refuse them). Local. Preserving mtime on plain copies as well matches what cp -p and other file managers do; if that is not wanted, restrict it to the move path.

### fileops-31: .trashinfo Path is written raw but percent-decoded on restore

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:606` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. trashinfo Path= is percent-encoded on write (trash and redo) and decoded as bytes on restore.

**What is wrong.** The XDG spec requires Path to be URL-encoded. Fox writes the display() string (also lossy for non-UTF-8 names; a newline in a name breaks the line format) and read_trashinfo_path/percent_decode decodes %XX on the way back. Any name containing '%' followed by two hex digits is restored under a different name; other trash tools will mis-decode Fox's entries the same way. undo.rs:182-185 writes the same unencoded form.

**Trigger.** Trash 'My%20Report%41.pdf' (typical of web downloads), open Trash, Restore: it comes back as 'My ReportA.pdf' (verified decode), or as 'My ReportA (restored 1).pdf' if that name exists.

```rust
"[Trash Info]\nPath={}\nDeletionDate={}\n",
entry.path.display(),
```

**Skeptic's check.** trash_selected writes `Path={}` with entry.path.display() (605-609) and undo.rs:182-185 does the same on redo, while read_trashinfo_path runs percent_decode on the value. I ran the function: `/home/a/My%20Report%41.pdf` decodes to `/home/a/My ReportA.pdf`, so such a file is restored under a different name. display() is also lossy for non-UTF-8 names and a newline in a name breaks the line format.

**Fix.** Percent-encode on write in both file_ops.rs and undo.rs from as_os_str().as_bytes(), leaving unreserved characters and `/` unescaped. Raw paths without `%` decode identically before and after, so existing entries keep working; entries already written with a literal `%XX` stay wrong, as they are today. percent_decode's from_utf8 fallback should eventually return bytes (OsString) rather than the undecoded string.

### fileops-36: Permanent delete inside Trash leaves orphan .trashinfo files; uppercase archive extensions create an empty folder

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:688` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Permanent delete inside Trash removes the .trashinfo sidecar; extraction matches archive suffixes case-insensitively and only makes the folder for a known type.

**What is wrong.** Two small leftovers. (1) 'Delete Permanently' in the Trash view goes through delete_selected, which removes Trash/files/<name> but never the matching Trash/info/<name>.trashinfo, so sidecars accumulate until Empty Trash. (2) is_archive lowercases the name, but extract_selected matches '.tar.gz' etc. case-sensitively (lines 912, 926-956): for 'BACKUP.TAR.GZ' the menu offers Extract Here, the folder 'BACKUP.TAR' is created (line 922) and then the loop hits `continue` without extracting.

**Trigger.** (1) Open Trash, right-click an item -> Delete Permanently, then look in ~/.local/share/Trash/info. (2) Extract Here on an archive with an upper-case compound extension: an empty folder appears.

```rust
let res = if path.is_dir() {
    std::fs::remove_dir_all(path)
} else {
    std::fs::remove_file(path)
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** (1) delete_selected (675-705) removes only the entry path; nothing touches Trash/info/<name>.trashinfo, and CTX_TRASH inside Trash goes through it, so sidecars are orphaned until Empty Trash. (2) is_archive lowercases the whole path (93) but extract_selected matches `name.ends_with(".tar.gz")` etc. on the raw string (912, 926-956). For BACKUP.TAR.GZ the stem falls to file_stem = BACKUP.TAR, create_dir_all runs at 922, ext is `gz`, and the chain ends in `continue`: an empty folder and no extraction. Single upper-case extensions (.ZIP, .7Z) work because `ext` is lowercased.

**Fix.** (1) After a successful delete of a path whose parent is Trash/files, also remove info/<file name>.trashinfo. (2) Lowercase the file name once and match the compound suffixes on that, including the rsplitn stem derivation, and create the extraction folder only after the archive type is recognised. Both local.

### fs-16: Children started with spawn() and never waited on become zombies (xdg-open fallback, Open Terminal)

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:733` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. All spawn-and-drop children go through desktop::spawn_reaped / xdg_open, which wait on a thread.
- **Also reported as:** fileops-32, app-29

**What is wrong.** launch_app in desktop.rs reaps its child, but the fallback path used whenever no default app is resolved drops the Child immediately. Rust does not wait on drop and Fox installs no SIGCHLD handler, so each such child stays as a <defunct> entry under Fox once it exits. Same pattern at wayland_actions/click.rs:423, wayland_loop.rs:974, wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:568 and 578, and file_ops.rs:1015-1018 (lntrn-terminal for 'Open Terminal Here').

**Trigger.** Open files with no extension or an unmapped extension, or use Open Terminal Here and close the terminal. `ps` shows one more `[xdg-open] <defunct>` or `[lntrn-terminal] <defunct>` per use until Fox exits.

```rust
std::thread::spawn(move || {
    let _ = std::process::Command::new("xdg-open").arg(&path).spawn();
});
```

**Skeptic's check.** Grep of src: the only wait on a launched child is desktop.rs:271 (launch_app) plus sudo.rs's wait_with_output; there is no SIGCHLD handling. The sites that call `.spawn()` and drop the Child are file_ops.rs:733 (xdg-open fallback), 748 (dead-code open_with), 1015-1017 (lntrn-terminal), click.rs:423, context_menu.rs:568 and 578, wayland_loop.rs:974. Rust does not reap on drop, so each becomes a defunct entry under Fox once it exits, for the life of the Fox process (the --desktop instance never exits). Impact is one process-table slot per launch, hence low rather than medium.

**Fix.** One helper used by all sites: on the already-dedicated thread, `if let Ok(mut c) = cmd.spawn() { let _ = c.wait(); }`, with stdin/stdout null and process_group(0) as launch_app does. The thread then lives as long as the child (terminal sessions included), which launch_app already accepts.

### fs-21: lsblk is spawned on the render thread every two seconds while frames are being drawn

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:441` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): src/devices.rs: detection runs on a 'fox-devices' thread that sleeps in poll() until a block/USB uevent, a mount-table change or a kick, sends lists only when they changed and wakes the loop. The frame path only kicks it every 2 s. Unprivileged uevent bind and a quiet /proc/self/mounts poll were verified on this machine.

**What is wrong.** wayland_loop.rs:1350-1354 calls refresh_drives and refresh_phones every 2 s from the frame path. detect_drives forks lsblk and waits for it, parses /proc/mounts and statvfs-es each mount; detect_phones walks /sys/bus/usb. On this machine lsblk alone took 4-19 ms, which is a whole 16 ms frame budget, so smooth scrolling and drags drop a frame every two seconds. draw_phone_row additionally calls is_path_mounted (read /proc/mounts plus realpath) for every phone on every frame (sections/sidebar.rs:416).

**Trigger.** Scroll a long folder smoothly or drag a file for several seconds: a hitch every 2 s. Worse if lsblk has to wake a sleeping disk.

```rust
let output = match std::process::Command::new("lsblk")
    .args(["-J", "-b", "-o", "NAME,LABEL,FSTYPE,SIZE,RM,MOUNTPOINT,PATH,TYPE"])
    .output()
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland_loop.rs:1350-1354 calls refresh_drives and refresh_phones every 2 s from the frame path; detect_drives runs `lsblk ... .output()` synchronously (441-448) plus /proc/mounts and statvfs, and detect_phones walks /sys/bus/usb. No thread hop. draw_phone_row calls is_path_mounted (read /proc/mounts and canonicalize) per phone per frame (sections/sidebar.rs:416). One caveat on the trigger: this block only runs when a frame is rendered (the loop `continue`s at line 161 when idle), so the cost is a dropped frame every 2 s during scrolling or drags, nothing when idle. I did not time lsblk myself.

**Fix.** Move detection to a background thread that sends Vec<Drive>/Vec<Phone> over a channel, and cache each phone's mounted state from that result. Because the poll is frame-gated, an idle window also never notices a hot-plug until the pointer moves; a background thread that pokes the loop fixes both. Threading change.

### fs-23: slugify slices the USB serial at byte 4 and can panic; the serial is also used unsanitised in the mount path

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:661` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category panic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. slugify takes the first four ASCII alphanumerics of the serial by char.

**What is wrong.** The serial comes from sysfs as UTF-8 converted from the device's UTF-16 string descriptor. If a multi-byte character straddles byte offset 4 the slice panics (confirmed with "123é"). detect_phones runs in App::new and every 2 s on the render thread, so Fox would crash at startup and whenever that device is attached. The name part is restricted to ASCII alphanumerics, but `short` is appended verbatim, so a serial starting with characters such as '/' produces a nested or unexpected mount directory.

**Trigger.** Attach an MTP/PTP device whose serial string has a non-ASCII character within its first four characters positioned across byte 4 (garbage serials on cheap devices). Fox panics in detect_phones.

```rust
let short = if serial.len() >= 4 {
    &serial[..4]
} else {
    serial
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** slugify slices `&serial[..4]` whenever serial.len() >= 4 (661-665). Reproduced the panic with `123é` (`end byte index 4 is not a char boundary`). The serial comes from sysfs through read_to_string (577, 620-624), so valid non-ASCII UTF-8 passes through, and detect_phones runs in App::new and every 2 s on the main loop, so such a device crashes Fox at startup and on attach. Devices with non-ASCII serials are rare. `short` is also appended unsanitised, so a `/` in the first four characters yields a nested mount directory.

**Fix.** `let short: String = serial.chars().filter(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric()).take(4).collect();`. This changes the mount directory name only for serials with non-alphanumerics in the first four characters, which is harmless.

### fs-24: mount_phone never tells jmtpfs which device to open

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:730` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Phone carries usb_address (sysfs busnum, devnum) and phone_mounts::mount passes -device=<busnum>,<devnum> to jmtpfs. The option was confirmed in this machine's jmtpfs --help; not tested with two devices attached.

**What is wrong.** Without `-device=<bus>,<dev>` jmtpfs opens the first MTP/PTP device it finds. detect_phones lists every USB device with a class 06 interface (phones, cameras, some multifunction printers) as separate sidebar rows, each with its own mount point, but clicking any of them mounts the same first device, or fails as busy and shows the misleading "Unlock it and set USB mode" message.

**Trigger.** Connect a phone and a camera (or two phones). Click the second row: the first device's storage appears under the second device's name, or an unlock error is shown for an unlocked phone.

```rust
let status = std::process::Command::new("jmtpfs")
    .arg(&phone.mount_point)
    .status()
```

**Skeptic's check.** Confirmed in code: mount_phone runs `jmtpfs <mount_point>` with no device argument (730-732) although detect_phones emits one row and one mount point per class-06 USB device, and Phone carries no bus/device number. What is unknown: how jmtpfs behaves with two MTP/PTP devices attached (first device mounted under the second row's name, or a busy failure reported as `Unlock it`). That comes from jmtpfs documentation and was not tested.

**Fix.** Read busnum and devnum from the device's sysfs directory in detect_phones, store them on Phone and pass `-device=<busnum>,<devnum>`. Small, but the option syntax and behaviour need checking against a real second device before shipping.

### fs-33: Eject unmounts only the representative partition of a multi-partition removable disk

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:924` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Eject unmounts every mounted partition of a removable disk and reports the first error.

**What is wrong.** group_removable_by_disk collapses all partitions of a stick into one sidebar row represented by the largest mounted partition. Eject unmounts just that one device. Other mounted partitions of the same disk stay mounted (format already has unmount_all_on_disk for this, eject does not use it).

**Trigger.** Mount two partitions of one USB stick, click Eject. Fox navigates home and the row now represents the other, still mounted, partition; pulling the stick at that point risks losing writes on it.

```rust
pub fn unmount_drive(drive: &Drive) -> Result<(), String> {
    let output = std::process::Command::new("udisksctl")
        .args(["unmount", "-b", &drive.device])
```

**Skeptic's check.** group_removable_by_disk collapses all partitions of a removable disk into one row represented by the largest mounted partition (302-326). eject_drive calls only fs::unmount_drive(&drive) (places.rs:137), which unmounts drive.device alone (924-934). Other mounted partitions of the same disk stay mounted and the row then represents one of them. Fox itself can only mount the representative, so this needs a second partition mounted by something else (automounter, Ventoy-style sticks), hence low.

**Fix.** For removable drives unmount every mounted partition whose parent_disk matches. unmount_all_on_disk already does the walk but swallows errors; eject needs a variant that returns the first error so a busy partition is still reported. Local change.

### fs-31: Slow-mount table is up to 2 s stale right after mounting a phone, so the first operations treat it as a fast disk

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:1063` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category race; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The slow-mount table is invalidated after every mount and unmount Fox performs.

**What is wrong.** on_phone_click calls mount_phone and immediately navigate_to(mount_point). If the cached table was refreshed less than 2 s ago (it is refreshed from the frame path) it does not contain the new jmtpfs mount, so reload() takes the synchronous branch, the watcher tries to watch it, the mtime poll stats it and git is run on it, all on or from the render thread, until the TTL expires.

**Trigger.** Click a phone whose jmtpfs mount completes in under two seconds: the first listing of the phone root is done synchronously and a `git rev-parse` is started inside the MTP mount.

```rust
let stale = guard
    .as_ref()
    .map_or(true, |t| t.refreshed.elapsed() >= MOUNT_TABLE_TTL);
```

**Skeptic's check.** MOUNT_TABLE is written only inside is_slow_path and trusted for 2 s (1061-1075). The loop calls is_slow_path every rendered frame (wayland_loop.rs:1292), so the table was refreshed at most 2 s before any click. on_phone_click calls mount_phone then navigate_to(mount_point) (places.rs:201-202); if jmtpfs returns before the TTL lapses, reload() sees a fast path and lists the phone root synchronously (nav.rs:152), and the same frame's watcher and git.refresh run against it. Timing-dependent but reachable whenever the mount completes quickly. The work done is small (one root readdir, one git rev-parse), hence low.

**Fix.** Add `pub fn invalidate_mount_table()` that sets MOUNT_TABLE to None and call it at the end of mount_phone, mount_drive, unmount_drive and unmount_phone. Local.

### fs-30: Git badges are missing when the folder is reached through a symlink, and for names starting with a space

- **Where:** `src/git_status.rs:129` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Git marks are matched against the canonical directory and names keep their leading spaces.

**What is wrong.** `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` prints the canonical path, but `dir` is the path as the user navigated. If any component of the navigated path is a symlink, strip_prefix fails for every record and the folder shows a branch chip but no badges (verified: toplevel of a repo reached via a symlink is the real path). Separately, the record is `XY<space>path`; trim_start removes all leading whitespace, so a file named " notes.txt" is looked up without its leading space and gets no badge (verified porcelain output).

**Trigger.** Open a repo through a symlinked path such as ~/proj -> /mnt/storage/proj and modify a file: no badge. Or create a file whose name begins with a space in any repo.

```rust
let abs = root.join(rel.trim_start().trim_end_matches('/'));
...
let Ok(below) = abs.strip_prefix(dir) else {
    continue;
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** scan() joins record paths onto `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (104-107, 129), which is the physical path, then requires abs.strip_prefix(dir) with `dir` as navigated (133-135). If any component of the navigated path is a symlink the prefix never matches and every record is skipped, leaving the branch chip without badges. Separately `rel.trim_start()` (129) strips all leading whitespace from `<space>path`, so a file whose name begins with a space is looked up under the wrong name. Both follow directly from the code; the porcelain -z record shape is XY, one space, path.

**Fix.** Canonicalise `dir` once in scan(), strip against the canonical dir, and still key the marks by the navigated `dir.join(first)` so render lookups match. Replace `rel.trim_start()` with `&rel[1..]` (the record is filtered to len > 3 and XY is ASCII, so the index is safe). Local.

### media-13: Symlinked images and videos keep a stale thumbnail when the target changes

- **Where:** `src/icons.rs:228` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C6): Falls out of fs-2: for a symlink the listing now carries the target's size and mtime, and those are what the thumbnail cache key in src/icons.rs (unchanged) is built from, so a rewritten target gets a fresh texture. Covered by the listing test in src/links/tests.rs (target rewritten -> new stamp).

**What is wrong.** The in-memory key relies on the listing's size and mtime, but fs::list_directory fills those from DirEntry::metadata(), which is an lstat. For a symlink the size is the length of the link text and the mtime is the link's own, neither of which changes when the target file is edited. The worker's disk-cache name does follow the link (std::fs::metadata), so the on-disk side is right, but the GPU texture under the unchanged key keeps being served.

**Trigger.** ln -s ~/work/chart.png ~/Desktop/chart.png, open ~/Desktop so the thumbnail loads, then overwrite ~/work/chart.png. The link's thumbnail keeps the old picture until Fox restarts or the >512 purge happens.

```rust
let stamp = entry
    .modified
    .and_then(|t| t.duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH).ok())
    .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
    .unwrap_or(0);
format!("thumb:{}:{}:{}", entry.path.display(), entry.size, stamp)
```

**Skeptic's check.** The thumb key uses entry.size and entry.modified (228-233), which list_directory fills from DirEntry::metadata(), an lstat (fs.rs:99-102; verified it does not follow links). For a symlink those are the link's own length and mtime and do not change when the target is rewritten, so the cached texture keeps being served. thumbs::cache_file uses std::fs::metadata (follows), so only the in-memory key is stale. Rare case.

**Fix.** Falls out of the fs-2 fix: take size and modified from the link target for symlink entries. Do it there rather than special-casing the key, and it inherits fs-2's review needs.

### media-14: SVG pixels are premultiplied twice (tiny-skia output fed to a shader that premultiplies)

- **Where:** `src/icons.rs:419` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. SVG rasters are demultiplied before upload/caching (icons, thumbnails, Quick Look); the thumbnail cache generation was bumped and old files are pruned.

**What is wrong.** tiny_skia::Pixmap stores premultiplied RGBA. lntrn-render's texture shader expects straight alpha and does `vec4(color.rgb * color.a, color.a)`. Folder icons (icons.rs:419), SVG file thumbnails (thumbs.rs:237, pixmap.take(), which is also saved into the PNG disk cache as if it were straight alpha) and Quick Look SVGs (quick_look.rs:179) therefore have every partially transparent pixel darkened by its alpha a second time. Opaque pixels are unaffected, so it shows as dark fringes on anti-aliased edges and dimmer translucent fills.

**Trigger.** Look at any folder icon or SVG thumbnail against a light theme: edges have a dark outline; an SVG with a 50% white overlay renders at roughly a quarter brightness instead of half.

```rust
Some(tex.upload(gpu, pixmap.data(), w, h))
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read both sides. tiny-skia 0.12.0 (the version resvg 0.47 pulls in per Cargo.lock) documents Pixmap as `A container that owns premultiplied RGBA pixels`, and lntrn-render/tex/src/lib.rs fs_main returns `vec4(color.rgb * color.a, color.a)` with One/OneMinusSrcAlpha blending, i.e. it expects straight alpha. icons.rs:419 uploads pixmap.data(), thumbs.rs:237 wraps pixmap.take() as an RgbaImage (and saves it to the PNG disk cache), quick_look.rs:179 does the same. Every partially transparent SVG pixel is therefore multiplied by alpha twice: dark fringes on anti-aliased edges and dimmer translucent fills. Opaque pixels are unaffected. Cosmetic.

**Fix.** Use take_demultiplied() (present in tiny-skia 0.12.0, pixmap.rs:269) at all three sites; in rasterize_svg_bytes upload `&pixmap.take_demultiplied()`. Bump the thumbnail disk-cache hash input so SVG thumbnails cached with premultiplied data regenerate. Visible effect is slightly lighter icon edges, which is the correct rendering.

### render-20: Sidebar layout has no clip or scroll: sections that do not fit are unreachable and draw past the sidebar bottom

- **Where:** `src/layout.rs:237` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): The sidebar now scrolls and is clipped to its strip. New src/layout/sidebar.rs (moved out of layout.rs) lays the rows out inside a viewport with a clamped scroll offset and a scrollbar gutter; App::sidebar_layout(hf, s) in src/app/places.rs is the single source used by render.rs, the right-click hit-test (wayland_actions/context_menu.rs) and the favourite-drag release (wayland_loop.rs). src/sections/sidebar_zones.rs registers only the visible part of each row/header as a zone; src/sections/sidebar.rs draws inside a painter+text clip and shows a 16px*scale scrollbar (ZONE_SIDEBAR_SCROLLBAR, draggable, track-click jumps). Wheel over an overflowing sidebar scrolls it (src/scrollbar.rs wheel_aside, hooked in wayland_loop.rs). In pick mode the strip ends at the pick bar.

**What is wrong.** build_sidebar_layout stacks Places (40*s each), Favorites (40*s each), drives (64*s) and phones (56*s) downward from the nav bar with no bound, and neither it nor draw_sidebar clips to `sidebar_rect` or offers scrolling. Rows that land below `content_bottom` are drawn over the status bar area or off-window, and their zones lose to the status-bar zones registered later.

**Trigger.** Add a dozen favorites with a few drives plugged in, or make the window short (the minimum is 300px high, less than the Places section alone): the Devices section and lower favorites are cut off and cannot be clicked or scrolled to.

```rust
let mut y = nav_bar_y(s) + header_gap;
...
for _ in 0..num_favorites {
    favorite_items.push(Rect::new(item_x, y, item_w, place_h));
    y += place_h;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** build_sidebar_layout stacks Places, Favorites, drives and phones downward from the nav bar with no bound (237-288). sections/sidebar.rs contains no clip or scroll (grep finds none), and render.rs:602-638 registers a zone for every item rect without intersecting sidebar_rect. With the window minimum at 300 px high (wayland.rs:371) nine Places rows at 40 px already exceed it, and a dozen favorites plus a few drives overflow a normal window: rows draw over the status bar or off-window and cannot be reached.

**Fix.** Give the sidebar its own scroll offset and clip (ScrollArea over sidebar_rect), intersect item zones with that rect, and route wheel events over the sidebar to it. The same offset must be applied in handle_right_click's build_sidebar_layout hit-tests and in the favorite drag code. New UI behaviour.

### render-16: Preview pane never yields below 220*s: in a narrow window it leaves a negative-width file list and is drawn over the sidebar

- **Where:** `src/layout.rs:442` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The preview pane is dropped when it would leave the list under 200px; the resize cursor follows.

**What is wrong.** The doc comment promises 0 when the pane would not fit, but the function always returns at least 220*s while open. render.rs:106-112 then computes `content.w = full_content.w - preview_w_px`, which is negative once the content area is under 220*s, and `preview_pane_rect` places the pane at `content.x + content.w - preview_w`, i.e. left of the content origin, on top of the sidebar. The file list is clipped to nothing and the resize handle zone sits inside the sidebar.

**Trigger.** List or Tree view with the preview pane open, shrink the window toward its 400px minimum width (anything under about 460 logical px): the file list vanishes and the preview panel overlaps the right part of the sidebar.

```rust
/// content area. Returns 0 if the preview is closed or would not fit.
...
let min = PREVIEW_MIN_W * s;
let max = (content_w_px * PREVIEW_MAX_FRACTION).max(min);
(preview_w_logical * s).clamp(min, max)
```

**Skeptic's check.** preview_effective_w's doc says it returns 0 when the pane would not fit, but while open it returns `(w*s).clamp(min, max)` with max = max(0.6*content, min), so never less than 220*s (437-444). render.rs:106-112 subtracts it from full_content.w, which is window - 240*s; below 460 logical px wide the result is negative and preview_pane_rect places the pane left of the content origin, over the sidebar. The window minimum is 400x300 (wayland.rs:371), so the state is reachable in List/Tree view with the preview open. No caller re-checks the remaining width.

**Fix.** Return 0.0 when `content_w_px - PREVIEW_MIN_W*s` is below a usable list width (for example 200*s). render.rs already treats 0 as no pane. Also gate the EW-resize cursor test at wayland_loop.rs:242-251 on the effective width being > 0, otherwise the resize cursor appears at the content edge with no pane. Local.

### wayland-22: std::env::args() panics at startup when any argument is not valid UTF-8

- **Where:** `src/main.rs:353` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category panic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Arguments are read with args_os; flags are matched on a lossy copy and paths taken from the raw values.

**What is wrong.** All four argument scans use std::env::args(), which panics during iteration on a non-Unicode argument. A file manager is routinely started with a directory path, and directory names in legacy encodings are not valid UTF-8.

**Trigger.** `lntrn-file-manager $'/mnt/old/caf\xe9'` (or Command Center's 'open folder' on such a directory): Fox panics before opening a window. Confirmed with a standalone rustc test that args() panics on such input.

```rust
let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().skip(1).collect();
// also main.rs:353, 363, 369
```

**Skeptic's check.** main.rs:353 (first statement of main), 363, 369 and parse_args:296 all iterate std::env::args(); no args_os anywhere. Compiled a scratch binary with rustc 1.98.1: `./t1 args $'caf\xe9'` panics in std/src/env.rs (Result::unwrap on Err "caf\xE9"), exit 101. So any non-UTF-8 argv element (a legacy-encoded directory passed as start dir or --start-dir) kills Fox before a window opens. The first site reached is line 353, not 296. Rare input, hence low.

**Fix.** Collect once with std::env::args_os() into Vec<OsString>; compare flags via to_str() (non-UTF-8 never matches a flag), build start_dir / --start-dir PathBuf straight from the OsString, and use to_string_lossy only for --title/--filters/--save-name. Local to main.rs, no behaviour change for valid UTF-8.

### wayland-23: --desktop daemonizes but keeps the terminal as stderr; after the terminal closes the next eprintln! panics

- **Where:** `src/main.rs:385` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category panic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The --desktop daemon points stdin at /dev/null and stdout/stderr at ~/.lantern/log/fox-desktop.log.

**What is wrong.** The stated purpose is to survive terminal close, but stdio is not redirected. Once the pty master is gone, writes to stderr fail with EIO, and eprintln! panics on any write error other than EBADF. On the main thread that ends the desktop instance; on the cloud sync thread (cloud::log_line echoes with eprintln!) it silently stops syncing. Not reachable when launched by the compositor, which already uses stdio = /dev/null, and nothing in the repo currently launches --desktop.

**Trigger.** Run `lntrn-file-manager --desktop` from a terminal, close the terminal, then cause any log line (a cloud sync message, a launch error, the xdg Close message). Verified with a scratch binary: eprintln! panics when stderr is a hung-up pty slave.

```rust
if desktop {
    unsafe {
        let pid = libc::fork();
        ...
        libc::setsid(); // new session leader
    }
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Code is as described: fork + setsid with no stdio redirection (385-396). I verified with a scratch binary that eprintln! panics when stderr writes fail (`2>/dev/full` -> exit 101), and cloud::log_line (cloud/mod.rs:67) does eprintln! on the sync thread. What I could not establish is reachability: grep over the whole Lantern-DE tree finds no launcher passing --desktop (only main.rs:363 reads it), so it needs a manual `--desktop` run from a terminal that is then closed. Did not reproduce the EIO-on-hung-pty case myself.
