**Fix.** After setsid(), open /dev/null (or append-open ~/.lantern/log/fox-desktop.log for 1/2) and dup2 onto fds 0,1,2. Local, only affects the --desktop path. Sending stderr to a log file is preferable to /dev/null so the diagnostics are not lost.

### cloud-net-24: CLI login prompt says the password will not echo, but it is read with plain read_line (and --password exposes it in argv)

- **Where:** `src/main.rs:438` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category security; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The CLI password prompt turns terminal echo off (and restores it); usage notes that --password is visible in the process list.

**What is wrong.** Nothing disables terminal echo, so the password typed after this prompt is displayed on screen and kept in terminal scrollback despite the message. The alternative `--password <pw>` puts it in the process argument list (visible in /proc/<pid>/cmdline and shell history).

**Trigger.** Run `lntrn-file-manager --cloud-login --email me@x.com` in a terminal and type the password: it is shown in clear text.

```rust
eprintln!("password (will not echo on stdin):");
let mut buf = String::new();
if std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut buf).is_err() {
```

**Skeptic's check.** run_cloud_login prints "password (will not echo on stdin):" then calls std::io::stdin().read_line with no termios handling anywhere in main.rs, so a tty echoes the password. --password also takes it from argv (line 417-420). CLI-only helper, so low.

**Fix.** When libc::isatty(0), tcgetattr, clear ECHO, read, restore (restore on the error path too), print a newline. Minimum: fix the misleading message. Keep --password for scripting but document it as visible in /proc/<pid>/cmdline. Local to run_cloud_login.

### sections-18: Pick bar filter button width is unbounded: a long filter name hides the filename field and covers Cancel/Save

- **Where:** `src/pick_bar.rs:74` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The picker filter button width is clamped and its label truncated.

**What is wrong.** The filter button is sized from the caller-supplied filter name with no upper bound and is registered after the Confirm and Cancel zones, so where it overlaps them it wins the click. When it leaves less than 50*s px, the Save filename field is neither drawn nor given a zone.

**Trigger.** An app passes a filter whose name is ~126+ characters at the default 1580 px picker width (or a shorter one once the picker window is narrowed/tiled): the filename field disappears in Save mode; a longer name puts the filter button over Cancel/Save, and clicking them cycles the filter instead.

```rust
let filter_w = (filter_label.len() as f32 * 10.0 * s).max(120.0 * s);
...
let input_w = cancel_x - gap - left_x;
if input_w > 50.0 * s {
```

**Skeptic's check.** filter_w = len*10*s with only a lower bound (line 74); the filter zone is added after CONFIRM and CANCEL (lines 52, 62, 76) and InteractionContext::on_left_pressed picks the last-added zone (lntrn-ui input.rs:111), so an overlapping filter button does win the click; the filename field is skipped when input_w <= 50*s. Arithmetic checks out: at the 1580 px default the field disappears at about 130 bytes and Cancel is covered at about 137. Not established: that any real client sends a filter name that long through the portal, so reachability is unproven. Note len() is bytes, so non-ASCII names hit it about 3x sooner.

**Fix.** Clamp: filter_w = filter_w.min((cancel_x - gap - pad - 220.0*s).max(120.0*s)) and truncate the label with sections::truncate_to_width so it does not spill out of the button. Local to pick_bar.rs.

### media-20: Preview pane wraps the file name by an estimated 0.52 em per character; wider names lose characters at the wrap point

- **Where:** `src/preview.rs:90` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The preview pane wraps the file name by measured width.

**What is wrong.** wrap_two_lines() splits by character count assuming every glyph is 0.52 em wide. Each piece is then queued with max_width(inner_w). lntrn-text wraps at max_width and, with no clip stack active (the preview pane is drawn outside ScrollArea::begin/end), clips to a single line box, so whatever part of line one is wider than inner_w wraps to an invisible second line. Those characters are not on the drawn second line either, so the name is shown with a hole in the middle. Digits, capitals and CJK are all wider than 0.52 em in the default font.

**Trigger.** With the preview pane at its default width select a file such as Screenshot_20260812_153045_Chrome.png or a Japanese-named file: the first line ends one to several characters early and the second line continues after the gap.

```rust
let char_w = name_font * 0.52;
let lines = wrap_two_lines(&entry.name, inner_w, char_w);
for line in &lines {
    TextLabel::new(line, inner_x, cy)
        .size(FontSize::Custom(name_font))
        .color(palette.text)
        .max_width(inner_w)
```

**Skeptic's check.** wrap_two_lines splits by char count at 0.52 em and each piece is queued with max_width(inner_w). draw_preview_pane runs at render.rs:923 after the ScrollArea was closed, and Fox has no other push_clip, so lntrn-text's queue_entry uses the default bounds [0,0,screen_w,y+1.2*size] (engine.rs:145): the layout wraps at max_width and the wrapped tail is clipped away. I measured Inter (the active family) from ~/.lantern/fonts/Inter.ttf: 'Screenshot_20260812_153045' averages 0.559 em/char, digits 0.593, capitals 0.632. At the default 360 px pane, inner_w = 328*s and max_chars = 28; 28 such characters are about 345 px, so the last one or two glyphs of line one vanish and are not on line two either.

**Fix.** Build both lines by measurement: longest prefix with text.measure_width(prefix, name_font) <= inner_w - ~2px slack (binary search like sections::truncate_to_width), then truncate_to_width for the second line. TextRenderer is already a parameter. Keep the slack: exact measured width as max_width is the known last-glyph-clip trap.

### media-17: format_date year loop is O(years): a file with a far-future mtime hangs the frame

- **Where:** `src/preview.rs:218` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The per-year loops are gone; out-of-range stamps show a dash.
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-28

**What is wrong.** The year is found by subtracting one year at a time. btrfs and tmpfs store 64-bit second timestamps, so an mtime of 9e18 seconds is accepted; the loop then runs about 2.9e11 iterations. Measured: 1e16 s takes 230 ms per call, so i64-range values take minutes, and the function is called every frame for the selected file (twice: Modified and Created). views.rs:552 has the identical loop and runs it for every visible list row, so there the folder cannot even be displayed.

**Trigger.** touch -d @9000000000000000000 f on the btrfs home (confirmed accepted here), select f with the preview pane open (or just view the folder in list view): Fox stops responding. A crafted tar archive that restores such an mtime does the same.

```rust
let mut y = 1970u64;
let mut remaining = days;
loop {
    let leap = y % 4 == 0 && (y % 100 != 0 || y % 400 == 0);
    let yd = if leap { 366 } else { 365 };
    if remaining < yd {
        break;
    }
    remaining -= yd;
    y += 1;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** The year is found by a one-year-per-iteration loop with no clamp on secs (preview.rs:216-226; identical loops at views.rs:562, properties.rs:361, dialogs.rs:1161, file_ops.rs:21). I confirmed `touch -d @9000000000000000000` is accepted on this machine's btrfs and that std returns Ok(9000000000000000000) for it, i.e. roughly 2.9e11 iterations per call. preview.rs runs it per frame for the selected entry and views.rs:335 per list row, so the UI thread hangs. Needs a garbage timestamp (hand-made or from a broken archive), so low.

**Fix.** Replace the loops with one shared helper: libc::localtime_r (returns NULL on overflow -> show "—"), or the closed-form civil-from-days algorithm with a sanity clamp (year > 9999 -> "—"). Same helper as the timezone fix.

### dialogs-26: Properties silently refuses to open for a broken symlink (and for /)

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:162` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Properties opens for a dangling symlink (describing the link) and for /.

**What is wrong.** symlink_metadata succeeds and the link target is read, but the following metadata() call follows the link and fails for a dangling one, so from_path returns None and the caller does nothing. The Symlink section (with its "Unknown" fallback) can therefore never show the one case where it matters. file_name() is None for the filesystem root, so Properties from empty space in / also does nothing.

**Trigger.** Right-click a dangling symlink, Properties: no dialog, no message. Right-click empty space while viewing /, Properties (via any target resolving to the current dir): nothing.

```rust
let meta = std::fs::metadata(path).ok()?;
let name = path.file_name()?.to_string_lossy().to_string();
```

**Skeptic's check.** from_path: symlink_metadata + read_link succeed, then `std::fs::metadata(path).ok()?` follows the link and returns None for a dangling one; `path.file_name()?` is None for '/'. The CTX_PROPERTIES handler (wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:730-735) only acts on Some and shows nothing otherwise, and ContextTarget::Empty maps to current_dir, so Properties on empty space in / is also a silent no-op.

**Fix.** `let meta = std::fs::metadata(path).or_else(|_| if is_symlink { Ok(sym_meta.clone()) } else { Err(..) })` (i.e. fall back to sym_meta), and name = file_name().map(lossy).unwrap_or_else(|| path.display().to_string()). is_dir then comes from the link itself (false), which is what you want for a broken link.

### dialogs-30: Media Details is populated once from a placeholder and never refreshed

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:281` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Media details are re-read from the probe cache each frame until they arrive.
- **Also reported as:** fs-29

**What is wrong.** FileInfoCache::get returns an instant placeholder and queues a probe when the path is not cached. populate_media_info is called once when the dialog opens, so for a file that was not already probed (the status bar only probes a single selected entry) dimensions and duration stay None and the Media Details section is missing for the life of the dialog, even after the probe lands.

**Trigger.** Tree view: expand a folder, right-click a nested image (this clears the selection), Properties: no Media Details. Close and reopen: now it is there. Same from a search result.

```rust
pub fn populate_media_info(&mut self, file_info: &mut crate::file_info::FileInfoCache) {
    let info = file_info.get(&self.path);
    self.image_dimensions = info.dimensions;
    self.media_duration = info.duration.clone();
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** FileInfoCache::get (file_info.rs:56-71) inserts an extension-only placeholder and queues a probe on a miss. populate_media_info has one caller (context_menu.rs:733) and copies dimensions/duration once, so on a miss has_media_section() stays false for the life of the dialog. It normally works only because status.rs:138 has already probed a single selected file; it fails for tree nested rows, search results, favourites and multi-selections, where no probe happened.

**Fix.** In render.rs, just before draw_properties_dialog, re-run props.populate_media_info(file_info) while both fields are None (render_frame already holds &mut file_info; it is a map lookup). The panel grows by one section when the probe lands, which is acceptable. file_info.poll() already forces a frame when a probe lands.

### dialogs-25: Device row decodes st_dev with 8-bit masks

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:957` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Device row uses libc::major/minor.

**What is wrong.** Linux dev_t carries a 12-bit (plus high bits) major and a split 20-bit minor. Masking to 8 bits misreports any major above 255 and any minor above 255.

**Trigger.** Properties on a file that lives directly on an NVMe partition (ext4/xfs/vfat, block major 259): the System section shows "3:2" instead of "259:2".

```rust
let dev_major = (props.device_id >> 8) & 0xFF;
let dev_minor = props.device_id & 0xFF;
```

**Skeptic's check.** device_id is MetadataExt::dev() and is decoded as (dev>>8)&0xFF : dev&0xFF. Linux dev_t has a 12-bit+ major and split minor, so major 259 (NVMe/blkext) prints as 3 and minors >255 are truncated. This PC's home is btrfs (anonymous major 0) so it only shows wrong here once the btrfs minor passes 255 or on ext4/xfs/vfat mounts.

**Fix.** Use libc::major(props.device_id as libc::dev_t) and libc::minor(..). Local one-liner.

### media-22: Icon picker hits the disk every frame: read_dir plus sort per frame, and failed SVGs are re-read and re-parsed every frame

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:1365` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The icon picker's file list is cached per tab and failed SVGs are remembered.
- **Also reported as:** dialogs-29

**What is wrong.** Despite its name and doc comment, picker_icons_cached() calls list_picker_icons() (std::fs::read_dir, collect, sort) on every frame while the picker is open. In addition IconCache::ensure_svg_path() (icons.rs:167-174) has no negative cache: wayland_loop.rs:1282-1286 calls it for every picker cell after every rendered frame, and a file that fails to rasterize is read from disk and parsed again each time.

**Trigger.** Open Properties on a folder, click the icon to open the picker and move the mouse over it: each rendered frame re-lists ~/.lantern/icons/folders/<tab>. Put one malformed .svg in that folder and it is re-read and re-parsed on every frame.

```rust
fn picker_icons_cached(props: &FileProperties) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    list_picker_icons(props.picker_tab)
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** picker_icons_cached is literally `list_picker_icons(props.picker_tab)` (read_dir + collect + sort) and is called from draw_icon_picker_body on every rendered frame; there is no cache field on FileProperties. IconCache::ensure_svg_path (icons.rs:167-174) only checks self.cache, so a failing SVG is re-read and re-parsed on each call from wayland_loop.rs:1282-1286. Cost is per rendered frame, not constant, so low.

**Fix.** Add `picker_icons: Option<(IconPickerTab, Vec<PathBuf>)>` on FileProperties, refilled when the tab changes or the picker opens. In ensure_svg_path, check and insert into self.failed (note ensure_dir clears `failed` on directory change, which is fine).

### dialogs-22: Level-triggered is_active() clicks in the icon picker and checksum row are lost when press and release land in the same loop iteration, and repeat every frame while held

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:1517` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): New src/props_click.rs: FileProperties::on_press(zone) and App::properties_pressed handle the folder-icon toggle, picker tabs, icon cells, Reset, Back, Choose Custom Image and the checksum copy once, on the press event, from the Properties branch of the press handler in src/wayland_loop.rs. All is_active() actions and icon_was_active were removed from src/properties.rs (draw_properties_dialog / draw_icon_picker_body no longer return events; render.rs lost the event plumbing). The checksum row copies once per click instead of every frame while held. Zone ids fixed: Back is ZONE_PROPS_PICKER_BACK (84, no longer 85 = ZONE_SPLIT_TOGGLE) and Choose Custom Image has its own ZONE_PROPS_PICKER_CUSTOM (806).

**What is wrong.** The folder-icon toggle, picker tabs, icon cells, Reset, Back, Choose Custom Image and the checksum copy row act when the zone's state is Pressed during a draw, instead of on the press event the loop already receives. wayland_loop.rs handles left_pressed (sets the active zone) and then left_released (clears it) before render_frame in the same iteration, so when both events arrive in one dispatch batch the draw never sees Pressed and the click does nothing. Conversely the checksum row calls clip.set_text on every frame drawn while the button is down (each call also appends to /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log and creates a new data source in clipboard.rs).

**Trigger.** Touchpad tap with tap-and-drag disabled (press and release are emitted together), a synthetic click from a button-binding tool, or any click delivered while the loop was busy in a blocking call: tapping an icon in the picker or the folder icon does nothing. Holding the mouse on the SHA-256 row while moving slightly copies it repeatedly.

```rust
if state.is_active() {
    return Some(PropertiesEvent::IconChosen(path.clone()));
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** All of these act on zone.is_active() during draw (674, 1138, 1426, 1458, 1517, 1566, 1590). is_active is Pressed|Dragging (lntrn-ui input.rs:17), which holds for every frame while the button is down. The repeat half is certain: the checksum row returns CopyText on each rendered frame while held, and render.rs:1453 calls clip.set_text each time, which appends to /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log (clipboard.rs:43-50); any pointer jitter during a normal click gives several copies. The lost-click half is also traceable: the left_pressed block (wayland_loop.rs:599) and left_released block (828, ending in input.on_left_released()) both run before render_frame in one iteration, so if both events were read in one batch the draw never sees Pressed. How often press and release arrive together (touchpad tap, loop stalled by blocking I/O) I did not measure.

**Fix.** Handle these zone ids in the properties branch of the press handler (wayland_loop.rs:638-670), which already has the zone id, and drop the is_active checks and icon_was_active. Watch id collisions when you do: the picker's Back button uses ZONE_PROPS_PICKER_BACK+1 = 85 = ZONE_SPLIT_TOGGLE and 'Choose Custom Image' reuses ZONE_PROPS_PICKER_BACK (84); harmless today only because handle_click is not reached while Properties is open. Give them their own ids. Touches input dispatch, so not mechanical.

### audio-16: Cover art keeps being painted at its old position when the Audio section is collapsed

- **Where:** `src/properties_audio/draw.rs:106` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. art_rect is cleared at the top of each dialog draw.

**What is wrong.** art_rect is only ever assigned inside draw_art_tile and never cleared. properties.rs:758 skips audio.draw() when section_open[SEC_AUDIO] is false, but render.rs:1428-1466 still pushes a TextureDraw for (texture, art_rect) every frame. The picture stays on screen at the last rect, on top of whatever section moved into that space. properties.rs already clears picker_cell_rects each frame for exactly this reason.

**Trigger.** Properties on an MP3 with cover art, click the "Audio" section header to fold it. The cover image remains floating over the General/Disk headers.

```rust
self.art_rect = Some(Rect::new(
    tile.x + inset,
    tile.y + inset,
    size - inset * 2.0,
    size - inset * 2.0,
));
```

**Skeptic's check.** art_rect is written only in draw_art_tile (draw.rs:106) and never cleared. properties.rs:758 skips audio.draw() when SEC_AUDIO is folded, but render.rs:1428-1432 and 1464-1466 push a TextureDraw for (texture, art_rect) whenever both are Some. So the cover stays at its last rect over whatever section moved up.

**Fix.** At the top of draw_properties_dialog next to picker_cell_rects.clear(): `if let Some(a) = props.audio.as_mut() { a.art_rect = None; }`. draw_art_tile sets it again when the section is open.

### audio-17: Fox redraws at 60 fps for as long as the artwork picker is open; "Saved" status never expires on its own

- **Where:** `src/properties_audio/mod.rs:206` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (B): New src/properties_audio/activity.rs. busy() no longer returns true for the whole time the picker is open: while picking it is true only (a) for 1.5 s after a frame that something else caused (PickWatch), (b) once the picker process has exited and the picture is being prepared, (c) when a worker result is waiting. So an open picker costs a 1.5 s burst when it opens and when focus returns, not 60 fps for minutes. The 'Saved' status now keeps busy() true until its 3 s TTL, so it disappears by itself. Also fixed a decode-slot race that could leave `decoding` (and 60 fps) stuck forever when an older decode finished after a newer one. Then (lead, after batch 2): wayland_loop.rs asks AudioEdit::frame_wanted() on every wake-up and draws a frame when it says so; the save, decode and picker threads call bg::wake() when their result is in, so the loop wakes for it at once. 'Saved' no longer costs 3 s of 60 fps frames: it is taken down by the loop's idle wake-up (within half a second of expiring).

**What is wrong.** wayland_loop.rs:1378 ORs busy() into needs_anim, which forces `state.frame_done = true` every 16 ms. `picking` stays true until the child `lntrn-file-manager --pick` exits, so the main window does a full GPU frame 60 times a second while the user browses for an image (minutes), just to poll a mutex. Conversely the 3 s status TTL (line 338) is only evaluated inside poll(), which only runs on rendered frames; once busy() goes false no frame is scheduled, so "Saved" stays up until the next input event.

**Trigger.** Properties, click the art tile, leave the picker open: Fox sits at a constant 60 fps render. After a save, do not move the mouse: "Saved" stays indefinitely.

```rust
pub fn busy(&self) -> bool {
    self.decoding || self.picking || self.saving
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** busy() = decoding || picking || saving and wayland_loop.rs:1375-1379 ORs it into needs_anim, which forces frame_done every 16 ms. `picking` is only cleared in poll() when the child `lntrn-file-manager --pick` has exited, so the main window renders at 60 fps for as long as the picker is open. The status TTL (338-342) is only evaluated inside poll(), which only runs on rendered frames; once busy() is false nothing schedules a frame, so 'Saved' stays until the next input.

**Fix.** Simply dropping `picking` from busy() would leave the result unpolled until an input event (usually arrives when focus returns, but not guaranteed). Proper fix: have the pick/decode/save threads poke an eventfd that is in the main poll set and set frame_done from it; for the TTL, keep needs_anim true (or a timed wake) until the status expires. Changes loop wake-up design.

### audio-18: Edits made while a save is in flight are discarded or leave the editor out of sync with the file

- **Where:** `src/properties_audio/mod.rs:329` (Audio tags and tag editor)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category race; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Field edits, zone presses and Tab are ignored while a save is in flight.

**What is wrong.** save() snapshots the tags and clears focus, but field zones, the art tile and Remove stay live while `saving` is true. When the result arrives, sync_bufs() overwrites anything typed meanwhile, and art_change is reset even if it now refers to a newer pick or a Remove made during the save. Result: typed text vanishes, or the tile shows artwork (or none) that is not what is in the file, with is_dirty() false so there is no Save button to fix it.

**Trigger.** Save a large MP3 that needs a full rewrite (takes seconds), and while "Saving…" shows click Title and keep typing, or click Remove under the art. On completion the typing disappears / the tile shows no art although the file still has it.

```rust
self.meta = meta;
self.art_change = None;
self.saved_texture = self.texture.clone();
self.sync_bufs();
```

**Skeptic's check.** save() snapshots current_tags() and clears focus, but draw_field (draw.rs:200-201) still registers field zones and the art/Remove zones while saving; on_zone_pressed, edit_key, pick_artwork and remove_artwork do not check self.saving (only save() and the ESC branch do). When the result lands, poll() does self.meta = meta; art_change = None; saved_texture = texture.clone(); sync_bufs(), wiping anything typed and any art change made meanwhile, with is_dirty() false afterwards. Needs a save slow enough to interact with (large file rewrite), so low.

**Fix.** Early-return from on_zone_pressed and edit_key when self.saving (and from the Tab/Enter arms of handle_dialog_key). Optionally draw the inputs un-hovered while saving. Local to properties_audio/mod.rs.

### media-18: Quick Look text view: the "… N more lines" label overlaps the last visible line and the count is capped at 500

- **Where:** `src/quick_look.rs:354` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Quick Look's text footer takes the last row instead of overlapping it, and says 'more' without a number when the file was truncated.

**What is wrong.** max_lines fills the whole padded panel height, and the footer label is then drawn at the bottom of that same area, so no row is reserved for it. With line_h = 1.45 * line_font the footer overlaps the last text line whenever the leftover height is under 0.55 * line_font, which is about 38% of window heights (up to a 55% overlap). Separately, load_text() keeps at most 500 lines / 256 KB, so for a long file the number shown is the number of loaded lines not displayed, not the number of lines in the file.

**Trigger.** Press Space on a text file longer than the panel (any source file) and resize the window height: at many heights the grey "… N more lines" text is drawn on top of the last line. On a 10,000-line log it reads about "… 460 more lines".

```rust
let max_lines = (((panel.h - pad * 2.0) / line_h).floor() as usize).min(lines.len());
...
if lines.len() > max_lines {
    let more = format!("… {} more lines", lines.len() - max_lines);
    TextLabel::new(&more, panel.x + pad, panel.y + panel.h - pad - line_font)
```

**Skeptic's check.** max_lines = floor((panel.h - 2*pad)/line_h) uses the full padded height and the footer is drawn at panel.y + panel.h - pad - line_font with no row reserved. With leftover r in [0, line_h), the gap between the last line's top and the footer's top is 0.45*line_font + r, so they overlap whenever r < 0.55*line_font (about 38% of heights). load_text caps at TEXT_MAX_LINES = 500 / 256 KB before lines.len() is used, so the count is of loaded lines only. Cosmetic.

**Fix.** Compute fit = floor(H/line_h); if lines.len() > fit draw fit-1 lines and put the footer on the last row at panel.y + pad + (fit-1)*line_h. Have load_text return a `truncated` flag and print '… more' without a number when it is set.

### render-14: Breadcrumb widths are guessed as bytes x 0.45em: labels overdraw their separators and neighbours, hit zones do not match the text, and the overflow test under-counts

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:306` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): Segment widths are measured (memoised through sections::fit_label), not bytes*0.45em. The click/hover rect of each segment is the box its text is drawn in; separators are centred in their own gap; the overflow test uses the real widths; labels carry a max_width. The duplicated arithmetic in render.rs and nav.rs is gone (both call crumbs::layout); sections::truncate_with_ellipsis (char estimate) had no users left and was removed.

**What is wrong.** Zone widths (render.rs:303-361) and the drawing code (sections/nav.rs:219-311) both size each segment as byte-length x 9.9px at s=1, but the text is drawn with real Inter metrics and no max_width/clip. Measured from Inter's hmtx at 22px: 'Home' is 58.3px (estimate 39.6), 'Documents' 118.6 (89.1), 'Downloads' 116.0 (89.1). 'Home' therefore runs 12.7px past its box and sits on top of the '/' separator; 'Documents' covers the whole separator gap and reaches into the next segment's box. The clickable/hover rect ends well before the visible word does, so clicking the last letters hits the gap (no zone) or the next segment. The same under-estimate feeds the overflow test (`total_w > p_rect.w`), so a trail the code believes fits can be up to ~25% wider than the path strip and is drawn over the split/preview/sort/search buttons. For non-ASCII names the error flips (3 bytes per CJK character) and boxes are far too wide.

**Trigger.** Open ~/Documents/anything: the faint '/' after 'Home' and after 'Documents' is hidden under the last letters; clicking the 'ts' of 'Documents' does nothing or navigates to the child. In a narrow split pane with a long path the last segment draws over the sort/search buttons.

```rust
let char_w = font * 0.45;
...
let seg_width = |name: &str| -> f32 { name.len() as f32 * char_w + pad_x * 2.0 };
```

**Skeptic's check.** render.rs:303-306 and sections/nav.rs:219-222 both size a segment as name.len() * 0.45*font + 12*s, while the label is drawn with real metrics and TextLabel's default 800 px max_width outside any clip. My own measurement of Inter at 22 px: 'Home' 61.6 px (estimate 39.6), 'Documents' 118.6 (89.1), 'Downloads' 116.0 (89.1). So 'Home' runs about 16 px past its 51.6 px box and over the faint '/', the hit zone ends well before the word, and the overflow test under-counts. Lowercase-heavy names (0.48 em) are close, and for non-ASCII the byte count over-estimates instead. Cosmetic plus slightly-off click targets.

**Fix.** One shared helper (in sections/mod.rs) returning measured segment widths via text.measure_width, used by render.rs for zones and nav.rs for drawing, with the last segment truncated to the remaining width. render.rs registers zones before draw_nav_bar and has `text` available, so that works, but two files plus click.rs's breadcrumb_skip must stay in agreement, so not a mechanical edit. Fix together with sections-7.

### render-17: Inline rename box is drawn outside the scroll clip (and 32*s into the sidebar for the first grid column)

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:712` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The rename box is clipped to the content area, never starts left of it, and registers only its visible part as a zone.

**What is wrong.** All three rename inputs (grid :702-723, list :782-800, tree :872-897) are drawn after `area.end`, positioned from the scrolled `base_y`, with no clip and no visibility test. The wheel handler does not stop scrolling while a rename is active, so the text box and its zone follow the item out of the content area and paint over the tab bar, nav bar or status bar. In Grid, the first column's cell starts at content.x + 8*s, so `input_x = ir.x - 40*s` is 32*s left of the content area and the box overlaps the sidebar edge; the right side is clamped but the left is not.

**Trigger.** Press F2 on a file, then scroll the wheel: the rename field slides over the tab bar/status bar. Rename any file in the leftmost grid column: the field's left end covers the sidebar's right edge.

```rust
let input_w = (ir.w + 80.0 * s).min(content.x + content.w - (ir.x - 40.0 * s));
let input_x = ir.x - 40.0 * s;
let input_rect = Rect::new(input_x, input_y, input_w, input_h);
input.add_zone(ZONE_RENAME_INPUT, input_rect);
```

**Skeptic's check.** All three rename inputs (702-723, 782-800, 872-897) are drawn after draw_content_* has closed its ScrollArea, positioned from the scrolled base_y, with no clip (Fox has no push_clip at all) and no visibility test; the wheel handler (wayland_loop.rs:555-596) has no `renaming` guard, so the box and its zone follow the item over the nav/tab bar. In Grid, file_item_rect x for column 0 is content.x + 8*s, so input_x = ir.x - 40*s is 32*s inside the sidebar; only the right side is clamped. The zone is registered after the sidebar zones, so it also steals clicks on that strip.

**Fix.** input_x = (ir.x - 40*s).max(content.x) with the width reduced to match; wrap the three TextInput draws in painter.push_clip(content)/text.push_clip(..) and pop, and register the zone only for input_rect.intersect(&content). TextInput pushes its own clip internally (text_input.rs:184) and Painter/Text clips nest by intersection, so this composes.

### render-19: Scrollbar thickness and grab band are fixed physical pixels, ignoring the display scale

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:905` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): New src/scrollbar.rs builds the lntrn-ui Scrollbar struct (its fields are public) with scaled geometry: 16px*scale bar, 48px*scale minimum thumb, 10px*scale grab margin, scaled radius. Used at every site: render.rs, sections/split.rs, the three sites in wayland_loop.rs, plus the new sidebar and Properties bars. sections::draw_scrollbar draws through it.

**What is wrong.** Everything else in render.rs multiplies by `s`, but the Scrollbar widget takes no scale: the bar is 14 physical px wide with a 10px grab margin and a 30px minimum thumb at every scale. At scale 1.4 that is a 10 logical-px bar and a 21 logical-px minimum thumb, noticeably smaller than at 1.0, and the hover band extends 6 physical px past the content edge regardless of scale.

**Trigger.** Move the window from a scale-1.0 output to a scale-1.4 output (or run on the laptop): the scrollbar and its clickable band shrink by about 30% relative to the rest of the UI.

```rust
let scrollbar = Scrollbar::new(&content, total_content_h, app.scroll_offset);
input.add_zone(ZONE_SCROLLBAR, scrollbar.hover_zone());
// lntrn-ui scroll.rs:133-135, 196
const MIN_THUMB_H: f32 = 30.0; const SCROLLBAR_W: f32 = 14.0; const SCROLLBAR_PAD: f32 = 4.0; let pad = 10.0;
```

**Skeptic's check.** lntrn-ui scroll.rs:133-135 and 196: MIN_THUMB_H 30, SCROLLBAR_W 14, SCROLLBAR_PAD 4 and hover pad 10 are raw constants and Scrollbar::new takes no scale, while Fox draws in physical pixels with everything else multiplied by s. At 1.4 the bar is 10 logical px wide. Relevant given the 'make it bigger' rule for click targets, but cosmetic.

**Fix.** The right fix is a scale parameter on lntrn-ui's Scrollbar, which is a sibling crate and needs Alva's approval first. An in-crate workaround (own geometry in Fox) would have to be mirrored in render.rs, sections/split.rs and the three Scrollbar::new sites in wayland_loop.rs.

### cloud-net-25: Cloud status pill is only refreshed when something else causes a redraw

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:965` (Cloud transport and auth)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. An idle window redraws when the cloud sync status changes (500 ms poll).

**What is wrong.** The sync thread changes SyncStatus under a mutex but never wakes the main loop, and nothing in the loop compares the status between frames or sets frame_done for it (needs_anim has no cloud term). After an upload-only pass, which causes no directory-watcher event, the pill keeps showing 'Syncing…' (or 'Synced' while an error has occurred) until the user moves the mouse or presses a key.

**Trigger.** Open ~/Cloud, save a file there from another app, and do not touch the Fox window: the pill stays on whatever it last drew.

```rust
app.cloud_sync.as_ref().map(|h| h.status())
```

**Skeptic's check.** render.rs:965 is the only reader of cloud_sync.status(); SyncHandle::status is a mutex read and nothing in cloud/ touches an eventfd or frame_done. The loop skips everything when frame_done is false (wayland_loop.rs:161) and needs_anim has no cloud term, so the pill only changes when some other event causes a frame. Worse than the finder assumed: even a directory-watcher event does not cause a frame while idle (see extra findings).

**Fix.** Keep `last_cloud_status` in run_loop and, next to the theme poll that runs before the frame_done gate (lines 153-160), compare with app.cloud_sync.status() when current_dir is under cloud_root and set frame_done on change. One mutex lock per 500 ms idle wake.

### render-12: Video play-button badges are drawn unclipped on layer 1 and are also stamped on folders whose name ends in a video extension

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:1355` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Play badges are clipped to the content area and skipped for folders.
- **Also reported as:** media-15

**What is wrong.** The thumbnail textures carry `clip = content_clip`, but the badge is painted after `painter.set_layer(1)`, which clears the painter clip stack, at the centre of the unclipped icon rect. A video whose cell is partly scrolled out still has `has_icon` true (the cell intersects the content), so its badge is drawn over the tab bar / nav bar above or the status bar below. Separately, the filter never checks `is_dir`, and `is_video_file` matches on `rsplit('.')` of the name, so a directory named 'mov', 'mp4', 'clips.mkv' etc. gets a play badge over its folder icon.

**Trigger.** Grid view of a folder of videos at a large icon zoom: scroll slowly and watch dark circles with white triangles slide over the tab bar and the status bar. Create a folder named 'mov' or 'avi': it shows a play button.

```rust
.filter(|&i| has_icon[i] && icons::is_video_file(&entries[i].name))   // render.rs:1250, 1272, 1295
...
for (dx, dy, dw, dh) in &video_overlays {
    let cx = dx + dw * 0.5;
    let cy = dy + dh * 0.5;
    let r = (dw.min(*dh) * 0.28).clamp(7.0 * s, 26.0 * s);
    painter.circle_filled(cx, cy, r, Color::rgba(0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.55));
```

**Skeptic's check.** Painter::set_layer (lntrn-render draw/src/painter.rs:282-296) clears clip_stack, and the overlay loop at render.rs:1355 draws at the centre of the unclipped icon rect. has_icon is true when the cell merely intersects content (144-149), so a video row partly scrolled under the nav/tab bar gets its badge painted over the chrome on layer 1. The three filters (1250, 1272, 1295) do not test is_dir, and is_video_file takes rsplit('.').next() (icons.rs:476), so a folder named 'mov' or 'clips.mkv', which has a folder texture, gets a play badge.

**Fix.** Add !entries[i].is_dir / !te.entry.is_dir to the three filters; wrap the list-overlay loop in painter.push_clip(content)/pop_clip and draw the preview-pane badge after the pop (track where the preview entry starts in video_overlays, it is pushed last).

### media-12: Properties dialog can never show a thumbnail for a file: its synthetic FileEntry has modified: None so the cache key never matches

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:1411` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The Properties header thumbnail uses the listing's (lstat) size and mtime, so the cache key matches.

**What is wrong.** icons::cache_key() for thumbnail-capable files is "thumb:{path}:{size}:{mtime nanos}" (icons.rs:228-233). The entry built for the Properties header has modified: None, which yields stamp 0, while the texture was stored under the listing's real mtime. icon_cache.get(&entry) at render.rs:1459 therefore always returns None for images, videos and audio with artwork, and only the faint fallback circle is drawn. Folders still work because their key has no mtime.

**Trigger.** Right-click any image that already shows a thumbnail in the grid and choose Properties: the header shows an empty tinted circle instead of the picture.

```rust
let entry = crate::fs::FileEntry {
    name: props.name.clone(),
    path: props.path.clone(),
    is_dir: props.is_dir,
    size: props.size_bytes,
    modified: None,
    selected: false,
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** The synthetic FileEntry has modified: None, so icons::cache_key yields 'thumb:{path}:{size}:0' (icons.rs:228-233) while the listing stored the texture under the real mtime nanos; icon_cache.get(&entry) at render.rs:1459 therefore misses for every image/video/audio file. Folders match because their key has no mtime. Plain file types never have a texture anyway, so the header shows only the tinted circle for every file.

**Fix.** Add `modified_raw: Option<SystemTime>` to FileProperties (from the same metadata call) and put it in the synthetic entry. Check that the size also matches the listing: from_path uses metadata() (follows symlinks); if fs::list_directory uses lstat sizes the key still differs for symlinks.

### render-13: A failed begin_frame leaves the text queue and layer state uncleared; the next good frame draws stale text and puts all new text on the overlay layer

- **Where:** `src/render.rs:1532` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. text.clear() at the top of render_frame.

**What is wrong.** The text renderer is only reset inside `text.render_layer(1, ..)` (lntrn-text lib.rs:600-605). When `ctx.begin_frame` returns an error nothing is rendered, so `entries`, `queued`, `layer_breaks` and `current_layer == 1` all survive. On the next frame `text.set_layer(1)` is a no-op (`layer <= current_layer`), so the old break index is reused: layer 0 renders the previous frame's base text, and layer 1 renders the previous frame's overlay text plus ALL of the new frame's text, i.e. on top of modal panels. If no further redraw is requested, that glitched frame stays on screen. While errors repeat (Lost/Outdated are never answered with a surface reconfigure, and GpuContext::resize early-returns when the size is unchanged) the queue grows by a full frame of quads per attempt.

**Trigger.** Any wgpu surface error (Timeout, Outdated, Lost) on one frame followed by a successful one, e.g. around a compositor hiccup or output hot-plug: text appears doubled/bold and shows through an open dialog until something else triggers a redraw. The only instance in the logs is the 'swap chain has been lost' line when the compositor died, so this is rare in practice.

```rust
painter.clear();
input.begin_frame();          // render.rs:57-58, no text.clear()
...
Err(e) => eprintln!("[fox] render error: {e}"),
```

**Skeptic's check.** The logic is as described: render_frame calls painter.clear() and input.begin_frame() but never text.clear(); TextRenderer state is only reset at the end of render_layer for the last layer (lntrn-text lib.rs:600-605). On the Err arm nothing is rendered, so entries/queued/layer_breaks/current_layer==1 survive; on the next frame set_layer(1) is a no-op (lib.rs:561), layer 0 replays the old base text and layer 1 gets the old overlay text plus all new text. GpuContext::begin_frame just propagates get_current_texture errors and resize early-returns on unchanged size, so nothing reconfigures on Lost/Outdated. Not established: that a surface error followed by a good frame actually occurs on this stack; the only logged instance was at compositor death.

**Fix.** Call text.clear() next to painter.clear() at the top of render_frame (TextRenderer::clear resets queue, layers and clip stack; harmless on the normal path). Reconfiguring the surface on Lost/Outdated needs access to ctx.surface/config, which are in lntrn-render; if that needs an API change it is a sibling-crate edit and needs approval.

### sections-16: Grid labels size text by byte length and a fixed 0.52 em estimate: spurious ellipsis on non-ASCII names, wrapped lines overlapping on selected long names

- **Where:** `src/sections/mod.rs:166` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Grid labels are truncated, wrapped and centred by measured width; truncate_with_ellipsis counts chars.

**What is wrong.** truncate_with_ellipsis decides by bytes but truncates by chars, so a name that fits gets an ellipsis appended with nothing removed. grid.rs:204-224 then centres with line.len() (bytes) and, for selected entries, splits the name every floor(max_w / (0.52*font)) chars; digits and capitals are wider than that estimate, each piece is queued with max_width = max_label_w inside the scroll-area clip, so lntrn-text wraps the overflow onto a second row 19.2*s lower while the next piece is drawn 20*s lower: the rows print on top of each other. List/Tree already moved to the measured truncate_to_width; Grid did not.

**Trigger.** Grid view: a 13-character Japanese name (39 bytes) is shown in full with a trailing ellipsis and pushed to the left margin. Select a file such as 'IMG_20260914_153045_HDR_edited_final.jpg': its two-line label renders with the tail of line one overlapping line two.

```rust
let est_w = name.len() as f32 * char_w;
if est_w <= max_w {
    return name.to_string();
}
...
let truncated: String = name.chars().take(max_chars).collect();
format!("{truncated}\u{2026}")
```

**Skeptic's check.** truncate_with_ellipsis decides with name.len() (bytes) but truncates with chars().take(max_chars), so a multibyte name that fits gets '…' appended with nothing removed. grid.rs:204-224 centres with len() and, for selected entries, splits every floor(max_w / 0.52em) chars. Grid labels are drawn inside area.begin (grid.rs:34), so the text clip is the content rect and wrapped overflow is visible: digit/capital-heavy names (I measured 0.56-0.63 em/char in Inter) exceed max_label_w and the tail wraps 19.2*s lower while the next piece is drawn 20*s lower. The comment on truncate_to_width in the same file describes exactly this failure for the estimate-based helper.

**Fix.** Unselected: truncate_to_width(text, name, max_label_w - 2px, label_font) and centre with text.measure_width. Selected: build lines by measured prefixes instead of wrap_lines. draw_content_grid already has `text`. Keep a little slack below max_width to avoid the exact-width last-glyph clip.

### sections-21: Sidebar rows are neither clipped nor scrollable: with enough favorites and drives the Devices rows draw over the status bar and cannot be reached

- **Where:** `src/sections/sidebar.rs:161` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): Same change as render-20 (same defect seen from the drawing side): rows are clipped, scrollable by wheel and scrollbar, and zones follow the scrolled rects. Row labels (places, favourites, drive name/usage/subtitle, phone name/status, busy line, empty-favourites hint) are now cut by measured width, because text inside a clip is bounded by the clip and a wrapped second line would otherwise show over the next row. Row text/usage-bar widths derive from the row rect so they make room for the scrollbar.

**What is wrong.** build_sidebar_layout stacks 9 places (40*s each), favorites (40*s), drives (64*s) and phones (56*s) with no regard for sidebar_rect.h, and draw_sidebar draws every row with no clip. Rows past the bottom are painted across the status bar / pick bar and below the window; their zones sit under later-registered status zones or outside the surface.

**Trigger.** At the default 1000 px window height: seven favorites plus three drives (fewer on the laptop's shorter screen, or after shrinking the window). The last device rows overlap the status-bar text and the bottom one cannot be clicked without collapsing a section.

```rust
for (i, drive) in app.drives.iter().enumerate() {
    let r = layout.drive_items[i];
    let is_hovered = hov.drives.get(i).copied().unwrap_or(false);
    draw_drive_row(painter, text, palette, drive, r, is_hovered, screen, s);
```

**Skeptic's check.** build_sidebar_layout (layout.rs:219-304) stacks headers (42*s each), places and favourites (40*s), drives (64*s) and phones (56*s) with no reference to sidebar_rect.h, and draw_sidebar draws every row with no clip (no push_clip exists in Fox outside ScrollArea). With about nine places, 1000 px default height and rice mode the budget is 966 px: 42+360+42+7*40+42+3*64 = 958, so one more favourite or device overflows into the status bar, whose zones are registered later and win. Reached simply by adding favourites or on a shorter window.

**Fix.** Needs a scrollable sidebar body (ScrollArea + wheel routing when the cursor is over the sidebar + zones intersected with the sidebar rect). A clip-only stopgap hides the rows but leaves them unreachable, so it is not a real fix.

### sections-6: Sidebar phone row stats the MTP mount point and re-reads /proc/mounts on the render thread every frame

- **Where:** `src/sections/sidebar.rs:416` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category freeze; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Phone mounted state is computed at the 2-second device refresh, not per frame.

**What is wrong.** draw_phone_row runs once per phone per rendered frame. fs::is_path_mounted (fs.rs:681-698) does path.canonicalize() (an lstat walk whose last component is the jmtpfs FUSE root) and then reads and parses the whole of /proc/mounts. This is exactly the slow-mount access the rest of the code keeps off the render thread: while jmtpfs holds its device lock for a whole-file download, a getattr on the mount root can queue behind it and the frame blocks. Frames run at 60 Hz precisely while a copy from the phone is in progress (op_progress keeps needs_anim true), so the stat is issued continuously during the worst window.

**Trigger.** Plug in a phone, open it from the sidebar, start copying a large video off it (or let image thumbnails load on the slow lane). While the transfer runs, the progress strip animates, every frame calls is_path_mounted on the mount point, and the UI stalls whenever the kernel's 1 s attr cache expires and the FUSE daemon is busy.

```rust
let status = if crate::fs::is_path_mounted(&phone.mount_point) {
    "Connected"
} else {
    "Tap to open"
};
```

**Skeptic's check.** The per-frame work is real: draw_phone_row calls fs::is_path_mounted for each phone on every rendered frame, which is a canonicalize plus read_to_string and parse of /proc/mounts (fs.rs:681-698), with no caching. The freeze claim is not supported: the finder says canonicalize is 'an lstat walk whose last component is the FUSE root', but glibc since 2.33 (this machine has 2.43) implements realpath with readlink per component and no stat of the final component, and a readlink on a mount root returns EINVAL in the VFS without a request to the FUSE daemon. I could not trace syscalls (no strace here), so this is from the glibc source as I know it. What remains is a small per-frame waste.

**Fix.** Compute `mounted` once in refresh_phones() (already every 2 s) and store it on Phone, comparing phone.mount_point against the mount table without canonicalize; unescape \040 etc. like unescape_mount does. mount_phone/unmount paths can keep calling is_path_mounted since they are user-initiated.

### fileops-34: sudo 'password required' detection depends on English stderr text

- **Where:** `src/sudo.rs:153` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. sudo runs with LC_ALL=C so the password-required detection holds on a translated system.

**What is wrong.** sudo localizes its messages. Under a non-English locale the `sudo -n` failure is classified as Failed, priv_run only eprintln!s it, and the password modal never opens - every privileged fallback silently does nothing. Related: after a multi-source Move partially succeeds and a later command fails, resubmitting the modal reruns all commands from the first one, which now fails ('cannot stat') and blocks the remaining sources forever.

**Trigger.** Run Fox with LANG/LC_MESSAGES set to a language sudo has translations for, then delete a root-owned file: no prompt, nothing happens.

```rust
let needs_password = stderr.contains("password is required")
    || stderr.contains("a terminal is required")
    || stderr.contains("askpass");
```

**Skeptic's check.** run_with_sudo classifies by English substrings of sudo's stderr (153-155); anything else becomes Failed, and priv_run's Failed arm only eprintln!s (file_ops.rs:1043-1045), so no modal opens. That is fragile and as described, but both machines run en_US.UTF-8 (checked `locale` here), so the trigger needs a non-English LC_MESSAGES with an NLS-enabled sudo; not reachable in the current setup. The retry remark is also accurate: the modal re-runs every sub-command from the first, so after a partial multi-source Move the first `mv` fails with 'cannot stat' on every retry.

**Fix.** .env("LC_ALL", "C") on the sudo Command is a one-liner (the child cp/mv/rm then also print C-locale errors, which is fine since the text is shown raw). More robust: probe with `sudo -n true` and decide on its exit status. The resume-after-partial issue is separate and not mechanical. Also show the Failed message to the user instead of only logging it.

### fileops-33: EmptyTrash builds a root `sh -c` script by interpolating $HOME unquoted

- **Where:** `src/sudo.rs:188` (File operations)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category security; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Empty Trash as root passes the trash path as a positional parameter, built from dirs_home().

**What is wrong.** HOME is pasted into a shell command line that runs as root. A home path containing whitespace or shell metacharacters is word-split/interpreted: with HOME='/home/my user' the script runs `rm -rf -- /home/my user/.local/...` as root. With HOME unset it becomes '/.local/share/Trash/...'. It is also inconsistent with dirs_home(), which falls back to '/'.

**Trigger.** Run Empty Trash with a root-owned item in the Trash on an account whose home directory contains a space or shell-special character.

```rust
let script = format!(
    "rm -rf -- {home}/.local/share/Trash/files/* {home}/.local/share/Trash/files/.[!.]* \
     {home}/.local/share/Trash/info/* {home}/.local/share/Trash/info/.[!.]* 2>/dev/null; true"
);
```

**Skeptic's check.** build_argv for EmptyTrash formats $HOME unquoted into an `sh -c` script that runs under sudo (187-192), and falls back to an empty string when HOME is unset (then it targets /.local/share/Trash, inconsistent with dirs_home()). The word-splitting is real, but it needs a home path containing whitespace or shell metacharacters; here HOME is /home/alva, so I could not establish reachability.

**Fix.** Keep the globbing but pass the path as a positional parameter: sh -c 'rm -rf -- "$1"/files/* "$1"/files/.[!.]* "$1"/info/* "$1"/info/.[!.]* 2>/dev/null; true' sh <trash_dir>, with trash_dir from dirs_home(). The proposed argv enumeration also works but can hit ARG_MAX with a very full Trash. Note the existing globs miss names starting with '..'.

### media-8: On-disk thumbnail cache is never pruned

- **Where:** `src/thumbs.rs:188` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C8): New src/thumbs/prune.rs. The first thumbnail request of a run starts one background thread ('fox-thumb-prune', std::sync::Once) that scans ~/.cache/lntrn-file-manager/thumbs and removes: files of an older cache generation (this replaces the previous synchronous prune_old_generations, which could run on the render thread), files not used for 90 days, and, if the rest still exceeds 256 MiB, the least recently used until 192 MiB remain. Only regular *.png files are considered. A cache hit moves the file's mtime forward (at most once a day per file, prune::mark_used called from thumbs::generate) so the order approximates least-recently-used. src/thumbs.rs wires both in.

**What is wrong.** Every generated thumbnail is written to ~/.cache/lntrn-file-manager/thumbs/<hash(path,size,mtime)>.png and nothing in the crate ever deletes from that directory. Because the name includes size and mtime, every modification of an image or video leaves the previous PNG behind and writes a new one, and thumbnails of deleted files, unplugged drives and phone photos stay forever. On this machine the directory already holds 5,017 files / 185 MB after under four months. A file that is rewritten repeatedly while visible (a script regenerating plot.png, an .mkv being recorded) adds one cache file per directory reload (the watcher debounce is 120 ms).

**Trigger.** Normal use: browse photo and video folders over months and the cache only grows. Fast version: keep Fox open on a folder where a PNG is rewritten every second; after a day there are about 86,000 extra cache files.

```rust
let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(thumb_cache_dir());
let _ = thumb.save(&cached);
```

**Skeptic's check.** grep shows no reference to thumb_cache_dir or the thumbs directory outside thumbs.rs, and thumbs.rs only creates and writes. The key hashes path+size+mtime so every modification orphans the previous PNG. The live directory holds 5017 files / 185 MB (checked read-only). Real unbounded growth, but slow; I rate it low rather than medium.

**Fix.** A startup prune on a background thread (oldest-first by mtime past a size/count budget) is the right shape, but the budget and the extra thread are policy decisions for Alva, not a mechanical edit. Touching the cache file on a hit is needed for the order to approximate LRU. If the cache key gets a version byte for the EXIF fix (media-10), the prune also cleans up the orphaned old entries.

### media-16: Videos of one second or less never get a thumbnail or Quick Look frame (-ss 1 seeks past the end)

- **Where:** `src/thumbs.rs:244` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Video frame extraction retries at 0 s when 1 s yields nothing (thumbnails and Quick Look).

**What is wrong.** Both video_frame() and quick_look::load_video_still() (quick_look.rs:191) always seek to 1 s. For a clip that is 1 s or shorter ffmpeg exits 0 with empty stdout, which the code treats as failure: the file keeps the generic icon and Quick Look says "Could not extract video frame". The failure is retried (a fresh ffmpeg spawn) on every visit to the folder.

**Trigger.** Put a 0.5 s .mp4 in a folder and open it. Verified with the exact argument list: exit status 0, 0 bytes on stdout; a 3 s clip returns a PNG.

```rust
.args(["-ss", "1", "-i"])
.arg(path)
```

**Skeptic's check.** Reproduced in the scratchpad with the exact argument list on a generated 0.5 s clip: ffmpeg exits 0 with 0 bytes on stdout, which video_frame() treats as failure. quick_look.rs:191 has the same -ss 1. No fallback branch exists in either function.

**Fix.** When stdout is empty, retry once with -ss 0. Apply to both video_frame() and load_video_still(). Costs one extra ffmpeg spawn only for short or unreadable clips.

### render-18: List/Tree font size follows the continuous zoom slider, so every slider position rasterises a new glyph set into an atlas that never evicts

- **Where:** `src/views.rs:111` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category leak; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): layout::list_zoom_multiplier() is quantised to 17 steps (16 intervals) of the zoom slider. All List/Tree geometry and fonts (24/20/16 * multiplier * scale) come from that one function, so rows, icons and text step together and every hit-test stays consistent. Endpoints and the default (0.8 / 1.5 / 2.2) are unchanged.

**What is wrong.** `m = 0.8 + 1.4 * zoom` comes straight from the View > Scale slider. lntrn-text keys glyph rasters by (face, glyph, size on a 0.25px grid, 4 subpixel bins) and its atlas only grows (1024 -> 2048 -> 4096 -> 8192, 'Per-glyph LRU eviction is deferred', atlas.rs:16); at the 8192 cap new glyphs are dropped and cached as zero-area, i.e. blank. Sweeping the slider at s=1 walks the 24*m font through about 134 quarter-pixel sizes and the 20*m font through 112; with ~60 distinct name glyphs that is roughly 20 million texels, enough to push the atlas to 4096x4096 (64 MB) or 8192x8192 (256 MB of VRAM) for the rest of the session, and repeated sweeps in different folders can exhaust it.

**Trigger.** List view in a folder with varied file names; open View and drag the Scale slider slowly end to end a few times. VRAM use of the process jumps and never comes back; after enough sweeps newly needed glyph/size combinations render as blanks ('[lntrn-text] glyph atlas at max size' in the log).

```rust
let font = FontSize::Custom(24.0 * m * s);
let small_font = FontSize::Custom(20.0 * m * s);
let path_font = FontSize::Custom(16.0 * m * s);
```

**Skeptic's check.** Confirmed the mechanism: layout.rs list_zoom_multiplier is 0.8 + 1.4*zoom straight from the slider, views.rs uses 24*m*s / 20*m*s / 16*m*s as font sizes, lntrn-text glyph_cache_key includes the quarter-pixel size and a 2-bit subpixel bin, and atlas.rs has no eviction (grows to 8192 then drops glyphs). What is not established is the magnitude: the texel totals are estimates, nothing was measured, and there is no log line from Fox itself showing atlas growth. How many of the 134 sizes a real drag actually hits depends on frame pacing.

**Fix.** Snapping the List/Tree font sizes to whole pixels (or snapping m to a dozen steps) bounds the set of sizes. It is a one-line change per font but makes text size step while rows scale smoothly, which is a visual choice for Alva. The underlying gap (no eviction) lives in lntrn-text, a separate crate that must not be touched without asking.

### render-11: List rows scroll underneath the fixed column header: row text, tints and orphan icons overlap 'Name / Size / Modified'

- **Where:** `src/views.rs:152` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C7): layout::list_rows_rect() (content minus header) is now the clip for list rows (views.rs, nested inside the ScrollArea clip), link emblems, row zones, icon visibility and icon texture clip, play badges and the inline rename box (render.rs), and the same in the unfocused pane (sections/split.rs and its icon pass in render.rs). Drop-on-folder and search right-click hit-tests ignore rows hidden under the header (wayland_actions/drag_drop.rs, context_menu.rs).

**What is wrong.** The header labels are drawn at a fixed position with a deliberately transparent background, but the clip pushed by `area.begin` is the whole content rect including the header band, and a row is drawn in full as soon as any part of it is below the header. A partly scrolled row therefore paints its name, size and date (same x positions as the header labels) and its stripe/selection pill into the header band. render.rs makes it worse: icon visibility at :160/:167 and zone registration at :737 test against `content.y`, not `content.y + hdr_h`, and the icon clip at :1088 is the whole content rect, so a row that draw_content_list has already skipped still gets its icon drawn (and a clickable zone) inside the header band.

**Trigger.** List view, scroll by anything that is not a whole row (smooth wheel scrolling always passes through such offsets): the top row's text slides over the 'Name', 'Size' and 'Modified' labels and a clipped icon appears in the header strip next to 'Name'. Clicking the header band selects the row hidden under it.

```rust
area.begin(painter, text);
let base_y = area.content_y();
let content_top = content_rect.y + hdr_h;
...
if y + row_h < content_top || y > content_bottom {
    continue;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** lntrn-ui scroll.rs ScrollArea::begin clips to the viewport passed to ScrollArea::new, which render.rs:131 builds from the whole content rect including the header band. draw_content_list draws header labels with no background, then draws any row with y + row_h >= content.y + hdr_h in full. Scrolled by exactly one row, row 0's name sits right on top of 'Name'. render.rs:160/167 (icon visibility), :737 (zone) and :1088 (icon clip) test against content.y, so a row the list has already skipped still gets an icon and a clickable zone inside the header band.

**Fix.** Use one rows rect (content minus hdr_h) for the row clip in draw_content_list, the icon visibility test, the icon clip and the zone intersection. lntrn-text push_clip intersects with the current clip, so a nested clip works. It is uncontroversial but has to be applied at four or five sites that must stay in sync, including the unfocused pane in sections/split.rs.

### render-15: Name labels are queued with max_width equal to the width they were truncated to; quantisation can push the ellipsis onto a visible second line

- **Where:** `src/views.rs:316` (Rendering and layout)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category other; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Truncated list/tree/search labels are queued with 4px of slack.

**What is wrong.** `truncate_to_width` accepts the longest candidate whose measured width is <= max_w, then the label is queued with max_width = that same max_w. queue_entry rounds max_width to the nearest 0.25px (lntrn-text engine.rs:135), which can round DOWN by up to 0.125px, and line::build wraps when `pen + advance > max_width`. When the chosen candidate lands within that sliver of max_w, the final glyph (the ellipsis) wraps to a second row, and inside the ScrollArea the text bounds are the whole viewport (engine.rs:137-143), so the wrapped glyph is visible on top of the row below. Same pattern in the tree view (views.rs:524-529) and the search rows (:273-278, :298-303). This is the known 'exact measured width clips the last glyph' class, here appearing as a stray ellipsis rather than a clipped one.

**Trigger.** List or Tree view with many truncated names; resize the window or drag the preview divider slowly. For a given width roughly 0.5% of truncated rows hit the sliver, so with 60 truncated rows on screen a stray '...' appears under some row at about one width in three.

```rust
truncate_to_width(text, &entry.name, max_name_w, 24.0 * m * s)
};
TextLabel::new(&display, name_x, text_y)
    .size(font)
    .color(name_color)
    .max_width(if entry.selected { 9999.0 } else { max_name_w })
```

**Skeptic's check.** The pattern is there: truncate_to_width accepts a candidate whose measured width is <= max_w exactly, the label is then queued with max_width = max_w, lntrn-text engine.rs:135 rounds max_width to the nearest 0.25 px (can round down by up to 0.125), and line.rs:184/192 wraps when pen + advance > max_width. Same pattern at views.rs:278, :302 and :528. Not established: that measure_width and the layout pass sum advances identically, and how often a candidate lands in the 0.125 px sliver. Not reproduced.

**Fix.** After truncating, queue with slack (max_name_w + 4.0*s, or 9999.0 since the string is already cut to fit). Apply to the list name, tree name and both search labels. No layout change.

### wayland-28: Startup lists pinned tabs and the restored split pane synchronously, bypassing the slow-mount guard

- **Where:** `src/wayland.rs:480` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category freeze; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C4): Startup no longer stats or lists slow mounts before the first frame: pinned tabs, the restored split pane and favourites as in app-16. App::new no longer runs lsblk; drives and phones arrive from the watcher thread.

**What is wrong.** Everywhere else a slow mount (jmtpfs, sshfs, NFS) is listed on a worker thread via spawn_dir_load after an is_slow_path check. The pinned-tab restore here and App::restore_split (app/split.rs:144-155, called at wayland.rs:508) stat and list directly on the main thread before the first frame, so the window does not appear until they return.

**Trigger.** Pin a tab on a phone or sshfs folder, or quit with the split pane on one, then launch Fox while that mount is alive and busy (for example another Fox window is copying from the phone, holding the jmtpfs lock): the new window stays invisible for the length of the transfer.

```rust
if path.is_dir() {
    ...
    tab.entries =
        crate::fs::list_directory(&path, app.show_hidden, app.sort_by, app.sort_dir);
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland.rs:480-485 calls path.is_dir() and fs::list_directory for every pinned path on the main thread before the first frame, and app/split.rs restore_split does the same for the saved right pane, with no fs::is_slow_path check. reload() and reload_tab() both route slow mounts through spawn_dir_load. A tab pinned on a phone or sshfs folder therefore stats and lists the device synchronously at startup. Not reproduced with a device, but the path is direct; if the mount is absent is_dir() fails fast and nothing happens.

**Fix.** For slow paths, create the tab with empty entries and call reload_tab(idx) / reload_inactive_pane() after the tabs are installed. Note path.is_dir() itself is a device round trip on MTP, so the slow check has to come before it. Changes startup loading order, so not mechanical.

### sections-20: Picker results are printed with Path::display(), one per line: non-UTF-8 or newline-containing names reach the caller corrupted

- **Where:** `src/wayland.rs:544` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** partly fixed. Batch 2 (C8): New src/pick_output.rs and src/pick_args.rs. Results are written as the exact bytes of each path (OsStrExt::as_bytes) instead of Path::display(), so a non-UTF-8 name is no longer replaced by U+FFFD on Fox's side; for ordinary names the output is byte-for-byte what it was (one path per line). New opt-in flag --pick-print0: each path followed by one NUL byte, nothing else, which is unambiguous for every possible path. In the default line framing a path containing a line break cannot be transmitted, so the picker refuses to return it and shows a notice explaining why (finish_pick / confirm_save_pick), instead of handing the caller two wrong paths. In a Save picker in line framing the typed name is checked and returned with trailing whitespace removed, because every existing caller trims the line (otherwise the Replace check would look at a different file than the one the caller writes). Fox's own two main-lane consumers (folder icon picker in src/props_click.rs and src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs) now pass --pick-print0 and read raw bytes through pick_output::choose_folder_icon / parse (which falls back to line parsing if an older picker binary ignores the flag). A failed write to stdout exits with status 1 instead of panicking. REMAINS: The consumers in other crates still decode lossily, split on lines and trim, so a non-UTF-8 name (and a name with leading/trailing whitespace on open) still reaches them corrupted: lntrn-portal/src/filechooser.rs (from_utf8_lossy + lines() + trim()), lntrn-notepad/src/actions.rs (open, save-as, export: from_utf8_lossy + trim()), lntrn-command-center/src/controls/bluetooth/worker/mod.rs and lntrn-command-center/src/notes/export.rs. They need to pass --pick-print0, split stdout on NUL and build paths from the raw bytes (the portal must percent-encode bytes, not a lossy string). Those crates are outside my lane. Inside Fox, src/properties_audio/mod.rs (audio lane) still uses the line framing with trim(). Then (lead): the audio artwork picker inside Fox now uses pick_output::choose_file (NUL framing); what remains is only the other crates.
- **Also reported as:** wayland-30

**What is wrong.** display() is lossy (invalid bytes become U+FFFD) and the protocol is newline-delimited, so a selected file whose name is not valid UTF-8 or contains '\n' comes back as a different path (or as two paths). lntrn-portal splits on lines and Notepad uses the text as a path.

**Trigger.** Pick a file with a Latin-1 encoded name (old archives, some camera cards) through any Open dialog: the app receives a path that does not exist and fails to open it.

```rust
for p in &paths {
    println!("{}", p.display());
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland.rs:543-545 prints each path with Path::display() and a newline. display() is lossy for non-UTF-8 names and the newline framing breaks names containing '\n'. Consumers confirmed: lntrn-portal filechooser.rs:262-266 uses from_utf8_lossy + lines() + trim(), lntrn-notepad actions.rs:117 uses from_utf8_lossy + trim(). The trim() calls also mangle names with leading or trailing spaces.

**Fix.** Writing raw bytes (OsStrExt::as_bytes) on the Fox side is harmless and identical for valid UTF-8 names, but on its own it fixes nothing because both consumers decode lossily and split on lines. A real fix needs a NUL-delimited mode plus matching changes in lntrn-portal, lntrn-notepad and lntrn-command-center, which are separate crates and need Alva's approval first.

### input-26: Tree rows and search results open on a single click regardless of double_click_to_open, so a double-click launches twice

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/click.rs:403` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): src/app/select.rs: App::on_tree_row_click and App::on_search_result_click with the same rule as on_item_click (click_activates: open on double click when [input].double_click_to_open is set, otherwise on every click), double clicks tracked by path; a double click is consumed so a third click does not open again. The tree-row release branch in src/wayland_loop.rs and the search-result press branch in src/wayland_actions/click.rs call them (the search branch now returns ClickAction::Consumed, so no rubber band starts under a result click). With the setting on, the first click selects a top-level Tree row, marks a nested row or a search result, and the second opens/expands. With the setting off (the default, and this machine's value) behaviour is unchanged.

**What is wrong.** Grid and List go through on_item_click, which honours double_click_to_open and the 400 ms double-click window. Search results act at press time (click.rs:403-427) and Tree rows act on release (wayland_loop.rs:955-978), both unconditionally. With the setting on, a habitual double-click starts the application twice; on a search-result folder the second click lands in the freshly opened folder and opens whatever is under the pointer. There is also no way to merely select a file in Tree view with a plain click.

**Trigger.** System Settings, Mouse, double-click to open: on. In Tree view or in search results double-click a file: two instances of the viewer start.

```rust
if app.searching && !app.search_buf.is_empty() {
    // Search result clicked — navigate to parent and highlight, or open file directly
    if idx < app.search_results.len() { ... desktop::launch_app(&app.exec, &path);
// wayland_loop.rs:955-978: pending_tree_open.take() -> launch on release
```

**Skeptic's check.** double_click_to_open is read only in App::on_item_click (grep). Search results launch at press time (click.rs:403-427) and non-pick Tree rows launch or expand on release through pending_tree_open (wayland_loop.rs:955-978), both unconditionally. With the setting on, a double-click therefore launches twice and a Tree file cannot be merely selected. Rated low because the setting defaults to false and is false in this machine's lantern.toml, where single-click activation is the intended behaviour.

**Fix.** Route both paths through the same activation rule as on_item_click (track last click path and time, activate on double click only when the setting is on, otherwise select). Tree selection of nested rows has no entries index, so this needs a path-based selection for Tree; a behaviour change, not a mechanical edit.

### input-28: xdg-open fallbacks spawn a child and drop it: one zombie per opened file

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/click.rs:423` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. All spawn-and-drop children go through desktop::spawn_reaped / xdg_open, which wait on a thread.

**What is wrong.** Command::spawn returns a Child that is dropped without wait(), and Rust does not reap on drop. xdg-open exits as soon as it has handed off, leaving a <defunct> entry under Fox until Fox exits. desktop::launch_app got this right (it waits in its thread); the fallback sites did not: click.rs:421-424, context_menu.rs:566-569 and 577-579, wayland_loop.rs:972-975, file_ops.rs:732-734 and the terminal launch at 1014-1018.

**Trigger.** Open any file whose extension has no entry in mime_from_extension or no default app (for example .blend or a file with no extension), or use Open on a search result: `ps` shows a new defunct xdg-open each time.

```rust
std::thread::spawn(move || {
    let _ = std::process::Command::new("xdg-open").arg(&path).spawn();
});
```

**Skeptic's check.** Each site spawns a thread that calls Command::spawn() and drops the Child without wait(); Rust does not reap on drop and the crate installs no SIGCHLD handling (grep). Sites confirmed: click.rs:421-424, context_menu.rs:566-569 and :577-579, wayland_loop.rs:972-975, file_ops.rs:732-734, and the terminal launch at file_ops.rs:1014-1018. desktop::launch_app already waits in its thread, with a comment explaining why.

**Fix.** Replace .spawn() with .status() inside the existing thread (or spawn then wait), or route through launch_app. The thread is already dedicated, so blocking in it is fine; for lntrn-terminal the thread simply parks until the terminal exits.

### app-28: Picker 'Open'/Enter with nothing eligible selected closes the dialog as Cancelled

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/click.rs:503` (App state)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Picker Open/Enter with nothing eligible selected (or an invalid save name) keeps the dialog open instead of exiting as cancelled.
- **Also reported as:** wayland-29, sections-11

**What is wrong.** confirm_pick() leaves pick_result = None when there is no selection (Open/Mixed), only folders are selected in Open mode, or the Save name is empty. Both the confirm button and Enter (key.rs:742, 616) then stop the loop unconditionally; wayland::run sees no result and exits with status 1, which callers treat as Cancel. The user loses the dialog (and their navigation) instead of it staying open.

**Trigger.** In an Open File picker, navigate somewhere and press Enter (or click Open) before selecting a file: the picker vanishes and the calling app behaves as if Cancel was pressed.

```rust
crate::ZONE_PICK_CONFIRM => {
    app.confirm_pick();
    return ClickAction::Close;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** confirm_pick (select.rs:90-154) leaves pick_result None when Open/Mixed has nothing eligible selected or the Save name is empty. click.rs:503-506 returns ClickAction::Close regardless, and key.rs:616-620 and :742-745 set running = false regardless. wayland.rs:548-551 then exits 1, which callers treat as cancel. pick_bar.rs registers ZONE_PICK_CONFIRM unconditionally with no disabled state.
