**Fix.** `app.confirm_pick(); if app.pick_result.is_some() { return ClickAction::Close; }` and the same condition before `*running = false` at both Enter sites in key.rs (at :617 keep save_name_editing true when nothing was produced). Combine with input-8, which requires pick_result to be cleared at the start of confirm_pick so a stale result cannot satisfy this check.

### media-19: Quick Look is not modal for right-click or the wheel: the hidden view underneath still reacts

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:131` (Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Right-click is ignored while Quick Look is open (wheel handled in the main loop fix).

**What is wrong.** Left click (click.rs:41) and keys (key.rs:676) are swallowed while Quick Look is open, but handle_right_click has no such guard, and neither does the wheel handler in wayland_loop.rs:559. Sidebar items are hit-tested by geometry, so a right-click on the overlay above the hidden sidebar opens that item's menu ("Empty Trash" for Trash, "Eject / Format to ext4…" for a drive). Elsewhere zone_at() returns ZONE_QUICK_LOOK, which falls into the empty-area branch: the selection is cleared and the folder background menu (Paste, New File, New Folder…) opens on top of the preview. The wheel scrolls the hidden listing.

**Trigger.** Select a file, press Space, right-click on the preview: a background or sidebar context menu appears over Quick Look and the file is deselected; after Esc the selection is gone and the list may have scrolled.

```rust
let Some((cx, cy)) = input.cursor() else {
    return;
};
// (no app.quick_look check before the sidebar / content hit-tests)
```

**Skeptic's check.** handle_right_click has no app.quick_look check; the sidebar is hit-tested by geometry before any zone lookup, so a right-click over the hidden sidebar opens that item's menu (Empty Trash, drive Eject/Format). Elsewhere zone_at returns ZONE_QUICK_LOOK (905, last zone added, lntrn-ui zone_at searches in reverse), which matches no item range, so the empty-area branch clears the selection and opens the background menu. The wheel block at wayland_loop.rs:559 has no quick_look check either. Left click (click.rs:41) and keys (key.rs:676) are swallowed correctly.

**Fix.** `if app.quick_look.is_some() { return; }` at the top of handle_right_click, and skip the scroll block (or zero state.scroll_delta) while Quick Look is open.

### input-32: Text fields use a hardcoded US-QWERTY keycode table and ignore Caps Lock

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:28` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): src/keyboard.rs + src/wayland_actions/key.rs: the hardcoded US table (keycode_to_char) is gone. Text comes from the xkb state (layout, Shift, Caps Lock via mods_locked, AltGr, numpad, dead keys/Compose loaded lazily on first use); Ctrl/Alt/Super chords never type. Shortcuts use a layout-independent key: editing keys by keysym, letters by the letter the key types (Ctrl+A on AZERTY is the key labelled A), with a fallback to another layout of the keymap and then to position for non-Latin layouts. handle_key now takes a KeyPress; all seven text entry points (sudo, cloud login, audio tag editor entry, search, path bar, rename, save name) insert the translated char. The search field's cursor was a byte index used with String::insert/remove (panic on non-ASCII): it is now a character index like the path bar, and Delete works there. Key repeat now also reaches the audio tag fields, only keys the keymap marks as repeating are held, and the second repeat no longer waits a second 300 ms.

**What is wrong.** Characters are derived from raw evdev keycodes plus the Shift bit. The keymap sent by the compositor, Caps Lock (mods_locked), AltGr and the numpad are ignored, and Ctrl is not checked in the search branch (Ctrl+A types 'a'). With Caps Lock on, rename and search type lowercase and a password typed into the sudo prompt does not match what the user typed everywhere else; any non-US layout types the wrong characters.

**Trigger.** Turn on Caps Lock and type in the rename box or the sudo password field: letters come out lowercase and the password is rejected.

```rust
fn keycode_to_char(key: u32, shift: bool) -> Option<char> {
    let ch = match key {
        2..=11 => { let base = b"1234567890"[(key - 2) as usize]; ...
```

**Skeptic's check.** keycode_to_char maps raw evdev keycodes plus the Shift bit. wayland_dispatch.rs:371-375 reads only mods_depressed bits 1, 4 and 64 and ignores the Keymap event and mods_locked, so Caps Lock, AltGr, the numpad and any non-US layout are wrong in rename, search, path, save-name, login and the sudo password field. The search branch (key.rs:416-421) also never checks ctrl. Low here because the machine uses a US layout; the Caps Lock case is the one that would actually bite.

**Fix.** Reading mods_locked for Caps Lock (bit 2) and applying it to letters is a small step. Proper layout support means interpreting the wl_keyboard keymap, which under the build-our-own rule is a real piece of work and probably belongs in a shared crate, so it needs a plan rather than a patch.

### dialogs-24: Ctrl+V / Ctrl+A / Ctrl+C in the sudo and cloud-login fields type the letter instead of pasting or selecting

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:329` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Ctrl chords no longer type letters in the sudo, cloud-login and search fields (real paste is still not implemented).

**What is wrong.** Both modal key handlers discard `ctrl` (`let _ = ctrl;`) and fall through to keycode_to_char, so a Ctrl chord inserts its letter. There is no paste path at all, and keycode_to_char ignores Caps Lock, the keypad and the active keymap, so some passwords cannot be entered correctly. In the password fields the stray character is hidden behind a bullet.

**Trigger.** Copy the cloud password from a password manager, focus the Password field, press Ctrl+V: a single bullet appears (the letter v) and sign-in fails.

```rust
_ => {
    if let Some(ch) = keycode_to_char(key, shift) {
        if let Some(d) = app.cloud_login.as_mut() {
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read handle_key in /home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-file-manager/src/wayland_actions/key.rs. The sudo branch (185-258) and cloud-login branch (261-345) both do `let _ = ctrl;` and their `_ =>` arms call keycode_to_char(key, shift) and insert the result, so Ctrl+V inserts 'v', Ctrl+A inserts 'a'. No paste path exists in either branch. keycode_to_char only looks at shift (no Caps Lock, no keypad codes 71-83, fixed US layout), so that side claim is also true. The search branch (394-425) has the same missing ctrl guard.

**Fix.** Minimal safe part: `if ctrl { return; }` at the top of the sudo and cloud-login branches (and the search branch) so chords stop typing letters. Adding real Ctrl+V paste is feature work (needs a clipboard read path; only set_text is used today) and should strip newlines; do that separately. Nothing relies on Ctrl+letter typing a letter.

### input-31: Esc cannot close the context menu in desktop mode

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:386` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Esc closes the inline context menu in desktop mode.

**What is wrong.** In desktop (layer-shell) mode popup_backend is None and the menu is drawn inline, so the Esc branch does nothing while every other key is swallowed by the early return. The click-outside path in wayland_loop.rs:673-677 has the missing `else { context_menu.close() }`.

**Trigger.** On the desktop surface right-click to open the menu and press Esc: the menu stays and the keyboard is dead until you click elsewhere.

```rust
if context_menu.is_open() {
    if key == KEY_ESC {
        if let Some(backend) = popup_backend {
            context_menu.close_popups(backend);
        }
    }
    return;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** key.rs:384-391: when context_menu.is_open() the Esc arm only closes via `if let Some(backend) = popup_backend`, and then returns for every key. wayland.rs:403 creates the popup backend only when !desktop, and context_menu.rs opens the menu inline with context_menu.open(..) in that case. So in --desktop (layer-shell, OnDemand keyboard) mode Esc does nothing and all keys are swallowed until a click (wayland_loop.rs:671-677 has the missing else branch). Only affects the --desktop surface, which may be legacy now that lntrn-desktop exists.

**Fix.** Add `else { context_menu.close(); }` to the Esc arm, mirroring wayland_loop.rs:675. Local and mechanical.

### input-30: Typing a rename while the search bar is open goes to the search box

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:394` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. A rename, path edit or save-name field started while search is open gets the keyboard.

**What is wrong.** The search branch is tested before path editing, rename and save-name and returns unconditionally. With the search bar open but empty the normal listing is shown, so Rename from the context menu or New Folder (which auto-starts a rename) opens a rename box that never receives keys; the first typed letter starts a search and replaces the view with results.

**Trigger.** Click the search button, then right-click a file, Rename, and type: the letters appear in the search field and the view switches to search results.

```rust
if app.searching {
    match key { ... _ => { if let Some(ch) = keycode_to_char(key, shift) { app.search_buf.insert(app.search_cursor, ch); ... app.run_search(); } } }
    return;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** key.rs tests `app.searching` (394) before path editing (428), rename (543) and save-name (606) and returns unconditionally. With the search bar open and search_buf empty, render shows the normal listing (is_searching requires a non-empty buffer) and handle_right_click takes the normal item path, so CTX_RENAME -> start_rename works; start_rename (app/edit.rs:8) does not close search. Typed keys then go to search_buf and run_search switches the view. Only focus_pane closes search. F2 is unreachable while searching, so the trigger is the context-menu Rename / New Folder path.

**Fix.** Move the rename, path-edit and save-name branches above the search branch. Rename cannot be started from a search-result row (ContextTarget::SearchItem has no rename), so the reorder does not change any working flow.

### wayland-24: A WouldBlock from event_queue.flush() is treated as fatal and exits Fox

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:93` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** plausible; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Flush WouldBlock is no longer fatal.

**What is wrong.** wayland-backend 0.3.17's flush returns Err(WouldBlock) when the socket send buffer is full (it deliberately does not store that as a connection error). Fox breaks out of the main loop on any error, so a compositor that stops reading for long enough while Fox is producing requests makes Fox quit instead of waiting.

**Trigger.** Compositor stalls for tens of seconds (GPU hang, long blocking operation) while Fox is animating (copy in progress, or the stuck-key 60 fps state): the outgoing buffer fills, flush returns WouldBlock, and the Fox window is gone when the compositor recovers.

```rust
if let Err(e) = event_queue.flush() {
    eprintln!("[fox] flush error: {e}");
    break;
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** Confirmed in wayland-backend 0.3.17 (sys client flush + store_if_not_wouldblock_and_return_error): a full socket returns Err(WouldBlock) without poisoning the connection, and wayland_loop.rs:93-96 breaks the main loop on any flush error. What I could not establish is reachability: Fox emits only a few hundred bytes per frame, and while the compositor is stalled the swapchain acquire/present is likely to block before the send buffer fills. So the defect is real but I cannot show a user can hit it.

**Fix.** Match the error: on WaylandError::Io with kind WouldBlock, do not break; fall through to poll (optionally add POLLOUT for the wayland fd that iteration). Break only on other errors. Local change with no downside.

### wayland-17: After an outgoing Wayland drag the button release never arrives, so InteractionContext keeps its capture and hover is dead until the next click

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:477` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Starting a Wayland drag releases the zone capture and press state.

**What is wrong.** handle_click called input.on_left_pressed() on the file zone at press time, setting active_zone. Once start_drag hands the grab to the compositor, the wl_pointer release is consumed by the DnD grab and `left_released` is never set, so input.on_left_released() is never called. compute_state returns Idle for every other zone while active_zone is Some, and the captured item draws as Pressed/Dragging.

**Trigger.** Drag a file from Fox into another application and drop it. Back in Fox, nothing highlights on hover and the dragged item looks pressed until you click somewhere.

```rust
dd.start_drag(Some(&source), surf, None, state.dnd_serial);
state.dnd_active = true;
// Clear internal drag — compositor owns the drag now
app.drag_item = None;
```

**Skeptic's check.** handle_click calls input.on_left_pressed() on the file zone at press, which sets InteractionContext.active_zone (/home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-ui/src/gpu/input.rs:107-115). It is cleared only by on_left_released (117), which the loop calls only in the left_released branch (997). After start_drag the compositor's DnD grab (Smithay sets it with Focus::Clear) owns the button, so the release is never delivered to Fox. compute_state (153-172) returns Idle for every other zone while active_zone is Some, so hover is dead and the captured item shows Pressed/Dragging until the next click.

**Fix.** Call input.on_left_released() and reset press_shift/press_ctrl/press_pos/suppress_rubber_band where the Wayland drag is started (next to clearing drag_item at 480-482). Doing it on DndFinished/Cancelled is harder because the dispatch handler has no access to `input`.

### wayland-18: dnd_active is set even when the compositor silently refuses start_drag, leaving it stuck true

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:478` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): src/dnd_out.rs: State::hand_off_drag refuses to hand off when the button release is already in this batch, and otherwise sends wl_display.sync right behind start_drag. Between the two, wl_pointer leave and left-release are recorded (src/wayland_dispatch.rs). When the sync answers: a drag that started took the pointer away (leave) and swallowed the release; anything else means the compositor refused, and end_drag_out() clears dnd_active, dnd_paths and destroys the source (which also ends the drag should the verdict ever be wrong). Checked against vendor/smithay: StartDrag sets the grab with Focus::Clear, which sends wl_pointer.leave inside the request handler, before the sync is answered. DndFinished/Cancelled go through the same end_drag_out. dnd_active no longer keeps the loop at 60 fps, and a new in-window drag is not started while an earlier one is still in the compositor's hands (it would swap dnd_paths under a receiver).

**What is wrong.** Smithay drops a start_drag whose serial does not match a live implicit grab without sending Cancelled (vendor/smithay data_device/device.rs: 'denying drag from client without implicit grab'). If the button release is dispatched in the same batch as the motion that crosses the window edge, this block runs before the release branch, the request is denied, and nothing ever resets dnd_active or clears dnd_paths. needs_anim then stays true (60 fps) and `!state.dnd_active` blocks every later drag-out until restart. The data source object is also never destroyed on any path.

**Trigger.** Drag a file toward the window edge and release just as the pointer crosses it while Fox is in a slow frame (for example a reload of a large directory), so the motion and the release are read together.

```rust
dd.start_drag(Some(&source), surf, None, state.dnd_serial);
state.dnd_active = true;
```

**Skeptic's check.** Read /home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/vendor/smithay/src/wayland/selection/data_device/device.rs:64-117 and pointer has_grab (input/pointer/mod.rs:187): StartDrag is honoured only while a grab with exactly that serial is active; otherwise it logs 'denying drag from client without implicit grab' and sends nothing. Fox sets dnd_active = true unconditionally after start_drag (478) and clears drag_item. dnd_active is reset only in the DndFinished/Cancelled handler, so a denied request leaves it true forever: needs_anim stays true (1369, 60 fps) and `!state.dnd_active` (457) blocks every later drag-out; dnd_paths is never cleared either. Denial happens when the button is released before the request reaches the compositor (release in the same batch as the edge-crossing motion, or in transit), or when dnd_serial was overwritten by another button event. It is a timing race, so rare, but the stuck state is permanent until restart. The source object is also never destroyed on any path.

**Fix.** Skip the hand-off when state.left_released is already set in this frame, and treat a wl_pointer Button/Enter on our surface while dnd_active as proof the grab is not ours: reset dnd_active only. Do NOT clear dnd_paths at that point as the finder proposes: after a real drop the pointer can re-enter Fox before the receiver has finished reading, and the Send handler would then write an empty list. Clear dnd_paths on DndFinished/Cancelled or at the next drag start, and destroy the source there. Touches DnD state handling, so review.

### input-29: Key repeat fires again after a handler that blocked: one Enter on a failing cloud login sends two sign-in requests

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:535` (Input handling)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. No key repeat in the frame that handled the press, and none for Enter/Esc/Tab.
- **Also reported as:** cloud-net-20, wayland-25

**What is wrong.** The repeat block runs in the same iteration right after the first handle_key. submit_cloud_login blocks on the HTTP round-trip; when it returns with an error the dialog is still open, held_key is still set (the release is unread in the socket) and the 300 ms deadline has passed, so Enter is handled a second time and a second sign-in is sent. The gate also looks at which mode flags are set rather than which handler consumed the key: with the search bar or path bar active and a conflict dialog open, holding Enter repeats Replace across successive conflicts.

**Trigger.** Open the cloud login, enter a wrong password, tap Enter once: two failed attempts are made and the UI is blocked twice as long.

```rust
if (app.renaming.is_some() || app.path_editing || app.save_name_editing || app.searching
    || app.sudo_prompt.is_some() || app.cloud_login.is_some())
    && std::time::Instant::now() >= state.repeat_deadline
{
    handle_key(app, settings, context_menu, &mut state.popup_backend, key, ...);
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland_dispatch.rs:360-363 arms repeat_deadline = press time + 300 ms. In the frame, the key_pressed block (499-525) calls handle_key(KEY_ENTER) -> submit_cloud_login, which blocks for the HTTPS round trip (app/nav.rs:22-58). On failure the dialog stays, submitting is reset and the password is kept, so CloudLoginDialog::can_submit (dialogs.rs:62) is true again. The repeat block (528-553) runs in the same iteration with no dispatch in between, held_key is still Enter, cloud_login.is_some(), and the deadline has passed if the request took 300 ms or more, so a second sign_in is sent. The sudo prompt is safe only because it clears the password on failure (file_ops.rs:1073). The gate also keys off mode flags rather than which handler took the key, so with search/path edit active a held Enter can repeat conflict-dialog Replace.

**Fix.** After handling the initial press, re-arm: state.repeat_deadline = Instant::now() + 300ms (or skip the repeat block in a frame that consumed key_pressed). Additionally restrict repeat to non-committing keys (exclude KEY_ENTER/KEY_ESC/KEY_TAB). Both are local and do not change normal repeat feel.

### dialogs-23: A sudo prompt that appears while Properties is open cannot be clicked

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:638` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. The Properties press branch yields to a sudo/conflict/login/drive modal stacked on top.

**What is wrong.** The left-press chain tests app.properties before it reaches handle_click, where the sudo/conflict/login/drive modals are handled. The sudo prompt can be raised asynchronously by poll_op_progress when a background copy finishes with permission failures. It is drawn on top, its zones are topmost, and the Properties branch ignores those ids, so neither dialog reacts to the mouse (keyboard still works). The Properties icon and picker thumbnails, drawn in the later texture pass, also paint over the prompt.

**Trigger.** Start a large paste in which a later item fails with permission denied, open Properties on some file while it runs. When the copy ends the password dialog appears above Properties; Cancel, Authenticate and the scrim do nothing on click.

```rust
} else if app.properties.is_some() {
    // Properties dialog is open
    if let Some(zone) = input.on_left_pressed() {
        if zone == 800 || zone == 801 {
```

**Skeptic's check.** Left-press chain: popup, pending_drop, then `else if app.properties.is_some()` (638) before the final branch that calls handle_click, where the sudo/conflict/login/drive modals are handled. poll_op_progress (file_ops.rs:343-358) can raise sudo_prompt asynchronously via priv_run when a background copy ends with permission failures. render.rs draws Properties at 1385 and the sudo prompt at 1478, so the sudo zones (65-68) are topmost; the Properties branch gets one of those ids from on_left_pressed and matches none of 800/801/810-817/830-843/802, so the click does nothing. Keyboard still works (key.rs checks sudo before properties). Needs Properties open while a failing paste finishes, so rare.

**Fix.** Guard the branch: `else if app.properties.is_some() && app.sudo_prompt.is_none() && app.conflict_dialog.is_none() && app.cloud_login.is_none() && app.drive_dialog.is_none()` so the higher modals fall through to handle_click. Local; Properties clicks behave as before when no other modal is up.

### sections-13: Scrollbar press uses the single-pane content rect in split/pick layouts, and the unfocused pane's scrollbar starts a rubber band instead of a drag

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:688` (UI sections)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Scrollbar press uses the same pane-aware rect as the bar and the drag. (The unfocused pane's scrollbar still starts a rubber band.)
- **Also reported as:** input-34, wayland-16

**What is wrong.** The grab test rebuilds the scrollbar from layout::content_rect (full width, tab bar, status bar) instead of the local split/pick-aware active_content_rect helper that the drag at line 303 uses. In split view the column count, content height and (for the right pane) the top edge differ, so the computed thumb is not where the drawn thumb is: a press on the thumb is treated as a track click and scroll_offset jumps. Separately, only ZONE_SCROLLBAR is checked: a press on the unfocused pane's ZONE_P2_SCROLLBAR falls through to handle_click, is translated to ZONE_SCROLLBAR (click.rs:545), matches no arm, and the loop then clears that pane's selection and starts a rubber band (or a window edge-resize on the outer 10 px).

**Trigger.** Split view, Grid mode, a folder long enough to scroll: press on the scrollbar thumb of the focused pane and the view jumps before the drag starts. Press on the other pane's scrollbar and drag: items get band-selected instead of the pane scrolling.

```rust
if input.zone_at(cx, cy) == Some(crate::ZONE_SCROLLBAR) {
    let content = content_rect(wf, hf, s);
    let total_h = view_content_height(app, content.w, s);
    let bar = Scrollbar::new(&content, total_h, app.scroll_offset);
```

**Skeptic's check.** render.rs:905 builds the ZONE_SCROLLBAR bar from the pane/pick/preview-aware content rect, and the drag update at wayland_loop.rs:303 uses active_content_rect. The press handler at 688 uses layout::content_rect (full width, tab-bar top, status-bar bottom). In split view the width (grid columns, hence total height) and, for the right pane, the top differ; in pick mode the bottom differs by PICK_BAR_H. Scrollbar::new derives thumb y/h from viewport and total (lntrn-ui scroll.rs:146-175), so the thumb test and grab offset are for a different bar and a thumb press can be treated as a track click. Second half also holds: only ZONE_SCROLLBAR is tested, so ZONE_P2_SCROLLBAR falls to edge_resize (if within 10 px of the window edge) or to handle_click, where translate_split_zone maps it to ZONE_SCROLLBAR (click.rs:545), no arm matches, ClickAction::None comes back and the loop clears the selection and starts a rubber band (780-790).

**Fix.** Replace content_rect(wf, hf, s) at 688 with active_content_rect(app, wf, hf, s); purely mechanical and identical in the non-split, non-pick case. For the other pane's bar: if zone_at is ZONE_P2_SCROLLBAR, focus_pane(other) first and then run the same grab code; that part changes focus behaviour slightly and should be eyeballed.

### wayland-15: xdg-open fallback is spawned without wait(), leaving a zombie per launch

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:972` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category leak; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. All spawn-and-drop children go through desktop::spawn_reaped / xdg_open, which wait on a thread.

**What is wrong.** The Child is dropped immediately; Rust does not reap on drop and Fox installs no SIGCHLD handling, so each xdg-open that exits stays as a <defunct> child until Fox quits. desktop::launch_app was fixed for exactly this (it waits in its thread); this copy and the identical one in wayland_actions/click.rs:421 were not.

**Trigger.** In tree view, click a file whose extension has no default app (so the xdg-open branch runs). `ps` shows a defunct xdg-open under lntrn-file-manager for each such open.

```rust
std::thread::spawn(move || {
    let _ =
        std::process::Command::new("xdg-open").arg(&path).spawn();
});
```

**Skeptic's check.** wayland_loop.rs:972-975 spawns xdg-open inside a thread and drops the Child without wait(); std does not reap on drop. No SIGCHLD/SA_NOCLDWAIT/waitpid handling in Fox, and the compositor no longer sets SA_NOCLDWAIT (lntrn-compositor/src/main.rs:720-725 says it was removed), so nothing is inherited. desktop::launch_app (desktop.rs:266-272) does wait, these sites do not. Same pattern at wayland_actions/click.rs:421 and context_menu.rs:566 and 577. Each fallback open leaves a <defunct> xdg-open until Fox exits.

**Fix.** In each thread: `if let Ok(mut c) = Command::new("xdg-open").arg(&path).spawn() { let _ = c.wait(); }`, or route all four sites through one helper / launch_app("xdg-open", &path). The thread already exists, so waiting there costs nothing.

### dialogs-12: Right-click is not blocked by any modal: a context menu opens over Properties / conflict / sudo / format dialogs and its actions run on the view underneath

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:1002` (Dialogs and Properties)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it medium; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Right-click is ignored while any modal is open.

**What is wrong.** Left clicks and keys are captured per modal, but the right-click branch and handle_right_click (src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:121) never look at app.properties, conflict_dialog, sudo_prompt, drive_dialog, cloud_login or pending_drop. With a modal up, the content-area hit test resolves to the modal's scrim zone, falls into ClickedRow::None, clears the selection and opens the empty-area menu (New File, New Folder, Paste, Select All, Open Terminal, window controls); sidebar hit tests use raw rects, so drive Format/Eject/Properties and Empty Trash are reachable too. Paste from that menu overwrites app.pending_paste while a conflict dialog is waiting, abandoning the first operation mid-way; a second drive dialog can replace the one on screen.

**Trigger.** Open Properties (or reach a Replace/Skip conflict dialog), right-click anywhere in the file area: a context menu pops up above the dialog, the selection underneath is cleared, and New File / New Folder / Select All act on the folder behind the modal.

```rust
if state.right_clicked {
    state.right_clicked = false;
    ...
    handle_right_click(
        app,
        context_menu,
```

**Skeptic's check.** The right-click branch (1002-1029) and handle_right_click (context_menu.rs:121-502) contain no checks for properties, conflict_dialog, sudo_prompt, cloud_login, drive_dialog, pending_drop or quick_look. Sidebar hit tests use raw layout rects; the content hit test reads input.zone_at, which returns the modal's zone id, and most of those ids are below 1000 so it falls to ClickedRow::None: selection cleared and the empty-area menu opens as an xdg_popup above the modal, with New File/Folder, Select All, Paste, drive Format/Eject reachable. (ZONE_PROPS_ICON_BASE 2000+ is even misread as a file row.) The pending_paste overwrite needs an extra Copy first because the clipboard is taken during a paste, so that sub-case is contrived. Main effect is a menu acting on the view behind a modal; I rate it low rather than medium.

**Fix.** At the top of the right-click branch: if any of quick_look, conflict_dialog, sudo_prompt, cloud_login, drive_dialog, properties, pending_drop is set, clear right_clicked and skip. Local and mechanical.

### wayland-26: lantern.toml is read and parsed four times on every rendered frame

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:1061` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it low; fix safe: yes
- **Status:** FIXED. Palette and opacity are resolved when lantern.toml's stamp changes (checked every 500 ms), not four file reads per frame.

**What is wrong.** FoxPalette::current() calls active_variant(), active_accent() and active_background_color(), each of which does its own std::fs::read_to_string of the config and a line scan; background_opacity() is a fourth. This happens on the UI thread for every frame, so 240 file reads per second while anything animates or the pointer moves, even though a 500 ms theme poll already exists a few lines earlier.

**Trigger.** Move the mouse over Fox or hold it in any animating state: strace shows four open/read/close of ~/.lantern/config/lantern.toml per frame.

```rust
lntrn_theme::background_opacity()
...
*palette = FoxPalette::current();
```

**Skeptic's check.** FoxPalette::current() (/home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-ui/src/gpu/palette.rs:110-120) calls active_variant, active_accent and active_background_color; each does its own read_to_string of lantern.toml (lntrn-theme/src/config.rs:62, 197, 226), and background_opacity() at wayland_loop.rs:1057 is a fourth read (a fifth at 736 on each left press). No caching in either crate. So four file reads and scans per rendered frame on the UI thread. The file is small and page-cached, so the cost is modest, but it multiplies with the stuck-60fps bugs (wayland-3, wayland-11).

**Fix.** Refresh palette and opacity inside the existing 500 ms theme poll (or on config mtime change), compare the whole resolved palette/opacity rather than just the variant to decide the redraw kick, and reuse the cached values per frame. Theme changes then land within 0.5 s instead of on the next frame, which is the existing latency for the idle case anyway.

### wayland-27: Each watcher-triggered reload also spawns a git scan, with no rate limit, for as long as files keep changing

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:1299` (Wayland core)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category perf; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): Same change as fs-18: watcher-driven reloads are capped at one per second with a closing reload (src/dir_watch.rs ReloadGate, driven from src/housekeeping.rs), each reload asks for at most one git scan and scans never overlap (src/git_status.rs). reload_pending no longer keeps the loop at 60 fps: a pending reload is a timer, not an animation. The mtime fallback stands back while a watcher reload is already scheduled, so it cannot bypass the rate limit.

**What is wrong.** The debounce is 120 ms from the first event of a burst, and Modify events re-arm it immediately, so during a sustained write (a download, an extraction) the listing is rebuilt about eight times a second on the UI thread and each rebuild starts a new thread running `git rev-parse` (and, inside a repo, a second rev-parse plus `git status`). Scans are not cancelled, only their results are discarded by generation, so slow scans overlap. reload_pending() also keeps needs_anim true for the whole duration.

**Trigger.** With Fox showing ~/Downloads (after one mouse move so frames are running), download a large file: Fox redraws at 60 fps, re-lists the directory and forks git roughly every 120 ms until the download ends.

```rust
if dir_watcher.take_due_reload() {
    app.reload();
    ...
    git.refresh(&app.current_dir);
}
```

**Skeptic's check.** dir_watch.rs: the watcher flags Create/Remove/Modify, take_due_reload fires 120 ms after the first event of a burst and the next event re-arms it, and reload_pending keeps needs_anim true. wayland_loop.rs:1299-1307 does app.reload() (synchronous listing on the UI thread) plus git.refresh, and GitStatus::refresh (git_status.rs:53-66) spawns a thread running git rev-parse (plus a second rev-parse and git status inside a repo) with no in-flight guard; stale results are only discarded by generation. So during a sustained write in the viewed directory Fox renders at 60 fps, re-lists about 8 times a second and forks git each time. Today this needs frames to be running (one mouse move) because of wayland-2; fixing wayland-2 makes it happen in the background too. I agree there is no feedback loop: the watch is non-recursive so .git/index.lock is not seen from the work tree, and inside .git `rev-parse --show-toplevel` fails before status runs.

**Fix.** Skip git.refresh while a scan is in flight (track an `in_flight` generation and re-run once when it lands), cap watcher-driven git scans to about one per second, and consider a longer or trailing debounce for pure Modify(Data) events on files already listed. Changes refresh timing, so tune by eye. Also set GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 on the git commands (see extra finding).

### fs-22: Git results never request a redraw: previous repo's branch chip stays on screen, badges appear only after input

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:1310` (Filesystem layer)
- **Verdict:** confirmed; category logic; finder rated it low; fix safe: no
- **Status:** FIXED. Batch 2 (C5): src/git_status.rs: the worker is a bg::Task, so a finished scan wakes the loop; poll() returns true only when branch or badges actually changed and the loop then draws a frame (src/housekeeping.rs). enter(dir) drops the previous folder's badges at once and its branch unless the new folder is inside the same work tree (so the chip does not blink while walking through a repo). Housekeeping::git_follow is also called just before render_frame in src/wayland_loop.rs, so the first frame of a new folder never shows the old branch chip. A result for a folder the view has left is discarded.

**What is wrong.** The scan is started after the frame that shows the new directory has been rendered, the old branch and marks are not cleared when a non-slow directory is entered, the worker has no way to wake the loop, and git.poll() neither sets frame_done nor feeds needs_anim. So the frame on screen after a navigation still shows the previous directory's branch, and the new result sits in the channel until an unrelated input event causes two more frames. poll_dir_loads for Tab/Inactive targets has the same problem because dir_loading() only counts Focused loads.

**Trigger.** With the keyboard, go from a repo folder up into a non-repo folder and leave the mouse still: the status bar keeps showing the old branch name. Enter a repo the same way: no badges or branch until the pointer moves.

```rust
git.poll();
if app.current_dir != git_dir {
    git_dir = app.current_dir.clone();
    last_git_poll = Instant::now();
    if slow_dir {
        git.clear();
    } else {
        git.refresh(&app.current_dir);
    }
```

**Skeptic's check.** git.poll() (1310) runs after render_frame and neither sets frame_done nor feeds needs_anim (1356-1379 has no git term). refresh() bumps the generation but leaves the old branch/marks in place (only clear() resets them, used for slow dirs), and the worker has no way to wake the loop. After a keyboard navigation (Backspace/go_up, path bar Enter) the press frame renders with the old branch, the scan is spawned after that render, and the key-release frame renders before git.poll applies the result, so the status bar keeps the previous repo's branch (and new badges are missing) until another input event. The 5 s re-scan sits behind the same frame gate as wayland-2. Marks are keyed by absolute path, so stale marks do not show on the wrong files; the visible defect is the branch chip and late badges.

**Fix.** Clear branch/marks when the directory changes (generation bump as clear() does, keeping in_repo logic intact), make poll() return whether it applied a result and set frame_done from it, and give the worker a wake-up (write the watcher's eventfd or a second one in the poll set) with git.poll hoisted above the frame gate. The wake-up part is new plumbing, hence not mechanical.

## Unverified extras (42)

Noticed by the skeptics while verifying other findings. Nobody has double-checked these.

### extra-1: Paste into the folder the items were copied from, then Replace, deletes the originals

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:217`; severity critical.

advance_paste and apply_single_conflict_action never check whether a source and its computed target are the same path. Copy a file and paste in the same directory: target = dest.join(name) is the source itself, target.exists() is true, and the conflict dialog opens. Replace (which is also the Enter key, key.rs:172-176) runs remove_file or remove_dir_all on the target, which is the source, and then queues a copy or rename from a path that no longer exists; the error is discarded. PendingPaste::new (conflict.rs:62) does no filtering. The drag-drop path has a same_dir guard (drag_drop.rs), the clipboard path does not. Read from code, not executed.

**Trigger.** Select a file or folder, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V in the same folder, press Enter on the 'already exists' dialog (or click Replace). The item is permanently deleted and nothing is created. Same with Ctrl+X then Ctrl+V in place.

**Fix.** In advance_paste and apply_single_conflict_action, when src == target (compare paths, and dev+inode via symlink_metadata for case-insensitive drives) never offer Replace: for Copy go straight to unique_keep_both_path, for Cut skip the item. Not verified by running the app.

### extra-2: Pasting into the folder the item was copied from and choosing Replace (the Enter default) deletes the source itself

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:456`; severity critical.

paste() has no src == target check. Ctrl+C then Ctrl+V in the same folder makes target = dest.join(name) equal to the source, target.exists() is true, and the conflict dialog opens. key.rs:171-176 maps Enter to ConflictAction::Replace. apply_single_conflict_action (456-462) and the apply-to-all branch (220-227) then run remove_file / remove_dir_all on the target, which is the source. The queued copy (or the inline rename for Cut) fails with NotFound and the error is swallowed (ops.rs:196, file_ops.rs:87). The file or whole folder is gone, not in Trash. Traced by reading; not executed.

**Trigger.** Select a file or folder, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V in the same directory, press Enter (or click Replace) in the conflict dialog.

**Fix.** In advance_paste, when the resolved target equals the source (compare canonical paths), never offer Replace: treat Copy as Keep Both (duplicate) and Cut as a no-op skip. Independently stop deleting at resolution time (see fileops-7).

### extra-3: Replace on a paste or drop into the file's own folder deletes the source

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:456`; severity critical.

advance_paste computes target = dest.join(name). When the destination directory is the source's own parent, target is the source itself, target.exists() is true and the conflict dialog opens. Replace (apply_single_conflict_action :456-462, and the apply-to-all branch :220-226) runs remove_file / remove_dir_all on that target, i.e. on the source, then pushes the (src, target) pair to the copy worker or calls rename, both of which fail silently because the source no longer exists. Enter in the conflict dialog is bound to Replace (key.rs:172). Traced by reading; likely the 'paste finding' that input-5 refers to.

**Trigger.** Select a file or folder, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V in the same folder (the usual way to make a duplicate). The conflict dialog appears; press Enter or click Replace. The original is permanently deleted and nothing is copied.

**Fix.** In advance_paste, before the exists() check, detect src == target (compare paths, ideally canonicalised): for Copy go straight to unique_keep_both_path (a duplicate), for Cut skip the item as a no-op. Never run the Replace removal when target and source are the same path.

### extra-4: full_due never clears after a failed pass: once Fox has been up 6 hours, any persistent per-file failure makes the loop run a full Firestore list about twice a second

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:168`; severity high. **FIXED.** A failed full pass is retried after 15 minutes instead of on every loop turn; last_full advances on any non-rate-limited full attempt.

In run_loop, last_full is only updated in the Outcome::Ok arm. After FULL_EVERY (6 h) full_due stays true until a pass succeeds, and the condition `pending_dirty || poll_due || full_due` is then true on every iteration of the 500 ms recv_timeout loop. reconcile_with returns Err whenever any single path fails (unwritable target, 402, oversized file, EISDIR temp rename), so a persistent per-file failure turns into back-to-back sync_pass(full=true) calls, each a full list of the collection (4,370 docs in the current index, 4,198 of them tombstones) plus a full local scan and a retry of every failing transfer. That exhausts the 50k/day Firestore read quota in roughly a dozen passes, after which both machines are 429-limited. Traced in code; not observed live.

**Trigger.** Leave Fox (or the --desktop instance) running for more than 6 hours while one file in ~/Cloud persistently fails to sync.

**Fix.** Set last_full = Instant::now() whenever a full list was fetched successfully, regardless of reconcile outcome (have sync_pass report that), and apply a minimum retry interval after Outcome::Err.

### extra-5: Pasting a copied folder into itself (or into one of its sub-folders) starts a runaway recursive copy that cannot be cancelled

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:153`; severity high.

paste() has no check that the destination lies inside a source (only drag_drop::valid_dest has one). In Copy mode the pair (X, X/X) goes to the worker, and copy_dir_recursive (file_ops.rs:60-72) creates the target and then reads the source, which now contains the target, so it descends X/X/X/... until the path exceeds PATH_MAX. I replicated copy_dir_recursive in the scratchpad: 1984 nested levels and 1984 copies of the folder's file before ENAMETOOLONG. With a large folder this fills the disk first. The worker only checks cancel between top-level items, and the final error is swallowed by run_copy.

**Trigger.** Ctrl+C on a folder, open that folder (or any folder inside it), Ctrl+V.

**Fix.** In paste() and start_drag_drop(), drop or refuse any source for which dest == src or dest.starts_with(src) (after canonicalising), with a message. Add a cancel check inside copy_dir_recursive.

### extra-6: Copy worker swallows every error except PermissionDenied: disk-full, I/O errors and unsupported entries end as a silent partial copy

- **Where:** `src/ops.rs:196`; severity high.

run_copy matches `Err(_) => {}` for anything but PermissionDenied and still reports Done; poll_op_progress shows nothing. copy_dir_recursive aborts the whole folder at the first failing entry with `?`. I verified std::fs::copy on a symlink that points to a directory returns an error (`the source path is neither a regular file nor a symlink to a regular file`), so a folder containing such a link (or a FIFO/socket) stops copying part-way, as does ENOSPC, leaving a truncated destination that looks finished. The same pattern is in queue_cut (file_ops.rs:87) and duplicate_selected.

**Trigger.** Copy a folder to a stick that fills up, or copy a project folder that contains a symlink to a directory. The progress strip completes and no error is shown.

**Fix.** Collect (path, error) for failed items in OpProgress::Done and show a summary dialog; remove a partially written target file on failure; in copy_dir_recursive recreate symlinks with std::os::unix::fs::symlink instead of calling fs::copy on them, and continue past per-entry errors while recording them.

### extra-7: Every Fox process, including file pickers and the desktop-mode daemon, starts its own sync thread and begins with a full Firestore list

- **Where:** `src/wayland.rs:441`; severity high.

wayland::run calls app.init_cloud() unconditionally before the pick/desktop branches, so each window, each --pick dialog and the --desktop daemon spawns fox-cloud-sync. run_loop's first action is sync_pass(.., full=true) (sync.rs:130) even though the remote index was just loaded from disk: 4,370 document reads per process start today (tombstones are never purged: 4,198 of 4,370 docs are deleted=true, so this cost only grows). About eleven launches a day use the whole free-tier read quota; this is the '50k reads' failure class again, and the log holds 2,680 'status code 429' pass failures and 862 quota pauses. The concurrent threads also share manifest.json/remote-index.json/~/Cloud with no cross-process lock: besides lost manifest updates, two processes resolving the same conflict can have the second one rename the freshly downloaded remote version over the first one's '(conflict from …)' copy, destroying the local edits the copy was meant to keep, and then upload those bytes under the old local sha.

**Trigger.** Open and close file dialogs or Fox windows a dozen times in a day; or have a window and the desktop instance running when a both-changed conflict is resolved.

**Fix.** Run sync in exactly one process (flock on ~/.lantern/cloud/sync.lock; others only read status, and pick mode never starts it). Make the start-up pass a delta pull when a non-empty index with a cursor was loaded, keeping the periodic full list for drift. Purge old tombstones.

### extra-8: Root mode has no on-screen indicator

- **Where:** `src/app/nav.rs:282`; severity medium.

root_mode changes Delete and Move to Trash into permanent sudo deletes (file_ops.rs:566-568, 698-702) and routes paste, rename and new file/folder through sudo, but the only thing that would show it is window_title(), which is #[allow(dead_code)] and never called. Nothing in render.rs or sections reads root_mode. open_as_root toggles it for the current directory whenever no folder is selected (file_ops.rs:1008).

**Trigger.** Right-click a file (not a folder) and choose Open as Root: nothing visibly changes, but the current directory is now in root mode and the next Delete is a permanent removal (silent if a sudo ticket is cached).

**Fix.** Show a clear ROOT badge in the nav bar or path strip while root_mode is set, and do not toggle root mode from Open as Root when the target is not a folder.

### extra-9: Favourites whose target is missing at startup are dropped and then permanently erased by the next favourites save

- **Where:** `src/app/places.rs:24`; severity medium.

load_favorites_from filters the persisted list with `.filter(|p| p.exists())`. The in-memory list is then shorter than settings.favorites, and the next time the favourites change (wayland_loop.rs:759, 877, 947) settings.favorites is overwritten from app.favorites_paths() and saved, so the filtered-out entries are gone for good. p.exists() is also a stat on the UI thread at startup, which blocks on a slow mount.

**Trigger.** Add a folder on a USB stick, the phone or an unmounted network share to Favorites, quit, start Fox with the device absent, then add or reorder any other favourite: the device favourite never comes back.

**Fix.** Keep all persisted favourites in memory and only mark missing ones as unavailable (greyed row), or keep the unfiltered list for saving; do the existence check off the UI thread or skip it for slow paths.

### extra-10: Rename survives a tab switch from the context-menu chevrons: the next click or Enter renames an unrelated file in the other tab

- **Where:** `src/app/tabs.rs:68`; severity medium.

switch_tab calls focus_pane(Left), which is a no-op unless split view is on, then swaps entries via sync_from_tab. It does not commit or cancel an active rename, and sync_from_tab bypasses apply_listing (the only place `renaming` is re-resolved by path). handle_right_click has no renaming check, and the popup menu's controls row offers CTX_PREV_TAB/CTX_NEXT_TAB (context_menu.rs:537-553). After the switch app.renaming still holds the old index and rename_buf the old file's name. handle_click commits a rename on any click outside the input (click.rs:155-158), and commit_rename (app/edit.rs:34-69) renames entries[idx] of the NEW tab to rename_buf.

**Trigger.** With two tabs open: press F2 on a file in tab 1, right-click, click the next-tab chevron in the menu's top row, then click anywhere (or press Enter). The file at the same index in tab 2 is renamed to the tab-1 file's name (undoable, but silent).

**Fix.** In switch_tab and close_tab, cancel (or commit) an active rename and path edit before sync_to_tab, the same way focus_pane does; also clear selection_anchor/last_click_idx/pending_open there.

### extra-11: Tree view: switching, opening or closing a tab leaves the previous tab's tree on screen

- **Where:** `src/app/tabs.rs:75`; severity medium.

tree_entries is rebuilt only in cycle_view_mode (entering Tree), toggle_tree_expand, apply_listing and pick-mode startup. switch_tab, new_tab and close_tab call sync_from_tab, which copies path, entries and scroll offset but never rebuilds the tree, and the loop treats a current_dir change as navigation and does not reload (wayland_loop.rs:1327-1337). In Tree view the path bar shows the new tab's directory while the rows, and what a click launches or drags, are still the old tab's. Not verified visually.

**Trigger.** Tree view with two tabs on different folders: click the other tab. The listing does not change until something in the new folder changes on disk or the view is toggled.

**Fix.** In sync_from_tab (or its three callers), call rebuild_tree() when view_mode == ViewMode::Tree.

### extra-12: Remote doc paths are joined onto ~/Cloud without validation, so a doc path with '..' or a leading '/' writes or deletes outside the sync root

- **Where:** `src/cloud/reconcile.rs:147`; severity medium. **FIXED.** Remote doc paths are validated (no leading /, no empty/./.. component, no NUL) when parsed and again in the action loop.

abs_for is cloud_root().join(rel) with rel taken verbatim from FileDoc.path. PathBuf::join with an absolute path replaces the base, and '..' components are not rejected. download_remote then create_dir_all + write + rename at that location, and delete_local remove_file's it. Only the account owner's machines write these docs, so it needs a compromised account or a corrupted doc, but the effect is arbitrary file write/delete as the user.

**Trigger.** A Firestore doc under users/{uid}/files whose path field is '/home/alva/.zshrc' or '../.ssh/authorized_keys' with a matching blob.

**Fix.** In from_fields or at the top of reconcile_with, drop any doc whose path is absolute or has a component that is not Component::Normal, and log it.

### extra-13: Pasting a copied folder into itself recurses until the path is too long

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:60`; severity medium.

paste() has no check that the destination is inside a source (drag-drop has valid_dest, drag_drop.rs:15-17). copy_dir_recursive creates the destination first and then iterates the source, which now contains the destination. Reproduced the function in a standalone rustc test: copying Proj into Proj/Proj returned an InvalidFilename error after creating 793 nested levels, each level re-copying what the previous one held.

**Trigger.** Ctrl+C a folder, open that folder, Ctrl+V. A chain of hundreds of nested copies is created before the worker errors out; with large contents this can fill the disk.

**Fix.** In paste() (and the root-mode branch), drop or refuse sources for which dest.starts_with(src), the same rule valid_dest applies to drops.

### extra-14: Starting a second paste while a copy is running replaces op_progress and orphans the first worker

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:317`; severity medium.

finalize_paste assigns self.op_progress = Some(handle) (lines 291 and 317) without checking for a running operation, and nothing blocks Ctrl+V or a drop while one is active. The earlier OpHandle is dropped, so its worker keeps copying but its Done payload is never processed: no undo entry, permission failures never escalated, no final reload, and the progress strip and cancel button now belong to the new operation only. Traced by reading.

**Trigger.** Paste a large folder, and while the progress strip is showing paste something else.

**Fix.** Queue operations (Vec<OpHandle> polled in order) or refuse a new paste with a message while op_progress is Some.

### extra-15: Duplicate copies a regular file synchronously on the UI thread and records undo before the copy has succeeded

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:830`; severity medium.

duplicate_selected runs std::fs::copy(&path, &dest) inline for files (830), while directories get a detached thread. A multi-GB file freezes the window for the whole copy, and on a jmtpfs mount the read pulls the entire file under the global lock. The undo entry is pushed unconditionally (832-835) even when the copy failed or, for directories, is still running, so Ctrl+Z can remove_dir_all a tree that the background thread is still writing. I did not check whether Duplicate is hidden on slow mounts.

**Trigger.** Select a large video and choose Duplicate: Fox stops responding until the copy finishes.

**Fix.** Route duplicate through spawn_copy_worker with pre-resolved (src, dest) pairs so it gets the progress strip, cancel, and an undo entry only for what was created.

### extra-16: Privileged operations run sudo cp/mv/rm synchronously on the UI thread

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:1036`; severity medium.

priv_run calls sudo::try_cached and submit_sudo_prompt calls sudo::run_with_password directly from the click/paste handlers. Both go through run_with_sudo, which spawns sudo and blocks in child.wait_with_output() for every sub-command. The window cannot repaint or dispatch Wayland events until the whole cp -r / mv / rm -rf finishes; the `submitting = true` relabel at line 1063 is never drawn. A Failed result is only eprintln!'d, so the user sees nothing.

**Trigger.** Enable Open as Root (or paste into a root-owned folder with a cached sudo ticket) and paste a multi-GB folder: the window is frozen for the whole copy and the compositor may flag it unresponsive.

**Fix.** Run the sudo commands on a worker thread with a result slot polled by the loop (same shape as the copy worker / OpHandle), keep the modal in a 'working' state, and surface Failed messages in a dialog.

### extra-17: Directory listing uses lstat, so symlinks to folders are shown and handled as files

- **Where:** `src/fs.rs:99`; severity medium.

list_directory takes is_dir, size and mtime from DirEntry::metadata(), which does not follow symlinks. A symlink to a directory is listed among the files with is_dir = false, cannot be entered, expanded in Tree view or chosen as a folder in a picker, and activating it goes to the default-app / xdg-open path. A symlink to a file shows the link's own size and mtime. It is also why a symlink to a device or FIFO reaches the thumbnail workers (media-6, media-7). Not verified visually.

**Trigger.** ln -s ~/Projects ~/link and open the home folder: 'link' appears among the files with a size of a few bytes and clicking it does not navigate into it.

**Fix.** When the entry's file type is a symlink, call std::fs::metadata(&path) for is_dir/size/mtime and fall back to the lstat data if the target is missing; skip the extra stat on slow mounts.

### extra-18: Properties on an audio file reads its tags on the UI thread, with no slow-mount check

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:223`; severity medium.

FileProperties::from_path calls AudioEdit::load, which calls audio_tags::read synchronously (properties_audio/mod.rs:114): open, read the ID3 block plus 128 KiB, and read the last 128 bytes. properties.rs has no is_slow_path check (grep finds none). On jmtpfs the first read downloads the whole file under the device lock, so the window freezes for the full download. For directories the same function does read_dir(path).count() on the UI thread (line 187). The thumbnail path already refuses audio on slow mounts (icons.rs:133-147); Properties does not.

**Trigger.** Open a phone's Music folder, right-click a large MP3 or WAV, choose Properties: Fox is frozen until the phone has delivered the whole file.

**Fix.** When fs::is_slow_path(path), skip AudioEdit::load (or load it on a worker and fill the section when it lands, as the save path already does) and skip the directory item count.

### extra-19: Properties on an audio file reads its tags synchronously with no slow-path check

- **Where:** `src/properties.rs:223`; severity medium.

PropertiesState construction calls properties_audio::AudioEdit::load(path) for every non-directory, which calls audio_tags::read (File::open plus reads of the tag region and, for WAV, one pread per chunk across the file) on the calling thread. There is no fs::is_slow_path guard here, unlike icons.rs:133 and file_info.rs:59. On a jmtpfs phone mount the first read downloads the whole file under the mount's global lock, so opening Properties on an MP3/WAV on the phone freezes the window for the duration. Not traced further than the call site (I did not confirm which thread constructs PropertiesState, but nothing here spawns one).

**Trigger.** Browse the phone over MTP, right-click a large .wav or .mp3, Properties.

**Fix.** Skip AudioEdit::load when fs::is_slow_path(path) (show the dialog without the tag editor), or load the tags on a worker thread and fill the section when it lands.

### extra-20: Redo of a copy pairs sources with created targets by position, so a skipped or failed item shifts every later file under the wrong name

- **Where:** `src/undo.rs:235`; severity medium.

UndoAction::Copy stores `sources` = all originals (file_ops.rs:337-340) and `created` = only the targets that succeeded (ops.rs:191). execute_forward zips the two lists. If any item was skipped in the conflict dialog or failed, the lists have different lengths and order, and redo copies source[i] into created[i]: for originals [a, b, c] with a skipped, redo writes a's content to dest/b and b's content to dest/c.

**Trigger.** Paste three files where the first one conflicts and is skipped; Ctrl+Z; then redo. The destination files exist under the right names with the wrong contents.

**Fix.** Store Vec<(PathBuf, PathBuf)> of (source, created) pairs in UndoAction::Copy, built by the worker from the pairs that actually succeeded.

### extra-21: Stale context_override_paths after a dismissed context menu makes Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X / drag act on the previously right-clicked tree row

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:403`; severity medium. **FIXED.** The path override is cleared whenever the context menu is no longer open.

Right-clicking a nested tree row sets app.context_override_paths. It is cleared only at the start of the next right-click (302), after a menu Action (835) or in focus_pane. Closing the menu with Esc or a click outside leaves it set, and selected_paths() (select.rs:231) returns the override ahead of the real selection. copy_selected and cut_selected use selected_paths(), as does the drag source list at wayland_loop.rs:394.

**Trigger.** Tree view: right-click a file inside an expanded folder, press Esc, click another top-level item, Ctrl+X, navigate, Ctrl+V. The file that moves is the one that was right-clicked earlier, not the selected one.

**Fix.** Clear context_override_paths (and context_target) whenever the context menu closes without an action: in the Esc path, the click-outside paths and the popup_closed handler.

### extra-22: Watcher events do not refresh the listing while the window is idle

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:122`; severity medium.

The watcher's eventfd wakes poll() and drain_fd() runs, but nothing sets state.frame_done, so the loop hits 'if !state.frame_done { continue; }' at line 161 and never reaches dir_watcher.take_due_reload() at line 1299. needs_anim includes reload_pending() but is only recomputed at the end of a rendered frame. The dirty flag stays set, so the reload happens about 136 ms after the next input event. The 3 s mtime fallback poll, the git poll and poll_dir_loads for Tab/Inactive targets sit behind the same gate. grep shows frame_done is set only by dispatch handlers and loop lines 146, 158, 551, 1260, 1263.

**Trigger.** Open ~/Downloads in Fox, move the pointer to another window, let a download finish or create a file from a terminal. The listing does not change until the pointer moves over Fox or a key is pressed.

**Fix.** After poll(), set state.frame_done = true when the watcher fd was readable (or whenever dir_watcher.reload_pending() is true), so the debounce and reload run without input. Do the same wake for worker results that are not covered by needs_anim.

### extra-23: Directory watcher wake-up never schedules a frame: an idle window does not refresh until the next input event

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:122`; severity medium.

When the watcher's eventfd fires, the loop only calls dir_watcher.drain_fd(). take_due_reload, poll_op_progress, git.poll, the 3 s mtime fallback and the 2 s device poll all live after `if !state.frame_done { continue; }` (line 161), and frame_done is only set by Wayland input/configure events or by needs_anim, which was computed at the end of the last rendered frame (false when idle). So the wake is wasted: a file landing in the viewed folder, a finished git scan, or a hot-plugged drive is not shown until the pointer moves over the window. This is the `gated behind a frame` class again. Traced by reading, not run.

**Trigger.** Leave Fox open and untouched on a folder, create a file there from a terminal: the listing does not change until the mouse moves over the window.

**Fix.** After draining, set state.frame_done = true when dir_watcher.reload_pending() (and likewise wake for pending git results and the periodic device/mtime timers), or move those polls above the frame_done gate and request a frame only when they change state.

### extra-24: Directory-watcher wake is thrown away while idle: listing, device hot-plug, mtime fallback and git polls only run on rendered frames

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:161`; severity medium.

The poll loop wakes on the watcher eventfd and calls dir_watcher.drain_fd() (lines 122-124), but nothing sets state.frame_done, so `if !state.frame_done { continue; }` (161) skips the rest of the iteration. dir_watcher.take_due_reload() (1299), the 3 s mtime fallback (1338), the 2 s drive/phone poll (1350), the git poll (1319) and poll_op_progress all live after that gate, and needs_anim (which includes dir_watcher.reload_pending()) is only recomputed at the end of a rendered iteration. grep shows frame_done is set only by Wayland input/configure handlers, the popup backend, the theme poll and lines 1260/1263. So with an idle window the eventfd is drained and the reload never happens until an input event arrives.

**Trigger.** Open ~/Downloads in Fox, move the pointer away and do not touch the window, then save a file there from a browser (or plug in a USB stick): the listing and sidebar do not update until the pointer moves over the window or a key is pressed.

**Fix.** Right after the poll/dispatch and before the gate: `if dir_watcher.reload_pending() { state.frame_done = true; }`, and move the timed housekeeping (device poll, mtime fallback, git poll) above the gate so it sets frame_done when something changed. The 500 ms idle timeout already provides the periodic wake.

### extra-25: Rename accepts path separators, so a typed name can move the file elsewhere

- **Where:** `src/app/edit.rs:38`; severity low. **FIXED.** Save picker accepts only a plain file name (same guard as rename).

commit_rename builds new_path = old.parent().join(&rename_buf) with no validation. '/' is typeable (keycode 53). A name such as ../x or sub/x moves the file to another directory, and a name starting with '/' replaces the whole path because PathBuf::join with an absolute path discards the base. Names '.' and '..' are not rejected either.

**Trigger.** F2 on a file, type /tmp/x, Enter: the file is moved to /tmp/x and disappears from the folder.

**Fix.** Reject (or strip) names containing '/' and the names '.' and '..' before building new_path, keeping the rename box open.

### extra-26: A failed rename still pushes an undo entry and reports nothing

- **Where:** `src/app/edit.rs:84`; severity low.

perform_rename does `let _ = std::fs::rename(&from, &to);` and then unconditionally pushes UndoAction::Rename. A rename that fails (permission denied, read-only mount) is silent, has no sudo fallback unlike the other operations, and leaves a bogus undo entry; a later Ctrl+Z then runs rename(to, from), which can move an unrelated file that has since appeared at `to` onto the original name. In root mode the entry is pushed before pkexec has even run.

**Trigger.** Rename a file in a directory you cannot write to: nothing happens and no message is shown, and the undo stack now holds a rename that never took place.

**Fix.** Push the undo entry only when rename returned Ok; on PermissionDenied route through priv_run like the other operations, and surface other errors.

### extra-27: WAV in-place tag write truncates and rewrites before checking the 4 GB limit

- **Where:** `src/audio_tags/wav.rs:341`; severity low. **FIXED.** The 4 GB check happens before the file is touched, and the tail is written before the file is trimmed. (Extra-27 is the same.)

In the in_place branch of wav::write, set_len(keep_end) and write_all_at(&tail, keep_end) run before the 'new_len - 8 > u32::MAX' check at line 344. When the check fails the function returns an error after the old tags were already cut off and the new tail written, with the RIFF size field not updated and no sync. The rewrite branch checks first. Read from code, not executed.

**Trigger.** Save tags (for example add large cover art) on a WAV whose size is just under 4 GB: the dialog reports 'WAV would exceed 4 GB' but the file has already been modified.

**Fix.** Compute new_len and do the size check before set_len and the tail write.

### extra-28: Leftover debug log: every clipboard copy is appended to /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log

- **Where:** `src/clipboard.rs:46`; severity low. **FIXED.** The clipboard debug log (every copied string to /tmp) is gone.

WaylandClipboard::set_text opens /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log in append mode and writes the copied text on every call, and the clipboard thread's dbg() helper writes there too. The file is in a shared directory under a fixed name, grows without bound, and records everything Fox puts on the clipboard (paths, names, checksums). Combined with the level-triggered checksum-row click it gets several lines per click.

**Trigger.** Use Copy Path / Copy Name or click the SHA-256 row, then read /tmp/fox-clipboard-debug.log.

**Fix.** Remove the debug writes, or gate them behind an env var and write under ~/.lantern/log/ instead of /tmp.

### extra-29: Sync stat cache uses whole-second mtime plus size, so a same-size rewrite within the same second is never noticed

- **Where:** `src/cloud/manifest.rs:72`; severity low. **FIXED.** Stat cache refuses entries for files modified in the last 2 seconds (racy-clean rule), so a same-second same-size rewrite is re-hashed.

cached_sha returns the manifest sha when (size, mtime in seconds) match (reconcile.rs:74-91 truncates mtime with as_secs). upload_local records the scan-time (size, mtime) and download_remote records the post-rename stat. A file rewritten with the same length inside that same second keeps the old sha forever and is never uploaded, with no later re-hash to catch it.

**Trigger.** A program rewrites a fixed-size file (a config toggle, a fixed-width record) twice within one second around a sync pass.

**Fix.** Store mtime in nanoseconds, and do not trust a cache entry whose mtime is within a couple of seconds of the time it was recorded (the racy-git rule).

### extra-30: Rate-limit detection is a substring match for "429" over an error string that contains file paths, URLs and sha256 hex

- **Where:** `src/cloud/sync.rs:244`; severity low. **FIXED.** Rate-limit detection matches ureq's exact 'status code 429'; quota failures are sorted first in the summary.

classify_failure treats the pass as RateLimited when format!("{e:#}").contains("429"). The aggregate error from reconcile_with joins every failing path and its full error text, which includes blob URLs with 64-hex-digit hashes and arbitrary file names. Any failure whose path or hash contains the digits 429 (for example IMG_4291.jpg, or one of the logged 402 lines whose blob hash contains '…504294a…') pauses sync for 10 minutes, doubling to 2 hours, and shows the quota status for a non-quota problem. Conversely a real 429 on one file mixed with other errors is fine, but the classification is accidental either way.

**Trigger.** A file named e.g. 'scan_4290.pdf' fails to upload for any reason: status shows rate-limited and sync pauses with exponential backoff.

**Fix.** Carry the HTTP status structurally (a typed error or a flag set where ureq::Error::Status(429, _) is seen in http.rs) instead of searching the message text.

### extra-31: Conflict dialog shows sizes with the drive formatter: everything under about 512 KB reads `0 MB` on both sides

- **Where:** `src/dialogs.rs:1144`; severity low. **FIXED.** Conflict dialog sizes show B/KB; folders show just 'Folder'.

format_meta_line uses fs::format_size, which only has MB and GB with zero decimals for MB. For typical documents both `Existing` and `Replacement` read `File · 0 MB`, so the dialog that guards a destructive choice gives no way to tell the two apart by size.

**Trigger.** Paste a small text file over another small text file: both lines say `File · 0 MB`.

**Fix.** Use a formatter with B/KB/MB/GB steps for file metadata (keep format_size for drives), and for folders show an item count instead of the inode size.

### extra-32: A FIFO or other special file inside a copied folder blocks the copy worker forever

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:68`; severity low.

copy_dir_recursive sends everything that is not a directory to std::fs::copy, which opens the source for reading before checking its type; opening a FIFO read-only blocks until a writer appears. The fox-copy thread never returns, the progress strip stays up, cancel is only checked between top-level items, and op_progress.is_some() keeps the loop animating. Inferred from std's Linux copy implementation; not executed.

**Trigger.** Copy a folder that contains a named pipe (some build trees and application runtime dirs have them).

**Fix.** Check entry.file_type(): copy only is_file(), recreate symlinks, and skip (and report) FIFOs, sockets and device nodes.

### extra-33: Trash info Path= is written raw but percent-decoded on restore

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:605`; severity low.

trash_selected writes 'Path={}' with entry.path.display(), unencoded, while read_trashinfo_path runs percent_decode on the value. A name containing a literal percent followed by two hex digits is therefore restored under a different name, and other trash implementations will decode Fox's entries the same wrong way. A newline in a file name also breaks the line-based format. Read from code, not executed.

**Trigger.** Trash a file named 'report%20final.txt', then Restore it from Trash: it comes back as 'report final.txt'.

**Fix.** Percent-encode the path when writing the .trashinfo (everything outside the unreserved set and '/'), matching the decoder and the XDG trash spec.

### extra-34: xdg-open fallback and Open Terminal children are never reaped

- **Where:** `src/file_ops.rs:733`; severity low.

desktop::launch_app waits on its child, but the fallback paths spawn 'xdg-open' (and open_in_terminal spawns lntrn-terminal) inside a thread with Command::spawn() and drop the Child without wait(). There is no SIGCHLD handler or SIG_IGN in the crate (grep), so each exited child stays as a zombie until Fox exits. Sites: file_ops.rs:733, 748, 1015; wayland_actions/click.rs:423; wayland_loop.rs:974; wayland_actions/context_menu.rs:568, 578.

**Trigger.** Open several files that have no default app in Fox's own lookup, or use Open Terminal Here and close the terminals; 'ps' shows <defunct> children under lntrn-file-manager.

**Fix.** Call .status() or keep the Child and wait() in the same throwaway thread, as launch_app already does.

### extra-35: Background git status runs without GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0

- **Where:** `src/git_status.rs:159`; severity low. **FIXED.** git runs with GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0.

git_stdout runs `git status --porcelain` with the default environment, so each scan takes .git/index.lock and may rewrite .git/index. Scans run on every directory change, every watcher reload and every 5 s inside a repo. This cannot loop through Fox's own watcher (non-recursive watch, and inside .git rev-parse --show-toplevel fails first), but it can collide with a git command the user runs in a terminal at the same moment ('Unable to create .git/index.lock: File exists') and it triggers other tools that watch .git. This is the known ecosystem bug class from the lntrn-code Git panel.

**Trigger.** Leave Fox on a repo folder with frames running (mouse over it) and run git commit/rebase in a terminal repeatedly; occasionally the command fails on index.lock.

**Fix.** Add .env("GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS", "0") to the Command in git_stdout.

### extra-36: Root Empty Trash builds an `sh -c rm -rf` script from an unquoted $HOME

- **Where:** `src/sudo.rs:188`; severity low. **FIXED.** Empty Trash as root passes the trash path as a positional parameter, built from dirs_home().

PendingPrivOp::EmptyTrash formats `rm -rf -- {home}/.local/share/Trash/files/* ...` into a string run by `sudo sh -c`. HOME is interpolated without quoting, so a home path containing a space or shell metacharacters is word-split or interpreted by a root shell (for `/home/first last` the first argument to rm -rf becomes `/home/first`). Rare on Linux, but the command runs as root.

**Trigger.** A user whose HOME contains a space empties a Trash holding root-owned items.

**Fix.** Do not use a shell: pass the two directories to `find <dir> -mindepth 1 -delete` as separate argv entries, or single-quote-escape HOME before formatting.

### extra-37: Redo of a Create action recreates extension-less files as folders and dotted folder names as files

- **Where:** `src/undo.rs:197`; severity low.

execute_forward for UndoAction::Create decides by `path.extension().is_none()`: no extension means create_dir_all, otherwise write an empty file. New File creates `New File` (no extension), so undo then redo produces a folder named `New File`; a folder renamed to something like `v1.2` before the action is recorded would come back as a file.

**Trigger.** Right-click, New File, Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Shift+Z: a folder appears instead of the file.

**Fix.** Store the kind in the action (Create(Vec<(PathBuf, bool /*is_dir*/)>)) and recreate accordingly.

### extra-38: Search field has no Ctrl guard: Ctrl+A / Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V type the letter and start a search

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:416`; severity low. **FIXED.** Ctrl chords no longer type letters in the sudo, cloud-login and search fields (real paste is still not implemented).

The search branch ignores `ctrl` and its default arm inserts keycode_to_char(key, shift), same pattern as the sudo and cloud-login fields. Because the branch returns unconditionally, file shortcuts (Ctrl+C/V/X/A, Ctrl+Z) are also unreachable while the search bar is open, even with an empty query showing the normal listing.

**Trigger.** Open search, press Ctrl+V: 'v' is typed into the search box and a recursive search for 'v' starts.

**Fix.** Return early (or fall through to the browse shortcuts when search_buf is empty) when ctrl is held in the search branch.

### extra-39: Delete key inside the Trash view re-trashes the item: renames it and writes a .trashinfo whose restore path is the Trash itself

- **Where:** `src/wayland_actions/key.rs:751`; severity low.

KEY_DELETE calls trash_selected unconditionally; only the context menu checks in_trash(). Inside Trash/files the collision loop sees the item itself, picks `name.1`, writes name.1.trashinfo with Path=.../Trash/files/name and renames the item. The original .trashinfo is orphaned, the item shows under a new name, and Restore moves it back to Trash/files/name instead of its original location (a second Restore is needed).

**Trigger.** Open Trash, select an item, press Delete, then try Restore.

**Fix.** In the key handler mirror the menu: `if app.in_trash() { delete with confirmation } else { app.trash_selected() }`, or make trash_selected return early when in_trash().

### extra-40: wl_data_source is never destroyed after a drag

- **Where:** `src/wayland_dispatch.rs:491`; severity low. **FIXED.** The data source is destroyed when its drag finishes or is cancelled.

The DndFinished/Cancelled handler resets flags but never calls source.destroy(), and the source created at wayland_loop.rs:470 is dropped client-side without a destroy request. Each drag-out leaks one protocol object on both sides for the life of the connection.
