**Trigger.** Drag files out of the Fox window repeatedly; objects accumulate (visible with WAYLAND_DEBUG=1: no wl_data_source.destroy is ever sent).

**Fix.** Call _source.destroy() in the DndFinished and Cancelled arms.

### extra-41: Scrollbar press math uses the full-window content rect instead of the focused pane's

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:688`; severity low. **FIXED.** Scrollbar press uses the same pane-aware rect as the bar and the drag. (The unfocused pane's scrollbar still starts a rubber band.)

On a ZONE_SCROLLBAR press the handler builds `Scrollbar::new(&content_rect(wf, hf, s), view_content_height(app, content.w, s), ..)`, while render.rs and the drag update at lines 303-307 use active_content_rect (pane rect in split view, pick-bar-aware in pick mode). In split view the full width gives a different grid column count and therefore a different total height, and the right pane's rect also has a different y/h, so the thumb hit-test and the track-click target are computed against geometry that is not on screen. apply_icon_zoom (1408) has the same content_rect assumption.

**Trigger.** Split view with a Grid pane that scrolls: click the scrollbar track or grab the thumb; the view jumps to a different offset than the click position, then snaps again on the first drag frame.

**Fix.** Use the same `active_content_rect(app, wf, hf, s)` helper in the press branch (and in apply_icon_zoom) that the drag branch already uses.

### extra-42: Icon-zoom slider clamps the scroll offset against the grid height in List and Tree views

- **Where:** `src/wayland_loop.rs:1406`; severity low. **FIXED.** Icon-zoom clamp uses the real view height and the focused pane's rect.

apply_icon_zoom always calls ScrollArea::apply_scroll with grid_content_height and layout::content_rect, regardless of app.view_mode and of split/pick layout. In List or Tree view the grid height is usually much smaller than the real content height, so moving the zoom slider while scrolled down pulls scroll_offset back toward the top.

**Trigger.** List view in a long folder, scroll to the bottom, open the View menu and move the Scale slider: the list jumps upward.

**Fix.** Clamp with view_content_height(app, content.w, s) and active_content_rect(app, wf, hf, s) instead of the grid-only math.

## Refuted

- **sections-19** Pinned-tab drag release hit-tests against tab rects built without the close-button width (and with the non-split tab bar rect): The finder missed TabBar's default: /home/alva/Projects/Lantern-DE/lntrn-ui/src/gpu/tabs.rs:58 initialises `closable: true`. render.rs:485 passes .closable(app.tabs.len() > 1). A reorder needs at least two tabs (target_idx != src_idx), so in every case where the release code matters both builders have closable = true and tab_rects() are identical. The tab_bar_rect vs pane_tab_bar_rect difference is also harmless: tab_rects uses only rect.x/y/h and split_pane_cols returns lx = SIDEBAR_W*s, the same x.

- **wayland-14** poll_op_progress() result is ignored, so the frame that shows the finished copy (or the sudo fallback prompt) is not drawn until the next input event: The return value of poll_op_progress is indeed dropped, but the described symptom does not follow. In the iteration where the worker's Done is polled, needs_anim still holds the value computed at the end of the previous iteration, which was true because op_progress was Some (1368). So line 1249 already requested a frame callback before the commit at 1252, and the wl_callback handler (wayland_dispatch.rs:233-244) sets frame_done when it fires, which renders one more frame with the finished state and the sudo prompt. The only case with no pending callback is an operation that starts and finishes inside a single frame, and that frame is always followed by an input event (button/key release, pointer enter after the popup closes). For a hidden window nothing is drawn regardless.

## Coverage

- **File operations** (36 raw findings): All six files in my area were read in full, line by line. Outside my area I read only the parts needed to see how the code is driven, not the whole files: src/wayland_loop.rs (lines 60-190 event loop, 366-435 drag detection, 500-680 key repeat/drop modal, 905-965 drop release, 1200-1460 post-render polling and needs_anim), src/wayland_actions/key.rs (140-270 dialog/sudo key handling, 640-755 shortcuts), src/wayland_actions/click.rs (30-110 dialog clicks, 490-505 cancel zone), src/dialogs.rs (720-1050 sudo prompt and conflict dialog drawing), src/sections/status.rs (170-293 progress strip), src/app/mod.rs (180-340 fields, 520-586 dirs_home), src/desktop.rs (215-315 launch_app), src/fs.rs (1030-1105 is_slow_path), src/properties.rs (1090-1150 checksum section), src/main.rs (350-468), src/wayland_dispatch.rs (420-500 DnD). app/split.rs was only grepped for root_mode/focus_pane. Nothing was executed against the real app or real mounts: the vfat case-insensitive rename finding, the jmtpfs commit-on-close behaviour behind the folder-move finding, and the pkexec/no-polkit-agent finding rest on code reading plus system inspection (strings on pkexec, process list, tree grep), not on a live reproduction. Standalone rustc experiments in the scratchpad confirmed: copy_dir_recursive aborting on directory/dangling symlinks and dereferencing file symlinks, the self-copy recursion (396 nested copies), fs::copy blocking on a FIFO, rename replacing an existing file, fs::write truncation, EACCES when moving a read-only directory, remove_dir_all on a symlink removing only the link, the percent-decode mismatch, and tar option injection through file names. The scratch files were removed afterwards.

- **Filesystem layer** (36 raw findings): All six files in my area were read in full, plus the six src/app files listed above. Outside my area I read only the parts that drive my code: src/wayland_loop.rs lines 40-270, 368-392, 884-990, 1108-1122 and 1200-1460 (not the 270-880 input/render middle); src/wayland.rs 440-595; src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs 130-430, 540-665 and 690-840; src/wayland_actions/click.rs 360-480; src/file_ops.rs 680-770 and 985-1025; src/sections/sidebar.rs 270-427; short excerpts of src/render.rs, src/sections/status.rs, src/preview.rs, src/properties.rs, src/icons.rs and src/app/edit.rs. Not read: thumbs.rs, quick_look.rs, ops.rs, cloud/, audio_tags/, dialogs.rs.

Verified by experiment in the scratchpad (since removed): git 2.55 runs core.fsmonitor and clean filters from repo config under Fox's exact `git status` arguments; plain `git status` rewrites .git/index and GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 stops it; `rev-parse --show-toplevel` fails inside .git and returns the canonical path through a symlink; DirEntry::metadata does not follow symlinks; `&"123é"[..4]` panics; HashMap iteration order of two keys varies per instance; the O(n·m) selection carry timings; lsblk and xdg-mime timings.

Not verified on hardware (no phone or USB stick attached, app not launched per the rules): the jmtpfs behaviours behind the stale-mount, multi-device and unmount-on-exit findings, and the busctl 25 s timeout during a real format. Those are marked medium confidence where the conclusion depends on them.

Checked and found not to be a problem: the list_directory sort comparator is a total order (no sort_by panic), and sorting cost is negligible (3.6 ms for 10k names). The git-status/.git/index watcher feedback loop is not reachable because the watch is non-recursive and git refuses to run status inside .git. launch_app reaps its child and passes the file as a separate argv entry, so there is no shell or argument injection through file names. The dir watcher creates one inotify thread and one eventfd for the process lifetime, so nothing leaks per navigation. is_path_mounted's canonicalize should not block on a busy FUSE mount with glibc 2.43 (realpath uses readlink per component), so I did not report it as a freeze; strace was not available to confirm.

- **Cloud sync core** (26 raw findings): All six files in my area were read in full, plus the other six files in src/cloud to see how the core is driven. Outside the area I only read excerpts: src/app/nav.rs lines 1-110 (init_cloud, login flow), src/main.rs argument parsing and main(), src/wayland.rs around line 441 (the unconditional init_cloud call), and grep hits in src/wayland_loop.rs, src/undo.rs, src/git_status.rs, lntrn-portal/src/filechooser.rs and lntrn-notepad/src/actions.rs. I checked the vendored ureq 2.12.1 agent defaults and notify 6.1.1 inotify watch mask by grep, not by a full read.

To ground the findings I read, for counts and timestamps only, the user's ~/.lantern/cloud/manifest.json, ~/.lantern/cloud/remote-index.json and ~/.lantern/log/fox-cloud.log, and listed ~/Cloud (file counts, sizes, empty dirs). Nothing was modified. One rustc experiment was compiled in the scratchpad; it created and removed a throwaway directory under the system temp dir.

Not verified: the Firestore and Storage security rules (affects who can exploit the path-traversal finding), the real clock offset between the two machines (affects the clock-skew finding), and the cause of the logged 402 responses. hash.rs has no findings. I did not report issues that live purely in auth.rs/session.rs/firestore.rs (for example auth::refresh deleting the cached session on any HTTP error status from the token endpoint, the session file being written before its permissions are tightened, and Firestore doc-id limits for long or `__x__` names); they are outside this area and only noted here.

- **Cloud transport and auth** (25 raw findings): Read only the cloud-relevant parts of the callers, not the whole files: src/main.rs (lines 330-470: arg parsing, main, run_cloud_login), src/wayland.rs (236-565: run(), where init_cloud is called and exit handling), src/app/mod.rs (320-500: cloud fields, App::new, Drop), src/dialogs.rs (1-290: CloudLoginDialog and draw_cloud_login), src/wayland_actions/key.rs (250-350: login key handling), src/wayland_actions/click.rs (100-126), src/wayland_loop.rs (80-135, 470-560, 1335-1380 plus greps for frame_done/held_key), src/wayland_dispatch.rs (350-370), src/sections/status.rs (1-110), src/render.rs (950-985), src/app/places.rs (210-225). In the vendored ureq 2.12.1 I read agent.rs defaults, error.rs Display, response.rs LimitedRead/stream_to_reader/into_json and unit.rs connect_inner, not the whole crate. Not verifiable without network access (so not reported as defects): how Firestore's REST frontend treats '%2F' inside a document ID (doc_id_from_path's `.replace('/', \"__\")` is dead code after percent-encoding; sync evidently works for nested paths so the literal-%2F ID is accepted), whether Firebase Storage accepts 'Bearer' for ID tokens and which status it returns for expired tokens, and whether Storage rejects an oversize upload before or after receiving the body. For evidence I read, read-only and with keys redacted in output, ~/.lantern/log/fox-cloud.log and ~/.lantern/cloud/{manifest,remote-index}.json, and checked file modes under ~/.lantern/config; nothing in the repo or those files was modified, cargo was not run, the app was not launched. Several findings (reconcile.rs, sync.rs, manifest.rs, mod.rs) sit in the neighbouring sync-logic area; I reported them because I traced them end to end while following how the transport is driven, so expect overlap with that auditor.

- **Cloud sync end-to-end scenarios** (27 raw findings): Callers read only in part: src/main.rs lines 320-469 (cloud login/logout CLI and main), src/wayland.rs lines 380-469 (where init_cloud is called), src/dialogs.rs lines 1-65 (login dialog state; the draw code was not read). The login key/click handlers (src/wayland_actions/key.rs, click.rs), the status pill (src/render.rs ~960, src/sections/status.rs) and src/git_status.rs were located by grep only. Outside the crate I read the ureq 2.12.1 agent defaults and the notify 6.1.1 inotify watch mask in ~/.cargo/registry, and grepped sibling crates for who spawns lntrn-file-manager. To ground the scenarios I inspected, read-only and in aggregate, ~/.lantern/cloud/manifest.json, ~/.lantern/cloud/remote-index.json and ~/.lantern/log/fox-cloud.log (counts and timestamps; the session file with tokens was not opened), and made one HEAD request to www.google.com to compare the local clock with the Date header. Nothing was run against Firestore/Storage and the app was not launched, so server-side behaviour (billing per returned doc, 402/429 meaning, how %2F in a document id is decoded) rests on the log and the API documentation, not on a live test. The full_due hot loop, the path traversal, the conflict-copy overwrite and the clock-skew revert are derived from the code path only; I found no instance of them in the log. Scenarios that came out clean: a hard failure or bad page in list_all/runQuery aborts the pass before reconcile (index untouched); edit-vs-delete across machines revives the edited file when the snapshot is fresh; names differing only by case are distinct paths on both machines.

- **App state** (29 raw findings): All ten files in my area (plus src/app/dir_load.rs, which is in the same module) were read in full. Outside my area I read in full the callers listed above. Only partially read / grepped, to confirm specific behaviour: src/wayland_dispatch.rs (lines 225-559 read, first 224 lines not), src/render.rs (tab bar 470-575, content zones 596-905, drag ghost and modal sections 1036-1056, 1318-1340, 1380-1500, 1538-1608; the rest only grepped for index uses), src/sections/split.rs (grep only), src/dialogs.rs (conflict dialog title code only), src/undo.rs (undo/redo entry points and rename reverse only), src/ops.rs (worker spawn signature only), src/cloud/{http,auth,session,mod}.rs (sign-in, try_load and timeout checks only), src/layout.rs (grid_columns / preview_effective_w / zoom helpers only), src/desktop.rs (launch_app only), src/pick_bar.rs (grep), lntrn-ui input.rs and scroll.rs (zone lookup and clamp functions only). Not looked at: views.rs, icons.rs, thumbs.rs, preview.rs, quick_look.rs, properties*.rs, git_status.rs, clipboard.rs, popup_backend.rs, audio_tags, cloud sync internals. Nothing was built with cargo or run; three findings are marked medium confidence because they are reasoned from code rather than observed (dir-load thread pile-up, cross-instance phone unmount succeeding, stale Drive index). Verified by standalone rustc experiments in the scratchpad: mpsc try_recv after sender drop, remove_dir_all on `dir/.` and `dir/..`, Path join/equality for '.', '..' and '/', String::remove/insert panics mid-char, and the O(n²) selection benchmark. I also read ~/.lantern/config/file-manager.json (read-only) to check which triggers are live on this machine: pinned_tabs is empty, sort is date/desc, favourites include /etc/portage.

- **Input handling** (35 raw findings): All five files in my area were read in full, as were the callers and App methods they drive (listed above). Read only in part, to confirm specific behaviour: src/render.rs (lines 20-210, 560-1040, 1440-1672: zone registration, rename input, drop modal; the rest not read), src/quick_look.rs (open, loaders; not the draw code), src/properties.rs (from_path and populate_media_info only), src/properties_audio/mod.rs (AudioEdit::load only; handle_dialog_key, which my key handler delegates to, was NOT audited), src/dialogs.rs (the two can_submit functions only), src/sudo.rs (NewFile/NewFolder command construction only), src/fs.rs (list_directory head and is_slow_path only), lntrn-ui context_menu.rs (lines 360-800) and scroll.rs (Scrollbar), lntrn-compositor/src/input/keyboard.rs and vendored Smithay keyboard focus code (to confirm modifier presses are forwarded and that leave carries no key releases). Not read at all: src/undo.rs (Ctrl+Z is dispatched from key.rs but the undo actions themselves were not checked), src/clipboard.rs, src/views.rs, src/sections/* other than split.rs, src/icons.rs, src/thumbs.rs, src/file_info.rs, src/lantern_config.rs (Set as Wallpaper writes config through it), src/cloud/*, src/audio_tags/*, src/git_status.rs, src/settings.rs, src/pick_bar.rs, src/popup_backend.rs, src/preview.rs. Several findings have their root cause outside my five files (file_ops.rs, app/edit.rs, app/tabs.rs, app/mod.rs, wayland_loop.rs, wayland_dispatch.rs, quick_look.rs); they are reported because the trigger is a click, key, menu action or drop from my area, and they may duplicate other auditors. Nothing was run against the real app: std behaviours (String::remove/insert off a char boundary, fs::write truncation, remove_dir_all on 'dir/.' and 'dir/sub/..', mpsc try_recv after sender drop, copying a folder into itself) were confirmed with small rustc programs in the scratchpad. Not verified on real hardware: case-insensitive behaviour on vfat/exFAT, jmtpfs lock behaviour for stat, and what the compositor does with a resize request on a maximized window.

- **Wayland core** (30 raw findings): All six files in my area were read line by line. Outside my area I read only what was needed to trace how my code is driven:

- Partially read: src/ops.rs (all but the first ~10 lines skimmed), src/cloud/sync.rs (lines 51-251), src/app/split.rs (restore_split, active/inactive_content_rect only), src/file_ops.rs (poll_op_progress, submit_sudo_prompt, clipboard helpers), src/fs.rs (detect_drives, detect_phones, unmount_phone, is_slow_path), src/render.rs (scrollbar zone, frame submit/error path), src/layout.rs (title bar and content rect helpers), src/desktop.rs (launch_app), src/dialogs.rs (can_submit only), src/pick_bar.rs (grep only).
- src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs: grepped for toplevel/running/popup usage, not read; handle_right_click and handle_ctx_event were not audited.
- Sibling crates: lntrn-ui context_menu.rs lines 400-720 only; lntrn-theme config.rs excerpts; lntrn-compositor input/keyboard.rs (lines 30-330 and 540-650), text_inject.rs, handlers/xdg_shell.rs resize_request, input/spawn.rs; vendored Smithay data_device StartDrag handler; wayland-backend 0.3.17 flush.
- Nothing was run against a live compositor. The two std behaviours I relied on (args() panic on non-UTF-8, eprintln! panic on a dead stderr/pty) were confirmed with a scratch rustc binary.

Observations I did not report as findings because I could not give a trigger or they sit in another crate:
- render.rs:1532 only logs a wgpu SurfaceError; GpuContext::resize early-returns on unchanged size, so a Lost/Outdated surface would never be reconfigured. I have no concrete way to provoke that on Wayland.
- lntrn-ui ContextMenu::close_popups destroys the root xdg_popup before its submenus (not-topmost-popup order). Smithay only enforces that in its grab path and Fox popups take no grab, so it is harmless on Lantern.
- lntrn-ui popup backend never destroys the popup's wp_viewport, and from_parent_shared does not clamp to the texture limit.
- wl_seat Capabilities creates a new wl_pointer/wl_keyboard on every event without releasing the old ones; the Lantern compositor adds its pointer and keyboard once at startup, so this cannot trigger here.
- git_status.rs runs `git status` without GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 on a 5 s timer inside repos; that belongs to whoever owns git_status.rs.

- **Rendering and layout** (20 raw findings): My three files (src/render.rs, src/layout.rs, src/views.rs) were read line by line. Outside my area I read only what was needed to trace how they are driven, so these are partial: src/wayland_loop.rs (lines 1-470, 540-710, 895-1460; the key/DnD/release sections between were not read), src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs (lines 200-470 plus grep hits), src/icons.rs (all but about 30 lines around the SVG rasterisers), src/sections/split.rs (lines 40-449), src/sections/status.rs (first 80 lines), src/file_ops.rs (lines 560-700 only), src/main.rs (lines 30-230), src/app/tabs.rs, nav.rs, split.rs, places.rs, dir_load.rs, select.rs (targeted excerpts), src/properties.rs (grep hits and lines 1485-1515), lntrn-render/draw/src/painter.rs (lines 30-470). Not read at all: src/sections/sidebar.rs and sections/icons.rs bodies, src/quick_look.rs, src/dialogs.rs, src/pick_bar.rs, src/thumbs.rs, and lntrn-ui's TabBar, TextInput, TitleBar and ContextMenu widgets, so tab-bar/rename-input internals and their own clipping were not verified. Nothing was run: no cargo, no app launch, no screen capture. Two standalone checks were done in the scratchpad: a rustc build of format_date (timing and UTC output) and a Python hmtx reader for Inter glyph advances; I also touched and removed one probe file in the scratchpad to confirm btrfs accepts far-future mtimes. Findings that depend on runtime behaviour I could not observe (FUSE/jmtpfs blocking on getxattr, wgpu surface errors, glyph-atlas texel totals, the exact-width wrap probability) are marked medium confidence."

- **UI sections** (21 raw findings): All eight files in my area were read line by line. Outside it I read only what was needed to see how the area is driven, so these are partial: src/wayland.rs (lines 400-595 only: app setup, pick-mode init, result output); src/wayland_actions/key.rs (20-130 and 380-755: text-editing and pick keys, not the rest); src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs (1-380 plus grep of the drive/favorite action arms, not the full action handler); src/fs.rs (145-770 and 940-1105: drives, phones, mount helpers, slow-path cache; skipped list_directory and the mount/format/unmount commands); src/file_info.rs (1-200); src/views.rs (1-120, row hit rects only; draw_content_list/tree bodies not audited); src/wayland_dispatch.rs, src/ops.rs, src/cloud/sync.rs, src/settings.rs (grep-level only); lntrn-text/src/lib.rs (40-580) and engine.rs (queue_entry only); lntrn-render/tex/src/lib.rs (render_pass only); lntrn-ui tabs.rs (tab_width/tab_rects only); lntrn-notepad/src/actions.rs (picker call sites only). Nothing was run except two tiny rustc experiments in the scratchpad (breadcrumb skip maths, String::remove/insert on a non-boundary, HashMap order stability); no cargo, no app launch, no screen capture, so every rendering/overlap claim is from geometry arithmetic, not observation. Findings whose blocking behaviour depends on jmtpfs internals (sidebar is_path_mounted, folder xattr reads) are marked medium confidence for that reason. Several findings sit in files owned by other auditors (key.rs, click.rs, select.rs, context_menu.rs, wayland_loop.rs, icons.rs, app/split.rs, app/tabs.rs); I reported them because they are reached directly from the picker bar, sidebar, split chrome or grid, so expect duplicates. The status bar itself came up clean: counts and the selected-size sum are O(entries) per frame with a u64 sum that cannot realistically overflow, total - dirs cannot underflow, percent() guards total == 0, and file_info.get never touches the file on the render thread.

- **Dialogs and Properties** (30 raw findings): My two owned files (src/dialogs.rs, src/properties.rs) were read line by line. Outside my area I followed the callers and read only what drives the dialogs: src/wayland_loop.rs lines 60-1095 and 1180-1460 (not 1-59 or 1096-1179), src/render.rs lines 1340-1673 only, src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs lines 100-918 (not 1-99), src/wayland_actions/click.rs lines 1-220 only, src/fs.rs lines 150-520, 760-950 and 1040-1105, src/icons.rs lines 86-430 in parts, src/file_info.rs lines 30-110, src/main.rs zone/constant tables only, src/audio_tags/mp3.rs first 46 lines, src/wayland_dispatch.rs lines 300-335. Several findings sit in files owned by other auditors (file_ops.rs, key.rs, context_menu.rs, edit.rs, places.rs, nav.rs) because the dialogs in my area drive them; expect overlap. Verified by standalone rustc experiments in the scratchpad: remove_dir_all on \"dir/.\" and \"dir/..\", fs::write truncation, same-path remove-then-copy, String::remove/insert off a char boundary, and the year-loop timing; a far-future mtime was confirmed settable on this btrfs. Nothing was run against the app itself, so findings marked medium confidence (sudo ticket scoping, busctl 25 s timeout, ureq default timeouts, device-name reuse, libinput tap timing, stale context index, Keep Both sudo fallback) are from code reading plus documented behaviour. Two scratch files (future, future2) and small test binaries remain in the scratchpad dlgprops directory; nothing in the repository was touched."

- **Audio tags and tag editor** (20 raw findings): All ten files in my area were read in full. Outside it I read only the parts that drive this code: properties.rs (lines 60-295, 500-790), render.rs (55-85, 1376-1535), wayland_loop.rs (85-160, 635-680, 1225-1380), wayland_actions/context_menu.rs (715-745), wayland_actions/key.rs (28-40, 325-365), icons.rs (49-200, 217-245, 425-460), fs.rs (is_slow_path only). Not read: lntrn-render TexturePass / TextRenderer internals (I relied on the existing frame.flush between the two texture passes and on TextInput's own clip), lntrn-text queue(), and the image crate's default decode limits used by decode_preview / prepare_artwork (thumbs.rs sets explicit limits, the Properties preview does not; I did not verify what the default cap is).

Verification: I did not run cargo. I compiled the real src/audio_tags module unmodified into a standalone rustc harness in the scratchpad (via #[path]) and reproduced the WAV truncation (data size 0, >4 GiB wrapped and saturated sizes on sparse files), the swallowed-tags and missing-pad cases, v1 track / IPRT reappearing, v2.2 frame loss, multi-APIC collapse, TDRC truncation, the ID3-size-overrun overwrite, the old-iTunes v2.4 misparse, the symlink replacement, the read-only error-after-commit and the keys overflow. A mutation fuzz in a debug build (overflow checks on) found no panics: 400k iterations of id3 parse -> apply -> build -> parse, 60k read-only file iterations and 40k read+write file iterations over WAV and MP3 seeds. So the parsers look panic-safe in release for malformed input; the problems are in the write path and in lossy round-tripping.

Not reproduced, reasoned from code only: the MTP freeze, the FIFO hang, the concurrent-save temp-file interleaving, the stale art_rect, the 60 fps picker loop and the edits-during-save race.

Out of scope but worth knowing: properties_audio/draw.rs uses LABEL_FONT 15 px and CAPTION_FONT 13 px ("Click to change", "Remove", field labels, the stream-facts line, status text), below the 18 px minimum in CLAUDE.md; I did not file it because style was excluded. Clicking outside the dialog or the backdrop closes Properties and silently drops unsaved tag edits (wayland_loop.rs:641, 668); that is a design choice in another auditor's file, so not filed either.

- **Thumbnails, preview, Quick Look, icons** (22 raw findings): All four files in my area were read in full. Outside the area I read only what was needed to see how the code is driven: src/render.rs (all except roughly lines 1015-1059), src/wayland_loop.rs (lines 1-330, 480-585, 1000-1460; the middle click/drag sections were skipped), src/fs.rs (list_directory and the slow-mount section only), src/properties.rs (FileProperties construction, header icon and icon-picker sections only), src/wayland_actions/context_menu.rs (right-click preamble and row matching only), src/wayland_actions/click.rs (Quick Look and tree branches only), src/sections/split.rs (inactive-pane icon loading only), src/views.rs (fallback-icon and format_date parts only), src/layout.rs (grid/preview sizing only), src/audio_tags/{mp3,wav,id3}.rs (read paths only, skimmed for panics; no finding raised there), lntrn-ui input.rs/scroll.rs/text.rs and lntrn-text engine.rs (specific functions only). Third-party sources checked in ~/.cargo/registry: image-0.25.9 (ImageReader::decode, thumbnail sizing), resvg-0.47.0 src/image.rs, tiny-skia-0.12.0 pixmap.rs. Not verified by running: the MTP/jmtpfs stalls (findings 1 and 2) are reasoned from kernel FUSE behaviour and the repo's own comments, not reproduced on a phone; SVG rasterization cost per folder icon was not measured; glyph widths for the preview-name wrap were estimated. Two experiments were run in the scratchpad only (ffmpeg on generated test clips, a rustc timing of the year loop plus a touch/stat of an extreme mtime); my scratch files were removed afterwards. No repository file was modified, cargo was not run, the app was not launched."
